The key is used by the import op, flat node names, lane addresses, `m2 bounce --track` and `--place`, bounce provenance, `TimelineTrack.module` and UI selection. `slug()` is deleted.
- *Today:* since 8b.4 `Module.muted` is in the IR, written only when true, so an IR without it keeps its JSON and its hash (`a_song_without_muted_has_the_same_ir_hash`). The checker refuses it on `song` and on a module that isn't a track, and lists the muted tracks in `Flat.muted` (`core/ir/src/check.rs`).

### Derived identity

**ID-12.** A number local to one build (site ids, call ordinals, node numbers, `Origin`, control ids such as `song.ts#2/0.hats.pan`) MUST NOT be used with any revision but the one it came from. To use a target in a later revision, resolve it again from its source position.
- *Why:* one inserted call renumbers every later site in the project.
- *Today:* `Origin.site` counts across the whole project in load order (`core/script/src/lib.rs`). At `92a19a3`, `revert()` carried the byte range forward but passed the stale origin to `apply()` (`core/session/src/edit.rs:752-761`): after one soundless call was added to `song.ts`, undoing a knob in `tracks/bass.ts` failed on every retry while `can_undo` said true. Step 1 fixed this: a value's undo recorded no origin. It found the control written at the carried bytes in the accepted revision and used that control's current origin. Since step 6 an undo commits its change's stored inverse, which names no origin, and both undo and `can_undo` refuse when its bases no longer apply ([WRITE-21](#undo); `Session::undo` and `Session::can_undo` in `core/session/src/session.rs`).

**ID-13.** A site's identity across two revisions is its file and the point of its argument list's `(`, in the reference coordinates of the comparison ([WRITE-8](#expectations-and-the-delta)). Nothing numbers sites. A Tx is judged in its own edits' coordinates; every other comparison, the engine's included, uses `Edits::between`: a line diff whose hunks a token-level Myers diff refines. A `key()` frame is named by its key. An unmatched or ambiguous site is new, and its state resets.
- *Why:* the arena keeps state by identity, and guessing would hand one oscillator's state to another. A `(` survives a wrap, a changed callee and an edited argument, wherever a diff puts the closing brackets. A session counter would be history: two processes would number one revision's sites differently and hash one Tx's delta differently, and the engine only ever compares what plays with what is accepted.
- *Today:* matches since step 7: `Site.open` is each call's `(` (`core/script/src/sites.rs`), delta names spell a call by it (`Side::called` in `core/session/src/tx/delta.rs`), and `Edits::between` refines each run of changed lines by a Myers diff over identifiers and numbers, strings and single punctuation characters; when more than 500 lines differ (a whole file re-indented, say), the token diff takes every line between the common first and last lines. Each run of inserted or deleted tokens is slid to start at a word. When it repeats a call's `name(`, the old `(` goes to the call whose arguments match the old call's: the inner one for a wrap in the same callee, the outer one for a call of the same callee put inside it. When neither matches, both calls are new (`slide` and `pick` in `core/session/src/text.rs`). So an effect wrapped around an instrument keeps the instrument, through the app's Tx or a file save (G7b's wrapped instrument); Add Effect and Remove Effect keep the user's own effect beside another of the same kind (`crates/editor/src/effect_tests.rs`); and two literals edited on adjacent lines are two value changes. What still resets: a call cut and pasted, one whose own tokens a reformat reorders, and every call in a stretch of changed lines where more than 500 tokens differ. What can still keep the wrong call: a call of the same callee written on lines of its own, above another (`const pre = delay(hook, …)` above `delay(hook, …)`) or around it with the old call moved to the next line. There the line diff decides before the tokens are compared, and the new call takes the old one's instance. Sites are still numbered per build (ID-12), for the tables of one build only.

**ID-14.** For repeated unkeyed sites in one parent instance (loops, `map`), state is kept by index only while the occurrence count and the surrounding structure are unchanged. If the count changes, every occurrence resets. `key()` or a keyed iteration helper keeps state through reorders.
Applied when the engine matches instances; a `key()` frame has no occurrence; a plugin with a state path is matched by that path ([ID-15](#derived-identity)).
- *Why:* an index alone would hand state to a neighbour.
- *Today:* matches since step 7: each delta name carries how many times each positional segment's call ran in its parent and how many nodes share it but for its occurrence (`Facts.counts`), and `continues` keeps a pair only while those are the same (`core/session/src/tx/continues.rs`). std's mixer runs its strips in a `map`, so adding a channel resets every strip; that is inaudible today (`std:gain`'s smoother starts at its target, and pan holds no state). Keying each strip by its channel isn't done: `key()`'s keys are unique per key frame, not per component call, so two mixers in one module with a channel key in common would be `key.duplicate` (tried in 8b.3b). So a channel's first pan renames the panners after it, and its planner asserts by channel ([WRITE-10](#expectations-and-the-delta)). A plain function (not a `component()`) makes no frame, so when one parent calls it more than once, its calls are matched by call order: swapping two such calls swaps their state. Make it a `component()`, or wrap each call in `key()`, to keep them apart.

**ID-15.** Derived ids are built as follows:
- SDK frames record `{ site, componentId, occurrence, key? }`. The frame chain continues inside component bodies, packages included.
- InstancePath = module key / frame chain.
- ControlId = InstancePath + the slot the callee declares ([PKG-3](#component-descriptors)).
- RuntimeId: a plugin with a state path ([ID-8](#keys)) has its module key and that path as its RuntimeId, whatever its place. Any other node's RuntimeId is InstancePath / the site of its `kernel()`, `plugin()`, `poly()` or `feedback()` call, matched by the same rules as [ID-13](#derived-identity) and [ID-14](#derived-identity); segments are `key:<k>` or `<ComponentId>@<file>:<point>#<occurrence>`. A body that calls `kernel("std:svf")` twice has two sites, so two RuntimeIds. A voice's RuntimeId is its slot in its kept pool.
- CellId = a cell's index in the active plan.
- *Why:* the arena key must be defined by the same matching as every other site, with no separate notion of a kernel's "role".
- *Today:* since step 5 the IR's `Module.frames` lists every component call and `key()` frame a module's build evaluated, as `{ site, component, occurrence, key, parent }` (and, for a component call with options, which argument held them, `options`), and a node names its innermost frame (`Node.frame`); a declared control's const names its frame and slot in its origin (`Origin.control`), and its delta name, InstancePath / `@<slot>`, is its ControlId (`core/session/src/tx/delta.rs`). A call site is taken only by the call it was set for: the SDK compares the arguments it received with what the call's instrumentation recorded, so a function native code calls (`xs.map(lowpass)`) takes no site and makes no frame (`core/script/src/sites.rs`, `takeSite` in `core/script/sdk/core.ts`). Sites are still numbered per build (ID-12). The session groups the view and recognises effects by frame chains (`Module::chain` in `core/ir`, `calls_of` in `crates/editor/src/effect.rs`), and `Node.within` (the component sites of the chain) is deleted.

**ID-16.** A project file MUST NOT contain a derived id. The saved roots are module keys, lane keys, plugin keys and `key()` frames. An optional editor index may live in `.music2/`; it is rebuildable at any time and used only for layout continuity. Seeds and musical defaults MUST NOT be derived from derived ids. Every id-bearing field in the format v1 schemas is a key, a clip id or a CLAP parameter id, and strict parsing ([FMT-8](#strictness)) admits no other field.
- *Why:* derived ids change with every refactor; saved ones must not. A schema that has no field for a derived id is checkable; "no file contains one" by looking at the files is not.
- *Today:* matches: nothing derived is saved, and a test refuses a derived id in every schema (`core/song/src/data/tests.rs`).

### Renames

**ID-17.** Renames work like this:
- **A label rename** in the UI is an `Identical` Tx.
- **A key rename** in the app is one Tx that edits the code and renames `data/<key>/`. It may also write `layout.json` ([WRITE-3](#the-two-write-primitives)).
- **A key rename by hand** leaves the data unclaimed: `data.unclaimed`, with a Relink fix ([DIAG-6](#fixes)): for a track key, of the whole `data/<key>/` folder; for a lane key, of one lane file.
- *Today:* matches for label renames and renames by hand: a label rename is an `Identical` Tx (step 6); a track key renamed in code leaves its folder `data.unclaimed`, offered whole as one `Rename` of the folder on its directory hash ("Move all to …"), and a lane key its lane file, in the app and in every CLI command that opens the project ([DIAG-4](#delivery), [DIAG-6](#fixes)). Restore isn't built.
- *Today, from polish (A5):* the app's key rename is built as **Change track ID…**, offered only in the Code view's file list (a track's file, right-click; `crates/editor/src/rekey.rs`, `POST /api/graph/track/rekey`, with `/api/graph/track/rekey/check` for the dialog's words as you type). One Tx renames the key in the `track()` call, the channel key when it equals the old track key (ID-18, said in its label), `data/<key>/` (a `Rename`), and the key in `layout.json` when it reads. The track's file and binding keep their names. Its expectation is in [WRITE-10](#expectations-and-the-delta). The server moves the track's `heights` and `hidden` in `.music2/view.json` on any delta with `Rekeyed` (the change, its undo, a rename by hand), and the page moves its selection and open clip. The track's instruments and plugins aren't carried over: a re-keyed track restarts its sounds (design-8b §11.3).

**ID-18.** Mixer channel keys are dictionary slots where the user's key is identity on purpose. The app writes the track key as the channel key of a new channel, so a new track's key is never one a channel already has, in any capitals (`a_new_key_skips_the_mixers_channel_keys`), but finds a track's channel by following the graph edge from the track's export into the mixer, never by comparing keys. A key rename Tx renames a channel key only when it equals the old track key, and says so in its label.
- Following the edge means: the mixer is the song's one frame whose ComponentId is `std:mixer`; its channels are the properties of the object its call is given in the project's code. From each audio export of a track that the song reads, the edges go downstream from audio outputs, through the song's nodes outside the mixer (a duck, a compress), to the first node inside it: the channel's panner or gain, which reads that channel's `pan` or `db` (`channels.kick.db`). An edge into a sidechain input only listens, so it isn't followed (PKG-13). A track in no channel, or in two, has no fader.
- *Why:* a channel whose key differs from its track's (written by hand, or after a rename) must still be found, and so must one written as a name bound elsewhere (`kick: k`).
- *Today:* matches since 8b.3 (`crates/editor/src/mix.rs`, `mix_tests.rs`), when every channel got a gain (PKG-5), so every strip has a node that reads its channel's controls. Only the song's own nodes are followed: a track that another track reads (a bus) reaches the mixer as that track. A panner whose `pan` is a signal passes the walk on to the gain after it; a strip whose `db` is a signal and that has no panner reads none of its channel's controls, so there the read's place in the channel's property says which. The track builders edit a channel's property, so they refuse one that doesn't name its track (`kick: k`), saying why. No mixer, two, or one whose channels aren't an object written in the project's code, and the track builders refuse to add a channel, saying so.

## Project format v1

### Layout

```
music2.toml                  [project] name, entry, format = 1, sample_rate = 48000
                             [dependencies] "@m2/core" = "2", std = "0.2", "@alice/felt-piano" = "1.2"
music2.lock                  the exact version and hash of each pack the song uses (PACK-8)
song.ts  tracks/*.ts  lib/   code
data/arrangement.json        {"tempo": [{"beat", "bpm"}], "meter": [{"beat", "beats", "unit"}]}   a beat is a quarter note
data/<module>/<lane>.json    one lane's clips
data/song/<lane>.json        lanes of the root module
data/<module>/<frames>/<plugin-key>.state   a CLAP state blob, with its .state.json sidecar
assets/<path>                audio files, never overwritten; <path>.json is its note: a bounce's provenance, where a sample came from (LIB-7), or a loop take's passes (MON-11)
layout.json                  shared presentation, keyed by track key; never part of a revision
.music2/                     local and gitignored: overlays, view settings (view.json), lock, tx journal, editor index
AGENTS.md  CLAUDE.md         for agents working on the song (FMT-23); never read by the build, never part of a revision
<cache folder>/music2/       outside the project, in the OS's cache folder (FMT-16): decoded and warped renders, the hash cache
```

**FMT-1.** Projects MUST use this layout. This app has no reader for format 0, today's layout: the 5 fixtures are converted once by script ([FMT-21](#conversion)). Formats after 1 follow [FMT-3a](#manifest-and-versions).
- *Why:* every project's bytes on disk are cheapest to fix before projects exist in the wild.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: the 5 fixtures are converted, and a project without `format` is `format.missing`.

### Manifest and versions

```toml
[project]
name = "Starter beat"   # required: the project's label
entry = "song.ts"       # optional, default "song.ts"
format = 1              # required: the version of the file layout
sample_rate = 48000     # required: the rate the engine runs and exports at

[dependencies]
"@m2/core" = "2"        # required: the SDK version the code is written against
std = "0.2"             # every package the code imports from must be listed
"@alice/felt-piano" = "1.2"                   # a pack from the store (PACK-7)
"@bob/strings" = { path = "../strings" }      # a pack in a folder, for its author trying it
```

**FMT-2.** `format` versions only the file layout. `[dependencies] "@m2/core"` versions only the code API. A data addition bumps only `format`; an SDK break bumps only `@m2/core`. Each reader checks only its own field. A requirement follows Cargo's caret rule: `"0.1"` means at least 0.1.0 and below 0.2.0, and `"1"` means at least 1.0.0 and below 2.0.0. A package declares its own version in `m2pkg.toml`.
- *Why:* the two change for different reasons and at different times, and a requirement needs one meaning.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song/src/manifest.rs`).

**FMT-3.** Opening MUST read `[project] format` first, on its own and leniently, before anything else in the manifest. A missing `format` is `format.missing` (a format-0 project: convert it with the script), an older one `format.older`, a newer one `format.newer`. In each case the project does not open, and the message says what to do. Then the build checks every `[dependencies]` entry: an unsatisfied `@m2/core` is `sdk.unsatisfied`, any other unsatisfied package `pkg.unsatisfied`, each naming the required and the installed version. An import from a package not listed is the compile error `pkg.undeclared`.
- The app bundles one std. A std release that changes the bits of any existing render MUST bump its version (the minor version while it is 0.x), and CI checks this against the fixtures' recorded renders. So an old project gets `pkg.unsatisfied`, never a silent change in its sound. For a song that asks for an older std than the bundled one, the message also says how to move: the requirement to write, and that the song may sound a little different. The first std release, after people have songs, that changes existing renders is the trigger for the lockfile and for shipping several std versions ([Deferred](#deferred-with-triggers)).
- *Why:* an old app must never rewrite a newer project, a strict parse must not hide a newer format behind `manifest.invalid`, and code must never run against an SDK or a std it wasn't written for.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: `format` is read first, `sdk.unsatisfied` and `pkg.unsatisfied` are checked at compile, and `pkg.undeclared` by the build. A test records each pure fixture's render under std's version and fails when one changes while the version stays (`fixture_renders_change_only_with_stds_version`, `core/session/tests/renders.json`); it first says which fixture doesn't build, and why. std 0.2 (the `sounds` merge, 2026-10-17) changed no existing render: it bumped because its new components and the effects' `playing` input are new API, which a song written for them must not meet in std 0.1 (the owner's choice). Every project the repo ships moved with it, and `every_shipped_manifest_is_met` (`core/session/src/lib.rs`) checks that the bundled std meets every `music2.toml` under `examples`, `templates`, `core/session/tests/fixtures` and `e2e/fixtures`. One std is bundled; no lockfile yet, as no one had songs. The hint in `pkg.unsatisfied` (`check_dependencies` in `core/song/src/manifest.rs`, with `Requirement::admitting`) comes only for std, and only when the bundled std is newer than the song admits, and first, since the app shows a message on one line cut at the window's edge: "Press Upgrade it in the status, or run `m2 upgrade <song>`: it moves the song to this music2 in one step. This app has std 0.9. To use it, change the std line in music2.toml to std = "0.9". The song may sound a little different." (`unmet_dependencies`, `unmet_dependencies_block`; the upgrade's words since track `upgrade`, [AGENT-14](#agents)).

**FMT-3a.** After format 1, a format or SDK break MUST ship with a migrator outside core, `m2 upgrade <song>`, in the same release: it moves an old song to the current format, `@m2/core`, std and packs in one step, one undo step that shows what changed. Migrating is explicit, journaled like a Tx, and never applied on open. Before music2 1.0 the app keeps no older `@m2/core` major after a break: the migrator is the way an old song comes forward.
- *Why:* "no backwards compatibility" holds only while the fixtures are the only projects. Once people have songs, a break must not orphan them. Before 1.0, carrying two majors of `@m2/core` would cost more than it saves: the owner, 2026-10-31: "I don't think I need backwards compatibility this early, not until wave3 is finished", and yes to an `m2 upgrade <song>` command right after wave 3 that moves an old song to the current std and packs in one step.
- *Today:* the first SDK break is wave 3's `@m2/core` 2.0.0 ([PACK-22](#packs)). `m2 upgrade` ([FMT-25](#conversion), [AGENT-14](#agents)) came with track `upgrade`, from wave 3's list of renames, in the same release as 2.0.0: a song made before wave 3 (or before the packs, std 0.4 to 0.8) is told to run it, or to press Upgrade, first ([PACK-24](#packs)).

**FMT-4.** After `format`, the manifest MUST be read strictly: unknown keys are the error `manifest.invalid`. It blocks acceptance (the last accepted revision keeps playing, or on open nothing plays), but never `save()`, so the manifest can be fixed.
- *Why:* the lenient reader drops unknown fields when a file is rewritten.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song/src/manifest.rs`).

### Paths

```rust
enum DataPath {
    Manifest, Lock, Code(PathBuf), Arrangement, Module { module: Key }, Lane { module: Key, lane: Key },
    PluginState { module: Key, path: Vec<Key> }, Asset(AssetName), Presentation, Local, Unknown,
}
impl DataPath { fn path(&self) -> PathBuf; fn parse(p: &Path) -> DataPath; fn class(&self) -> Class }
```

| Path | `DataPath` | Class | Plane |
|---|---|---|---|
| `music2.toml` | `Manifest` | Manifest | code |
| `music2.lock` | `Lock` | Manifest | code |
| `*.ts` outside `data/`, `assets/`, `.music2/` and `node_modules/` | `Code` | Code | code |
| `data/arrangement.json` | `Arrangement` | Arrangement | arrangement |
| `data/<module>/`, the folder itself | `Module` | Data | data (renamed only by a `Rename` op) |
| `data/<module>/<lane>.json` | `Lane` | Data | data; prepared data for an audio lane |
| `data/<module>/…/<plugin-key>.state` and its `.state.json` | `PluginState` | Data | prepared data |
| `assets/<path>` | `Asset` | Data | prepared data |
| `layout.json` | `Presentation` | Presentation | none |
| `.music2/**`, a commit's staging folder `.music2/tx/<id>/` included | `Local` | Ignored | none |
| anything else, `.js` and `.mjs` files included | `Unknown` | Ignored | none |

**FMT-5.** `core/song` MUST be the only place a project path is spelled or classified, through `DataPath`. The canonical lane writer moves there, beside the reader.
- *Why:* every copy of a path rule drifts.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: paths are spelled only by `DataPath` (`core/song/src/path.rs`), and lane files are written by `m2_song::canonical`.

**FMT-6.** A snapshot MUST read only Manifest (`music2.toml` and `music2.lock`), Code, Arrangement and Lane files and `.state.json` sidecars. Assets and state blobs are not part of a snapshot; revisions record their hashes.
- *Why:* only those files define the song, and binary files are too big to snapshot.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: revisions record the hashes of the assets their lanes name and of state blobs (`core/song/src/lib.rs`).

**FMT-6a.** The build MUST read project code only from the snapshot. A project import resolves only against the snapshot's Code paths, byte for byte (the path, then with `.ts`, then `/index.ts`), with no filesystem probe and no case folding. Any other import (a `.js` file, anything under `data/`, `assets/` or `.music2/`, a path outside the project, or a spelling that differs in case) is the compile error `import.outside-code`, naming the file. Package files are read from disk, and each revision records the content hash of every package file its build read ([RENDER-2](#offline)). A package import that leaves its package (the package it names, or the importing module's own for a relative import, never another one loaded beside it), passes through a symbolic link, or differs in case from the file on disk, is also `import.outside-code`; a package file that simply doesn't exist is `code.build`.
- *Why:* a revision must be a function of its snapshot. A file read from disk would change what compiles without making a revision, and a case-insensitive disk would compile `./Tracks/bass` on a Mac and fail it on Linux CI.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: project code comes only from the snapshot, and every revision records the package files its build read (`core/script/src/lib.rs`).

**FMT-7.** A file under `data/` that `DataPath` can't parse raises the Notice `file.unknown`. Other unknown files are ignored silently.
- *Why:* a typo in a data path should be visible; a README should not.
- *Today:* matches since step 4, except that the notice reaches a running session only with its next revision, since the unknown file alone makes none ([PLANE-3](#planes-of-change)).

### Strictness

**FMT-8.** The manifest, lane files, clips, notes, the arrangement and state sidecars MUST reject unknown fields.
- *Why:* a lenient reader silently drops what it doesn't know when the file is rewritten.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song`).

**FMT-8a.** Data files MUST have one canonical form, written by the canonical writer in `core/song`: a fixed key order per schema; numbers in their shortest round-trip form, never `-0`; one clip per line and one note per line; clips sorted by `(start, id)` and notes by `(start, pitch)`. Readers put clips and notes in the same order before use, so the order of an array never changes the sound; overlapping control clips with the same start are decided by id, never by file order. Reading and then writing a canonical file gives the same bytes.
- *Why:* files must diff cleanly, two writers must agree byte for byte, and a sort, a merge or a hand reorder must never change the sound with no value changed.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song/src/lane.rs`); a test checks that every fixture's data files are canonical (`core/song/tests/fixtures.rs`). The reader and writer were made about 3 and 6 times faster for G11 (no copies of each clip, a pitch read without an error made per name, the sort's lines made only for ties, one buffer); the old ones are kept as test oracles (`core/song/src/lane/oracle.rs`), and the fast ones give the same data, the same errors and the same bytes on every lane file in the repository and on generated ones (`the_fast_read_is_the_old_read`, `canonical_bytes_are_unchanged`).

**FMT-9.** Only manifest, code and arrangement errors block acceptance. The arrangement is a deliberate addition to the plan's "only code and manifest", because every clip's timing depends on it. A lane file that fails to parse, or doesn't match its declaration, MUST NOT stop anything else, and raises an Error (`lane.invalid` or `lane.unit-mismatch`) that names the file, the field and the fix:
- when the session has accepted data for that lane, the last accepted data keeps playing, and the message says so;
- when it has none (on open, or for a new lane), the lane plays empty.

A file or folder that can't be read at all (permissions, an I/O error) is reported with the code of its class, and the message carries the system's error: `lane.invalid` for a lane file or a folder under `data/` (including one no lane reads), `state.unverified` for a state blob, its sidecar or a folder holding them, `arrangement.invalid` when `data/` itself can't be listed, `code.build` for a code file or a folder that may hold code, and `manifest.invalid` when the project folder can't be read. No separate code is needed: the fix is the same as for a file that doesn't parse.

Export renders the accepted revision, as it does for code.
- *Why:* one bad hand edit must not silence the whole song, and a lane file caught half-written must not drop out of a running session.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`Project::data` in `core/song/src/data.rs`, given the last accepted data by the session).

**FMT-10.** Lane files carry no `kind`. Notes and audio clips fail as the wrong shape, and a file with no clips fits any kind. Control lanes record their `unit`; a unit that differs from the declaration raises `lane.unit-mismatch` with Retag and Convert fixes. A lane of names records `choice` or `switch` where a unit goes ([CARD-15](#kinds-of-values)); one that differs gets `lane.unit-mismatch` too, saying what the file holds and what the lane needs, with neither fix, since names aren't numbers.
- *Why:* the kind is in the code; writing it twice invites disagreement.

### Lane files and clip ids

```json
{"clips": [{"id": "k3f9q2zt", "start": 0, "length": 16,
            "notes": [{"pitch": "A1", "start": 0, "length": 0.5, "velocity": 0.9}]}]}
```

```json
{"unit": "hz", "clips": [{"id": "q7m2c8xa", "start": 32, "length": 8, "points": [[0, 500], [8, 2500]]}]}
```

```json
{"clips": [{"id": "z1b4r9td", "start": 64, "length": 4, "asset": "vox/lead.wav", "hash": "9f2c3a1be07d44c1",
            "offset": 0, "gain_db": -3, "fade_in": 0.01, "fade_out": 0.05, "reverse": false, "pitch": 0, "warp": []}]}
```

Notes, control (masks are control lanes in `unit01`) and audio lanes, in that order. Fields are format 0's plus `id`, `unit` and `hash` (the first 16 hex digits of the asset's SHA-256). They are read and written in `core/song/src/lane.rs`.

**FMT-11.** A clip id is chosen by whoever builds the op that writes the clip, and travels in the Tx; core never mints one during `check` or `commit`. Ids are 8 characters from the lowercase Crockford base32 alphabet (`0-9a-z` without `i`, `l`, `o`, `u`). A builder outside a sandbox MAY draw them at random. A sandboxed package tool, the CLI and a fix derive them: the first 8 Crockford characters of SHA-256(`tx.id` ‖ lane address ‖ n), so `tx.id` is the only random input. Readers MUST accept any id matching `^[0-9a-z]{1,16}$`.
- *Why:* random ids survive merges, other processes and git. A sandbox has no randomness, `check` and `commit` must write the same bytes, and a scripted run given its `tx.id` must be reproducible.
- *Today:* matches since step 4. The app draws ids at random; the CLI and fixes derive them from SHA-256 of the Tx id, a NUL, the lane address `module/lane`, a NUL and `n` in decimal (`ClipId::derive` in `core/song/src/key.rs`).

**FMT-12.** A clip id is unique within its lane, and a clip's address is `(LaneAddr, ClipId)`. Moving a clip to another lane keeps its id. Copying or duplicating a clip always mints a new id, and a writer that finds its id taken in the target lane mints another.
- A clip without an id is anonymous: it can be addressed only within one revision, and raises the Notice `clip.anonymous` with an AssignClipIds fix.
- An id that breaks the rule, including one that isn't a string, is read as anonymous and kept as written, and raises the Notice `clip.invalid-id` with the same fix. "Kept as written" keeps the JSON value; the canonical writer may re-space it (`{"a": 1}` becomes `{"a":1}`).
- A duplicate id raises the Warning `clip.duplicate-id` with a RenumberClips fix, never a parse failure.
- Ids are never assigned on read. Note ids come later.
- A builder that writes a lane gives every clip of it without a usable id (none, one that breaks the rule, or one an earlier clip of the lane has) a fresh one, so `clip.anonymous` clears where the producer edits.
- *Why:* one hand edit must never make a lane unreadable.
- *Today:* matches since 8b.5 for clip gestures (`crates/editor/src/clips.rs`; `touching_a_lane_gives_its_anonymous_clips_ids`, `a_move_onto_a_taken_id_mints_one`, `every_copy_mints`). A gesture names an anonymous clip by where it starts and its rank there (`at`). The piano roll's whole-lane write (`notes.rs`) gives ids only to the clips it was sent without one.

### Arrangement

**FMT-13.** `data/arrangement.json` holds `tempo` (`[{beat, bpm}]`) and `meter` (`[{beat, beats, unit}]`, the time signature `beats`/`unit`). A beat is a quarter note.
- Tempo: the first entry is at beat 0, each is 20–999 bpm, and beats strictly increase.
- Meter: an empty list means 4/4 from beat 0; otherwise the first entry is at beat 0. `beats` is 1 to 64, and `unit` is 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 or 32. A bar lasts `beats × 4 / unit` quarter notes. Each change falls on a bar line of the meter before it, and beats strictly increase.
- Anything else raises `arrangement.invalid`, naming the entry, and blocks acceptance.
- *Why:* time signatures are needed for bars, plugins and the tempo bar, and every reader must count bars the same way. Two entries at one beat would be decided by file order.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song/src/arrangement.rs`).

### Sections

A section is a span of bars across every track: an intro, a verse, a chorus. Its gestures copy, move and empty it, and insert or delete bars, each as one Tx on the data files (gaps #23).

**ARR-1.** A section is a span of whole bars, `[start, end)` in beats: both ends on bar lines of the song's time signatures ([FMT-13](#arrangement)), `start` 0 or more, `end` after it. It reaches every lane the accepted revision declares: each track's and the song's own, lanes of names (a choice's or a switch's, CARD-15) included. A gesture names it by its beats, and a span off the bar lines, empty or not a number is refused in words, which count bars and beats from 1 (`bar 9 beat 2`). A marker is where a section starts, never a second kind of mark: the span from a marker to the next is a section when both are on bar lines.
- *Why:* pop songs are built in sections, and bars are what musicians count. Spans on bar lines keep every gesture's edges on the grid the time signature changes keep.
- *Today:* matches since `sections` (`crates/editor/src/sections.rs`; `a_span_off_the_bar_lines_is_refused`).

**ARR-2.** The section gestures:

| Gesture | What it does |
|---|---|
| `paste {section, at}` | puts a copied section ([ARR-5](#sections)) at bar line `at` |
| `duplicate {start, end, to?}` | puts a copy of the section at bar line `to`, or right after itself |
| `move {start, end, to}` | puts the section at bar line `to`, and leaves a gap where it was |
| `delete {start, end}` | empties the section, and leaves a gap |
| `insert-bars {at, bars}` | opens empty bars at bar line `at`; everything from there on moves later ([ARR-6](#sections)) |
| `delete-bars {start, end}` | takes the bars out; everything after moves earlier ([ARR-7](#sections)) |

Each is one Tx and one undo step ([WRITE-21](#undo)): a `Clips` op for each lane it changes (the whole lane when its clips can't all be named by id, as a clip gesture writes it), a `Write` of `data/arrangement.json` and of `layout.json` when they change, and `Confined { the lanes it changes }`, which admits the arrangement it writes ([WRITE-6](#expectations-and-the-delta)). The planner asserts that each lane reads every clip as written and none deleted, and that the arrangement is the one asked for. No code changes, so a song whose draft doesn't build takes them, checked against its last accepted lanes ([WRITE-14](#bases-and-revisions)). A section reaches every lane, so while any lane file of the song doesn't read (it plays the clips it had before, FMT-9), every section gesture is refused in words naming the file. A gesture that changes nothing writes nothing and says so: a move to where the section is, a duplicate onto itself.
- *Why:* copying a chorus across every track is one gesture in other DAWs, and must be one undo step here. A lane whose newer text can't be read can't move with the rest.
- *Today:* matches since `sections` (`sections::apply`, pure, and `sections::edit`; `each_gesture_is_one_undo_step_byte_for_byte`, `a_song_that_doesnt_build_takes_them`, `a_lane_file_that_doesnt_read_refuses_them`, `a_gesture_that_changes_nothing_writes_nothing`).

**ARR-3.** A clip that crosses an edge of a span a gesture reads, empties or opens is split at the edge for it, as Split splits a clip: a note that crosses is shortened to the edge, automation takes its value at the edge on both sides, a lane of names' later part starts with the name or the switch in effect at the edge, and an audio clip's later part starts later in its file. What is inside is the section's; what is outside stays where it is. An audio clip Split can't cut (its pitch changed; reversed and warped) refuses the gesture in words naming its track and bar, and what to change in the app.
- *Why:* a section is exact to the bar, whatever the clips' own edges are.
- *Today:* matches since `sections` (`Clip::split` in `crates/editor/src/clips.rs`; `a_clip_crossing_each_edge_is_split_there`, `lanes_of_names_move_with_the_bars`).

**ARR-4.** What a section puts at a bar line replaces what was there: on each lane the section has, the span it lands on is emptied ([ARR-3](#sections)) and its clips are placed, so those bars play what the section played, an empty lane of it included. A lane it doesn't have keeps its clips. A section lands on whole bars: a paste, duplicate or move whose span would end inside a bar where it lands (the time signatures differ there) is refused in words. A placed copy gets a fresh id ([FMT-12](#lane-files-and-clip-ids)); a move keeps the id of a clip that was inside whole.
- *Why:* a paste into bars that hold music must not layer two parts on one lane, and a section that half covers a bar is no longer bars. To make room instead, insert bars first.
- *Today:* matches since `sections` (`a_paste_replaces_what_was_there`, `a_section_lands_on_whole_bars`).

**ARR-5.** A copied section is its span, on bar lines of the song, and for every lane of the song its kind and the clips that overlap the span, whole, as the lane format writes them: `{"start": 64, "end": 96, "lanes": [{"module": "bass", "lane": "notes", "kind": "notes", "clips": [...]}]}` (an automation lane's kind carries its `unit`, a choice's its `options`). The server makes it (`sections::section`; the window asks `POST /api/graph/sections/copy` at ⌘C), so the window's copy is the one the tests check, lanes of names included. `paste` cuts each clip to the span ([ARR-3](#sections)) and places it on the lane of the same keys, when that lane takes the copy's kind (as a clip paste checks it). A lane the song doesn't declare now is skipped when the copy holds no clip for it; one it holds clips for refuses the paste, naming every such lane. A note belongs to the bars it starts in, as Split has it: a note that starts before the copied bars stays where it is, and isn't copied. `duplicate` and `move` read their section from the accepted revision the same way.
- *Why:* a copy keeps what was copied when the song changes before the paste (a cut, then the paste), and the cut is made in one place, the server's, with the song's tempo.
- *Today:* matches since `sections` (`a_cut_then_a_paste_keeps_the_copy`, `section_copy_over_http`).

**ARR-6.** Insert bars opens `bars` bars (1 to 999) at bar line `at`, in the time signature just before it (at the song's start, its own). Everything at or after `at` moves later by them: clips with their notes, points and audio, markers, and tempo and time signature changes. The song's first tempo and time signature stay at beat 0. A clip that crosses `at` is split there ([ARR-3](#sections)), and its second part moves; an automation or names clip's first part holds its value at `at` over the new bars, so they play what played there, while notes and audio leave them empty. While `layout.json` or `data/arrangement.json` doesn't read, insert and delete bars are refused in words naming the file, as its markers or changes couldn't move with them.
- *Why:* what starts at a bar line belongs to the music after it: a chorus's marker and its tempo change move with the chorus. A sweep with bars opened in it must not jump to the knob's default there.
- *Today:* matches since `sections` (`insert_bars_moves_everything_after`, `insert_bars_inside_a_clip_splits_it`, `a_file_that_doesnt_read_refuses_the_time_gestures`).

**ARR-7.** Delete bars takes the span's bars out: its clips go (split at its edges, [ARR-3](#sections)), and everything at or after its end moves earlier by its length. Of the markers, the tempo changes and the time signature changes inside the span, only the one in effect at its end stays, moved to its start, so what follows plays, and is named, as before; a tempo or time signature change that would say what plays already there is left out. When nothing follows the bars (no clip, marker or change at or after their end), the markers and changes inside them go, and the song's own first tempo and time signature stay.
- *Why:* deleting the first bars of a verse must not take its name or its tempo with them; deleting the end of a song must not leave its last tempo at bar 1.
- *Today:* matches since `sections` (`delete_bars_keeps_what_follows`, `delete_bars_keeps_what_follows_in_time`, `delete_bars_to_the_end`).

**ARR-8.** Only insert bars and delete bars move the song's time: its markers, tempo changes and time signature changes. Paste, duplicate, move and delete change clips only.
- *Why:* a section is what the tracks play; the tempo, the meter and the markers say how the song's time runs, and only a gesture that changes that time moves them.
- *Today:* matches since `sections` (`each_gesture_is_one_undo_step_byte_for_byte` checks both files for every gesture).

**ARR-9.** One route takes every section gesture: `POST /api/graph/sections` `{session, gesture}`, answered as `POST /api/graph/clips` answers ([AGENT-8](#agents); `m2 call` reaches it); `POST /api/graph/sections/copy` `{session, start, end}` reads a copy ([ARR-5](#sections)). `m2 sections` runs a gesture from the terminal, its bars counted from 1 (`9-16`) or named by a marker (`Chorus`: to the next marker, or the song's end), and takes the lock ([WRITE-18](#the-project-lock)). In the window, the ruler's top band, where the markers are, selects bars: a drag there selects whole bars (each end at the nearest bar line), a click the bar under it, and a marker's menu has Select to next marker. A drag on the rest of the ruler sets the loop and selects nothing, as before (taste.md TL-10). One selection at a time: selecting bars lets go of the selected track, clips and automation point, and selecting any of those lets go of the bars. The selected bars look as a marquee does, a 1 px accent outline and an accent wash across every lane, and are lit in the ruler's top band. ⌘C and ⌘X copy and cut them, ⌘V pastes the copy at the playhead's bar, ⌘D duplicates them, ⌫ empties them, ⌘I inserts as many bars where they start, ⇧⌘⌫ deletes them, Esc lets go, and the lanes' right-click menu over them has each.
- *Why:* agents and the musician reach the same gestures, as for clips (WRITE-9); a loop set to listen must never become what ⌘D or ⌫ act on.
- *Today:* matches since `sections` (`crates/server/src/routes_8b.rs`, `apps/m2/tests/sections.rs`, `e2e/sections.mjs`).

### Assets and hashes

**FMT-14.** An asset MUST never be overwritten: a new version gets a new name, and assets are created with create-only semantics (`O_EXCL`). An asset name is a relative path under `assets/`, with `/` between folders. It is NFC, and has no empty, `.` or `..` segment, no segment that starts with `.`, and no `\`, `:` or control character. A name with a `:` is a package's file instead ([PACK-5](#packs)), so the two never collide. An import whose name is taken gets a numeric suffix (`kick 2.wav`). An asset is always inside the project: importing copies the file. `m2 bounce --force` is removed.
- *Why:* an overwritten asset can't be undone and silently changes every clip that uses it. Sample-heavy projects need folders, and a reference outside the project breaks when the project moves.
- *Today:* matches for what writes assets today: `m2 bounce` creates its asset create-only and `--force` is gone, an exact render that `m2 render` or Export Audio writes into `assets/` is created, never written over (`Render::write_for` in `crates/editor/src/render.rs`, since step 6), a Tx's `Write` of an asset must have the base `Absent`, and names are checked as `AssetName`s (`core/song/src/path.rs`). Since `studio`, an import (File ▸ Import Audio…, a drop, `m2 import`) copies the file in create-only under the first free name, `kick 2.wav` when `kick.wav` is taken, and the same bytes already there are used, not copied again (`crates/editor/src/import.rs`: `an_import_copies_the_file_create_only`, `a_taken_name_gets_a_number`, `the_same_bytes_use_the_asset_there`). An audio take is written into `assets/` the same way ([RENDER-30a](#audio-lanes)).

**FMT-15.** A clip's asset reference carries a `hash` pin: the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the file's bytes. The app always writes it.
- A reference without a pin is read, and raises the Notice `asset.unpinned` with a RepinAsset fix.
- A pin that differs from the file raises the Warning `asset.changed` with a RepinAsset fix, and the clip plays the file as it is.
- A reference MUST equal an entry of `assets/` byte for byte, found by listing the folder, never by `open()`. A name that differs only in case is `asset.missing`, with a fix that rewrites the reference to the exact name. A file whose name on disk isn't NFC can't be referenced at all ([FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes)), so a name that differs only in Unicode normalisation is `asset.missing` whose message says to rename the file to its NFC form; no fix renames the file yet; the message says to.
- A missing or unreadable asset raises the Error `asset.missing`, and that clip is silent.
- *Why:* an asset changed behind the project's back must be visible, and a case-insensitive disk must not make a clip play on a Mac and go silent elsewhere.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song/src/data.rs`).

**FMT-16.** A hash cache keyed by `(path, size, mtime, ctime, inode)`, in the OS's cache folder (`m2_os::dirs::cache`: `~/Library/Caches/music2/` on macOS, `~/.cache/music2/` on Linux), refreshed by the watcher, covers assets and state blobs. An entry whose mtime lies within the volume's timestamp resolution of the moment it was cached is unverified, and is hashed again on next use (git's racy-timestamp rule). Compile MUST NOT decode audio.
- *Why:* every compile decoding every WAV makes data edits slow. On exFAT, with 2-second timestamps, a same-length file rewritten in place would otherwise keep its old hash.
- *Today:* matches since step 4, with the racy window at 2 s on every volume, since a volume's resolution can't be read portably. The CLI and the app keep the cache in `hashes.json` there (`core/song/src/hash.rs`): the app saves it after each read of a project, `m2` as each command ends, so what a command hashed (an installed pack's files, [PACK-6](#packs)) is a stat the next time.

**FMT-24.** music2 reads two kinds of audio file: **WAV** and **FLAC** (lossless, about half a WAV's size). A file's kind is told by its first bytes (`RIFF`, then `WAVE` at byte 8; or `fLaC`, after an ID3v2 tag when one comes first), never by its name, by one module, `m2_runtime::decode` (`kind_of`, `sniff`). Every reader of a file asks it the kind first: a kernel's file (a Sampler's recording, a pack's), an import's check, a sample's measure and its preview, `m2 pack check` and `m2 pack import-sfz`. Then a FLAC is read by `decode::Flac`, a WAV by hound (whole, or in pieces by the lanes' reader, `stream::Wav`). A file of another kind is refused in words that say which kinds music2 reads.
- A FLAC is read when its header says 8, 16 or 24 bits in a sample and how long it is. Any other (12, 20 or 32 bits; a length not said) is refused in words. It ends at the frames its header says: what follows them (an ID3v1 tag) is no audio. Its Vorbis comment is never read, so a tag that isn't UTF-8 (older taggers) is no problem. A file that holds fewer frames than its header says, a block past them or with another number of channels, or a sample outside its bits is refused as damaged, never played. A part of FLAC that the decoder (claxon 0.4.3) doesn't decode (a partition whose numbers are written plainly, a frame that takes its bits from the header) is refused in words that say just that, not that the file is damaged (gaps #18).
- **What one file may hold.** Before a sample is decoded, its header must say no more samples (frames × channels) than a WAV's 32-bit sizes hold (under 4 GiB of samples), and an import's checks hold (a rate of 8,000 to 384,000 Hz, at most 3 hours, [FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes)). A file decoded whole (a kernel's file; a sample, measured and previewed; a preview's instrument) is held, from its header, for a WAV as for a FLAC, to **2^27 samples** (134,217,728: about 23 minutes of stereo at 48 kHz, 512 MiB as the engine holds it in 32-bit floats); one with more is refused before a sample is read, in words that say so: in a song, it plays silent, and says it is too long to hold (`asset.unreadable`); offline, the render fails. A FLAC of silence can say a long length in very few bytes, so its size on disk proves nothing.
- **What one pack may hold.** `m2 pack check` ([PACK-12](#packs)) adds up what the header of every WAV and FLAC in the pack says, never decoding one, and refuses a pack whose files hold more than **2^28 samples** in all (268,435,456: 1 GiB as the engine holds them), and any one file over 2^27, in words that say what to do. So do `m2 pack install` and `m2 pack import-sfz`, which check, and so will the registry's robot. The lite Salamander grand (208 recordings, 24-bit stereo) holds 102,024,134: under 40%. A bigger instrument (the full Salamander, four times as many recordings) waits for streaming from the disk (gaps #22).
- **Numbers only.** A sample of a 32-bit float WAV that isn't a number (NaN) or is infinite reads as 0, decoded whole or read in pieces, by the one function both use (`decode::finite`): one such sample in a dropped file would otherwise spread through a kernel (a wavetable's spectrum, a Sampler's voice) and silence the whole song. An integer WAV or a FLAC holds none. Tested by `a_float_wavs_samples_that_arent_numbers_read_as_0` (`core/runtime/src/tests/decode.rs`) and `a_file_with_samples_that_arent_numbers_plays_in_the_wavetable_and_the_sampler`.
- **Exact.** A FLAC decodes to the same numbers as the WAV of the same samples: each integer scaled as a WAV's is (`v / 2^(bits − 1)`, as an `f32`), so both give the same bits, and a song plays a pack's FLAC recording with the bits it plays the WAV of it with ([RENDER-2](#offline)).
- **A song's own audio stays WAV.** A clip streams its file with exact seeking ([RENDER-30](#audio-lanes)), which FLAC doesn't give. So where a FLAC comes into a song (File ▸ Import Audio…, a drop, `m2 import`, a Sampler's card, adding a sample, `m2 add-sample`), music2 writes a WAV of its same samples with its same bits (8 → 8, 16 → 16, 24 → 24) into `assets/`, named as the FLAC with `.wav` (`kick.flac` comes in as `kick.wav`), create-only as any import ([FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes)); the same WAV bytes already there are that asset. The FLAC is read once: its header, its audio and its hash come from the same bytes, so a file changed after it was first looked at comes in as it was read. Its note beside it (`assets/kick.wav.json`, [LIB-7](#library)) says what it was made from, `"made_from": {"file": "kick.flac", "format": "FLAC", "sha256": "<the FLAC's SHA-256>"}`, after the sample's `from` when it came from the library. Each file coming in has a part file of its own, so `kick.flac` and `kick.wav` in one import both come in (`kick.wav`, `kick 2.wav`). A clip whose file in `assets/` is a FLAC (put there by hand) plays silent, and says so in words that name the file and how to bring it in (`asset.unreadable`); it never plays noise. A clip's file of no kind music2 reads (empty, cut in its header, an AIFF) can't be read, as a damaged file can't.
- A pack's recordings and samples may be FLAC ([PACK-12](#packs)): a kernel's file is decoded whole, a FLAC as a WAV.
- *Why:* FLAC halves the size of a pack of recordings with no loss, and most free sample instruments ship it. A song stays WAV, so every clip still seeks exactly and a song made today plays where FLAC isn't read. A pack comes from a stranger, and its recordings are decoded whole into memory: what one file and one pack may hold is read from their headers, so a few bytes can never ask for gigabytes.
- *Today:* since track flac, through the `claxon` crate (0.4, Apache-2.0, no dependencies): `core/runtime/src/decode.rs` (`kind`, `kind_of`, `sniff`, `header_of`, `budget`, `WHOLE`, `PACK`, `Flac`, `load`, `write_wav`), tested in `core/runtime/src/tests/decode.rs` against fixtures that libsndfile made, each FLAC beside the WAV of the same numbers, and files made byte by byte where libsndfile can't (`core/runtime/tests/fixtures/flac/`, with `make.py`): `a_flac_gives_the_bits_of_the_wav_of_its_samples` (8, 16 and 24 bits; mono, stereo and 3 channels; whole and block by block), `the_kind_is_told_by_the_first_bytes`, `a_flac_music2_cant_read_is_refused_in_words`, `a_damaged_flac_is_refused_never_played`, `a_flac_too_big_for_a_wav_is_refused_before_decoding`, `a_flac_written_as_a_wav_keeps_its_samples_and_bits`, and since its review `a_whole_decode_is_held_to_its_budget_before_decoding` (a FLAC of silence, 26 KB, that says 2^27 + 1 samples; a WAV's header too), `a_flac_with_tags_around_it_reads_as_its_audio`, `a_flac_with_a_part_claxon_cant_decode_is_told_so`. The import (`crates/editor/src/import.rs`, `Staged::name_noted`): `a_flac_comes_in_as_a_wav_of_its_samples_with_a_note`, `the_same_flac_twice_is_one_asset`, `a_flac_music2_cant_read_is_refused_and_leaves_nothing`, `one_import_of_two_files_of_one_name_lands_both` (and `one_import_of_a_flac_and_a_wav_of_one_name_lands_both`, `crates/server/src/graph/import_tests.rs`), `a_flac_is_read_once_for_its_wav_its_header_and_its_note`, `a_tagged_flac_comes_in_as_its_audio`. A clip on a FLAC (`stream::NotWav`, its own fault `Code::NotWav`, told as `asset.unreadable`): `an_export_of_a_clip_on_a_flac_fails_naming_it`, `only_a_clip_on_a_flac_is_told_it_isnt_a_wav` (`core/runtime/src/tests/stream.rs`), `a_clip_on_a_flac_is_silent_and_says_why` (`crates/server/src/graph/faults_tests.rs`). A kernel's file too long to hold (`decode::TooLong`, `Code::TooLong`): `a_sampler_file_too_long_to_hold_is_told_so_and_never_decoded` (`faults_tests.rs`). Samples: `a_flac_sample_is_measured_as_its_wav`, `a_flacs_note_of_yours_gives_way_to_a_packs`, `a_sample_over_the_budget_is_unreadable_before_it_is_read` (`samples_tests.rs`), `a_flac_samples_preview_is_the_wav_of_it`, `a_flac_whose_wav_is_over_64_mb_has_no_preview`, `adding_a_flac_sample_brings_in_its_wav_with_its_note` (`routes_library_tests.rs`). Packs: `a_flac_sample_is_copied_and_renders_as_its_wav` (an SFZ of FLACs renders the bits of the same SFZ of WAVs), `a_pack_whose_audio_is_over_its_budget_is_refused` and `a_pack_of_samples_alone_checks_and_its_samples_are_checked` (`apps/m2/tests/pack.rs`; `audio_budget` in `crates/editor/src/packs.rs`), `a_flac_is_copied_and_another_kind_or_a_missing_sample_stops_the_import` (`sfz_tests.rs`). Not yet (gaps #18, #22, #48): 12- and 20-bit FLAC (claxon reads them; they would come in as a 16- or 24-bit WAV of the same sound, with the same `f32` bits, but that isn't written yet), 32-bit FLAC (claxon doesn't read it), the two parts of FLAC claxon doesn't decode, AIFF and MP3, and a song's total of whole decodes across its packs (each pack and each file is held, a song that plays many packs isn't).

### Plugin state and parameters

**FMT-17.** A plugin's state is the CLAP blob in `data/<module>/<frames>/<plugin-key>.state`, with a required sidecar `<plugin-key>.state.json` that holds exactly `{plugin, plugin_version}`: no parameter values and no hash. A missing state file means the plugin's defaults. A blob whose sidecar names another plugin than the code does raises the Error `state.plugin-mismatch`; a blob without a sidecar raises the Error `state.unverified`. Either way the blob isn't loaded, the plugin plays its defaults, and the fixes are Relink (when another key now has that plugin) and DeleteData. A state write always writes both files in one Tx.
- *Why:* state belongs to its module's data, and parameter values belong to code or the blob, never both ([HOME-4](#homes-of-values)). One plugin's state loaded into another crashes many plugins, and a hash beside the blob is a second truth that a merge can split from it.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: `m2 plugin-state` writes the blob and its sidecar as one Tx, undone as one step (`plugin_state` in `crates/editor/src/data.rs`).

**FMT-18.** A plugin parameter is addressed in code as `<name>_<id>`: its display name, lowercased, with other characters turned to `_`, then its CLAP parameter id (`mix_12`). The build binds by the id alone, and the name part is a label: when it differs from the plugin's current display name, that is the Notice `plugin.param-renamed`, never a rebind. A name without an id (`mix`) is the compile error `plugin.param-unaddressed`, which suggests the full address. Editors and Tx builders always write the full form. The IR stores CLAP parameter ids, and the runtime binds by id.
- *Why:* a display name is not identity (P1). A plugin update that renames parameters, swaps two names or adds one with a clashing name must not rewire anything, and code that compiled must keep compiling.
- *Today:* matches since step 4. The IR keeps each address as written; `PackageSet::bind_plugins` binds it by its id before the check, so the runtime binds by id (`core/runtime/src/packages.rs`).

**FMT-18a.** A plugin that isn't installed MUST NOT stop the song. It raises the Error `plugin.missing`, which doesn't block acceptance. Its node is typed from the code as written: stereo audio in and out, and each `<name>_<id>` port a plain control. An instrument (no `in`) outputs silence; an effect passes its `in` through to its `out`. Its code and its state blob are never rewritten. An export made while a plugin is missing carries `plugin.missing`.
- *Why:* a project opened on a collaborator's Mac, in CI or in an agent's sandbox without one plugin must play everything else, as in every DAW.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/runtime/src/packages.rs`; the node in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`).

### Presentation and local files

**FMT-19.** `layout.json` holds shared presentation keyed by track key: track order and colour, markers, later canvas positions. It is never part of a revision and never read by the build. Unknown keys in it are ignored.
- Its schema: `order`, the track keys top to bottom; `tracks.<key>.colour`, one of the palette's twelve names (a `#rrggbb` written by hand reads as the nearest name by hue, and is rewritten as a name when that track's colour is next written); `markers`, a list of `{"beat", "name"}`, sorted by beat, one a beat, each name one line of 1 to 40 characters; `key`, the song's musical key, `{"root": "A", "scale": "minor"}` (roots in ASCII: `C`, `F#`, `Bb`; a scale by its name in [NOTE-1](#notes-and-scales)'s table), which nothing that plays reads.
- A key that names no track (one commented out of the song for a while) is ignored when read and kept when the file is written: in `order` after the key before it, in `tracks` with its colour. Only deleting the track removes its key. Unknown keys are written back as read, in the order read. The canonical form is two-space JSON with `order`, `tracks` and `markers` first. Markers are written by `POST /api/graph/markers` (the whole list) and the key by `POST /api/graph/song-key`, not by `/api/graph/layout`; each is a layout-only Tx, one undo step with no revision.
- The server reads it, watches it, and publishes an outside change with no revision. A Tx that writes it is based on what the disk says when it is planned, since no snapshot holds it, so an outside write in between is a conflict.
- A file that doesn't parse is `layout.invalid`: the view shows the defaults; the track builders leave it out of their Tx; reorder, recolour and markers refuse; Reset layout writes the defaults over it as one undo step.
- *Why:* presentation must never trigger a compile or change the sound. A git merge that leaves conflict markers in it must cost order or colour, never the gestures that make tracks.
- *Today:* matches since 8b.1 for order and colour (`crates/editor/src/layout.rs`; the watch is `Graph::watch`'s stat of the file, `crates/server/src/graph.rs`: with file-system events, when an event names it or a tick walks everything, and then every tick until it holds still, so an idle project is watched with no stat, as WRITE-19 says (`an_idle_project_is_watched_without_a_stat`); by walks alone, every tick. An outside edit shows within a second: s8-tracks measured 87 ms on Linux's inotify). The effective order is the stored order of the tracks there are, then the mixer's channel order, then the order the build made them, never alphabetical; with no colour stored, colours go by place in the palette's default order; a track without one gets the first colour no track uses, or the least used; and every layout write writes every track's colour, so the first one freezes them all (`layout_tests.rs`). Markers are read, shown on the ruler and written since polish (B5, `crates/editor/src/markers.rs`), and so is the key (D2, `crates/editor/src/songkey.rs`); the timeline view publishes both (`markers`, `key`).

**FMT-20.** `.music2/` is local to one machine. New projects MUST get a `.gitignore` line for it. Caches live outside the project ([RENDER-30](#audio-lanes), [FMT-16](#assets-and-hashes)). A project in a synced folder (iCloud Drive, Dropbox) works with these rules:
- The app marks `.music2/` for the sync services that honour a marker (Dropbox's ignore attribute), and raises the Notice `project.synced` when the project is under a known synced folder.
- The lock file and every journal record the host name. A journal from another host is reported as `tx.foreign`, with no fix ([WRITE-16](#commit-journal-and-recovery)).
- Another machine editing the project is an outside writer ([WRITE-19](#outside-writers)).
- *Why:* overlays, locks, journals and caches are not the song, and a lock or a journal synced from another machine means nothing on this one.
- `.music2/view.json` holds this machine's view settings of the song: `loop`, and the page's `zoom` (2 to 192 px a beat), `follow`, `heights` (by track key, and `track.lane` for an automation lane), `hidden` (lanes, as `track.lane`), `grid` (each view's grid and snap), `audition` and, since track `notes`, `roll` (the piano roll's Lock to key, folded rows and row height, [NOTE-9](#notes-and-scales)). The page writes them with `POST /api/graph/view` (`{session, set}`: a map merged in, `null` takes a key out; only those keys), one lock for every write of the file. A new song, or a copy made by Save As, starts with the defaults. Since `studio` it also holds `guide: {open, folded, done}` (Help ▸ Make Your First Song). The dock and the browser panel stay per window, in the browser's storage. `~/.music2/settings.json` is this computer's, for every song: the output and its buffer, where new songs go, `record` (the count-in, snap), `midi: {off: [names]}` (keyboards turned off, by the names `m2_os::midi` gives) and, since track io, `input` (the sound input and hearing yourself, [MON-8](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).
- *Today:* new projects, from the app and `m2 new`, get a `.gitignore` with `.music2/`, and `.music2/` is Local to `DataPath` (step 4). Since step 6 the first writer also puts a `.gitignore` of `*` in `.music2/`, and the lock file and every journal record the host name, with the machine id (`m2_os::host::machine_id`: the hardware UUID on macOS, `/etc/machine-id` on Linux) as what tells another host's journal (`core/session/src/lock.rs`; the step 6 design's C18). The sync marker and `project.synced` are not built. `~/.music2/session.json` is per user, not per project (`apps/music2-desktop/src/main.rs`), and so is `~/.music2/recent.json`, the recent songs (newest first, 20 kept), whether the newest was open when the app quit (`open`), and the guard that keeps a song that crashed the app at launch from opening again (`reopening`). Only the app and the headless server write it (`crates/server/src/recent.rs`). So is `~/.music2/settings.json` since `next` (gaps #31): the sound output (by its id, with its name), its buffer size and where new songs go, this machine's, never a song's, with no undo, applied at once (`crates/server/src/settings.rs`). The metronome is a view setting, per song, in `.music2/view.json` (`metronome`).

**FMT-23.** A new project made from a template or an example (New Song and Examples in the app, `m2 new`) MUST get two files for agents, unless what it is copied from has its own: `AGENTS.md`, the text of `templates/song-agents.md` with its one line that starts `m2 for this song: ` naming the exact path of the `m2` found, and `CLAUDE.md`, the line `@AGENTS.md`. The `m2` named is, in order: the running program when it is `m2`; `m2` beside the running program, unless the program runs from inside a mounted AppImage (under `$APPDIR`, a folder that goes away when the app quits); the source tree's `target/release/m2`, then `target/debug/m2`; the source tree's `target/release/m2` not built yet, with the command that builds it; else the line stays as the template has it. Save As copies a song as it is and adds nothing. The build never reads either file, and neither is part of a revision. `music2.app` MUST carry `m2` beside its own program (`Contents/MacOS/m2`), and the Linux packages beside theirs (`/usr/bin/m2` in the `.deb` and in the AppImage), built by the same `tauri build` that makes the app ([PLAT-8](#platforms)).
- *Why:* an agent opened in a song's folder reads `AGENTS.md` (Claude Code reads `CLAUDE.md`, which imports it) before anything else; it must learn there how to work on the song, and where its `m2` is, since `m2` is on no PATH.
- *Today:* built in the docs plan (`docsite`). `project::fill` writes both files after the copy (`crates/server/src/project.rs`, which embeds `templates/song-agents.md`); `resources::m2_program` finds the `m2` (`crates/server/src/resources.rs`). The app's `beforeBundleCommand` runs `apps/music2-desktop/bundle-m2.sh`, and `bundle.macOS.files` puts its `m2` at `Contents/MacOS/m2`; since the release track, `bundle.linux.deb.files` and `bundle.linux.appimage.files` put it at `/usr/bin/m2`, and a program in a mounted AppImage names none (`find_m2`'s `appdir`). A test pins the config to the finder. Tested in `project.rs`'s and `resources.rs`'s tests and `apps/m2/tests/docs.rs`.

### Conversion

**FMT-21.** A throwaway script converts the 5 fixtures from format 0 to format 1. Core MUST NOT contain migration code; later migrations live in `m2 upgrade` ([FMT-3a](#manifest-and-versions)).
- *Why:* there are no users yet, so there is nothing to migrate but fixtures. Once there are, migrating is an explicit tool, never a side effect of opening.

**FMT-25.** `m2 upgrade <song>` (and the app's Upgrade it, [AGENT-14](#agents)) MUST move a song made for an older music2 to this one in one step, keeping its sound, and rewrite only what music2's own changes moved or renamed, by one table (`m2_editor::upgrade`), reading the code with the build's parser, never with patterns over its text:
- `[dependencies]`: the `@m2/core`, std and music2's own packs' lines ([PACK-15](#packs)) that ask for an older version than this app has are written for the one it has (`std = "0.9"`, `"@m2/synths" = "2"`), and each of music2's own packs the code imports now gets its line. The `@m2/core` line is the major alone (`"@m2/core" = "2"`), as every song the repo ships writes it: that line speaks for the song's own code (FMT-2), which the upgrade writes with nothing added since the major's first version, as [PACK-23](#packs) asks a pack to; a pack the song plays asks for the minor its own code uses (`@m2/synths`' `"2.1"`), checked against this app on its own ([PACK-2](#packs)). Then `music2.lock` is written as `m2 pack lock` writes it ([PACK-8](#packs)), music2's own packs locked to the very files this app comes with, as `packs/versions.toml` records them (`m2_song::pack::own`): an older version a store keeps may be made for an older `@m2/core` ([PACK-22](#packs)), and a lock of the same version with other files names a build from before its release. Nothing else in the manifest changes, its comments included.
- An import or re-export of what left std for the packs (`m2_script::MOVED`: `std/instruments`, `std/synth`, `std/fm`, `std/drums`, and `std/fx`'s effects) is from the pack (`mono` and `Voice` from `std/dsp`): the names a statement keeps stay in it, with their comments, and the moved ones get a statement of their own after it. An import of a name no pack has now that nothing reads goes.
- A kernel id that moved, written in a `kernel()` call, is its id now (`m2_ir::MOVED_KERNELS`).
- The simple `kick` and `hat` and the nine single drums (`drums.kick` … `drums.shaker`) are the Drum ([CARD-33](#music2s-own-sounds-on-the-card)): `drums.drum(notes, { drum: "snare", … })`, the kick with no `drum`, its default. A single keeps its sound: its knobs are converted as the Drum reads them, from the value the old drum played (clamped as it clamped it): a tune or tone in Hz to semitones from the drum's own pitch (12 × log2(Hz ÷ own)), a decay in seconds to times the drum's own length, a kick's punch less 0.5 and a snare's snap less 0.6 as `punch`; a tom's decay times sqrt(tune ÷ 130 Hz), since the Drum rings a tom longer the lower it is tuned; its voices (`voices: 4`, where the Drum's own 6 aren't the single's). The hats single's closed and open hats (and its pedal hat's 25 ms) each keep their length when the hats its lane plays need one `decay`, which the Drum sets for every hat; a hats call's two decays are written as one. Each value is written in the fewest decimals, from three, that give it back to half a millionth. A knob a lane drives (`lane.control(…)`) has the lane's unit and fallback in the code converted the same way, and its file's unit and every point; a tune lane, a straight line in Hz, gets points between, so it stays within a cent of its old line. A unit that no code reads any more leaves its import. The simple kick and hat were other sounds than the Drum's: each is named to check.
- `synth.bass`'s `decay` is `biteDecay` (its `decay` is the envelope's now).
- A track that plays the drum machine (`drums.kit`) is named, with **Make it a kit** and its command ([KIT-24](#drum-kits)), and nothing is written for it: a kit can't keep each hit's noise ([KIT-22](#drum-kits)), so it is the musician's own edit, never part of an upgrade, which keeps a song's sound exactly. The drum machine still plays ([KIT-24](#drum-kits)), so such a song is made for this music2 all the same: the upgrade of one says so, and names the track.

The renames are of what a name meant. A name no pack has now (the simple kick, `drums.snare`) means the old one whatever the manifest says, so a song whose lines were moved by hand is upgraded too; the bass's `decay`, which still exists with another meaning, is renamed only from std's moved modules or a pack the song asks an older major of, so a song made for this music2 keeps it. The lock is written only for a song that moves.

All or nothing: what it can't convert exactly is named by its file and line, with the line to write, and then nothing is written at all: a value worked out by code, a name used other than called, a module imported whole whose names moved (with the imports to write), a knob the Drum hasn't got or a value past what it plays, a lane two knobs read in different units, hats whose lengths the Drum's one `decay` can't keep, a tom's tune on a lane. Otherwise it is written as one save of every file it changes ([WRITE-2](#the-two-write-primitives)), only when the song then builds: one undo step, whose undo puts back every file as it was, byte for byte (the lock it made goes). Once undone, the old files don't build, so the song plays the upgraded revision still; redo, or upgrading again, writes the upgrade back, since a raw change that makes the files the revision that plays builds whatever the draft's errors ([WRITE-21a](#undo)). Its own edits never overlap: ones that would are refused, and nothing is written. The lines it adds end as the file's do. It never runs on open ([FMT-21](#conversion)), leaves a song made for this music2 as it is, saying so, and refuses a song made for a newer one, saying so. `--check` says what it would change, and whether the song would then build, and writes nothing. The musician's presets are theirs, outside the song and its undo: never changed. Those that name a sound that moved or was renamed (a single drum's, the simple kick's, `std:synth-pluck`, a bass preset with `decay`) are named, with what to do.
- *Why:* the owner, 2026-10-31: one small command right after wave 3 that moves a song to the current std and packs in one step, shows exactly what changed, as one undo step; not backwards compatibility (old songs still don't run untouched), but a one-time helper. The lead, from the review: an upgrade keeps the song's sound, or says exactly where it can't, and never writes half a conversion. Exact, not clever: a migrator that guesses would change a sound without saying so; one table, read by the parser, changes only what it names.
- *Today:* since track `upgrade`. `crates/editor/src/upgrade.rs` (`upgrade`, `needed`), its one save `Session::save_all_built`, which is the code editor's save for several files (`Session::save_files`, `core/session/src/take.rs`). Tested by `crates/editor/src/upgrade_tests.rs` (each rename; a lane converted with its knob; a lane it can't convert, and a call, named while nothing is written; each hat's length; a tom's ring; the singles' voices; a tune lane's ramp within a cent; lines moved by hand; re-exports and whole imports; presets named and never changed; comments, line ends and unused removed names; edits that clash refused; a current song, and its bass's `decay`, left alone; one undo putting back every byte; redo and upgrading again after it; an older own pack, or one locked with other files, locked to the files music2 comes with), and by `apps/m2/tests/upgrade.rs`, on real old songs from the repo's history kept in `apps/m2/tests/old-songs/` (first-song at std 0.4 and 0.8, the sampler at 0.4, the sounds demo at 0.5 and 0.8): each builds and renders once upgraded; the sampler is today's example to the bit; the sounds demo, every single drum converted, renders from 0.5 and from 0.8 the bits the music2 it was made for rendered (`SOUNDS_THEN`, from `m2` built at f93d901a). Presets: named, not moved: converting them is gap work (gaps.md). Since track `kit-upgrade` a drum machine track is named and left as it is (`Code::machine`, with the track's name from its `track()` call), in an old song and in one made for this music2, which `m2 upgrade` lists as "to do when you choose": tested by `a_drum_machine_track_is_named_and_never_made_a_kit` (`crates/editor/src/upgrade_tests.rs`) and in `apps/m2/tests/upgrade.rs` (the sounds demo's drum machine named, its bits `SOUNDS_THEN` still).

MIDI files are converted at the edge, and never kept in the song. Since `studio`, `crates/editor/src/smf.rs` reads Standard MIDI Files of format 0 and 1 in ticks a beat, refuses the rest in words, and writes format 1 at 960 ticks a beat, outside the song only (Exports, or a file picked; a file picked inside the open song's folder is refused, whoever asks). A MIDI part is a track and a channel with notes: channel 10 plays the Drum kit, the others a std sound by General MIDI's families. An import into a lane keeps the song's tempo; one as new tracks (track pm: File ▸ Import MIDI…, a `.mid` drop) asks first when the tempos differ (`crates/editor/src/midifile.rs`).

### Presets

**FMT-22.** A preset is a sound's settings under a name: one JSON file, `<name>.json`, two-space, its keys in this order: `m2preset` (the format's version, `1`; a newer one is refused in words), `name`, `sound`, `values`, and for a plugin `plugin_version` and `state` (its CLAP state, base64).
- `name`: one line of 1 to 40 characters, none of `/ \ : * ? " < > |`, not starting with a dot, no space at either end and no dot at the end, so it is a file name on every OS. A name taken in other capitals is taken, as for songs ([PLAT-7](#platforms)).
- `sound`: what it is for, a ComponentId (`@m2/synths:synth-pluck`) or a plugin's id (`clap:<id>`). A preset's identity is its `sound` and its `name`, never its folder (P1): a file whose `sound` isn't the call's is left out of that call's list.
- `values`: numbers by name: a std control by its slot, in base units (`{"brightness": 1800}`); a plugin's parameter by its CLAP id ([FMT-18](#plugin-state-and-parameters)).
- Where: yours in `m2_os::dirs::presets()` (`~/Music/music2/Presets`, under `$HOME`, [PLAT-7](#platforms)), in a folder per sound named from its id, `<package>/<name>` (`std/sampler`; every character outside `A–Z a–z 0–9 . _ -` turned to `_`). A package's own in its folder, `presets/<component>/` ([PACK-11](#packs)): music2's own sounds' in `@m2/synths`' and `@m2/effects`', shipped with the app ([PACK-15](#packs)); a scoped sound's folder of yours is `_alice_felt-piano/felt-piano` (`_m2_synths/synth-pluck`), never nested. A plugin's own come from the plugin, never from a file music2 writes.
- Saving writes that file and no song file: no Tx, no Undo. It is written to a temporary file, then renamed. It holds every declared control of the call that holds a number (a dictionary's are left out): the numbers written, and the defaults of the ones the call leaves out. A control a lane or other code drives is left out, and named. A plugin's holds the state its instance that plays saves now, and the parameters its code writes as numbers.
- Loading is one `Values` Tx ([WRITE-6](#expectations-and-the-delta)), one Undo step. A number written in the call is rewritten through its lens, keeping its spelling (`khz(0.9)`). A control the call leaves out is written in ([PKG-3](#component-descriptors)), several as one insertion. A control the preset doesn't name keeps its value, so a preset from an older std still loads. A control a lane or other code drives, a value other calls share, and one written in other units keep what they have, and are named. A preset for another sound is refused in words, and nothing is written.
- A plugin's loads as one `Confined` Tx: its state and sidecar ([FMT-17](#plugin-state-and-parameters)), and each parameter its code writes as a number rewritten. A parameter the code doesn't write is the state's. Then the usual state load reaches the instance that plays ([RENDER-26b](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). Its plugin needs a key (its state file, [HOME-6](#homes-of-values)), else it is refused in words. The plugin's own presets load the same way, made on an instance of their own ([HOST-8](#pluginhost)).
- *Why:* one file to copy or share; a folder that is easy to find and back up, not hidden like `~/.music2`. A load is a gesture like a knob's, so its Undo and its checks are a knob's.
- *Today:* matches for std's instruments and effects since track br (`crates/editor/src/presets.rs`; `GET /api/graph/presets`, `POST /api/graph/preset/save` and `/load` in `crates/server/src/routes_presets.rs`; the card's **Presets** in `web/src/components/PresetPopover.tsx`). std shipped ten: two each for Synth pluck, Synth pad, Synth bass, Reverb and Delay; since track starter `@m2/synths` and `@m2/effects` ship them (`every_factory_preset_loads`). A plugin's since track br's slice 3: `capture_plugin` and `load_plugin` (`saving_a_plugin_preset_keeps_its_state_and_code_values`, `loading_a_plugin_preset_is_one_undo_step`, `a_preset_for_another_plugin_is_refused_in_words` in `crates/server/src/graph/presets_tests.rs`); a load's state writes are `data::plugin_state`'s. A plugin that marks itself dirty after a load makes no second Undo step, as long as the state it saves then is the one loaded (the probe's is).

## Writes

### The two write primitives

**WRITE-1.** Every write to a project file through music2 MUST be a `save()` or a `Tx`, except creating a new asset with create-only semantics ([WRITE-18](#the-project-lock)). A file music2 creates outside a Tx (a new asset, a bounce's provenance, a new song's files) is made durable, its bytes pushed and its folder synced, before anything names it. These bypasses close: `write_whole` becomes private, `PUT /api/graph/file` becomes `save()`, `m2 bounce --place` becomes a data Tx, and `m2 plugin-state` becomes a Tx that writes the state and its sidecar. That Tx may replace existing state as one undoable step, so `m2 plugin-state --force` goes away.
- *Why:* each bypass skips the lock, the journal or the diagnostics.
