- *Today:* matches since step 6: the only writer is the commit's staging (`core/session/src/tx/stage.rs`); `PUT /api/graph/file` is `Session::save` (`crates/server/src/files.rs`); `m2 bounce --place` and `m2 plugin-state` commit Tx, and `--force` is gone (`crates/editor/src/data.rs`, `apps/m2/src/main.rs`); an exact render into `assets/` is created, never written over. `the_write_bypasses_are_closed` (`crates/editor/tests/crate_graph.rs`) keeps it so.

**WRITE-2.** `save(file, base_sha, text)` is a draft write: compare-and-swap on `base_sha`, then an atomic write. It is never rejected for errors. The change is then taken in like an outside write ([WRITE-19](#outside-writers)), and recorded for undo only if it compiles. Code editor saves and agents' free-form edits use it. An agent's save is the one exception ([AGENT-1](#agents)): it is written only when it is taken in as written, so it is one undo step, else it is refused and nothing is written.
- *Why:* a half-typed file must be saveable, and agents keep writing files directly (owner decision 5).
- *Today:* matches since step 6: `Session::save` compares and swaps on the bytes' hash, writes through the commit's staging, takes in the settled draft with that one file (never a fresh read of the folder), and records an undo step only when it was accepted as written (`core/session/src/take.rs`). Since the review of `ag-fwd`, `Session::save_built` takes the draft with the file in before it writes, and writes only when that takes the file in as written: an agent's save (the app's save route with the tools key, and `m2 edit` and `m2 write` while the code doesn't build). A file that isn't UTF-8 is saved over from the lossy text the code editor shows (`crates/server/src/files.rs`).

**WRITE-3.** Every other write is a `Tx`:

```rust
Tx   { id, base: Map<Path, Base>, label: (before, after), ops: Vec<Op>, expect: Expect, hint: Option<CodeRev> }
Base = Sha | Absent | WithText { sha: Sha, text: String }                    // the base text itself (WRITE-12)

Op   = TextEdit { file, edits: Vec<{ start: Pos, end: Pos, expected, text }> }
     | Write { file, bytes }                                                  // JSON: "text" or "base64"
     | Remove { file }
     | Rename { from, to }                                                    // a file, or a folder data/<module>/
     | Clips { lane: LaneAddr, put: Vec<Clip>, delete: Vec<ClipId> }          // at most one per lane
Pos  = Byte(u32) | LineCol { line: u32, utf16_col: u32 }
```

- Bases live only in `Tx.base`: every file an op reads or writes has exactly one, and a `Rename`'s `to` must be `Absent`.
- A `TextEdit` position is a UTF-8 byte offset into the base text, or a line and a UTF-16 column, which core resolves to bytes against the base text. `expected` still guards each edit: a mismatch rejects the Tx.
- `Clips` puts whole clips, in the lane format's schema, and deletes clips by id. It applies to a lane file newer than its base when every clip it puts or deletes is the same in the base and the current file, byte for byte, or absent from both. It is the only data op, so a new data feature changes the lane format, never the op set. The arrangement, plugin state (blob and sidecar) and new lane files are written with `Write`.

A Tx may touch several files. It is journaled and is one undo step. A Tx MAY also write `layout.json`; that write is journaled with the Tx but is never part of a revision or a delta.

The same JSON serves the server, `m2 tx`, MCP and later TypeScript tools. This Tx automates the hats pan in `examples/first-song/song.ts`: it gives the song function its `lane`, replaces the literal, and creates the lane file.

```json
{"id": "tx-7k2m9q4c",
 "base": {"song.ts": "sha256:4be1…", "data/song/hats-pan.json": null},
 "label": ["hats pan 0.3", "hats pan automated"],
 "ops": [{"op": "text_edit", "file": "song.ts",
          "edits": [{"start": 327, "end": 329, "expected": "()", "text": "({ lane })"},
                    {"start": 422, "end": 425, "expected": "0.3", "text": "lane.control(\"hats-pan\", \"bipolar\", 0.3)"}]},
         {"op": "write", "file": "data/song/hats-pan.json", "text": "{\"unit\": \"bipolar\", \"clips\": []}\n"}],
 "expect": {"confined": {"ranges": {"song.ts": [[327, 329], [422, 425]]}, "lanes": [["song", "hats-pan"]], "modules": []}}}
```

- *Why:* the next gestures (add track, automate, relink, delete track) all touch several files. A closed op set keeps core from growing with every data feature, and one base per file leaves nothing to disagree. Agents and TypeScript count in UTF-16, so a byte offset alone would make every Tx on a file with `é` or `ベース` in it fail.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`m2_song::tx` in `core/song/src/tx.rs`; `Session::check` and `Session::commit` in `core/session/src/tx/`), with what the step 6 design's C1 and C3 add for the owner to confirm: `Payload::Local` (a staged file or a before-image, for recovery and the undo of a binary write), `"whole"` in `Confined` ranges, and `Expect::Journal`. Every gesture is a builder of Tx in `crates/editor`; agents send any Tx to `POST /api/tx/check` and `/api/tx/commit`, or `m2 tx`.

**WRITE-3a.** Every path in a Tx (the keys of `base`, `file`, `from`, `to`, and a `Clips` lane) MUST be relative and `/`-separated, with no empty, `.` or `..` segment, and MUST parse through `DataPath::parse` to Code, Manifest, Arrangement, Module, Lane, PluginState, Asset or Presentation. A Local or Unknown path is refused with `tx.path`, and so is a target that is a symlink or whose parent resolves outside the project. `Tx.id` is metadata matching `^[0-9a-z-]{1,32}$`; core names the journal file itself.
- *Why:* Tx JSON comes from agents, MCP and later sandboxed tools, and `../../song` must never name a file. Ops that carried unparsed paths would also break [FMT-5](#paths).
- *Today:* matches since step 6: `Tx::validate` and `TxPath` check every path and the shape before anything is read, and the commit checks on disk, under the lock, links (a target that is one, or a folder on its way that is one, inside the project or not), names in other capitals, text that isn't UTF-8, and that every path, whatever its base, is spelled as the disk spells it: on a disk that ignores capitals or Unicode forms, `Song.ts` for `song.ts` would change the real file while the delta saw another (`core/song/src/tx.rs`; `hostile_paths_and_ids_are_refused` in `core/session/src/tx/props.rs`, `hostile_targets_on_disk_are_refused` and `a_file_spelled_another_way_is_refused` in `core/session/src/tx/tests.rs`).

**WRITE-4.** `check(tx)` compiles the candidate in memory and writes nothing. A Tx whose files are all class Data or Arrangement skips QuickJS, as an outside write to such files does ([WRITE-19](#outside-writers)). It returns the `GraphDelta`, the delta's hash and the diagnostics.

**WRITE-5.** `commit(tx)` returns one of:

```rust
Committed { code_rev, data_rev, delta: GraphDelta, diagnostics, patches }
Conflict  { files: Vec<{ file, current: Sha }> }   // every base that no longer matches
Rejected  { diagnostics, unexpected: Option<GraphDelta> }
```

### Expectations and the delta

**WRITE-6.** Core MUST verify a Tx against a `GraphDelta` it computes itself, using this closed set of expectations:

```rust
Expect = Identical                                  // an empty delta: source positions, labels, node numbering, site numbers
                                                    // and whether a control was written or inherited are ignored
       | Values(Vec<(Anchor, f64)>)                 // only these constants change, to these values (the same things ignored)
       | Confined { ranges: Map<Path, Vec<Range>>, lanes: Vec<LaneAddr>, modules: Vec<ModuleChange> }
       | Seen(DeltaHash)                            // commit only if the delta equals the one check() returned
Anchor = Written { file, range }                    // a value written in the code
       | Slot { site: { file, range }, slot }       // an inherited (unwritten) control, named by its slot (PKG-3)
```

`Confined` holds when, after nodes are matched by their delta names ([WRITE-8](#expectations-and-the-delta)): every node that differs was made by a site written inside a declared range (by its origin, `within` or `src`), or differs only in edges to such nodes; lane data differs only for the declared lanes; modules are added, removed, re-keyed or muted only as declared; and every byte outside the declared ranges is unchanged. A new kind of expectation needs a design review that changes this page.
- *Why:* gesture-shaped expectations would make core grow with every feature, and checks supplied by tools would take the guarantee out of core. Inserting an omitted argument turns an inherited control into a written one, which must still count as `Values`.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: core judges every Tx by its expectation on the delta it computes (`core/session/src/tx/expect.rs`), with the step 6 design's C1 (`Confined`'s rules on bytes and on states, assets and the arrangement) for the owner to confirm; each planner's own assertion stays in `crates/editor`. A node inside a voice was made within its `poly`, so from 8b.1 it is also placed where that `poly` is: a note field (`lane:gate`) has no place of its own in the project, and before, no range could admit a new track's voices (`scope` in `core/session/src/tx/delta.rs`). From 8b.1 the write API is recorded: every op, expectation, base, declaration, module change, anchor and position, matched with no wildcard, and one Tx per op and expectation that serialises back byte for byte (`the_write_api_is_step_6s` in `core/song/tests/write_api.rs`), so a new one fails CI until this page changes. Until track bus, slice 2, a node outside the ranges could differ only in edges to nodes added or removed there; now, as this says, also to nodes made there in both graphs, which a node added before them renamed (std's mixer numbers its nodes in order, so a new send renames the sends after it, and a song export that feeds a bus then names the renamed node).

**WRITE-7.** Core MUST take structured anchors, never `file#ordinal` strings. Those become debug labels made by `crates/editor`.
- *Why:* ordinals shift with every inserted call ([ID-12](#derived-identity)).
- *Today:* matches since step 6: core takes `Anchor`s; `file#ordinal/arg` ids are the editor's handles within one code revision (`crates/editor/src/controls.rs`), carried from an older one by the call's range (`controls::carry`).

**WRITE-8.** The `GraphDelta` lists nodes added and removed, inputs rewired, constants changed, lanes changed, plugin states changed, assets changed, whether the arrangement changed, and modules changed (added, removed, re-keyed, `muted`). Nodes are named so that inserting one can't rename another:
- A node made at a site is named by its module key; then its frame chain, each frame its ComponentId and its call's point ([ID-13](#derived-identity)), with nothing positional above a `key()` frame ([ID-7](#keys)); then the call that made it; then its argument path; then its occurrence within that site. Points are in the comparison's reference coordinates: through the Tx's own `TextEdit`s, or for an outside write and between revisions through `Edits::between` ([WRITE-12](#bases-and-revisions)). Sites inside packages keep their package offsets, since packages don't change within a Tx.
- A lane, `time` or import node is named by what it reads. A `time` node made inside a component call (or a `key()` frame) is named under that call's frame chain: each call that reads song time has its own.

`DeltaHash` is the SHA-256 of the delta's JSON in RFC 8785 (JCS) form, with nodes sorted by name. The delta drives the engine: values → `SetCell`, or an activation when other things change; notes and control lanes → `SwapLane`; audio lanes, assets, plugin states and the arrangement → prepared renders and loads, then an `Activate` of the same structure; a module's `muted` → its mute-in-song cell; structure → `continues`, prepare and `Activate`.
- *Why:* the engine needs to know exactly what changed, not guess. Names by walk order would let one inserted node rename every node after it: `Confined` would fail, the engine would replace instances it should keep, and two equal changes would hash differently.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`core/session/src/tx/delta.rs`, `delta_between`), with calls named by their `(` since step 7, in the base text's coordinates (the step 6 design's C17, now in the rule's words). Since the `sounds` merge a `time` node made inside a component call or a `key()` frame is named under that frame chain (the `Op::Time` arm in `delta.rs`), so adding a call that reads song time before another of its kind, or removing it, renames neither's (`a_call_reading_time_added_or_removed_beside_another_renames_nothing` in `core/session/src/tx/delta_tests.rs`). Before, std/fx's chorus reading `time.playing` would have made Add Chorus before another chorus a refused edit. Of what the delta drives, the server writes the cells of changed constants and swaps changed lanes into what plays when that is all the delta from what plays holds; for anything else it prepares an activation against what plays, which keeps every instance that continues, loads each changed plugin state into the instance it keeps, renders what audio needs, and switches the arrangement with it (`crates/server/src/graph/actor.rs`). The guess is gone.

**WRITE-9.** What a gesture means ("the wrap wrapped", "the named outlet is wired") MUST be asserted by planners in `crates/editor` and their tests, never by core. Adding a gesture MUST NOT change core: no core crate may depend on `crates/editor` (the crate graph checks this), and a change that adds a gesture and touches `core/*` changes this page first.
- *Why:* core stays small, and its guarantee stays the same for every client.

**WRITE-10.** Gestures map onto expectations as follows. Step 6 ports the 7 gestures that exist today (set a value, connect, disconnect, add an effect, remove an effect, write a notes lane, a code editor save) and step 1's two fixes; automate a knob, add a track, rename a label and mute in song are new builders.

| Gesture | Expectation |
|---|---|
| set a value | `Values` |
| connect, disconnect, add or remove an effect | `Confined`; the planners keep excise, rewire and wrap as their own assertions |
| write a notes lane | `Confined { lanes }` |
| relink unused data | `Confined { lanes: the lane it moves to }` |
| delete unused data | `Identical` |
| automate a knob | `Confined { the literal's range, a new lane }` |
| add a track | `Confined { the new file, the import and the mixer call in song.ts, the new lane, a new module }` |
| duplicate a track | `Confined { the new file (or the copied statement), the import and the mixer call in song.ts, its lanes, a new module }` |
| delete a track | `Confined { its file (or its statement), its imports, the mixer call, every excised call and every import that leaves unread, its lanes, the module removed }` |
| move a mixer channel's fader that the channel doesn't write (its first move) | `Values`: the `db` written into the channel's entry |
| a mixer channel's first pan (an optional control written in) | `Confined { the mixer call }`, at the drag's threshold; the planner asserts by channel |
| reorder, recolour, Reset layout | writes only `layout.json`: no revision, so `Identical` |
| rename a label | `Identical` |
| mute in song | `Confined { the track call's options, modules: that module's muted }` |
| set the tempo or the time signature, add or remove a change | `Confined {}`: check 5 admits the arrangement it writes, and nothing else may change |
| rename the song | `Identical` |
| markers, the song's key | write only `layout.json`: no revision, so `Identical` |
| change a track's instrument | `Confined { the instrument call, and the imports it changes }` |
| add a bus | `Confined { the new file, the import and the mixer call in song.ts, a new module }` |
| add or take out a send, set it before or after the fader, send a channel to a bus or the master | `Confined { the mixer call }`; the planner asserts every channel's tracks and the routing it wrote |
| change a track's ID | `Confined { the track's file, each read of it in another file, lanes: its lanes }` when every reading file is edited; else checked first and committed `Seen` ([WRITE-11](#expectations-and-the-delta)). Either way the planner asserts the modules are exactly `[Rekeyed]`, no value changes, and every lane plays the same clips under the new key |
| repeat a clip | `Confined { the lane }`, as duplicate |
| note tools (transpose, quantize, legato, humanize, swing) | `Confined { lanes }`, as writing a notes lane |
| code editor save | `save()` |
| bring audio in onto a lane, a take (notes or audio), Follow tempo | a clip gesture: `Confined { lanes }`, one `Clips` Tx |
| bring audio or a MIDI file in as new tracks | one Tx, as add a track for each |
| set a Sampler's file | `Confined { the call's range }` |

- *Today (8b.1):* add, duplicate and delete a track, reorder and recolour are built (`crates/editor/src/tracks.rs`, `layout.rs`; routes `POST /api/graph/track/{add,duplicate,delete}` and `/api/graph/layout`; `m2 add-track`, `duplicate-track`, `delete-track`, `layout`). The whole mixer call is declared because the new strip's nodes are std's, made inside it; each planner's assertion takes up the rest: exactly one module added or removed, no value changed, nothing of the song removed but an empty mixer's silent stand-in, and every other channel as it was, by key. A delete that other code reads answers with the reads (409 at the route): one an effect that takes audio first can lose (a duck's sidechain) goes with the track only when asked again with `cascade`; any other blocks it.
- *Today (8b.3b):* a channel's fader and first pan are built (`Insertion` in `crates/editor/src/lens.rs`, `mix::insert` in `mix.rs`; route `POST /api/graph/mix/insert`, which answers with the new knob's id). A fader's first move writes `db` into the channel's entry as one `Values` step: a bare entry is wrapped, keeping its text (`kick: kick.out` → `kick: { in: kick.out, db: db(-3) }`), and an object entry gets a property ([PKG-3](#component-descriptors)). The first pan writes `pan: 0` the same way, with the whole mixer call declared, since the new panner is std's, made inside it. A panner is named by how many panned channels come before it ([ID-14](#derived-identity)), so a first pan renames the panners after it, and the planner's assertion is by channel, through `mix::mixer`: every channel keeps its tracks and its controls' values and readers, this one gains only the pan, read by its new panner, and the graph gains that constant and that panner, net, and changes no value (`the_first_pans_assertion_refuses_more_than_the_pan`). It is inaudible: live, the song plays within 1e-5 of the same song with no edit, through the activation, on first-song and in a mix that is mono all the way (`a_first_pan_while_playing_is_inaudible` and `a_first_pan_in_a_mono_mix_while_playing_is_inaudible` in `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`).
- *Today (8b.2):* the song bar's tempo, time signature and title are built (`crates/editor/src/song.rs`; routes `POST /api/graph/tempo`, `/meter`, `/title`; `m2 tempo`, `meter`, `title`). The tempo and the time signature write `data/arrangement.json` whole, in its canonical form, and assert on the checked candidate that it plays exactly the arrangement asked for; a new time signature change lands on the bar line at or before where it was asked, and later ones keep their bar: each goes to where its bar now starts, so an edit and its inverse give back the same file (FMT-13; `a_later_meter_change_keeps_its_bar`). A ruler mark with both a tempo and a time signature change is removed in one step (`remove_changes`, the tempo route with `meter`). The title is a `TextEdit` of `[project] name` in `music2.toml`, whose delta is empty (`a_title_edit_is_identical`). Each is one undo step, with no toast.
- From 8b.4: mute in song is built (`crates/editor/src/mute.rs`): one `Confined` Tx for one or several tracks, each declared by the range it edits in its `track()` call's options (or the point after the key, for a call with none) and its `ModuleChange::Muted`; the planner asserts that each named track is as asked in the candidate. Its history label is "Kick → Kick, muted in song". It is reached by `POST /api/graph/track/mute` and `m2 mute-in-song`.
- From 8b.5: clip gestures are built (`crates/editor/src/clips.rs`): create, move (to another lane, a copy), resize (either edge), split, join, duplicate, paste, delete, quantize, one clip's notes (the piano roll's edit), one clip's points (automation drawn in place), and the general put-and-delete for agents. Each is one `Confined { the lanes it touches }` Tx: a `Clips` op per lane, or the whole lane when its clips can't all be named by id (a move across lanes has two). The planner asserts each lane reads every clip written as written and none deleted. A gesture is pure first (`clips::apply`, what it makes of the lanes, from the accepted revision), so the lane lease can preview the commit exactly. Notes, automation and audio clips never cross, and automation crosses only within its unit. It is reached by `POST /api/graph/clips` (`{session, gesture}`) and `m2 clips`. Labels say what changed: "clip at bar 1 (8 bars) → clip at bar 9 (8 bars)".
- From polish: change instrument (`crates/editor/src/swap.rs`, `POST /api/graph/track/instrument`), markers (`POST /api/graph/markers`), the song's key (`POST /api/graph/song-key`), change a track's ID (`POST /api/graph/track/rekey`), repeat a clip (the clips route's `duplicate` gesture with `times`, 1 to 64) and the note tools (a notes lane write, `POST /api/graph/lane`). Change instrument drops the old instrument's settings and every import only it used; a setting code works out (a lane, a sum) refuses it in words. The project routes `POST /api/project/save-as` (a copy without `.music2/`, opened; refused inside the song's own folder; works read-only) and `POST /api/project/rename` (the title Tx, then, with `folder`, the folder renamed with the song closed and opened again, its undo history with it ([WRITE-23](#undo)); refused while the code editor has unsaved edits unless `anyway`) are not Tx: Save As only reads the song, and the folder rename moves the project.
- From `studio` and step 9: imports (`crates/editor/src/import.rs`, `midifile.rs`), takes (`take.rs`: the file is finished and synced before the Tx names it), a Sampler's file (`sampler.rs`, track ab) and Follow tempo (the `audio` clip gesture's `follow`, track sx).
- From 8b.5: Remove automation is `Confined { the lane read's range, and each place the code reads its binding by name, lanes: the lane }`: a plain number written in a read's place is made where a call reads that name (PKG-3). Its history label is "cutoff automated → cutoff hz(1250)".

Core keeps `save`, `Tx`, `GraphDelta` and its checkers, undo, the journal, the raw site, origin and control tables, and diagnostics. `crates/editor` gets `view.rs`, `timeline.rs`, `lens.rs` (with unit spelling generated from `core.ts`), the cable and effect planners, labels, the source helpers the planners use (once in `core/script/src/sites.rs`, now `crates/editor/src/sites.rs`), and `render.rs` (export at host level).

**WRITE-11.** Agents opt in to verification by calling `check`, showing the delta, and committing with `Seen(hash)`.

### Bases and revisions

**WRITE-12.** A Tx's base MUST be the content hash (SHA-256 of the bytes) of each file it touches, or `Absent` for a file it creates. Anchors are positions in those texts.
- A base equal to the file's current hash always applies.
- Otherwise core carries the ranges from the base text into the current one, failing when a change touched them. The base text is the one the Tx carries (`WithText`, whose hash core checks), or else the one in the session's store of recent texts keyed by hash, which is only a cache. With neither, the Tx is a `Conflict` naming the file.
- So a Tx that carries its base texts has the same outcome in every process, whatever versions a session happened to see. Agents and `m2 tx` SHOULD send them.
- A directory's hash (for a `Rename` of `data/<module>/`) is the SHA-256 of the `\n`-joined lines `<path>\0<sha256 hex>`, one for each file under it that `DataPath` parses as Lane or PluginState, with paths relative to the folder, `/`-separated and sorted bytewise. Dot files such as `.DS_Store` aren't hashed and move with the folder; any other file makes the `Rename` refuse, naming it.
- A revision number is only a hint.
- *Why:* a revision number means nothing outside the session that issued it, the 300 ms poll can miss versions, and the Rust and TypeScript tools must compute the same directory hash.
- *Today:* since step 6 bases are content hashes, `Absent` or `WithText`, found in a 32 MiB store of recent texts when a Tx doesn't carry them (`core/session/src/texts.rs`), and carried by `Edits::between` ([ID-13](#derived-identity)): a line diff whose runs of changed lines a token-level Myers diff refines, each hunk trimmed to bytes, so a change on another part of the same line doesn't stop a range (`core/session/src/text.rs`); the editor's control ids, leases and cables carry by it too (`crates/editor/src/controls.rs`, `cable.rs`); the directory hash is `m2_song::dir_hash`. Except: the editor's notes write, relink and delete-data still name the revision they were planned at and plan on the session's remembered snapshot of it (`Session::snapshot`, the last 64), so the piano roll sends no lane hash. Since 8b.5 clip gestures plan on the accepted revision and name each clip they touch with the hash the window drew it with: each clip in the timeline carries `sha`, the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of its canonical line, and a clip changed since is refused as a conflict naming it (`a_gesture_on_a_changed_clip_is_changed`; `POST /api/graph/clips` answers 409). The roll's whole-lane write stays until the roll sends `notes` gestures; it takes clips as the timeline shows them, `sha` and all.

**WRITE-13.** A revision is a pair `(code_rev, data_rev)`. A change to Code or Manifest files bumps `code_rev`. A change to only Data or Arrangement files bumps only `data_rev` and stores lane diffs, not full snapshots. Control ids, gestures and carrying are scoped to `code_rev`, and a lease is re-resolved at every new `code_rev` ([WRITE-22](#gesture-leases)).
- *Why:* a lane edit must not invalidate a knob drag.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: a `Revision` is numbered `(code_rev, data_rev)` beside its sequence `number` (`core/session/src/session.rs`), and the server's leases and ids follow the session's `code_rev`. Except that the last 64 revisions each keep a full snapshot, not lane diffs.

**WRITE-14.** While the draft code is broken but the session has an accepted code revision, a data Tx MUST still work. It writes data files only and is checked against the last accepted lane declarations. Without an accepted code revision, a data Tx is rejected.
- *Why:* fixing code must not block editing notes.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`the_draft_rule`, `outside_data_is_taken_in_while_the_code_is_broken` in `core/session/src/tx/tests.rs`).

### Commit, journal and recovery

**WRITE-15.** A commit runs these steps, under the project lock:
1. compare-and-swap every target against its base;
2. write the journal `.music2/tx/<id>.json` and sync it. It holds the host name, the before and after hashes of every target, and the before bytes of every text target. A binary target's before bytes (a plugin blob) are stored once, by content, in `.music2/tx/blobs/<sha>`;
3. stage every write in `.music2/tx/<id>/`, which is on the same volume, then rename each into place;
4. accept the new revision;
5. remove the journal and the staging folder.

Between steps 1 and 2 the engine may hear the candidate as a preview ([PLANE-5](#planes-of-change)); nothing is accepted before step 4.
- *Why:* several files can't be replaced atomically, and the journal makes an interrupted commit recoverable. Staging beside each target would put temporary files under `data/`, where the watcher and `git status` see them. A 50 MB plugin blob must not be copied into the journal twice per save.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`core/session/src/tx/stage.rs`): the journal, then the staged files, pushed with `fsync`; one barrier (`m2_os::fs::barrier`: `F_FULLFSYNC` on macOS, `fsync` where a volume refuses it; `fsync` on Linux, where each push is already durable); renames checked at each step with an exchange and a create-only rename (`m2_os::fs::rename`: `renamex_np` on macOS, `renameat2` on Linux), rolled back in process when one finds an outside change. A volume without them (FAT, exFAT, SMB, NFS; NTFS always) reads and compares the target, then renames the staged file over it in one rename, so every target always holds its before or its after bytes; a rollback renames the before bytes back over it the same way. Step 5 comes later than this page says (the step 6 design's C9): a commit's journal and staging are removed after the next commit's barrier, or at close, and a completed journal found when the lock is taken only after its commit's folders are pushed and a barrier, so the removal is never durable before the renames are. A barrier that fails removes nothing: such a journal waits for the session's next barrier, as its own commits' do, and a close whose barrier fails leaves its journals to the next lock (`a_completed_journal_whose_barrier_failed_goes_after_the_next`, `a_close_whose_barrier_fails_keeps_its_journal_until_the_next_lock`). A folder sync that can't be done is skipped by every writer alike (`m2_os::fs::sync_dir`): on a volume with no folder sync (`vboxsf`), and for a folder that can be written but not listed, which can't be opened to sync it. Any other error (`EIO`) fails the write, except in step 8's push after the renames, which can't undo a commit that landed. A rollback pushes the folders it renamed into before its barrier; a staging folder is removed before its journal, and what a killed commit left half made (temporary files, a staging folder whose journal went) is swept when a writer next takes the lock. Killing the process at every disk call of a commit is tested (`core/session/src/tx/kill_tests.rs`). Since the G11 fix the pre-check (step 1) passes into a callback before the journal's first byte (`stage::commit`), where the session's host may send a data-only candidate to the engine; commits whose disk work fails there are swapped back ([PLANE-5](#planes-of-change)). A rollback whose barrier fails leaves its journal and staging to the next barrier, never tidied before the rollback is durable (`a_rollback_whose_barrier_fails_keeps_its_journal_until_the_next`).

**WRITE-16.** A journal left behind raises `tx.interrupted`, with FinishTx and DiscardTx fixes, when a process on the same host next takes the lock. Finish uses the staged files; Discard uses the recorded before bytes. Each fix is allowed only while every target still matches the hash the journal recorded before or after the Tx. If a target matches neither, the journal is set aside and reported, with no fix, and no project file is touched. A journal from another host (a synced folder) is `tx.foreign`, reported with no fix. Recovery never runs by itself.
- *Why:* recovery must never overwrite someone else's later edit, and a commit interrupted on another machine is that machine's to recover.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`core/session/src/tx/journal.rs`), with the step 6 design's C9 and C18 for the owner to confirm: a journal whose every target is at its after hash is a completed commit, tidied silently when the lock is taken next, a target at a newer journal's start or end (one that started where it ended, such as its undo) counting as at its after hash; another host is told by its machine id ([FMT-20](#presentation-and-local-files)), and its journals' before-images, and those of journals set aside, are kept. A Tx overlapping an interrupted commit's targets (a folder holding one included) waits for it. Finish and Discard apply while the interruption left code that doesn't compile (P3), and every CLI command that opens the project says them with their `m2 fix` commands, `m2 check` and `m2 render` included (`killed_at_every_commit_step_the_project_recovers`, `apps/m2/tests/recovery.rs`). The kill test runs every case twice: on a disk that swaps, and on one that can't (`Recorder { plain: true }`); one case writes `music2.toml`.

### The project lock

**WRITE-17.** A process that writes a project through music2 MUST hold an exclusive OS advisory lock on `.music2/lock` (the std file lock, `File::try_lock`, through `m2_os::fs::try_lock`; `core/session/src/lock.rs`) for as long as it may write: the app while the project is open, a CLI write command for its run. So one process at a time writes. The file holds `{pid, program, host, token, since}` only to make messages clear. The OS releases the lock when the process exits, so a crash leaves no stale lock.
- When the app opens a project another process holds (a long `m2 bounce --place`, a second app), it opens it read-only: it plays, says so naming the holder, and offers to retry. Read-only means no `save()` and no Tx.
- When the volume can't lock (`ENOTSUP` or `ENOLCK`, as on some SMB, cloud and FUSE volumes), the project opens read-only with the Error `lock.unsupported`. music2 never writes unlocked.
- *Why:* two writer processes with no lock race between compare and rename.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`core/session/src/lock.rs`). The app opens a held project read-only, with a Read only pill in its top bar whose popover names the holder and offers Retry (`POST /api/graph/lock/retry`), and its controls disabled, and answers every route the route table marks as a write 423 meanwhile ([AGENT-8](#agents), `crates/server/src/api.rs`). On a network volume (`m2_os::fs::is_local`: no `MNT_LOCAL` on macOS; on Linux an NFS, SMB, CIFS, 9p, Ceph, AFS, Coda or FUSE file system) it takes the lock and raises the Notice `lock.local`, as the step 6 design's C2 proposes. A lock whose file was removed while held (`git clean -fdx` takes `.music2/` away) excludes nobody, so every write checks it first: it is taken again, or, when another process took it meanwhile, the session turns read-only naming it (`a_lock_whose_folder_was_removed_is_taken_again`).

**WRITE-18.** A writer that can't take the lock MUST fail before changing anything, with a message that names the holder and what to do:

```
error: nothing was changed: this project is open for writing in music2 (pid 4121, since 14:02).
Make the edit there, or close the project in music2 and run this again.
```

The CLI's commands sort like this:
- `check` (and its alias `build`), `render`, `graph`, `controls`, `cables` and `effects` only read. They never take the lock, and open their own session.
- `kernels` and `plugins` open no project, and `new` creates a new folder. None needs the lock.
- `bounce` without `--place` only creates a new asset and its provenance, with create-only semantics ([FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes)). That can't race, so it needs no lock and works while the app is open.
- Every other command that changes a project takes the lock: `set`, `connect`, `disconnect`, `add-effect`, `remove-effect`, `bounce --place`, `plugin-state`, `relink`, `delete-data`, `tx`, `automate`, `label`, `add-track`, `duplicate-track`, `delete-track`, `layout`, `tempo`, `meter` and `title`. The message for `bounce --place` also names the lane file, and says the clip can be added by writing that file directly.
- From 8b.4: `mute-in-song` takes the lock too (`apps/m2/tests/mute.rs`).
- From 8b.5: `clips` and `unautomate` take the lock too (`apps/m2/tests/clips.rs`).
- Also: `import`, `fix`, `edit` and `write` take the lock too, and `listen` only reads. `undo`, `focus`, `watch`, `point` and `call` act only through the open app and take no lock; `routes` opens no project.

A writer that finds the app holding the lock forwards ([AGENT-1](#agents)); a lock held by any other program fails as above.
- *Why:* owner decision 10: the lock, then forwarding. While raw file writes are allowed (owner decision 5), the safest writer must not be the one blocked: it isn't, only asked to wait while the musician holds a file ([AGENT-3](#agents)).
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`apps/m2/src/main.rs`; `apps/m2/tests/lock.rs`), with `automate` and `label` among the writers, from 8b.1 `add-track`, `duplicate-track`, `delete-track` and `layout`, and from 8b.2 `tempo`, `meter` and `title` (`a_write_while_another_process_holds_the_lock_fails_and_changes_nothing`), and readers taking the lock for a journal scan when it is free, never while they read (the step 6 design's C19). Since `ag-fwd`, a lock held by `music2` is forwarded to when `m2` can reach that app (AGENT-1); one it can't reach fails naming it, and so does a lock held by any other program.

### Outside writers

**WRITE-19.** Outside writers (text editors, agents writing files, git, another machine through a synced folder) are first class. The watcher picks up their changes, classifies the paths with `DataPath`, and takes the same plane a Tx would. It waits for changes to settle: it batches them until the folder has been quiet for 150 ms and every changed path's `(size, mtime, hash)` is the same on two polls in a row, and while `.git/index.lock` exists. It then reads one snapshot and accepts it as one revision, on the slowest plane of the batch ([PLANE-2](#planes-of-change)), and, in the app, one undo step unless git wrote it ([AGENT-2](#agents)). The lock can't stop an outside writer; compare-and-swap narrows the race between an outside write and a commit but can't close it.
- *Why:* files are the truth, whoever wrote them. A checkout, an editor's save-all or an agent writing several files arrives over several polls, and accepting a half-applied state plays wrong music and makes diagnostics flap.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: the settle rule (`core/session/src/watch.rs`) is fed by the server's watcher (`crates/server/src/graph/watcher.rs`); a batch that settled is read once, its paths alone (`Snapshot::read_paths`, `a_settled_batch_reads_only_its_paths_and_takes_in_the_same`), and taken in whole, each path only while it reads as it settled (`Session::take_in_only`); the session's own writes are told to the rule by their stamps; a path there for one poll only settles as gone. Since the G11 fix, on a local volume the OS's file-system events name what changed (`m2_os::watch`: FSEvents on macOS, in-tree over CoreServices for `FSEventStreamFlushSync`; inotify on Linux, one instance per process, read at most every 10 ms as FSEvents' latency groups events, and an event is queued before the writer's call returns, so a flush is a read that never waits), and a tick stats only the named paths, the open batch's and what the session just wrote: an idle tick stats nothing. The whole project is walked at open, every 5 s, when events were dropped or more than 4,096 paths came at once, and whenever a batch is due: only a poll that saw every path makes a batch ready, so a change whose other half an event names late is never taken in half (`a_late_event_never_readies_half_a_change`; `a_scoped_poll_sees_what_a_full_poll_sees` replays the full-poll tests' scenarios). The watcher ticks every 50 ms, at once when an event names a change while no batch is open, and when a batch's quiet ends. A tick holds the watch lock only to plan and to apply, never over a stat (`the_watch_lock_is_never_held_over_a_stat`); what the session wrote meanwhile is left out of that tick, and every file the session wrote is looked at again by the next, so an outside write right after it is taken in (`an_outside_replace_right_after_an_own_write_is_taken_in`). A path is stat'd only through names spelled as the disk spells them (`a_case_only_rename_settles_as_one_path`), and a folder moved or renamed in is walked (`a_folder_moved_into_the_project_is_taken_in`, `an_assets_folder_replaced_by_a_rename_is_taken_in`). On a network volume (its events name only this computer's writes), when events won't start (inotify's per-user limits included), with `M2_WATCH=walk`, or once events missed changes twice in 10 minutes (a change a walk found that no event named within 60 s; `events_that_miss_changes_give_way_to_walks`), a walk of everything is the only source: every tick while a batch is open, else every 50 ms to 1 s (20 times the last walk's time). A walk lists an asset no lane names from its folder entry, with no stat. A walk that lost events asked for (a Rescan) counts no missed events (`a_burst_past_the_hint_limit_is_walked_and_never_missed`); a folder made, filled or renamed after open is watched from its events (`a_folder_made_after_open_is_watched`, `a_folder_made_and_filled_at_once_is_watched`, `a_folder_renamed_in_the_project_is_watched_by_its_new_name`). On Linux a folder put where the project's folder was (a restore, or the folder removed and made again) is watched from the next walk, and a folder that could be read only after it was made is watched once its mode changes (`m2_os::watch`'s tests); a change of a file's change time alone (a hard link to it made elsewhere) is no miss (`a_change_of_the_change_time_alone_is_no_miss`). With a 5,000-file `assets/` on p16 (`m2-gates --server watcher`, G11's watcher; release, M1 Pro, five runs; before: the step 6 watcher, three runs):
  - Idle, its ticks use 0.18 to 0.27% of a core: two walks of everything in 10 s, 4.6 to 7.4 ms each, and no stat in between (before: a walk every tick, 32 to 33%). By walks alone, 3.8 to 4.3%.
  - A walk of everything takes 4.2 to 4.7 ms at p50, and 6.0 to 7.2 ms with 1,000 of the files named by a lane (before: 14.8 to 16.5 ms and 17.7 to 18.9 ms).
  - An outside write of a lane file, in place or as an atomic save, is played 172 to 181 ms after its last byte at p50 and 180 to 190 ms at p95, of which 150 ms is the quiet (before: 210 to 233 ms and 267 to 295 ms; 10 writes each). By walks alone, 205 to 254 ms at p50 and 247 to 326 ms at p95 (5 writes each).

### Opening a project

**WRITE-20.** `Session::open` MUST NOT fail because of code, manifest content, arrangement or data errors. A project whose code doesn't compile opens with diagnostics and no accepted code revision: the code editor works, and playback is silent until the code compiles. Opening fails only when the folder can't be read, has no `music2.toml`, or its `format` is missing, older or newer than this app's ([FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions)). A project whose lock is held elsewhere, or can't be taken, opens read-only ([WRITE-17](#the-project-lock)).
- *Why:* a project with a half-typed `song.ts` must be fixable in the app (owner decision 6).
- *Today:* matches since step 6: `Session::open` opens a broken project with the empty revision and `draft_errors`, and the app opens it silent, in its code, and plays it once a save compiles (`a_broken_project_opens_and_plays_once_saved_fixed` in `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`).

### Undo

**WRITE-21.** Undo applies the stored inverse Tx. One Tx is one undo step, and so is a watcher batch that builds ([AGENT-2](#agents)). History survives closing the song: it is kept in `.music2/undo/` and read back when the song next opens for writing ([WRITE-23](#undo)). An inverse undoes only what its Tx did, or it is a `Conflict`:
- a `TextEdit` is located by the carried range plus the recorded value;
- a `Write`, `Remove` or `Rename` applies only while its target's hash is the one the Tx left (or still `Absent`);
- a `Clips` op applies only while every clip it touches has the content hash the Tx left (or is still absent); otherwise the `Conflict` names the lane and the clip id;
- the undo of a `save()` is a `Write` whose base is the hash the save wrote.
- *Why:* undo must restore the edit, not an old copy of the file over someone else's work.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: every step is a stored inverse Tx on one history, at most 500 steps and 64 MiB of retained text (`core/session/src/session.rs`, `TxStep` in `core/session/src/tx/mod.rs`); the inverse of a `Clips` op that meets an outside change to its clip is a `Conflict` naming the lane file and the clip (`undoing_a_clip_after_its_outside_change_conflicts`). Since `next` (gaps #29's names) each step keeps the revision it made and an optional name (`Session::name_step`, `step_names`; `Session::tops`), which follows it through undo and redo: the window that made an edit names it (`POST /api/graph/step`), and a step made elsewhere gets a plain name from what it changed ("Bass notes"). Undo's and Redo's tooltips and the app's Edit menu show them. Names live as long as the history; Tx, its ops and expectations are unchanged. Since `ag-steps`, each step also has an id and a source, and undo and redo name the step they mean by its id ([AGENT-2](#agents)). Since `trust`, the history outlives the session (WRITE-23 to WRITE-26).

**WRITE-21a.** A raw change (a save's step, an outside step's, an upgrade's: committed unjudged and taken in like an outside write) whose writes make every file where the files on disk differ from the revision now playing that revision's again MUST go ahead even though the files on disk don't build, and even though they wait ([WRITE-19](#outside-writers)): an undo, a redo, or a save that must go in as written. Its bases are still checked against the files on disk, as any Tx's. It builds, as it makes the files the revision that plays: the draft's errors clear, and its Before (the Compare key's, [AGENT-3](#agents)) is that revision. A judged change keeps the draft rule (`code.build`, `DraftInvalid`): its baseline is the files on disk, which don't build. A save that lands as written while the code is broken (a lane file by [WRITE-14](#bases-and-revisions)'s data path, or the files of the revision that plays) is one undo step ([WRITE-2](#the-two-write-primitives), [AGENT-1](#agents)).
- *Why:* once an upgrade ([FMT-25](#conversion)) is undone, its old files don't build, so the song plays the upgraded revision still; redoing it, or upgrading again, makes the files that revision, which builds whatever the draft says. A judged change is judged against a baseline it can't build.
- *Today:* since track `upgrade` (`Session::lands_on_accepted`, which `read_all` asks only for `commit_raw`, and `step_as`, `undone` and `blocked` only for a raw step; `Session::save_files`, the one save of the code editor, `m2 edit` and `m2 write` while the code doesn't build, and the upgrade). Tested by `core/session/src/tx/lands_tests.rs` (an agent's and the code editor's save of a lane file while the code is broken, each one step; a judged step that lands keeps the draft rule; a raw one goes ahead and its Before is what plays) and `after_undo_redo_and_upgrading_again_each_write_the_upgrade` (`crates/editor/src/upgrade_tests.rs`).

**WRITE-23.** The session that holds the lock MUST keep its undo history in `.music2/undo/`, and read it back when it next opens the song for writing. It keeps both stacks, undo's and redo's, and of each step its id, source, name, inverse Tx, and the hash each file it touches held before its change, which is what its undo puts back; and the song as the history last saw it, as one hash of its files. A change to the stacks that writes the song (a commit, a save that goes in as written, an undo, a redo, a change taken back) is written ahead of its files (WRITE-25); any other change to them (a step from outside, a skip) is written after it; a name goes with the next write, or at close. Ids go on after the ones read back, so none is used twice in one song's history (AGENT-2). A read-only session neither reads nor writes it. The history takes any Tx as it is, knowing nothing of what it means: a new gesture needs nothing of it.
- *Why:* closing the app, or a crash, must not lose the way back (gaps #29). `.music2/` is this machine's ([FMT-20](#presentation-and-local-files)): undo is never part of the song, and git never sees it.
- *Today:* since `trust` (`core/session/src/history.rs`). `index.json` holds the two stacks, oldest first, each step with its id, source (an agent's name too), name, the file it is shown as, whether it is raw or data only, the hashes it puts back, and the name of its record: its inverse Tx's JSON, in a file named by the SHA-256 of its bytes, written once. The song's hash (`history::song`) is of every file the session read (`layout.json` isn't one), an asset by its name alone. Every writer keeps it, the app and an `m2` command that writes with the app closed alike, so a change `m2` made while the app was closed is the agent's step in the app, by `--as`. A step read back keeps its id, source and name (the window's "Hats pan" too); the revision it made belongs to a session that is gone, so it is named by none. At 500 steps the index is 143 KB, and one write of it takes 3.3 ms on the reference Linux server's NVMe disk: once for each page edit, its name included. Tested in `core/session/src/history_tests.rs` and `apps/m2/tests/undo.rs`.

**WRITE-24.** A step read back MUST be offered only while it can still apply exactly. Each stack is walked from its newest step, from the files as they are now: a step whose every file holds the bytes it left (by hash) is offered, and the walk goes on from what its undo puts back; a step whose every file holds what it held before the step (it was undone, and its history's write was lost) is left out, and the walk goes on as it is. At any other step the walk ends there: that step and all older ones are dropped, whatever files they touch. So a change made outside music2 (git, an editor, an agent writing a file) while the song was closed, or one that made no undo step while it was open, ends undo at the newest step that touched its files: the steps after it can be undone, it and the ones before it can't. A new change ends redo (AGENT-2), across closing too: when the song isn't as the history last saw it, redo is dropped. The session that met such an end says so where Undo is, in words a pointer reaches: "Undo isn't possible past the change made outside music2". Redo the same. Once read back, steps are undone as WRITE-21 says.
- *Why:* an undo must put back what its step changed, byte for byte, never an old copy over a change it never saw. A step read back has no record of what happened while the song was closed, so only its exact bytes prove it still applies; and a step redone over a change made since would make a song that never was.
- *Today:* since `trust` (`Session::read_history` and `Session::walk` in `core/session/src/history.rs`). Each file's hash now is read as a Tx's target is (`Session::read_current`; a folder by its directory hash), and a before-image a step names must be in `.music2/tx/blobs/`. The state says why undo and redo end, `undo_end` and `redo_end` (`"outside"`, `"unread"` or null), for the session that met it only; the top bar shows the words on what holds Undo and Redo, which a pointer reaches while the buttons, disabled, take none (`web/src/components/TopBar.tsx`), and ⌘Z with nothing left answers with them. Tested by `undo_after_closing_puts_back_each_step_byte_for_byte`, `an_outside_change_while_closed_ends_undo_there`, `a_step_undone_before_its_history_was_written_is_left_out`, `a_new_change_ends_redo_across_closing`, `an_end_is_said_only_by_the_session_that_met_it`, and `e2e/tr-trust.mjs` (a hit test on Undo).

**WRITE-25.** A crash MUST NOT make the history offer a wrong undo, nor lose a step it can keep. A change that writes the song is written into the history ahead of its files: the stacks as they are, the step the change pushes and how it moves them (a commit pushes it on undo and ends redo; an undo moves undo's top to redo; a redo, redo's top to undo; a change taken back drops undo's top and redo), and the song as it will be. Read back, the files say whether it landed: it is taken as landed unless every file it touches holds what it held before it. Each record is written once, to a temporary name, pushed and renamed to its name; then the index, the same way; then records no step names go. A record that is missing, or whose bytes don't match its name, ends the walk there, and an index that can't be read (cut short, not JSON, another version's) opens the song with no undo; either way the Notice `undo.unread` says so, and Undo says it isn't possible past that point.
- *Why:* the change and its history can't be written as one, so the history goes first and the files decide: a crash at any point leaves the stacks as they were or as the change left them, never one step on the wrong one, and what is offered is proven against the files each time it is read.
- *Today:* since `trust` (`Session::history_ahead`, called before a commit's, a save's, an undo's or a redo's files are staged; `Move`). A change whose staging fails writes the history as it is again. Tested by `a_crash_right_after_a_redo_keeps_every_undo_step`, `a_crash_right_after_an_undo_puts_its_step_on_redo` (which also puts back a history written ahead of files that never landed), `a_commit_whose_history_wasnt_written_offers_no_wrong_undo`, `a_corrupt_or_cut_history_opens_with_no_undo_and_a_note` and `a_record_that_doesnt_match_its_name_ends_undo_there` (a record changed into another Tx that reads), and `a_skipped_step_stays_skipped_after_closing`, `a_change_taken_back_stays_taken_back_after_closing`. The crash tests copy `.music2/undo/` in the PLANE-5 hook, the moment before a change's journal, and put it back once the session is gone.

**WRITE-26.** The history MUST be bounded, over both stacks: at most 500 steps, 64 MiB of text and data in their inverses (as WRITE-21 counts them) and 256 MiB of before-images (a binary target's before bytes, such as a plugin's state, kept once by content in `.music2/tx/blobs/`, [WRITE-15](#commit-journal-and-recovery)), each image counted once however many steps name it. Past any of them undo's oldest steps go first, then redo's farthest, never either's top, after every change to the stacks and as the history is read back. The before-images a kept step needs are kept across closing and opening; the rest are tidied with the journals' ([WRITE-15](#commit-journal-and-recovery)).
- *Why:* a plugin's state is often 1 to 50 MB, and every autosave of it is a step: undo must not fill the disk, nor push every other step out for one plugin. A state that grows (a Sampler loading a sample) has a small image before it and a large one after it, which sits on redo once undone.
- *Today:* since `trust` (`Session::bound_stacks` in `core/session/src/session.rs`: a step knows each image it names, with its size, read when it is recorded or read back). Before, before-images weren't counted, and only the journals' were kept at close. Tested by `the_bound_drops_the_oldest_steps_on_disk_too`, `the_bound_counts_redo_and_each_image_once`, `the_bound_holds_for_a_history_read_back` and `a_plugin_states_before_image_survives_closing`.

### Gesture leases

**WRITE-22.** A drag uses a lease:
- `begin(anchor, code_rev)` resolves once through the session and returns the lease, its cells and the current value;
- `update(lease, seq, value)` goes straight to the engine as `SetCell`, never through the session, and out-of-order updates are dropped;
- `commit(lease, seq, value)` and `cancel(lease)` go to the session. A commit is one coalesced `Values` Tx, and the engine installs the committed value in the same step that ends the lease;
- at a `code_rev` change, the session carries the lease's anchor into the new revision ([WRITE-12](#bases-and-revisions)). If it carries and still reads the accepted value, the lease stays, with its cells mapped into the new plan. Otherwise the lease ends and the accepted value is put back.

The session runs as an actor on its own thread, so nothing that waits on a compile blocks the engine, the transport or a lease's updates.
- *Why:* previews must never wait on a compile, and an agent saving `tracks/drums.ts` must not snap back a knob being dragged in `tracks/bass.ts`.
- *Today:* matches since step 3b (`crates/server/src/graph.rs`, `graph/lease.rs`, `graph/actor.rs`), and its cells since step 5, with these differences until step 6:
  - `begin` resolves against what the session last published: a revision among the last 64, through its code revision, or an id of one of the two code revisions before the accepted one's, through aliases the session carries once per code change. Only a client further behind waits for the session, and so does one a code revision or two behind while a settle hasn't yet carried the aliases to the code revision it published (they are read only once they carry to the accepted one's). Its cells are a `CellTarget`, the `CellId`s of its value in the renderer that plays (`Begun.cells` counts them), and what can be leased is a literal written in code or a declared control a call leaves out, which plays its default until its commit writes it in (both `home: code`).
  - `update` is a cell write straight into the slots of the renderer the lease aims at (`Player::set_cells`, by its `CellTarget`), never through the queue, so it is never dropped or kept waiting (RENDER-28). It is written under the control plane's lock, as everything else for a lease is, and a renderer built while the lease lives gets its value before it is sent, so an activation loses no update. Its seq answers `Graph::applied` once a block has heard it: a block late at most, never early.
  - `commit` is one `Values` Tx since step 6 (`Gestures::set` in `crates/editor`), one undo step; for a control its call leaves out, it writes the argument in (the step 5 design's D5). An outside change in the knob's own file that compiles rides along in the commit's baseline, and doesn't end the drag (`a_knob_commit_after_an_outside_edit_in_its_file_keeps_both`). The committed value reaches the engine in the step that ends the lease: in a new plan prepared with it, else as a cell write.
  - At a code change, a lease is carried by its whole call (WRITE-12's carry, by `Edits::between`), which is stricter than its literal's range. A lease's value is written into every new plan before it is sent, so it plays from its first block, window included. When the new plan fails to prepare, the lease's control moves to the accepted revision while its updates go on to the plan that plays, by its own origins.
  - Pending the owner's decision on the step 3b design's clarifications: `cancel`, idle expiry and "the window closed" act on the control plane at once, never through the session (C2); a commit refused only because the draft doesn't compile is pending, the value plays on and the session writes it once the draft compiles, or cancels it after 30 s (C5); a lease ends when idle for its TTL (10 s, renewed by updates and the web's keepalive every 2 s while a knob is held; `ttl_ms` on `POST /api/graph/lease/begin`, at most 60 s), or when the WebSocket of the window that began it closes (`?client=`); the news and `GET /api/graph/leases` list every live lease, so an agent can cancel one (C6). A pending commit that ends unsaved (its 30 s, the project closing or another opening, an outside save that changes its control, a cancel) is said in a toast.
  - The knob routes (`/preview`, `/set`, `/preview/cancel`) find a lease by its gesture string and the control the request names, so a string holds a lease per control: a script that sends `"script"` for every knob reaches each one's own. A late request of a lease that ended says why, unless its own `/preview/cancel` ended it. `GET /api/graph` lists the live leases with their controls' ids as its view names them, so the page keeps a dragged knob mounted when a save renumbers it.
  - Since `ag-fwd`, an agent's change never ends a drag: it is refused while the drag lives; a drag begun while an agent's change lands takes the change back, and so does a change to any file that the drag couldn't carry to ([AGENT-3](#agents)).
  - Measured: `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs` (an outside save to another file during a drag never plays the code's value, not for one frame of the crossfade; saves above and below the dragged call keep it; a begin between a settle's phases; one gesture string on two controls; a pending commit that ends unsaved; a broken save then a release; a renderer that fails to build; a cancel during a commit; expiry and a closed window), and `m2-gates --server drag` (G9, p16: a drag through 12 saves, each a new renderer).

**WRITE-22a.** A lane edit made while playing (dragging a note, drawing automation) uses a lane lease: `begin(lane, clip)`; `update` sends a preview `SwapLane` straight to the engine with no file write, under the sounding-note rules of [RENDER-26a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps); `commit` is one `Clips` Tx; `cancel` swaps the accepted data back. A tempo drag is a lease too ([RENDER-27a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)).
- *Why:* a dragged note must be heard where it is during the drag, without a journaled commit, a revision and an undo entry for every step.
- *Today:* since `next` (8b.7; `crates/server/src/graph/lanes.rs`; routes `POST /api/graph/lane/lease/{begin,update,keepalive,commit,cancel}`). `begin` checks the gesture on the accepted revision the control plane last published, never waiting for the session (RENDER-29). Only drags of notes and automation (`move`, `resize`, `notes`, `points`, `create`) are previewed; a gesture on an audio lane needs renders, so it answers `previewable: false` and is heard on release, as before. `update` applies the newest gesture with the commit's own pure function and Tx id (`clips::apply_as`, Q21), and swaps each lane it edits into the renderer that plays (`SwapLane`, under RENDER-26a), timed in the tempo map that plays; a lane it no longer reaches gets its accepted data back. `commit` is one `Clips` Tx under the lease's Tx id, so its PLANE-5 preview is what the lease played and nothing heard changes; until it lands, a fresh engine plays what was let go (an activation keeps the swapped lane already). Each settle carries the live leases: a new plan, or new data for a lane a lease names, hears the preview again, applied on the new revision; a change to a dragged clip ends the lease ("Bass changed elsewhere: your drag was dropped."). Cancel, an idle TTL (10 s) and the window closing put the accepted data back. The page leases every drag while playing (clips, edges, automation points, notes in Notes); Esc cancels. Tested in `crates/server/src/graph/lanes_tests.rs`, `routes_leases_tests.rs` and `e2e/s8-leases.mjs`.

### Unsaved code

**WRITE-27.** The code editor's text that isn't saved yet MUST be kept in `.music2/drafts/` as it is typed (a second after the last key), each window's own, each with the hash of the file's text it was based on. It is never written to the song's files ([WRITE-1](#the-two-write-primitives)), and it goes when that window's file is saved or its text is the file's again. When the song next opens for writing, after a crash or any close that left one, or when a read-only window takes the lock, the drafts there are set aside, and the code editor offers each back: "song.ts has changes that weren't saved. Restore them?", with Restore and Discard. Each stays offered, and kept, until one of the two answers it, whatever is typed meanwhile. Restore puts the text in the editor, unsaved, as an edit the editor's own undo takes back; Discard throws it away; neither writes the song. A draft whose file changed since it was based on it is offered as such ("…and the file changed since then"), never applied silently; one whose file is gone offers its text to copy. A draft whose text is the file's is dropped without a word. A read-only window keeps none and offers none. Drafts are the musician's ([AGENT-11](#agents)): keeping and answering them, and their text, which an agent never reads.
- *Why:* code typed and not saved is work too, and a crash must not take it (gaps #30). A draft is the musician's text, not the song, so it lives with the editor's other local state and changes nothing that plays; an agent reads the song, never what the musician hasn't said yet.
- *Today:* since `trust` (`crates/server/src/graph/drafts.rs`). `POST /api/graph/draft` `{session, client, file, base, text}` (`text` null takes it out) writes `.music2/drafts/<16 hex digits of the path's SHA-256>-<16 of the window's>.json`, `{file, base, text}`, whole (`m2_os::fs::replace`). At the open (and Retry), read before the control plane's lock, they are moved into `.music2/drafts/left/`; `GET /api/graph/files` offers those as `left`: `{id, file, text, changed}`, and leaves them out for the tools key; the state lists their files as `drafts`; `POST /api/graph/draft/left` `{session, id}` says one was answered. The code editor posts each file's text a second after its last change, and null once it is clean (`web/src/lib/codeWorkspace.ts`); it shows each draft left in a bar, and the window says so in a notice with Show code when the song opens. Tested in `crates/server/src/graph/drafts_tests.rs` and `e2e/tr-trust.mjs`, which kills the server while a draft is kept, types in the file and edits elsewhere before answering, and copies a gone file's text.

## Agents

How the musician and agents share one open song: one writer, one undo history, and every change shown as it lands. The rules come from the [agents design](designs/agents/design.md), whose owner decisions D1 to D6 were all answered as it recommends (2026-10-23). Each of its tracks adds the rules it builds: AGENT-2 and AGENT-5 came with track 1 (`ag-steps`); AGENT-1, AGENT-3, AGENT-4, AGENT-9 and AGENT-10 with track 2 (`ag-fwd`); AGENT-6 with track 3 (`ag-see`); AGENT-8 and AGENT-11 with track 4 (`ag-routes`); AGENT-7 with track 5 (`ag-listen`), the last. AGENT-12 came with the docs plan (`docsite`), AGENT-13 with `flags`, and AGENT-14 with `upgrade`.

**AGENT-1.** While a music2 app holds a project's lock ([WRITE-17](#the-project-lock)), an `m2` command that writes the project MUST hand its Tx to that app instead of failing. It builds the Tx on a read-only session from the files on disk and checks it there, under its own expectation and its planner's assertion. It sends it to the app's `POST /api/tx/commit` with `seen`, the hash of that check, and the new assets it names, using the url and the tools key in `~/.music2/session.json` ([AGENT-10](#agents)). The app commits it only if its delta has that hash. `m2` forwards only when the lock's holder is the program `music2` with the pid that file names, and the app says this folder is the song it has open. Otherwise it fails before changing anything, naming the holder ([WRITE-18](#the-project-lock)). `m2 edit` and `m2 write` make a change to a song file a Tx with a base, so an agent changes files through the app while it is open. `m2 import` hands the files it brings in to the app's own import instead (`POST /api/graph/audio/import`), so they come into the song only inside the change that lands ([AGENT-3](#agents)). While the song's code on disk doesn't build, they save the file instead, as the code editor saves it ([WRITE-2](#the-two-write-primitives)), in the app while it is open: an agent's save lands only when the song takes it in as written, as one undo step, the agent's; otherwise nothing is written, and `m2` says why. A forwarded write prints what a local one prints, and says it is one undo step in the app. A forwarded request may be up to 144 MiB: room for a file's old text and its new one at what undo keeps (64 MiB each). `m2` says so when one is bigger, and sends nothing; an answer it can't read is said with its status, and never as a write that landed.
- *Why:* while the song is open there must be one writer and one history, and only a change that comes through the writer can be refused when the musician holds what it touches.
- *Today:* since `ag-fwd`. A read-only session can be given the lock's holder (`Session::forward_to`, the `Forward` trait in `core/session/src/forward.rs`): its `commit_with` checks, judges and asserts as a writer's does, then sends the Tx with its own expectation, the hash of that check, the files under `assets/` it took in, and what each target holds once it landed; once it landed, it takes the touched files in from disk. Its undo and redo are refused. The app's `POST /api/tx/commit` takes `seen`, `assets`, `as` and `why`: it takes the assets in, then commits under the Tx's own expectation, asserting the delta's hash is `seen` (422 otherwise), so the agent's step is judged when undone. Since the review of `ag-routes`, `m2 import` sends its files' paths, its target and its words to `POST /api/graph/audio/import` while the app has the song open: before, it copied them into `assets/` itself, then forwarded the Tx, so a busy import left them there and the watcher's batch closed the Compare pill (`m2_import_while_comparing_copies_nothing`). No `m2` command sends `assets` now (gaps). `apps/m2/src/app.rs` reaches the app (plain HTTP/1.1 to 127.0.0.1, waiting while its process lives) and says each case of the design's 5.2 in words; an app that went away mid-call is read from the disk (landed, a journal of this Tx, never started, or can't tell). `m2 fix` asks the app for the fix its problems offer (`POST /api/graph/fix`); `bounce --place` reads the song after its render; `m2 edit` and `m2 write` (`apps/m2/src/text.rs`) commit a lane file confined to its lane and anything else on its checked hash. While the code on disk doesn't build, they save the file (`Session::save_built`, which takes it in only as written, else writes nothing): forwarded to the app's `PUT /api/graph/file` (`Forward::save`), whose save for the tools key is that one, refused 422 with why; with the app closed, `m2` saves it itself. Other `m2` writes still need code that builds (gaps). `POST /api/tx/commit`, `/api/tx/check`, `/api/graph/fix` and `PUT /api/graph/file` take bodies up to 144 MiB (`TX_BODY` in `crates/server/src/lib.rs`), which `m2` checks before it sends. Tested in `core/session/src/tx/forward_tests.rs`, `apps/m2/tests/forward.rs` and `apps/m2/tests/edit.rs`.

**AGENT-2.** Every change the app accepts while it has the song open for writing (a Tx, a save, or a watcher batch that builds, [WRITE-19](#outside-writers)), other than an undo or a redo, MUST be exactly one undo step, with an id that is never reused in that history, and a source: the page, an agent by its name, or outside the app. An agent's step is named by the words it gives. Only the watcher's job makes outside steps: files the app takes in for its own Tx (an import's) make none. An outside step holds only what outside writers changed. Its inverse puts back each file they changed or removed as the app last knew it, a code-editor save that didn't build yet included, guarded by the hash the batch left, as a save's inverse is ([WRITE-21](#undo)), and removes each file they made, other than under `assets/`. A watcher batch makes no step when it only adds files under `assets/`; when git wrote it, which is when git's `index.lock` was seen while it settled and every file it changed is as git's index has it (it says so: git can undo it); and when its undo couldn't put every file back: it changed or removed a binary file (an asset or a plugin state), a file is a link or has another file's name in other capitals, or it is more than undo keeps (it says so, with how many other files it changed). A step undone and redone keeps its id and its source. Undo and redo name the step they mean by its id, and are refused when it isn't the newest (for redo, the newest undone). Undo's name says the source. A step whose undo is refused because a file it puts back changed since can be skipped: it leaves the history, nothing is written, and the steps before it can be undone. The window that makes a change keeps its step as its own as soon as it hears of it, whatever the route: from the change's answer, or for a take from the news that it was written. So its Undo takes the change back before the window shows it. The answer of every change says, at its top, the revision the change made (none when it made none, as a change of `layout.json` alone), its step, the files whose ids it moved (`moved`), whether it moved beats (`beats`: where music sits in time, as bars inserted or deleted, a tempo or a meter change), and whether that revision also took in changes made elsewhere (`mixed`: a file saved outside the app that rode along with the change, a commit's, an undo's, a redo's, a save's or a take's, as one that waited for the code to build; that file's change is outside the app's, not the window's). Which files a change moves is judged by its kind, never by comparing the two revisions' ids: a change that writes values where they are written, adding no call (a knob, a menu, a switch), and a change of data alone (a lane's clips and notes, `layout.json`) move none; every other change moves the ids of every file it touches (a code save, a fix, an effect added or moved, a track added, a value written as a new call, `db(-3)`, a preset); an undo or a redo moves what its step moved, beats included. Every request of the window's says which window it is, and the server keeps which window made each revision, and what it moved, whatever made it: a value let go while the code didn't build is that window's when it is written. A gesture the window starts while it doesn't show its own last change yet waits until it does. When a change of the window's own since the gesture was drawn moved ids in a file the gesture targets, or moved beats and the gesture names its places by beats (a Beat grid's step, a clip made, pasted, moved or trimmed, notes, points, bars), the gesture is not sent: it does nothing, says so ("The song changed while you pressed. Try again."), and the control is drawn as it is. Otherwise it goes from the window's newest own revision in the run of its own changes right after the revision it was drawn at (none of them mixed), or from the revision it was drawn at when there is none, and it says that revision to the server, as every gesture does: a value, a call, a clip, a lane, bars and the Beat grid's steps. The server refuses it, in words, when a change after that revision made by anyone but that window touched its target (bars: the arrangement or a lane; the Beat grid: its lane; a gesture named by beats, any change that moved beats, whatever lane it names), naming who made the newest such change: the agent by its name, outside the app, or in the app; an undo or a redo is its sender's. So neither the window's own change nor a change elsewhere to another target ever refuses its gesture, and a gesture never lands on a call it didn't press. A change of a list in `layout.json` (the markers, the track order) says the list it was made from, and is refused, in words, when the list changed since: no marker or move is ever lost silently. Changes that make no revision go one after another, each from the one before's result. A gesture's wait in which nothing moves for 2 s (none of the changes it waits for is answered, and the window shows nothing new) is said in words, and the gesture then does nothing.
- *Why:* whoever made a change, the musician can take it back with ⌘Z, sees who made it, and never takes back a step they didn't see named. Undo never takes back the musician's own work with someone else's change, and a step that can't be undone never blocks the ones before it. A quick musician is never refused for their own last change, and their gesture never lands on a call they didn't press: only a change made elsewhere to what they pressed can make it out of date, and the refusal says whose.
- *Today:* since `ag-steps`. Each `TxStep` has an id from a counter that never goes back, and a source, `Source::Page` or `Source::Outside` (`core/session/src/session.rs`); undo and redo carry both to the step they push. The watcher's job takes a batch in with `Session::take_in_batch` (`core/session/src/take.rs`), which records the outside step. It keeps, for each path a batch changed while it waits, what the session knew there before (a save takes its file out), so a batch that makes a waiting save build leaves the save out of its step; and it checks each path of the inverse as a commit would. The watcher says when it saw git's `index.lock` while the batch settled; the session then asks git (`git status`, which takes none of git's locks then) whether each changed file is as git's index has it. A read-only window records no step. `POST /api/graph/undo` and `/redo` take `{session, step}`, and answer 409 naming the newest step when `step` isn't it (`crates/server/src/graph/steps.rs`); without `step` they take the newest, as before. An undo refused because a file changed since answers 409 with `stuck`, and `POST /api/graph/undo/skip` `{session, step}` takes that step out of the history; the state's `undo_stuck` says the newest step can only be skipped. The state's `undo_name` and `redo_name` carry `step` and `source`; every edit answers with the `step` it made (`POST /api/tx/commit` too), and an undo or a redo with the step it took back and the tops after it. The page's Undo, ⌘Z and a toast's Undo send the step they name, or 0 when the window names none. The page keeps the ids of the steps its own edits made and names each one as its answer comes. The server makes an edit's step before its answer reaches the window, so a ⌘Z pressed in between named the step below and was refused, which a slow machine showed (`e2e/notes.mjs`). Pressed while the window's edits are on their way (`editing` in `web/src/lib/history.ts`; a timeline drag let go counts from then, while it waits for the state), Undo chooses its step at the press: the newest step the window keeps from the start of the first of those edits, once they are answered (an answer may come just before the press, its edit not over yet), or with none the step shown at the press, never one that lands meanwhile, so an agent's step landing during the wait is named, refusing, not taken back; refused because the newest is a step of the window's own it hadn't shown (a take's last pass), it takes that one (`undoTop` in `web/src/components/GraphCanvas.tsx`). It waits at most 2 s, then takes back nothing and says so; another ⌘Z pressed while it waits does nothing. A ⌘Z refused because the newest step is one of those, made a moment before the window showed it, is sent again for that step; any other newest step is named, never taken back. Since `ag-fwd`, a step made with the tools key has the source `agent`, with the name the agent gives (`Source::Agent`), and is named by its words, when it gives some (AGENT-10). Since the review of `ag-routes`, the routes the server names a step for (an audio import, a Sampler's file, an audio track, a MIDI file) name it in the job that makes it, by the agent's words when it gave some: before, a second job renamed it after its event was told, so `m2 call` and the Edit menu disagreed (`an_agents_step_is_named_by_its_words_in_the_same_job`, `m2_call_names_every_step_by_its_why`). A step of `layout.json` with no name is "the track order, colours, markers or the song's key". Tested in `core/session/src/tx/steps_tests.rs` and `crates/server/src/graph/steps_tests.rs`.
