- *Today:* since wave 3 (`@m2/synths` 2.0.0, `@m2/drums` 2.0.0, `@m2/effects` 1.2.0: `packs/@m2/*/src`). The polysynth's named sounds took `attack`, `decay`, `sustain`, `release` and `resonance` where their patch held them; `synth.bass`'s filter decay is `biteDecay`, beside `bite`, so `decay` means the loudness's on every sound; the FM keys and bell took `attack` and `sustain`. FM metal's release is its decay, so it draws no envelope; `synth.pluck`'s brightness is where its filter starts, not a cutoff that holds, so it draws no filter. Tested by `music2s_own_sounds_show_their_groups_and_views` (`crates/editor/src/views_tests.rs`: each sound's card has the groups and views its descriptor declares, More last; every instrument has groups; every sound with a whole envelope draws it, and with a cutoff and a resonance its filter), and every pure fixture renders what it did (`renders.json`), but those whose drums moved ([CARD-33](#music2s-own-sounds-on-the-card)).

**CARD-32.** The polysynth's wave shape MUST be a choice control, `shape: control.choice(["saw", "square", "triangle", "sine"], "saw")`, on `synth.custom` and on every named sound with a stack to shape (all but the 808 sub, a sine): a menu on its card, written by its name, played and automated with no rebuild ([CARD-10](#kinds-of-values)). It reaches the synth's kernel as its place, on the port `shape: control<choice<saw|square|triangle|sine>>` ([CARD-12](#kinds-of-values)), which says what the stack plays: at `saw`, its saws and pulses at their levels, as it always did (on a named sound, the sound as it was made); at `square`, `triangle` or `sine`, that wave alone (a square half high, half low, whatever `width` says; a sine with nothing of the pulse in it), as loud as the saws and pulses it replaces: the RMS of their mix, worked out from their levels and width, and with a sub at the note itself, which reinforces their fundamental. So a pick never jumps the loudness: each of music2's sounds, held, is within 1.5 dB of its saw at every shape, and a four-note chord of it stays under full scale. A change glides over the voice's control tick, as its levels do. The saw, its default, plays every bit it did.
- *Why:* the wave is the first thing a musician picks on a synth, and it is a name, not a number; a menu that made a sound 14 dB louder, or a "square" that is a thin pulse, would claim what isn't so (review Y2).
- *Today:* since wave 3: `synth.rs` (`packs/@m2/synths/kernels-src`, the `shape` port; at the saw the stack's code path is untouched, `shaped` false), `src/synth.ts`. Tested by `the_shape_picks_the_stacks_wave` (the kernel: in tune, the square's even harmonics gone, the triangle's falling faster than the saw's, the sine's almost none, a change mid-note no click), `each_shape_is_as_loud_as_the_saw_and_pulse_it_replaces` (the kernel: within 0.5 dB, open, for five mixes of saw, pulse and sub), `every_synth_sound_keeps_its_loudness_at_every_shape` (`core/session/src/gate_tests.rs`: every named sound and `synth.custom`, held, within 1.5 dB of its saw, and its chord under 0 dBFS), `a_synths_wave_shape_reaches_its_kernel_as_its_place` (`core/session/src/gate_tests.rs`: each name reaches the kernel as its place, and each sounds its own), and `engines_play_from_their_packs_with_the_bits_they_had_in_std`, whose render of `synth.custom` at its default shape is the one recorded before wave 3; the browser suite `dressed` picks one from a card.

**CARD-33.** `@m2/drums` MUST have one instrument for a single drum, One drum, `drums.drum(notes, { drum })`, where it had one for each drum: its menu, `drum: control.choice(["kick", "snare", "clap", "hats", "tom", "rim", "cowbell", "cymbal", "shaker"], "kick")`, labelled "sound", picks the drum every note plays. It is a control: its kernel, `@m2/drums:one`, reads it when a hit starts, so a pick plays from the next hit with no rebuild, and a lane can automate it (a lane of names, [CARD-23](#views-on-the-card)); its undo step reads "One drum sound". Every hit on its lane is of its one drum, so the kernel cuts a drum that chokes (kick, snare, clap, hats, cymbal) at the next hit, whatever group `drumhit` marked it with, and never one that rings on (tom, rim, cowbell, shaker). Its knobs move whichever drum it plays, from the drum's own, each audibly on every drum its menu offers: `tune` in semitones; `decay` times as long (the rim's ring, its level following, and the cowbell's too); `punch` more or less of a kick's thump or a snare's snap, and on the others how hard the hit's first 15 ms are (from softer at −1 to harder at 1); and `drive`. At 0, 1 and 0, and at its single's voices (4, or 6 for the hats and the tom; One drum's own default, one number for every drum, is 6), it plays each drum as its single instrument did, bit for bit, but for two hits of a drum that rings on, on one sample, which it counts as two hits where its single counted one (each hit's seed is its count). It names no notes for the piano roll: the hats' names (G#2 pedal, A#2 open) would name a kick's rows too, since a lane's names can't follow its menu yet (gaps.md, review Y14). The drum list is then Drum kit, One drum and FM metal: "Drum" beside "Drum kit" would be a row a word away from another, which the + Track list keeps apart (`the_instruments_are_stds_sounds_in_order`; build-notes-taste.md, T3). Since [KIT-2](#drum-kits) it is labelled Drum, its undo step reads "Drum sound", and the drum machine has left the list, so the Drums are Drum and FM metal. Since [KIT-20](#drum-kits) its menu holds every drum the drum machine plays, each a sound of its own, grouped by family: kick, kick 2, snare, snare 2, clap, hats, tom, rim, claves, cowbell, cymbal, cymbal 2, ride, ride 2, shaker, shaker 2 and tambourine.
- *Why:* nine instruments that differ in one word are one instrument with a menu (the owner, 2026-10-31); a value the built sound reads while it plays is a control (the design's §2.4); and a knob that moves "the drum it plays" must move every drum it can play, or the card claims what isn't so (review Y7).
- *Today:* since wave 3 (`@m2/drums` 2.0.0, which removed `drums.kick` … `drums.shaker`; `@m2/synths` 2.0.0 removed the simple `kick` and `hat`, and every song, template, test and suite that played them plays One drum). The drum kernel's `tune`, `decay` and `push` ports, each left out where it changes nothing, and its `one` entry (`packs/@m2/drums/kernels-src`). Tested by `the_drum_plays_each_drum_as_its_single_did` and `one_drum_plays_its_singles_bits_at_their_voices` (`core/session/src/gate_tests.rs`: each of the nine against its single's renders, recorded before they were removed: five hits, a dense beat, two hits on one sample), `one_drum_plays_each_drum_as_the_drum_kernel_does`, `one_drum_chokes_on_any_hit_where_its_drum_chokes`, `every_knob_of_one_drum_moves_every_drum_it_offers` and `a_drums_knobs_move_the_drum_it_plays` (the kernel), `music2s_own_sounds_show_their_groups_and_views` (its menu a choice control labelled "sound", voices its one setting), and the browser suite `dressed` (a drum picked from the menu, one undo step "One drum sound").

### Labels on the card

**In short:** a control or a setting may have a label, the words its card shows where its name in the code wouldn't read as words (`subOctave` is "sub octave"). One rule names a value everywhere: its label, else its name.

**CARD-34.** A setting MAY have a label, as a control has: `setting.int(-1).label("sub octave")`, by the one `.label()` controls and settings share, which takes text and gives back the same declaration, typed as it was (`@m2/core` 2.1.0). The descriptor carries a setting's label only when it is written, so every other descriptor keeps its bytes. A setting's label MUST be one line of 1 to 24 characters with no space at either end, which the build checks again, in words, since types can be bypassed. Every name a control or a setting has on its card, in its undo steps, in `m2 graph`, in the API and in a refusal MUST follow one rule: its label when it has one, else its name; a dictionary entry's by its key first, then its label or its name (`vox.to`, `vox.goes to`), the key also said apart (`entry`: `vox`), so a dot in a label is only text ("approx. time") and no one reads an entry out of it. That name is its row or its knob on the card ([CARD-7](#cards)), whatever drives its value (a knob, the code, a cable, a lane or a signal written in the call); its undo step, after the card's name as a sentence says it ("Polysynth sub octave": its first letter small unless it starts a word in capitals, the rest as written; a dictionary entry's by its own name, "Hats volume"; a plugin's parameter or a kernel's port, which no descriptor declares, in the lowercase words of its code address, as before); its line in `m2 graph`; its `label` and `entry` in the API's view ([CARD-1](#cards)); and the name a refusal, or a preset's words, say. The code still writes its name (`subOctave: -2`). Every value of std and of music2's own packs reads as words: one whose name is written in camelCase has a label.
- *Why:* a card that shows a setting by its key ("SubOctave", "TableA"), or an undo step that reads "Wavetable tablea", shows the code's spelling to a musician. One declaration names a value everywhere (the design's §2.1), as a control's label always did; one rule, so a card, an undo step and an agent never disagree on a name.
- *Today:* since track `labels` (`@m2/core` 2.1.0, `SDK_VERSION` in `core/script/src/lib.rs`): `Labelled`, the `label()` `ControlDecl` and `SettingDecl` share, and `labelProblem` in `core/script/sdk/core.ts`; `SettingDesc.label` in `core/script/src/component.rs`; `kind::named` in `crates/editor/src/kind.rs`, the one rule, which a setting's row (`settings.rs`), a control's knob (`controls.rs`), every input row of a card (`view.rs`: a read-only value, a cable, a signal) and a preset's words (`presets.rs`) call, and `kind::written`, which names by where it is written a value no descriptor declares; `controlLabel`, `inSentence` and `knobStep` in `web/src/lib/graph.ts`, which name a row from its `label` and `entry` and every undo step of a knob, a menu, a switch, a setting and a view's drag. An automation lane of a dictionary entry's control is named by its code name still (gaps.md). A control's label keeps `@m2/core` 2.0's check, a string, so every 2.x pack still builds ([PACK-22](#packs)); the next major checks it as a setting's (gaps.md). Labelled: `synth.custom`'s `subOctave` ("sub octave", `@m2/synths` 2.0.0, which so asks for `"@m2/core" = "2.1"`, [PACK-23](#packs), recorded again with its new files as no release has shipped it, [PACK-18](#packs)), the Wavetable's `tableA`, `fileA`, `tableB` and `fileB` ("table A", "file A" …; `@m2/wavetable` 1.0.0, asking for `"2.1"` and recorded again with its new files), std's Sampler's `root` ("root note") and EQ3's `lowFreq`, `midFreq` and `highFreq` ("low frequency" …); a voice count reads as words already (Voices), so it has none. std's version stays: no song's code or sound changes. Tested by `a_settings_label_is_carried_only_when_written`, `the_sdk_writes_a_settings_label_only_when_given` (the JSON the SDK writes, before serde reads it) and `a_settings_label_is_one_short_line` (`core/script/src/kinds_tests.rs`), `core/script/sdk/check/kinds.ts` (a labelled choice typed as before; a label that isn't text marked), `a_label_names_a_value_on_its_card` (`crates/editor/src/settings_tests.rs`: a setting's and a control's, in a dictionary entry too with its key apart, "approx. time" with none, and a refusal's words), `music2s_own_values_read_as_words` and `every_row_of_a_card_is_named_by_its_label` (`crates/editor/src/views_tests.rs`: EQ3's frequencies by their labels when the code works one out, a cable feeds one, or a signal written in the call does; review L2), `every_setting_of_every_std_component_is_on_its_card` (the Sampler's root note), `m2_set_reads_a_word_as_its_setting_takes_it` (`m2 graph`, `apps/m2/tests/settings.rs`), `a_pack_made_for_an_older_minor_of_core_still_builds` (`core/session/src/pack_tests.rs`: packs made for 2, 2.0 and 2.1, the last with a labelled setting) and the browser suites `cards` (a row "Hold A" and its undo step "Box hold A", a row "Voices", a knob "1.5x speed" and a row "Approx. track", whose undo step is "Box approx. track"; review L1) and `wavetable` (rows "Table A" and "Table B", never "TableA", and the undo step "Wavetable table A").


## Drum kits

**In short:** a drum kit is a group of Drum tracks that play into one bus: each drum has its own track, lane, fader and effects, and the kit has one fader too. Nothing about a kit is stored: it is worked out from the tracks and their outputs. The beat grid shows a kit's tracks as rows of steps. The design is [Drums, the FL way](designs/drums/design.md). Rule KIT-1 is what a kit is; KIT-2 to KIT-9 the Drum, its notes and making a kit (track `kits`); KIT-10 to KIT-19 the beat grid (track `beat`); KIT-20 and on, every drum of the drum machine a sound of the Drum's, making a drum machine track a kit (KIT-22 to KIT-24), and pads and recording a kit (KIT-25, KIT-26; track `kit-upgrade`).

**KIT-1.** A drum kit MUST be worked out from the song, never stored: a bus ([RENDER-34](#buses)) and every Drum track whose channel's output is that bus, in the song's track order (the timeline's, [FMT-19](#presentation-and-local-files)). A Drum track is a track that plays one `@m2/drums` Drum (`drums.drum`), from a notes lane of its own: a track with two Drums, or whose Drum's notes come from anything but one of its lanes, is in no kit. A track is in at most one kit, as its output names one bus. Deleting the bus, or sending a Drum track elsewhere, takes it out of the kit, and nothing else changes. A kit's name is its bus's label. One function MUST say which kits a song has, and the song's view, the beat grid, pads and `m2 graph` all read it: each kit's bus, its label and its rows, each row a track with its notes lane and the sound its Drum plays: its `drum` as written, or the default when the call leaves it out; null when the code works it out (a lane plays it, or a value no build can know).
- *Why:* one building block, the Drum on a track, and a group made of what music2 already has (tracks and a bus), so nothing new is saved or kept in step ([Drums](designs/drums/design.md) §2).
- *Today:* since track `beat`: `kits::drum_kits` in `crates/editor/src/kits.rs`, a Drum track and its sound by `kits::drum` (the voices of its one Drum frame read one lane node; its sound is the constant of its `drum` slot), the one place that says so, which a MIDI file's drums going out read too ([KIT-9](#drum-kits)); the graph view's `drum_kits` (`GraphView.drum_kits`, which the web reads as `DrumKit`, a name apart from the kit presets' `KitPreset`, [KIT-5](#drum-kits)), and a `kit` line per kit in `m2 graph`. Tested by `a_bus_with_three_drums_is_one_kit_in_track_order`, `a_drum_routed_to_the_master_is_in_no_kit`, `a_non_drum_track_on_the_bus_is_no_row`, `a_drum_fed_from_another_tracks_lane_is_no_row`, `deleting_the_bus_leaves_no_kit` and `a_sound_the_code_works_out_is_null` (`kits_tests.rs`), `m2_graph_says_each_drum_kit` (`apps/m2/tests/tracks.rs`), and through a running server by `a_kit_shows_in_the_view_and_takes_a_drum_and_its_steps` (`crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`). Pads don't read it yet (track `kit-upgrade`).

**KIT-2.** `@m2/drums`' one drum, `drums.drum` (id `drum`), MUST be labelled **Drum**: the one building block a kit is made of ([CARD-33](#music2s-own-sounds-on-the-card)). The drum machine on one track, `drums.kit` (id `drum-kit`), is labelled **Drum machine** and says `editor.unlisted` ([PKG-7](#component-descriptors)): + Track and Change instrument no longer offer it, and a song that plays it builds and sounds as before. Neither one's id or code changes. So the words "Drum kit" name only a group ([KIT-1](#drum-kits)), which + Track offers as a group of its own, **Drum kit (several tracks)** ([KIT-5](#drum-kits)); + Track's Drums are Drum and FM metal, and no two rows of + Track are a word or a letter apart (T3). A Drum's menu's undo step reads "Drum sound".
- *Why:* one building block, and one meaning for each word. "Drum" beside "Drum kit" as two instruments was a row a word away from another (T3); a group of several tracks, worded so, isn't ([Drums](designs/drums/design.md) D1). The drum machine leaves the list now and songs with `m2 upgrade` later (track `kit-upgrade`).
- *Today:* since track `kits` (`@m2/drums` 2.1.0, `packs/@m2/drums/src/index.ts`; every song the repo ships relocked, [PACK-18](#packs)). Tested by `the_instruments_are_stds_sounds_in_order` (`crates/editor/src/tracks_tests.rs`: Drum and FM metal the Drums, the drum machine left out and in the catalog as Drum machine, no two rows near), `swap_refuses_two_instruments` (`crates/editor/src/swap_tests.rs`: the drum machine is no instrument to change to), `a_drum_machine_lane_names_its_notes` (`crates/editor/src/note_names_tests.rs`: written in code, it names its rows as before), `music2s_own_sounds_show_their_groups_and_views` (the Drum's menu, by its card "Drum") and `tracks_are_added_duplicated_and_deleted_and_the_player_follows` (the server's list).

**KIT-3.** A drum's descriptor, an instrument of category "Drums" ([PKG-7](#component-descriptors)), MAY name kits, `editor.kits` (`@m2/core` 2.2.0): `{ "Basic kit": { db: -2, tracks: [{ name: "Kick", drum: "kick" }, { name: "Hats", drum: "hats", decay: 0.8 }] } }`, each kit by its name, with the tracks a new one makes, in order, and its bus's fader (`db`, in dB, as a mixer channel's: 0 when left out). Kits are a drum's only: the build refuses them on any other component, in words ("editor.kits: only a drum (editor.category \"Drums\") names kits"), and TypeScript marks the category; a wider kit can come later as an addition (a minor), where narrowing would be a new major ([PACK-22](#packs)). A kit's name and each track's `name` MUST be one line of 1 to 24 characters with no space at either end, a track's name once in its kit, and a kit has a track at least. A track's other fields, and the kit's `db`, are values of the instrument's own controls (and of a channel's fader), taken as a call takes them, with one meaning for a number: a choice by one of its names, a switch true or false, a number as a quantity in the control's unit (`ms(80)`, `semis(2)`) or a plain number in that unit's base, as a call reads it (`80` on an `ms` knob is 80 seconds), inside its range when the range is strict. A quantity of another unit is refused, even one the control accepts in a call (a kit has no note to turn semitones into hertz with). TypeScript checks each against the descriptor's own controls and their units, so a sound the Drum doesn't have is marked where it is written; the build checks it again, in words, since types can be bypassed. The descriptor carries `kits` only when there are some: each kit's `name` and `tracks`, and its `db` only when given; each track's `name` and `values`, each value as `insert` carries one (a choice's place, a switch's 1 or 0, a number in base units with the spelling it was written in, none for a plain number), all in order. So every other descriptor keeps its bytes, and a pack made for `@m2/core` 2.0 or 2.1 builds as before ([PACK-22](#packs)); a pack that names kits asks for `"@m2/core" = "2.2"` ([PACK-23](#packs)).
- *Why:* most things live in packs (the owner's rule): the pack that has the Drum says which kits a beginner starts from, as typed data in the descriptor the lists already read, so no new file kind, and a kit that names a sound the Drum doesn't have can't be written. A kit is drums because everything that makes one is (the group's words in + Track, the Drums bus, a drum's lane); a value means what it means in a call, so a pack's author can copy one in.
- *Today:* since track `kits` (`@m2/core` 2.2.0, `SDK_VERSION` in `core/script/src/lib.rs`): `Kit`, `KitTrack`, `KitsFit`, `kitNumber` and the kits' check in `check` (`core/script/sdk/core.ts`; a unit constructor's quantity remembers its spelling and written number, `writings`); `Editor.kits`, `Kit` (its `db`) and `KitTrack` in `core/script/src/component.rs`. Tested by `kits_are_carried_in_order`, `a_descriptor_with_no_kits_keeps_its_bytes` (the JSON the SDK writes, before serde reads it; a kit with no fader has no `db`) and `a_wrong_kit_is_refused_in_words` (`core/script/src/kits_tests.rs`: 21 refusals past the types, an effect's kits and a Keys instrument's), `core/script/sdk/check/kits.ts` (a sound the drum doesn't have, a place for a name, a switch's 1, `hz(2)` on a knob in semitones, a setting, a track with no name, a fader in seconds, a Keys instrument's kits: each marked, saying what is wrong) and `a_pack_made_for_an_older_minor_of_core_still_builds` (`core/session/src/pack_tests.rs`: packs made for 2, 2.0, 2.1 and 2.2, the last a drum with kits, every other byte of its descriptor as made for 2).

**KIT-4.** `@m2/drums` 2.1.0 MUST name three kits on the Drum, each one a beginner can start a beat from: **808 kit**, eight tracks (Kick, Snare, Clap, Hats, Toms, Rim, Cowbell, Cymbal), its kick the 808's (`punch: -0.5`: the kernel's punch at 0, the long boom); **909 kit**, seven (Kick, Snare, Clap, Hats, Toms, Rim, Cymbal), its kick the 909's (`punch: 0.5`) and its snare snappier; and **Basic kit**, three (Kick, Snare, Hats). Each kit plays its hats on one Hats track, closed, pedal and open by the note, so a closed hat cuts an open one ([Drums](designs/drums/design.md) §4). Each kit says its bus's fader so that its beat, two bars at velocity 0.8 (the kick on every beat, the snare and the clap with it on 2 and 4, the hats on the eighths, the last one open, a cymbal on the first beat, the rim and the cowbell on offbeats, a tom fill at the end, each as the kit has them), comes out of its bus under −1 dBTP, and as loud as the drum machine's beat was, about −17 LUFS (±2); where both can't hold, the peak holds, and the kit says so.
- *Why:* a beginner starts from a kit that sounds like one, and picks among a few, not twenty. One Hats track keeps the choke the hats have always had. Each drum alone peaks from −3 to −15 dB at full velocity, but a kick, a snare and a clap on one beat go over full scale: the kit takes off what it needs on its one fader, where a musician sees it, and sits as loud as the drums it replaces.
- *Today:* since track `kits` (`@m2/drums` 2.1.0, `packs/@m2/drums/src/index.ts`): the 808's snare more tone than snap (`punch: -0.3`), its toms and cymbal ringing long (`decay: 1.5`); the 909's snare snapping (`punch: 0.3`), its hats and cymbal brighter (`tune: 2`), its toms shorter (`decay: 0.8`). Their faders, measured on the beat out of the bus: the 808 kit at −4 dB, −21.1 LUFS and −1.5 dBTP (at −3.5 dB its fill would reach −1 dBTP, so it stays 2 LU under the drum machine's −16.8: the peak holds); the 909 kit at −3.8 dB, −18.9 LUFS, −1.2 dBTP; the Basic kit at −2 dB, −18.8 LUFS, −2.1 dBTP. Tested by `every_kit_plays_its_beat_under_its_peak_and_as_loud_as_the_drum_machine` (`crates/editor/src/drums_tests.rs`: `m2 listen`'s measures of the bus as heard) and `the_kits_are_the_packs_own`.

**KIT-5.** + Track MUST offer every kit the instruments name ([KIT-3](#drum-kits)) in a group of its own, **Drum kit (several tracks)**, right after the Drums. Adding one is one Tx, one undo step ([WRITE-10](#expectations-and-the-delta)): a **Drums** bus (a Group, `gain(input)`, as Add bus writes one, [RENDER-34](#buses), named as a track is, "Drums 2" when the name is taken), its channel's fader at the kit's `db` when it gives one (`drums: { in: drumsTrack.out, db: -2 }`: the fader the Mix view shows, [KIT-4](#drum-kits)), and the kit's tracks, each in `tracks/<key>.ts` playing the instrument from an empty notes lane of its own (`hits` for a drum), named, with the kit's values written in its call (`drums.drum(lane.notes("hits"), { drum: "kick", punch: -0.5 })`), and with a channel of its own that goes to the bus (`kick: { in: kick.out, to: "drums" }`). They go together after the selected track (else last), the bus first, then its tracks in the kit's order, each in a colour of its own. The planner asserts exactly those modules added, the bus a bus, each track in its own channel going to the bus, and no other routing. One undo takes all of it back. The graph state lists the kits the instruments name as `kit_presets` (each its name, its instrument's ComponentId and label, and its tracks' names: presets to make a kit from, apart from the song's own kits, `view.drum_kits`, [KIT-1](#drum-kits)); the route `POST /api/graph/track/kit` (`{session, component, kit, after?}`) and `m2 add-kit <project> <kit> [--after ID]` add one, by its name.
- *Why:* a kit is tracks and a bus ([KIT-1](#drum-kits)), so making one is what + Track and Add bus already write, at once, by one builder: one gesture, one undo step, and the song's code says all of it. Its level is the bus's one fader, never a second level hidden in its code.
- *Today:* since track `kits`: `presets`, `add_kit` and `kit_bus` in `crates/editor/src/drums.rs`, through `tracks::add_tracks` (`Play::Bus`, a new track's typed `values`, `to` (`Goes::New`, a bus of the same call; `Goes::Bus`, one the mixer has, KIT-15) and `db`, its planner's `routed_as`, the one check of every channel's output), which Add bus is too (`bus::add_bus`); `GET /api/graph` lists `kit_presets`, `POST /api/graph/track/kit` (`crates/server/src/routes.rs`), `m2 add-kit` (`apps/m2/src/main.rs`); + Track's group (`KITS`, `Row`, `kitRows` and `addKit` in `web/src/components/Tracks.tsx`: a kit row is found by its group's words too, its page id holds no space). Tested by `a_kit_is_a_bus_and_its_tracks_in_one_undo_step`, `a_kit_takes_free_names_after_the_selected_track`, `a_kit_nobody_names_is_refused` and `the_empty_templates_drum_lines_build_beside_a_kit` (`crates/editor/src/drums_tests.rs`), `the_instruments_are_stds_sounds_in_order` (the kits' rows near no other), `planned_call_forms_are_current` (each kit's tracks), the bus tests (`crates/editor/src/bus_tests.rs`, Add bus by the same builder), `the_kit_route_adds_a_bus_and_its_tracks` (`crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`), `tracks_are_added_duplicated_deleted_and_laid_out_from_the_terminal` (`apps/m2/tests/tracks.rs`) and the browser suites `kits` and `st-guide` (only a kit ticks the first song's Add drums).

**KIT-6.** One table MUST say which of the Drum's sounds answers to each General MIDI drum note, and what a kit's track of that sound is called: `m2_editor::drums::GM`, in the Drum's menu order, kick 36 (Kick), kick 2 35 (Kick 2), snare 38 (Snare), snare 2 40 (Snare 2), clap 39 (Clap), hats 42 44 46 (Hats), tom 41 43 45 47 48 50 (Toms), rim 37 (Rim), claves 75 (Claves), cowbell 56 (Cowbell), cymbal 49 52 55 (Cymbal), cymbal 2 57 (Cymbal 2), ride 51 53 (Ride), ride 2 59 (Ride 2), shaker 69 (Shaker), shaker 2 70 (Shaker 2), tambourine 54 (Tambourine), no note in two. Every note the drum machine plays, and every note its rows name (its `editor.notes`), is in it. Since [KIT-20](#drum-kits) each note the drum machine plays is its sound's very drum: at each, the drum machine and a Drum of that sound play the same bits, the hats' pedal and open too, but the toms, which the drum machine spreads 1.5 semitones a step (41 to 50) and a Drum's tom plays chromatically. One more table says which key of a Drum's chromatic tom plays each of the drum machine's toms, rounded to a key: 41 is 39, 43 is 42, 45 is 45, 47 is 48, 48 is 50 and 50 is 53 (`drums::TOMS`); Make it a kit ([KIT-22](#drum-kits)), a MIDI file's drums coming in ([KIT-8](#drum-kits)) and a Toms track going out ([KIT-9](#drum-kits)) all read it, so a tom fill goes round as it came. At 52, 53 and 55 (a Chinese cymbal, a ride bell, a splash) the drum machine is silent; a MIDI file's come in on the Cymbal and the Ride. A sound's own note, the first of its notes but the tom's 45, is the one music2 writes for another sound's note ([KIT-9](#drum-kits)).
- *Why:* a Drum answers to its sound's notes, worked out, never stored ([Drums](designs/drums/design.md) §7); one table for MIDI files in and out, for splitting the drum machine's lane and for pads, so they never disagree. One sound a note (KIT-20, track `kit-upgrade`): a drum machine's lane splits into tracks that play its very drums, where the table first gave each note to a family, and a Cymbal track played a ride as a crash.
- *Today:* since track `kits`, one sound a note since track `kit-upgrade`: `GM`, `sound_of`, `notes_of`, `own_note`, and the toms' `TOMS` and `tom_key` (read by `machine::plan`, `kit_of` and `general_midi`; tested by `a_kits_toms_go_round_as_the_drum_machines` in `crates/editor/src/machine_tests.rs`: a fill made a kit goes out as the drum machine's toms and comes back at the keys it went out from) in `crates/editor/src/drums.rs`; the drum machine's map is `kind_of` (`std/kernels-src/dsp/src/kit.rs`), and the Drum's sounds past the nine are `MORE` in `packs/@m2/drums/kernels-src/src/drum.rs`. Tested by `the_table_is_the_drum_machines` (the drum kernel's tests: the editor's table, read from its source, in the Drum's menu order, gives every note the drum machine plays to the sound that plays its very bits there, but the toms, and leaves exactly 52, 53 and 55 to a silent drum machine), `each_sound_answers_to_its_general_midi_notes` (`crates/editor/src/drums_tests.rs`: the table and its words, no note in two, every row the drum machine names, the presets' words the table's) and `the_drum_machine_plays_each_note_as_the_table_says` (rendered: silent off the table and at 52, 53 and 55; the same bits at every other note on a Drum of its sound, but the toms'; the nearest Drum of a tom note by band levels the Toms).

**KIT-7.** `m2_editor::drums::split` MUST turn one lane of the drum machine's notes into a lane for each sound of [KIT-6](#drum-kits) that its notes use, in the Drum's menu order, each note as it was (its pitch, start, length and velocity), so the hats keep closed, pedal and open, and the toms their pitches. The notes no sound answers to come back apart, in order: never dropped.
- *Why:* one function for every place a drum machine's lane becomes a kit: a MIDI file's drums here, a song's drum machine in `m2 upgrade` (track `kit-upgrade`).
- *Today:* since track `kits`: `split` and `Split` in `crates/editor/src/drums.rs`. Tested by `split_gives_each_sound_its_own_notes` (`crates/editor/src/drums_tests.rs`: a tuned note by the key it plays, every note as it was, the rest apart).

**KIT-8.** A MIDI file's drums, its parts on channel 10, MUST come in as one kit ([KIT-1](#drum-kits)): a Drums bus, where the first of them is among the file's parts, and one Drum track for each sound their notes use, by `split` ([KIT-7](#drum-kits)), in the Drum's menu order, each named by its sound as the table words it ([KIT-6](#drum-kits): Kick, Snare, Hats, Toms…), playing it, with that sound's notes, at their own pitches and velocities (the toms at the keys the toms' table gives them, [KIT-6](#drum-kits), since track `kit-upgrade`), in one clip from the import's beat to the bar line after the drums' last note. The notes no sound answers to go on one more track of the kit, Percussion, a Drum playing its tom, which plays each at its own pitch, so none is lost and each is heard. Every part of the file, the kit and the other tracks, comes in as one Tx, one undo step. Channel 10 no longer plays the drum machine. A file of one part goes into a lane it is dropped on whole only when the part isn't drums, or the lane isn't a Drum's (a drum machine's lane plays General MIDI, so it takes the drums as they are); a drum part dropped onto a Drum's lane comes in as a kit after that track. A file music2 wrote of a kit ([KIT-9](#drum-kits)), one MIDI track for each Drum track, so comes back as one kit, its Percussion too.
- *Why:* each drum on its own track, to mix alone, as a kit made from + Track has, and a part's notes all come in. A file often holds its drums as several tracks (one for the kick, one for the rest), and they are one kit. A Drum plays every note as its one sound, so a beat poured into one would come back as all kicks.
- *Today:* since track `kits`: `Part.drums`, `import_tracks`, `into_lane` and `import_into`'s refusal in `crates/editor/src/midifile.rs`, decided in the server's job (`crates/server/src/graph/exchange.rs`); `kit_of` in `crates/editor/src/drums.rs`. Tested by `a_general_midi_drum_file_comes_in_as_one_kit` (`crates/editor/src/midifile_tests.rs`: two tracks of drums and a bass; the tracks in order, Toms by the table's word, each going to the bus at 0 dB and playing its sound, each note back, the ride bell the cymbal's, Percussion's too, one clip each to one bar line, one undo), `a_drum_part_dropped_on_a_drums_lane_is_a_kit`, `a_percussion_track_round_trips`, `channel_10_is_the_drums`, `an_import_makes_a_track_for_each_part`, `an_exported_song_imports_back_as_tracks` (first-song's Kick and Hats, two parts, back as one kit) and `a_drum_loop_dropped_on_a_drums_lane_comes_in_as_a_kit` (`crates/server/src/routes_exchange_tests.rs`).

**KIT-9.** A MIDI file music2 writes MUST put a Drum track's notes on channel 10 at the General MIDI notes of the sound its Drum plays ([KIT-6](#drum-kits)): a note on one of the sound's notes keeps it (closed, pedal and open hats); a Toms track's note goes out by the toms' table ([KIT-6](#drum-kits): 39, 42, 45, 48, 50 and 53 as 41, 43, 45, 47, 48 and 50), another key as the tom of the nearest key in it (the lower one at an equal distance); a note of another sound goes to the sound's own note (a Kick track's D2, 38, goes out as 36); a note no sound answers to keeps its key when it is one of General MIDI's drums, 35 to 81 (a Percussion track's bongos and triangle go out as they came in), and any other goes to the sound's own note (first-song's Kick, written on C1, 24, goes out as 36). A Drum whose sound the code works out (a lane, a signal) keeps its notes' pitches.
- *Why:* a Drum plays any note as its sound, but another app plays channel 10 by General MIDI: a kick written on the snare's note must reach it as a kick, not a snare; and a note no sound of the Drum's answers to, but one General MIDI's drums have, is already that drum, which a round trip keeps; a note past them is no drum at all to another app, so it goes out as the track's.
- *Today:* since track `kits`: `general_midi` and `sounds_played` (each Drum track's sound, by `kits::drum`, [KIT-1](#drum-kits)) in `crates/editor/src/drums.rs`, `as_drums` in `crates/editor/src/midifile.rs`, for a song and for one clip. Tested by `a_drums_note_goes_out_as_its_sounds` (`crates/editor/src/drums_tests.rs`), `a_kits_tracks_go_out_as_general_midi_drums` (`crates/editor/src/midifile_tests.rs`: the 808 kit's Kick, Hats and Toms; a clip alone; a Kick whose sound a lane plays keeps its pitches), `a_percussion_track_round_trips` and `an_exported_song_imports_back_as_tracks`; the drum machine's lane stays on channel 10 as it plays (`a_drum_machine_lane_is_channel_10`).

**KIT-10.** The beat grid is a view of the dock, **Beat**, beside Notes (⌥5), shown while the selected track is a kit's bus or one of its rows. It shows that kit: a row per row of KIT-1, in its order, each with its track's colour, its name and its sound, then a column per step (KIT-11) across the song's bars and one bar more; a ruler of bars that plays from where it is clicked; the playhead; and a velocity lane for one row (KIT-13). It scrolls and zooms (⌘ + wheel, its zoom buttons) as Notes does. The dock's view stays the window's one choice ([taste.md §3.7](designs/step8/taste.md)): while the selection is in no kit, the dock shows Notes in Beat's place, and Beat again once a kit's track is selected; ⌥5 (or View ▸ Beat) then opens nothing and says why, in a toast. Clicking a row's name selects its track. A kit's bus says in its timeline row that it is the kit, which drums play into it, and that Beat (⌥5) writes their beat. Nothing of the grid is saved but its step, with the song's view settings, as the roll's grid is.
- *Why:* D4: a grid of tracks is a different thing from a grid of pitches, and it is a kit's, so it shows when a kit is what you work on.
- *Today:* since track `beat`: `web/src/components/BeatView.tsx`; the dock shows it by `dockView` (`web/src/lib/graph.ts`), which the focus record says too (its view `beat`, `crates/server/src/graph/focus.rs`); its step is the grid `beat` of `web/src/lib/grid.ts`; ⌥5 with no kit says why (`appCommand` in `web/src/App.tsx`), and a bus's row says how tracks reach it (`BusWords` in `SongTimeline.tsx`). The suite runs on a kit made as a musician makes one, + Track ▸ Drum kit ▸ Basic kit ([KIT-5](#drum-kits)). It draws only the steps near what shows, so a long song's grid costs what a short one's does (a 128-bar song's 8,000 cells redrew in about 120 ms; the 300 it draws, in a frame). Tested by `e2e/beat-grid.mjs`.

**KIT-11.** A step is the span from one line of the grid to the next. The grid's step is its own choice, as the roll's is: 1/4 to 1/64, in triplets or not, 1/16 at first; its lines start again at each bar line, so a bar's last step is cut at the bar line. A row's hits are the notes its lane plays, each cut to its clip ([FMT-8a](#lane-files-and-clip-ids)), wherever they are: a step is on when a hit starts in it. Two places in beats a millionth apart or less are one place (the lane format writes beats to a millionth): the web and the editor each say so with one constant, `SAME_PLACE`, for which step a hit starts in, where a step ends and whether a beat is on a line. A hit that starts on its step's start is on the grid, however long it lasts: a Drum sounds the same whatever a note's length. One that starts after it is off the grid: drawn where it starts and as long as it lasts, and never moved by the grid.
- *Why:* the grid shows what plays, and a note written in Notes or by an agent keeps its place. A short hats note or a long kick is a hit on its step like any other, at any step the grid shows.
- *Today:* since track `beat`: the grid's steps (`stepsTo`) and off-grid marks in `BeatView.tsx`; the server's step ends at the same lines (`triplet_steps_end_at_the_next_line`, `a_step_holds_every_note_that_starts_in_it`), and both place a note a millionth before a line in that line's step (`a_hit_a_millionth_before_a_line_is_in_that_lines_step`; in the app, `e2e/beat-grid.mjs`). The suite also checks first-song's short hats and the Kick at ⌘2's eighths show no marks.

**KIT-12.** The grid writes a row's lane only through one clip gesture, `hits` ([WRITE-10](#expectations-and-the-delta)'s clip gestures: `Confined { lanes }`, one Tx, one undo step). It names steps of one lane by their starts and the grid's step, and says what each becomes; a start that isn't on a line of the grid (counted from its bar's start, KIT-11) is refused, in words that say where steps start:

| `to` | What each step becomes |
|---|---|
| `on` | where no hit starts in it, a hit at its start, a step long, at the row's pitch (KIT-14) and the lane format's velocity (0.8) |
| `off` | every note that starts in it goes, off the grid or not |
| `{ "velocity": v }` | every note that starts in it plays at `v`, above 0 and at most 1 |

A hit goes into the clip that holds its start (of several, the one that starts last); a hit in no clip goes into a new clip the length of its bar, cut short where another clip is, which the gesture's other hits in that bar share. A lane no Drum track plays is refused. In the grid: a click on a step turns it on, or off when it is on; a drag along the row from it does the same to every step it crosses, as one gesture; dragging a hit's bar in the velocity lane sets its step's velocity, as one gesture.
- *Why:* what a step means is decided once, in the editor ([WRITE-9](#expectations-and-the-delta)), so the app, `m2 clips` and agents write the same notes. The lane is the one the timeline and Notes show, so an edit in any of them shows in the others.
- *Today:* since track `beat`: `Gesture::Hits` and `hits` in `crates/editor/src/clips.rs`, sent by the grid through `POST /api/graph/clips`. Tested by `a_step_is_one_undo_step_on_its_lane_at_its_pitch_and_time`, `a_hit_in_no_clip_gets_its_bar_cut_at_the_clips_beside_it`, `a_step_holds_every_note_that_starts_in_it` and `steps_are_refused_in_words` (a start off the lines among them) (`kits_tests.rs`), and in the app by `e2e/beat-grid.mjs` (a click, a drag along a row, an off-grid hit clicked away, a velocity drag: each one revision, one undo). The grid sends its gestures one after another, so two clicks land in the order made, whichever request is quicker (the suite holds the first back).

**KIT-13.** The velocity lane shows one row's hits, each a bar as tall as its velocity, as the roll's velocity lane does: the selected track's row, or the kit's first row while the bus is selected. A press on a row's step selects its track, as a click on its name does, so the lane shows the row worked on. The lane names its row.
- *Today:* since track `beat`, in `BeatView.tsx` (`e2e/beat-grid.mjs`).

**KIT-14.** A new hit plays at the pitch most of its row's notes play (of a tie, the lowest), and on a row with no notes at its sound's own note ([KIT-6](#drum-kits): kick 36, snare 38, hats 42 the closed hat, tom 45 the tom's own pitch…, and each sound [KIT-20](#drum-kits) adds at its note, kick 2 35, ride 51…). A Drum whose sound the code works out writes A2 (45), which plays every sound as itself.
- *Why:* any note plays a Drum, so a row keeps the pitch its notes have, and a new row writes the note a pad or a MIDI file uses for that drum. The hats row writes the closed hat: most hats in a beat are closed; an open hat is a note at A#2 (46), written in Notes or by a pad, and the grid then shows it as a hit like any other.
- *Today:* since track `beat`: `kits::pitch` in `crates/editor/src/kits.rs`, its own notes `drums::own_note` (KIT-6's `GM`). Tested by `a_rows_first_hit_plays_its_sounds_own_note` and `a_row_keeps_the_pitch_most_of_its_notes_play`.

**KIT-15.** **+ Drum** in the grid adds one Drum track to the kit, as one undo step: a track playing the Drum at the sound picked from its menu (the Drum's sounds), its `drum` written, named as a kit's track of that sound is ([KIT-6](#drum-kits): `Snare`, `Toms`; `Snare 2` when taken), with an empty `hits` lane, its mixer channel going to the kit's bus (`to`), placed in the timeline after the kit's last row. It is add a track's Tx ([WRITE-10](#expectations-and-the-delta)), and the planner also asserts the new channel's output.
- *Today:* since track `beat`: `kits::add_drum`, through `tracks::add_tracks` as + Track's Drum kit is ([KIT-5](#drum-kits)): its channel goes to the kit's bus (`Goes::Bus`, a bus channel the mixer has, checked before anything is written; `routed_as` asserts every channel's output on the candidate) and its sound is a typed value of its call (`kits::sound_value`, an `Insert` as a kit's values are); `POST /api/graph/kit/drum`. Tested by `plus_drum_adds_a_row_routed_to_the_bus`, `plus_drum_is_refused_in_words` and `a_new_track_goes_only_to_a_bus_there_is` (the refusals and `routed_as`, `tracks_tests.rs`); its call form is in `crates/editor/tests/forms/forms.ts` (PKG-11); `e2e/beat-grid.mjs` adds a Clap and hears it.

**KIT-20.** The Drum's menu MUST hold every drum the drum machine plays, each a sound of its own that plays at every note as that drum, as the Drum has always played its one sound (FL Studio's way: one sound a channel). The menu is grouped by family, each other drum the drum machine plays beside the drum it is most like, named as General MIDI names it, plainly: **kick**, **kick 2** (the drum machine's 35: the kick 2 semitones down and softer), **snare**, **snare 2** (40: 2 up and snappier), **clap**, **hats**, **tom**, **rim**, **claves** (75: two wooden sticks clicked together), **cowbell**, **cymbal** (a crash), **cymbal 2** (57: the crash 2 up), **ride** (51: a cymbal that rings on under a beat), **ride 2** (59: the ride 2 up), **shaker**, **shaker 2** (70: 3 up) and **tambourine** (54: a shaken frame of little cymbals). A song writes a sound by its name, and the drum kernel reads its place in this order (`SOUNDS`, read with `m2_dsp::kit::kind_of`, which doesn't change, so std doesn't): each name plays the drum it always played, every bit. Each other drum is the drum machine's at its own note, at any note: the same drum, tuning, level and length; its noise, seeded by the note's key as every Drum's is, follows the note it is on, so at its own note it plays the drum machine's very bits. The hats still play closed, pedal and open by the note, and the tom its pitch, A2 at its tune, each one instrument. The tom stays chromatic, where the drum machine spread its six toms 1.5 semitones a step (41 to 50): the toms' table moves those notes ([KIT-6](#drum-kits)).
- *Why:* one sound a track: a drum machine's lane splits into tracks that each play one of its drums, a kit made so has a Ride track of its own rather than a Cymbal that plays two cymbals, and a single drum's song, upgraded, stays exact at any note ([FMT-25](#conversion)). A beginner reads a menu of families, each drum's other kind beside it, with General MIDI's plain "2" for the second of one drum. A tom is a tuned drum: one note a semitone, as a beginner hears it on the keys, and the single tom's songs keep their sound.
- *Today:* since track `kit-upgrade` (`@m2/drums` 2.1.0 recorded again): `DRUMS` in `packs/@m2/drums/src/index.ts`, the `one` kernel's menu in its `m2pkg.toml`, and `SOUNDS` and `sound_at` in `packs/@m2/drums/kernels-src/src/drum.rs`; std is as it was. Tested by `each_other_drum_is_the_drum_machines_at_its_note` (its drum at any note, the drum machine's bits at its own), `the_table_is_the_drum_machines` and `the_first_nine_play_as_before` (the nine at twelve notes each, by their places in today's menu, the bits `@m2/drums` 2.1.0 played before, pinned), with the menu's seventeen in `one_drum_plays_each_drum_as_the_drum_kernel_does` and `every_knob_of_one_drum_moves_every_drum_it_offers` (`packs/@m2/drums/kernels-src/src/drum/tests.rs`); `a_one_sound_lane_plays_the_drum_machines_bits_on_a_drum` and `the_drum_machine_plays_each_note_as_the_table_says` (`crates/editor/src/drums_tests.rs`); the menu in `music2s_own_sounds_show_their_groups_and_views` and the browser suite `dressed`; the singles' tests and `SOUNDS_THEN`, whose bits stay.

**KIT-21.** Each new sound MUST choke as every Drum does, on its own lane, and so as the drum machine's drum does: a drum that chokes (kick 2, snare 2, cymbal 2) is cut by the next hit on its lane, and one that rings on (the ride, ride 2, the tambourine, shaker 2, the claves) never is. `@m2/drums` 2.1.0, which no release has shipped, is recorded again with its new sounds, every song the repo ships relocked ([PACK-18](#packs)); a kit preset ([KIT-4](#drum-kits)) keeps its tracks.
- *Why:* a lane plays one sound, so on its lane each drum cuts itself, as the drum machine's group says. Three of the drum machine's groups hold two drums (the kick and kick 2, the snare and snare 2, the cymbal and cymbal 2): on two tracks they no longer cut each other, which Make it a kit says where it happens ([KIT-22](#drum-kits)). Keeping that cut needs a Drum that hears another track's hits ([Drums](designs/drums/design.md) §4: later). The hats' closed, pedal and open, which cut each other always, are one sound.
- *Today:* the voice's `any_hit`, as for every Drum. Tested by `one_drum_chokes_on_any_hit_where_its_drum_chokes` (each of the seventeen) and `a_one_sound_lane_plays_the_drum_machines_bits_on_a_drum` (the drum machine's own chokes, bit for bit).

**KIT-22.** A track that plays the drum machine MUST become a kit only when the musician asks, with **Make it a kit**: one Tx, one undo step, "Make Drums a kit" (the track's name). The track becomes the kit's bus: its `track(` is `bus(`, its function takes `input`, and the drum machine's call is `input` (`gain(input)` where the call was all the track played, as Add bus writes a Group, [KIT-5](#drum-kits)), in its own file or in `song.ts`. So its key, name, colour and channel (fader, pan, sends, output), the code around the call, and what reads its exports, all stay; a comment right above its `track(` is the drum machine's, so it becomes the bus's ("// The kit's bus: its Drum tracks play into it."). Its notes go onto Drum tracks, by `split` ([KIT-7](#drum-kits)): one for each sound they use, in the menu's order, named as the table words it (Kick, Snare, Hats, Toms, Ride…, numbered when the name is taken, "Clap 2"), each in `tracks/<key>.ts` playing its sound from a notes lane of its own, `hits`, with the notes clip by clip where they were, at their pitches and velocities (the toms at the keys the toms' table gives them, [KIT-6](#drum-kits)), its import beside the bus's and its channel right after the bus's, going to it (`snare: { in: snare.out, to: "drums" }`), after the bus in the timeline, each a colour of its own. The notes the drum machine is silent at (52, 53, 55, and any note the table hasn't got) go, and are named. A drum machine with no note a Drum can play is no kit: it is refused, naming the notes it holds. The planner asserts the track a bus, nothing else of it changed but the drum machine's call, exactly the new tracks added, each going to it, and no other routing changed.
- What it keeps, in its own words wherever it is offered and done: "Same drums, pitches, levels and lengths; each hit's noise comes out a little different." Where the lane plays both drums of one of the drum machine's choke groups, it says that they no longer cut each other off ("the kick and kick 2 no longer cut each other off", [KIT-21](#drum-kits)). Each hit's noise and metal are seeded from its count of hits on its own lane, and a kit's track counts only its own, so a lane that plays one sound renders the same bits as its kit (within the file's last bit where a level moves onto a fader or tracks sum in another order), and every track of any kit renders the bits the drum machine renders when it plays only that track's notes: per hit the same drum, pitch, level, length and choke. The Toms are the same drums at the same pitches, but their notes moved, and a hit's seed is its key's too, so their noise differs from the first.
- *Why:* a lane of several drums can't keep its bits as a kit (measured: the noise differs from the first shared hit, 18 dB under the beat), so the change is the musician's, said plainly, and never part of `m2 upgrade`, which keeps a song's sound exactly ([FMT-25](#conversion)). The track becoming the bus keeps everything around the drum machine (its effects, its sends, its output, an export read for ducking) without moving any code. A kit counting its hits across its tracks would keep the bits; it waits for a Drum to hear another track's hits ([Drums](designs/drums/design.md) §4).
- *Today:* since track `kit-upgrade`: `make_kit` and its plan in `crates/editor/src/machine.rs`, through `tracks::add_tracks_as` with a `Becomes` (the track that becomes a bus: its file's edits and imports, written in the song's one edit when it is `song.ts` (`swap::import_edits_with`), the lanes it gives up, the drum machine's ComponentId, and its channel, which the new tracks go to, right after it (`tracks::channel_edits_after`), their imports beside its (`sites::import_end`); the planner lets only the nodes under the drum machine's call and its lanes' go, and asserts it a bus), `comment_above`, and `drums::SILENT`. Tested by `a_drum_machine_track_becomes_a_kit_in_one_undo_step`, `a_drum_machine_track_in_song_ts_becomes_a_kit`, `the_planner_lets_only_the_drum_machine_go`, `a_drum_machine_with_nothing_to_play_is_refused_in_words`, `a_pair_that_no_longer_cuts_each_other_off_is_said`, `a_one_sound_lane_renders_the_same_bits_as_its_kit` (the hats with their chokes, a ride, claves) and `each_track_of_the_kit_plays_its_notes_as_the_drum_machine_did` (`crates/editor/src/machine_tests.rs`).

**KIT-23.** Make it a kit MUST carry the drum machine's knobs to the Drums it makes by the conversions the single drums got ([FMT-25](#conversion)): `kickTune` to the Kick's and the Soft kick's `tune` (semitones from 48 Hz), `kickDecay` to their `decay` (times 0.45 s) and `punch` less 0.5 to their `punch`; `snareTune` to the Snare's and the Snappy snare's `tune` (from 190 Hz) and `snap` less 0.6 to their `punch`; `hatTone` to the Hats' `tune` (from 8 kHz), and `hatDecay` and `openDecay` to its one `decay` when the hats it plays need one factor; `tomTune` to the Toms' `tune` (from 130 Hz) and `decay` (times the square root of tune ÷ 130 Hz); `drive` to every Drum; each group's level to the faders of its tracks (`kickLevel` the Kick's and the Soft kick's; `snareLevel` the Snare's, the Snappy snare's, the Clap's and the Rim's; `hatLevel` the Hats'; `tomLevel` the Toms'; `percLevel` every other's); and its voices (16 when it doesn't say) to each Drum, so no drum runs out of voices where the drum machine didn't. A knob on a lane moves the lane to the Drum it moves, its unit and every point converted as a single drum's lane is. What it can't carry exactly is named by file and line, and nothing is written: a value worked out by code; a lane of a knob that moves more than one of the new Drums (`drive`, a shared level) or that moves none; one lane that two knobs read; a level on a lane (the drum machine sets a hit's level as it starts, where a fader moves it while it rings); the tom's tune on a lane; hats whose lengths the one `decay` can't keep (the pedal hat's own 25 ms among them). It is refused, in words, for a track that plays no drum machine, two, or another instrument with it, whose call is in other code or reads notes other than a lane of the track's own, that has another notes or audio lane (a bus has none), or whose channel isn't one of the song's mixer reading its `out` as it is.
- *Why:* the same drums at the same settings, each on its own track: a level becomes the fader the Mix view shows, never a second level hidden in code ([KIT-5](#drum-kits)). All or nothing, as an upgrade is: a kit that played some knobs and lost others would change the sound without saying so.
- *Today:* since track `kit-upgrade`: `KNOBS`, `Carry`, `hats_decay` and `lane_moved` in `crates/editor/src/machine.rs`, with the upgrade's `Knob` and `convert_lane` (`crates/editor/src/upgrade.rs`); a new track's `code` and `lanes` (`tracks::NewTrack`). Tested by `the_drum_machines_knobs_reach_its_drums`, `a_knobs_lane_moves_to_the_drum_it_moves`, `what_it_cant_carry_is_named_and_nothing_is_written` and `a_track_it_cant_make_a_kit_of_is_refused_in_words`.

**KIT-24.** Make it a kit MUST be offered on a track that plays the drum machine and that it can make a kit of, in its track menu (its header's and the Mix view's strip's) and on the drum machine's card, and as the route `POST /api/graph/track/make-kit` (`{session, track}`) and `m2 make-kit <project> <track>`; the state's `drum_machines` lists those tracks, and its words. A track it would refuse ([KIT-23](#drum-kits)) isn't offered it: `m2 upgrade` and the status name it with what to do by hand first, its refusal's words. `m2 upgrade`, and the status's Upgrade it, list each drum machine track under what to do when you choose, apart from what to check by hand, whether the song was made for an older music2 or this one, and write nothing for it ([FMT-25](#conversion)). The drum machine stays in `@m2/drums`, unlisted ([KIT-2](#drum-kits)), until music2 1.0: a song that plays it builds and sounds as before. Taking it out is a major of `@m2/drums`, after which such a song can only be made a kit (gaps.md).
- *Why:* the drum machine leaves + Track, and every song that plays it is offered the kit where it plays it. Taking it out now would be `@m2/drums` 3.0.0: every song asking for "2", every kit made since `kits` among them, would need `m2 upgrade` for nothing it plays, and a drum machine song couldn't play at all until made a kit, which changes its noise. Kept unlisted, it costs one component nobody is offered and the model -1 of its kernel's map.
- *Today:* since track `kit-upgrade`: the graph state's `drum_machines` (`machine::drum_machines`, the tracks `machine::plan` accepts; `machine::refusal` says why another can't be one, which `upgrade::chosen` writes beside it in the upgrade's `choose`, printed and shown under what to do when you choose: `Status.tsx`'s `upgraded-choose`; tested by `only_a_track_it_can_make_a_kit_of_is_offered` and `a_drum_machine_track_is_named_and_never_made_a_kit`), `POST /api/graph/track/make-kit` (`Graph::make_kit`), `m2 make-kit` (`apps/m2/src/main.rs`), and Make it a kit in the track menu (`makeKit` in `web/src/components/Tracks.tsx`), the Mix view's strip menu (`Mix.tsx`) and the drum machine's card (`NodeGraph.tsx`); the upgrade names a drum machine track ([FMT-25](#conversion)). Tested by `the_make_kit_route_makes_a_drum_machine_track_a_kit` (`crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`), `a_drum_machine_track_is_made_a_kit_from_the_terminal` (`apps/m2/tests/tracks.rs`) and the browser suite `make-kit`.

**KIT-25.** Keys MUST play a kit by sound, a MIDI keyboard's or pad controller's and the keyboard piano's alike: while what keys play ([MON-9](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song): the selected track, or the armed one) is a kit's bus or one of its Drum tracks ([KIT-1](#drum-kits)), a key goes to the kit's Drum track whose sound answers to its note ([KIT-6](#drum-kits)), of two with one sound the first in the kit's order, at the key's own pitch, and sounds there as any key does (its meter moves), and a take records it there ([KIT-26](#drum-kits)). A note no Drum of the kit answers to plays nothing, and is said once, until keys play something else: which note, and that + Drum adds a drum. The state's `midi.target` names the kit (`kit`, its bus). A Toms track in a kit so plays its General MIDI tom notes, not every key: to play a tom chromatically, take it out of the kit, or write its notes in the roll.
- *Why:* a pad controller plays a kit: each pad is a General MIDI drum note, so each lands on its own drum's track, whichever of the kit's tracks is selected, and a drum's own track records and mixes it. Most beginners have no pads and play drums on the computer's keys, so the keyboard piano follows the same rule: one meaning for "keys" on a kit. Which track answers is worked out from the kit, never stored ([Drums](designs/drums/design.md) §7).
- *Today:* since track `kit-upgrade`: `Pads` (`of`, `answers`), `Midi::unanswered` and the kit's branch of `midi_message` in `crates/server/src/graph/midi.rs`, from the kits the song shows (the view's `drum_kits`), never through the session; the keyboard piano's, whose auditions say `record`, in `Graph::audition` (`crates/server/src/graph/audition.rs`: its answer's `said` is toasted); the state's `midi.target.kit`; `playTarget` in `web/src/lib/playTarget.ts` (a kit's bus plays its first Drum track; the piano says it plays the kit) and `MidiStatus.tsx` ("plays the Drums kit"). Tested by `a_key_plays_the_kits_drum_that_answers_to_it` and `the_keyboard_piano_plays_a_kit_by_sound` (`crates/server/src/graph/midi_tests.rs`: from a Drum track and from the bus; the first Hats of two; a C4 said once; a note clicked in the roll plays its own lane), `a_take_on_a_kit_records_the_keyboard_pianos_keys_on_their_drums` (`crates/server/src/graph/record_tests.rs`), and the browser suite `make-kit` (an F#2 pad's meter on the Hats 2 track; C4 said).

**KIT-26.** Arming one Drum track of a kit MUST arm the kit's notes: keys play the kit by sound ([KIT-25](#drum-kits)) whatever is selected, and a take records each hit on the lane of the Drum track it played, every Drum track of the kit in the take: one Tx and one undo step at its end, and one for each pass of a loop, as a take of several lanes is ([MON-14](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)), named by the kit ("recording on Drums"). The kit is armed as one: a Punch replaces every Drum track's lane of the kit between the loop's edges once any of them is played there, the lanes not played left empty there; Overdub puts each hit into the clip under it on its own lane; each pass of a loop is one Tx of every lane played.
- *Why:* a pad controller plays the whole kit, so a take of the one armed track would lose every other pad's hits, or put them on one drum; a take of the kit puts each hit on its drum's own track, where the beat grid shows it, and one undo takes the take back.
- *Today:* since track `kit-upgrade`: a take's notes are a part for each lane (`Take.notes`, `notes_clips`, `take_label` in `crates/server/src/graph/record.rs`), a kit's every Drum track's when it records on one of them; each take note says its lane, and `Take.tsx` draws each on its own. A punch's lanes not played are each an empty `Replace` take (`Take.kit`, `notes_clips`), which `clips.rs` writes as no clip, the range emptied. Tested by `a_take_on_a_kit_writes_each_hit_on_its_drums_lane` (`crates/server/src/routes_record_tests.rs`: the take's lanes the kit's four, each pad's hit on its drum's lane, the first Hats of two, "recording on Drums", one undo), `a_punch_on_a_kit_replaces_every_drums_lane_in_its_range`, `overdub_on_a_kit_puts_each_hit_into_its_lanes_clip` and `each_pass_over_a_loop_on_a_kit_is_one_step` (`crates/server/src/graph/record_tests.rs`), and `an_empty_take_leaves_its_range_empty` (`crates/editor/src/clips_tests.rs`).

## Packs

A pack is a package other than std: instruments, effects, sounds and recordings anyone can make ([design](designs/packs/design.md)). To the build a pack is a package like std ([PKG-1](#component-descriptors) to [PKG-14](#component-descriptors)); these rules say how it is named, found, pinned and added.

**PACK-1.** A package is named `std` or `@owner/name`. `owner` and `name` are 1 to 39 characters of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`, neither starting nor ending with `-`. `@m2/` is music2's own scope, and `@m2/core` is the SDK, never a package. A name never holds `:`. One type checks it, `m2_song::PackName`, wherever a name comes in: `m2pkg.toml`, `[dependencies]`, `music2.lock` and the store.
- *Why:* the owner is the GitHub account that publishes (design D2), and one rule written once can't drift.
- *Today:* since packs (`core/song/src/pack.rs`; `pack_names` in its tests).

**PACK-2.** `m2pkg.toml` MUST be read strictly: an unknown key anywhere is refused, as [FMT-4](#manifest-and-versions) refuses one in `music2.toml`. `[package]` holds `name`, `version`, and optionally `license`, `description`, `authors = [..]`, `repository`, `tags = [..]` and `wasm`. `[dependencies]` may name only `"@m2/core"` and `std` (packs that need packs come later, design D4), each a version ([FMT-2](#manifest-and-versions)) checked against the SDK and the std this app has when the pack loads: `@m2/core` with the caret rule, std as a lower bound, so `std = "0.4"` is std 0.4.0 or any later one (whose kernels the pack calls by id may since have moved into one of music2's packs, [PACK-21](#packs)). A pack the song uses that doesn't load, or needs more than this app has, is the build error `pkg.broken`, naming why: for a pack made for another major of `@m2/core`, the major it was made for, this app's, and what to get ([PACK-22](#packs)).
- *Why:* std is one version, bundled with the app, and its minor moves with every addition (the sounds design, §4). A pack asking for the std it was made with must keep working when the app's std grows; a song's own std line, which the caret rule checks, is what keeps its sound ([FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions)).
- *Today:* since packs (`PkgManifest::read` in `core/song/src/pack.rs`; `a_package_manifest_is_read_strictly`, `a_pack_made_for_an_older_std_still_builds`; `every_shipped_manifest_is_met` checks every `m2pkg.toml` the repo ships too). One function says whether this app runs a pack, `pack::App::meets` (this app's SDK and std, `m2_session::app`), for the build, resolution, `m2 pack lock` and `update`, and the lists alike (`a_version_this_app_cant_run_is_never_chosen`).

**PACK-3.** An import's package is its first segment, or its first two when it starts with `@`: `"@alice/felt-piano"` is that pack's `src/index.ts`, `"@alice/felt-piano/keys"` its `src/keys.ts`, as `"std/dsp"` is std's `src/dsp.ts`. `pkg.undeclared` and the rest of [FMT-6a](#paths) hold for scoped names as for std. An import stays inside one package's folder: a bare one inside the package it names, a relative one from a package's module inside that module's own package. One that reaches another package's folder, even one loaded beside it, is `import.outside-code`, so a pack never uses another pack (D4).
- *Today:* since packs (`package_of` and `State::package_file` in `core/script/src/lib.rs`; `a_scoped_package_is_imported_by_its_two_segments`, `an_import_never_reaches_another_package`, `a_package_import_never_passes_through_a_link`). `m2 explain` names a scoped package whole (`a_missing_scoped_module_names_its_whole_package`).

**PACK-4.** A ComponentId or a kernel id is `<package>:<id>`: `@alice/felt-piano:felt-piano`, `@alice/felt-piano:grain`. A package name holds no `:`, so an id splits at its first `:`, and so does a plugin's `clap:<id>`. One function splits them, `m2_song::pack::split_id`, in Rust; the web splits the same way, at the first `:`.
- *Today:* since packs. `PackageSet::module_for`, the presets' folders and the bounce's provenance split at the first `:` or by the package's name (`ids_split_at_the_first_colon`).

**PACK-5.** An asset reference with a `:` names a file in a package: `"@alice/felt-piano:samples/C4.wav"`, `"std:kits/…"`. One without is the song's `assets/` ([FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes)). A package file resolves inside that package's folder only: its path is relative, `/`-separated, with no empty, `.` or `..` segment and no `\`, found by listing its folders byte for byte, with no case folding and no part that is a symbolic link ([FMT-6a](#paths)'s rules for package files), and only for std or a package the song's `[dependencies]` lists. It is resolved by its name, never by who passes it: a pack's code passes it to std's Sampler as a song would. Each revision records the hash of every package file its kernels read, beside its assets' ([RENDER-2](#offline)). One that isn't there, or leaves its package, is `asset.missing`, and that sound is silent. `@m2/core`'s `file(import.meta, "samples/C4.wav")` spells a module's own package's file, so pack code needn't spell its own name.
- *Today:* since packs (`AssetRef` and `package_file` in `core/song/src/pack.rs`, read by `Project::data` and `DataSet::asset_path`; `@m2/core` 1.7.0's `file`; `package_assets_resolve_inside_their_package`, `a_package_file_is_never_reached_through_a_link`). A bounce's provenance names each package file its kernels read, with its hash, and each pack's version hash (`m2 bounce`; `a_bounce_names_the_pack_files_it_played`).

**PACK-6.** The store is `m2_os::dirs::packs()`, `~/Music/music2/Packs` on every OS ([PLAT-7](#platforms)), one folder per version: `Packs/@alice/felt-piano/1.2.0/`. Installed files and folders are read-only. Every installed version holds `m2pack.json`: every other file of the pack, by sorted path, with its size and SHA-256, made by `m2 pack build` (in a folder) or by the install (in the store's copy), never by hand. An install never writes into the folder it installs from, which may be one it can only read (`installing_from_a_folder_writes_nothing_into_it`, review Y18). The version's hash is the SHA-256 of `m2pack.json`'s bytes, written `sha256:<hex>`. A version is never changed: before a build uses an installed version, every file in its folder is checked against its listing, its size and SHA-256 through the hash cache ([FMT-16](#assets-and-hashes)), so an unchanged file costs one stat; a file changed, missing, not listed, or a link is `pkg.changed`, naming it. A pack holds no symbolic link, nor anything else that isn't a folder or a plain file (a pipe, a device): `m2 pack check` names one, and build and install refuse it, found before any file is opened, never following or reading one. Installing a version that is there with another hash is refused (design P5); with the same hash it does nothing when the copy still matches its listing, and replaces the copy when it doesn't, which is how a changed one is put back. The changed copy is moved aside, the new one put in its place, then the old one removed; should the new one not go in, the old one goes back, so a failed repair leaves the version as it was. A build in the moment between says the version is being installed again (`pkg.missing`, in those words), never that it isn't installed, that it was changed, or takes an older version instead. A copy an install that stopped left aside is removed by the next install of that version.
- *Why:* a song keeps the sound of the version it was made with (design M5), so what plays must be what was installed. A synced `~/Music` or an editor saving in place can change a file without anyone meaning to.
- *Today:* since packs (`Store`, `Store::check` and `Listing` in `core/song/src/pack.rs`; `m2 pack install` and `m2 pack remove`, `crates/editor/src/packs.rs`; `installing_twice_is_a_no_op_and_a_new_hash_is_refused`, `a_changed_installed_pack_is_caught_and_reinstalling_repairs_it`, `a_store_version_is_checked_file_by_file`, `links_are_refused_in_a_pack`, `a_repair_that_cant_put_the_new_copy_in_puts_the_old_one_back`, `a_build_during_a_repair_says_it_is_being_installed`, `an_install_removes_a_copy_a_stopped_install_left_aside`). `m2` keeps the hash cache on disk as the app does ([FMT-16](#assets-and-hashes)), so an unchanged file costs a stat there too (`m2_keeps_the_hashes_of_a_packs_files`). A pipe or a device is refused before any file is opened since track `registry`'s review (`a_pipe_in_a_pack_is_refused_before_anything_is_read`).

**PACK-7.** A `[dependencies]` entry other than `@m2/core` and `std` names a pack, and is a requirement ([FMT-2](#manifest-and-versions)), `"1.2"`, or a folder, `{ path = "../felt-piano" }`, relative to the project: a pack its author is trying. A key that isn't a package name ([PACK-1](#packs)), a requirement that isn't one, or a table with any key but `path`, is `manifest.invalid`, naming the entry and what to write.
- *Today:* since packs (`Dependency` in `core/song/src/manifest.rs`; `a_bad_dependency_says_which_and_why`).

**PACK-8.** `music2.lock` is a project file, read strictly and written canonically by `core/song` ([FMT-5](#paths)), classed with the manifest ([FMT-6](#paths)): a change rebuilds the song. It holds one `[[pack]]` per pack the song depends on from the store, sorted by name: `name`, `version`, `hash`. Path packs and std are not locked. A lock that doesn't parse is `manifest.invalid`, naming it. The app never writes it on its own (P2, P3): only the edit that adds a pack's item ([PACK-10](#packs)), `m2 pack lock` and `m2 pack update`. Both write it from the manifest, the lock it replaces and the store, never from a build: `lock` keeps each entry the manifest's requirement admits whose version is installed, and puts the highest installed version the requirement admits that this app can run ([PACK-2](#packs)) in place of one it doesn't admit, one whose version isn't installed, and a pack with no entry; `update` puts the highest installed admitted version this app can run in place of every entry, or of the pack it names. The store is shared by every app on the computer, so a newer one may have installed a version this one can't run; it is never chosen. A locked version kept whose installed copy isn't the one locked (another hash, or files that no longer match its listing) is refused, saying how to put it back or move. An entry for a pack the manifest no longer lists goes. Neither needs the song to build first: while it doesn't, the lock is written only when the song then builds, as one undo step, as `m2 edit` saves ([AGENT-1](#agents)).
- *Why:* moving a song to another version is an edit its person makes (M5), and it must work from a song the move itself stopped building (a new requirement the old lock doesn't meet).
- *Today:* since packs (`Lock` and `relock` in `core/song/src/pack.rs`; `DataPath::Lock`; `packs::write_lock` in `crates/editor`; `the_lock_has_one_canonical_form`, `relocking_moves_only_what_must_move`, and the guide's recipe end to end, `moving_a_song_to_another_version`).

**PACK-9.** A song builds with std and its resolved packs, resolved each build from the manifest, the lock and the store:
- a path dependency is that folder, which must hold that package (else `pkg.missing`);
- else the lock's version, when installed and its hash is the lock's (another hash is `pkg.changed`); a locked version not installed is `pkg.missing`, which says what to install and how (`m2 pack install <folder>`); a locked version the requirement doesn't admit is `pkg.unsatisfied`;
- else, with no lock entry, the highest installed version the requirement admits that this app can run ([PACK-2](#packs)); none is `pkg.unsatisfied`, which names an admitted version installed that needs a newer app, and what it needs.

Each blocks acceptance, as [FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions)'s do. With no store (no home folder), a pack from the store is `pkg.unsatisfied`, saying so; nothing is looked for elsewhere. The packs a build resolved are part of its code, so a change to the manifest or the lock makes a new revision that resolves them again.

Each build gets its own view of the packages: std and one loaded version of each pack it resolved, nothing else. A loaded version is known by its folder and the hashes of its `m2pkg.toml` and wasm (a store version's files checked against its listing first, [PACK-6](#packs)), so a path pack whose `m2pkg.toml` or wasm changed is loaded again at the next build, and several versions of one pack can be loaded at once. A revision keeps the view it was built with: its plans run that view's kernels, and so does every render of it (its export, its stems, `m2 bounce` with or without `--track`, and `m2 listen`, each side of `--with` its own), whatever was built since. A render takes the view from the song it renders: a revision, a build, or a stem made from one, which keeps the view it was checked with; never from what the session shows. The session's lists, presets, the code editor's types and package lookups follow the accepted revision's view only, and are made again when a revision is accepted whose view differs from the one before (an item added from a pack the song didn't use, `m2 pack update` while the song is open). A draft that doesn't build is explained, and the package files its problems name are named, with the packs it resolved. So a check, a refused Tx or a planner's probe that builds with other packs never changes what the accepted revision lists or plays. A loaded version is let go when no view holds it any more (no revision, candidate or plan); a plan holds its own modules.
- *Why:* a song keeps the version it was made with (design M5), nothing changes it but an edit the person makes, and the bits of a revision's export never depend on what was checked before (RENDER-2).
- *Today:* since packs (`resolve` in `core/song/src/pack.rs`; `PackageSet::view_of`, `View` and `PackageSet::show` in `core/runtime/src/packages.rs`; `build_code` in `core/session/src/lib.rs`, and `Session::accept`, which shows the accepted revision's view; `every_resolution_case_says_what_it_is`, `a_view_holds_std_and_exactly_the_songs_packs`, `a_dry_run_never_moves_what_the_accepted_revision_plays`, `a_path_packs_new_wasm_reaches_the_next_build`, `a_pack_that_fails_to_load_never_panics_the_build`, and `core/session/src/pack_tests.rs`). A plan of the app's engine and an export or a render of a revision see its own view (`PackageSet::viewing`), taken from the song a render is given (`Song::view` in `crates/editor/src/faults.rs`), a stem's too (`Parts::of`), and `m2 listen`'s (`listen::hear`) (`a_stem_renders_the_view_it_was_planned_on`, `listen_with_renders_each_side_with_its_own_packs`). The lists and the code editor's types are made again on such an accept (`Session::accept`, the server's `Open::shown`; `the_lists_follow_the_accepted_view`, `the_editor_types_follow_the_accepted_view`, e2e `pk-packs`), and a failed draft is explained with its own packs (`Session::draft_packages`; `a_failed_draft_is_explained_with_its_own_packs`). Not yet: a path pack's files aren't watched, so an edit to one reaches the song with the song's next build (gaps #46).

**PACK-10.** Adding a pack's item (a track that plays its instrument, its effect, a preset of its sound) writes its import by the scoped name, its `[dependencies]` line (`"@alice/felt-piano" = "1.2"`, the major and minor of the version used) when the song doesn't list it, and its `music2.lock` entry when the lock lacks one or the song didn't list the pack (an entry left from before is replaced), as one Tx, one Undo step. The one helper is `packs::needs` in `crates/editor`, which every adder calls.
- *Today:* since packs, for + Track (and the browser's double-click and drag, which call it), changing a track's instrument (`swap`), and Add Effect (the browser, the Device view's +): `packs::needs_all` gives one `[dependencies]` edit and one lock for every pack a Tx adds (`adding_a_pack_instrument_writes_its_dependency_and_lock_in_one_undo`, `adding_a_pack_effect_writes_its_dependency_and_lock`, `changing_an_instrument_to_a_packs_writes_its_dependency`, `a_lock_left_from_before_gets_its_line_again`). A preset loads onto a call already in the song, whose pack the song lists already.

**PACK-11.** The lists of instruments, effects and presets offer std's items and those of the accepted revision's packs, and of every other pack in the store (its newest version this app can run: one whose `[dependencies]` this app meets, [PACK-2](#packs)), each knowing its package. A pack's own presets are in its folder, `presets/<component>/*.json`, listed with std's ([FMT-22](#presets)). A module of a store pack the song doesn't use that doesn't load leaves its components out of the lists, and puts no problem on the song.
- *Today:* since packs: the session's catalog is made as the song opens, after its first build, and again whenever a revision is accepted whose packs differ from the one before ([PACK-9](#packs)), from std and `PackageSet::offered` (`catalog` in `core/session/src/lib.rs`; `a_pack_this_app_cant_run_isnt_offered`, `a_store_pack_the_song_doesnt_use_puts_no_problem_on_it`, `the_lists_follow_the_accepted_view`), so a pack installed while a song is open shows when it opens again, or when its packs change (gaps #46); a Get from the library makes them again at once (`Session::store_changed`, [REG-15](#registry); `the_lists_offer_a_pack_installed_once_the_store_changed`). Adding an item takes the version the lists offer it from, never one installed since (`Session::offered_one`; `an_add_takes_the_version_the_lists_show`). Each list item names its pack and version (`packs::from` in `crates/editor`; `a_pack_in_the_store_is_offered`); `GET /api/packs` lists the store for the browser's Packs list (`crates/server/src/routes_packs.rs`; e2e `pk-packs`).
