**PACK-12.** `m2 pack check` checks what the registry's robot will (design P1 to P4, and P6 where it can): a strict `m2pkg.toml`; only `.ts`, `.wasm`, `.toml`, `.json`, `.md`, `.txt`, `.wav` and `.flac` files (in any capitals: `.WAV` is a WAV), and `LICENSE` with no extension, and no symbolic link; every component builds and has a category and a shape, or says `editor.unlisted` ([PKG-7](#component-descriptors)): code's alone, on purpose; every kernel loads sealed; every preset loads on its sound; every package file its code names resolves; a licence is named; its audio (every WAV and FLAC, by its header alone: no file over 2^27 samples, none over 2^28 in all, [FMT-24](#assets-and-hashes)); and its samples ([LIB-4](#library)): each WAV or FLAC under `samples/` decodes, and `samples.toml` reads strictly and names only files and folders that are there; and every word it gives the lists holds no control character (a line break, a tab, an escape): its `m2pkg.toml`'s description, licence, repository, authors and tags, its components' labels, descriptions, categories and notes' names, its sounds' names, its samples' tags and its files' names. A pack of samples alone needs no component. It prints each problem in words and exits 1 on any.
- *Today:* since packs (`check` in `crates/editor/src/packs.rs`; `apps/m2/tests/pack.rs`: `check_says_what_is_wrong`, `a_capital_extension_is_its_type`, `a_preset_outside_a_strict_range_is_named`, `links_are_refused_in_a_pack`). The words with no control character since track `registry`'s review (`a_packs_words_hold_no_control_characters`). A preset is checked as a preset of a component of the pack, in its component's folder, naming only that component's controls, each value inside its range where that range is strict ([PKG-8](#component-descriptors)); it isn't loaded onto a call in a song (gaps #46). Kernels are checked as a song loads them (the ABI section, the exports, an instance), and every instrument and effect is built in a scratch song, not each kernel prewarmed in a sealed sandbox on its own; a licence is any one named, not checked against the design's list (D6); tags aren't checked against a word list (P7) (gaps #46). Since track `samples` it checks samples (`a_pack_of_samples_alone_checks_and_its_samples_are_checked`). Since track `starter` a component that says `editor.unlisted` needs no category, and one that says neither is named, as one its author forgot (`a_component_with_no_category_is_named_unless_it_says_unlisted`, `check_says_what_is_wrong`); `@m2/synths`' first instruments say it ([PACK-15](#packs), `the_bundled_packs_pass_check`).

**PACK-13.** A pack MAY hold instruments written as data, for musicians who record and don't code (design §4.2, §4.3): `instruments/<id>.toml`, one instrument a file, `<id>` a component id (a key, [ID-1](#keys)). It is read strictly, as `m2pkg.toml` is ([PACK-2](#packs)):

| Key | Meaning | Left out |
|---|---|---|
| `label` | the name the lists show | required |
| `category` | the lists' group ([PKG-7](#component-descriptors)) | required |
| `description` | one line | `""` |
| `tags` | words for search, kept for the registry's `items.json` (design §6.3) | `[]` |
| `engine` | what plays it: `"std:sampler"`, std's Sampler, the only engine yet | required |
| `tune`, `level`, `attack`, `release`, `velocity` | the defaults of the Sampler's controls, each inside its control's range: semitones (−24 to 24), dB (−24 to 12), seconds (0 to 2), seconds (0 to 5), and how much velocity changes the level (0 to 1) | the Sampler's |
| `mode`, `voices`, `turns` | the Sampler's settings: `"oneshot"` or `"gate"`; how many notes sound at once, a whole number from 1 to 128; and whose count a round robin's turn follows, `"lane"` or `"key"` ([PKG-16](#component-descriptors)) | the Sampler's |
| `[[region]]` | one region each, with [PKG-15](#component-descriptors)'s fields; its `file` is a path in the pack, `"samples/C4.wav"` | at least one |

music2 makes each file into a component, as if it were written in TypeScript, so the descriptor rules hold ([PKG-1](#component-descriptors) to [PKG-14](#component-descriptors)): its ComponentId is `<package>:<id>`; its controls are those five and its settings those three, with the file's defaults; its body is std's `sampler(notes, { regions, … })`, each region's `file` the pack's file (`"samples/C4.wav"` in `@alice/felt-piano` is `"@alice/felt-piano:samples/C4.wav"`, [PACK-5](#packs)). The module `"<package>/instruments"` exports each, named by its id in camelCase (`felt-piano` is `feltPiano`; an id that starts with a digit gets `_` first, `808-kit` is `_808Kit`): `import { feltPiano } from "@alice/felt-piano/instruments"`. So it is listed, added (+ Track, the browser) and found as the pack's coded instruments are, with the same ids. The code made from a file is a function of its bytes alone, and the build records the file as a package file it read, with its hash ([PACK-5](#packs)). The file's name is checked before any code is made from it, and code is made only from a name that passed: a name that isn't a key, or whose name in code is a word JavaScript keeps for itself (`new`, `class`, `default`, `static` …), is refused at its file, and two files whose names make one name in code (`a-b` and `a--b`, both `aB`; `a-1` and `a1`, both `a1`) are both refused, naming both. The module made from a file names its own imports and values so that no instrument's name can be one of them (`__m2_sampler`, `__m2_regions` …). A key not in the table is refused, naming the nearest known one; a value of the wrong kind or outside its range, an engine other than std's Sampler, a `file` that isn't a plain path in the pack, and every refusal of [PKG-15](#component-descriptors) name the file and the line: `@alice/felt-piano/instruments/felt-piano.toml:12: …`, and `m2 pack check` prints them so. A pack whose `src/` has a module `instruments` of its own besides `instruments/*.toml` is refused by `m2 pack check`: the code's module would hide the data.
- *Why:* most instruments that are recordings need no code (design §4.2), and a file a musician edits must say where it is wrong, as code does. Made into the same component, a data instrument needs nothing new in the lists, the Device view, presets or the lock. A pack comes from a stranger, so its file names are data too: never code.
- *Today:* since track instruments: `m2_song::instrument` reads a file and writes its TypeScript; the build loads `"<package>/instruments"` and each file as modules made from data (`core/script/src/lib.rs`, given the reader by `m2_session`). The id is `m2_song::instrument::Id`, the one way to a name in code (`Id::binding`); the build gets it as `m2_script::DataReader`'s `binding`. Tests: `core/song/src/instrument_tests.rs` (strictness, the nearest key, kinds, engine, paths, lines, the code made, `a_value_outside_its_range_is_refused_at_its_line`, `ids_are_checked_before_any_code`, `the_modules_own_names_are_no_instruments`), `core/session/src/pack_tests.rs` (`a_data_instrument_is_a_component_of_its_pack`, `a_data_instruments_region_error_names_its_line`, `a_data_file_named_as_code_is_refused_at_its_file`, `two_files_with_one_name_in_code_are_both_refused`, `the_modules_own_names_are_no_instruments`, which builds and plays instruments named `sampler`, `regions` and `component`, and `sampler_controls_are_stds`, which keeps the controls' defaults and ranges std's Sampler's), `crates/editor/src/packs_tests.rs` (`a_data_instrument_is_offered_and_added_as_any_other`), `apps/m2/tests/pack.rs` (`check_refuses_code_that_hides_data_instruments`, `check_names_the_file_and_line_of_a_data_instruments_mistake`, `check_refuses_two_instruments_with_one_name_in_code`). The code editor checks imports of `"<package>/instruments"` against one module declaring each (`m2_song::instrument::types`, served with the packages' sources by `crates/server/src/files.rs`). Not yet: tags reach no list; only std's Sampler plays data (gaps #49).

**PACK-14.** `m2 pack import-sfz <file.sfz> <folder> --name @owner/name` makes a new pack from an SFZ instrument (SFZ: the open text format of thousands of free sample instruments): `m2pkg.toml` (version 0.1.0, its licence from `--license`, else from a licence file beside the SFZ when that file is clear, else the import stops and asks for it), one data instrument `instruments/<id>.toml` ([PACK-13](#packs); its id the pack's name without its owner, `@m2/grand-piano` makes `grand-piano`; its label `--label` or the SFZ file's name; its category `--category` or `Sampler`; 32 voices; `turns = "key"`, as SFZ counts a round robin's turns), the recordings it plays copied under `samples/`, `README.md` with the credits in the SFZ's comments before its first header, and the licence file as `LICENSE` when there is one. A licence file is `LICENSE`, `LICENCE` or `COPYING`, alone or with `.txt` or `.md`, in any capitals. It is clear when its first line is `SPDX-License-Identifier: <licence>`, or when it is the whole text of CC0 1.0, CC BY 3.0, CC BY 4.0, CC BY-SA 3.0 or CC BY-SA 4.0 (spaces and line breaks aside); a file that only mentions a licence is not, since sample libraries often put several in one file.

The import reads only plain files inside the SFZ's own folder and the folders in it: the SFZ itself, the files `#include` names, the recordings `sample` names (after `default_path`), and the licence file. A path that leaves that folder (`..` above it), an absolute path, a link anywhere on the way (never followed), and anything that isn't a plain file stop the import, naming the opcode and its line; capitals are matched only among the plain files in a folder. One recording named two ways (`Piano_C4.wav` and `piano_c4.wav`, `./a.wav` and `a.wav`) is one file. Its work is bounded: an SFZ file over 4 MB, text over 8 MB once `#define` names are filled in, more than 100 000 regions or 1 000 000 opcodes in all its regions, `#include` more than 16 files deep, and a file that includes itself stop it, in words.

What `m2 pack check` would refuse is refused before anything is written: the id ([PACK-13](#packs) checks it as a file's name: `--name @me/new` makes `new`, which is refused), and a round robin missing a take ([PKG-15](#component-descriptors); in SFZ that turn is silent). The pack is then written into a new folder beside the one named, checked as `m2 pack check` checks it, and renamed to the folder named only when it passes, so a failed import leaves nothing. The folder must not exist.

One parser reads the headers `<control>`, `<global>`, `<master>`, `<group>` and `<region>` (a region takes every opcode of the headers above it that it doesn't set itself), `#define` and `#include` (a `$NAME` no `#define` made stops the import, naming it), `default_path`, `<control>`'s `octave_offset` and `note_offset` (added to every note after them, as 12 × `octave_offset` + `note_offset`), notes as numbers or names (`c4` is 60, `c#4` and `db4` are 61), files that start with a UTF-8 byte order mark, and these opcodes (`loopmode`, `loopstart` and `loopend` are `loop_mode`, `loop_start` and `loop_end`):

| SFZ | Becomes |
|---|---|
| `sample` | `file`, copied to `samples/` |
| `key` | `lokey`, `hikey` and `pitch_keycenter`, where it is written: a region's `key` beats the ones it inherits |
| `lokey`, `hikey`, `pitch_keycenter` | `keys`, `root` |
| `lovel`, `hivel` | `velocity` |
| `seq_position`, `seq_length` | `round_robin` |
| `trigger` | `trigger`: `attack` or `release` |
| `loop_mode`, `loop_start`, `loop_end` | `loop` for `loop_continuous` and `loop_sustain`; the instrument's `mode` is `"oneshot"` when every region that plays at the attack is `one_shot`, else `"gate"` |
| `tune`, `transpose` | `tune` in cents, `transpose` × 100 added |
| `volume`, `pan`, `offset` | `volume`, `pan` ÷ 100, `offset` |
| `amp_veltrack` | the instrument's `velocity`, ÷ 100, as the next row says; the Sampler's velocity follows a straight line where SFZ's follows the velocity squared, so the import says how many dB louder soft notes are at most |
| `ampeg_attack`, `ampeg_release` | the instrument's `attack` and `release`, when 9 in 10 of the regions that play at the attack agree on one value, counting a region that doesn't set it as SFZ's default (`amp_veltrack` 100, the envelope 0); release regions don't vote. When they don't agree, the instrument keeps the Sampler's default and the import says so; a value beyond the Sampler's range ([PACK-13](#packs)) is its end, said |

Everything else is counted and named in plain words when the import ends, never dropped unseen ("left out: 88 regions play only with a controller (CC 64)"): a region with a condition music2 can't meet is left out: one that needs a controller moved from where `<control>`'s `set_cc` starts it, one played when a controller moves, one of a key switch other than the default, `trigger=first` or `legato`, one chosen at random other than the first; a region kept whose own value differs from the instrument's (`amp_veltrack`, the envelope, `one_shot`) is said to be changed; a region with a value music2 can't read (`tune=+5c`, `pitch_keycenter=sample`, the recording's own root, a note `h4`) is left out, naming the value: never read as 0; an opcode music2 doesn't play is ignored on the regions that have it, as are `<control>`'s opcodes other than `default_path`, `set_cc`, `set_hdcc`, `octave_offset` and `note_offset`, and opcodes before the first header. Key switches keep the regions of the default one (`sw_default`), and say so. Its recordings are WAV or FLAC, told by their first bytes ([FMT-24](#assets-and-hashes)), and each is copied unchanged, a FLAC as a FLAC; a recording of another kind (OGG), or a FLAC music2 doesn't read (one of 12, 20 or 32 bits), stops the import before anything is written, naming the file. What it writes passes `m2 pack check` ([PACK-12](#packs)).
- *Why:* free SFZ instruments are the fastest way to real recorded instruments (design §4.3), and a musician must know what an import couldn't keep. An SFZ folder comes from a stranger: what it names must never bring the musician's own files into a pack they may share, or take all their memory.
- *Today:* since track instruments (`crates/editor/src/sfz.rs`: the parser and the import, the folder read through `Folder`; tests beside it, `sfz_tests.rs`, for each header rule, opcode and note name, each refusal of a path (`a_licence_link_is_refused`, `a_sample_link_is_refused`, `dots_above_the_folder_are_refused`, `an_absolute_path_is_refused`), each bound (`a_file_too_big_is_refused`, `defines_that_grow_too_much_are_refused`, `includes_too_deep_or_in_a_loop_are_refused`, `an_include_that_isnt_a_plain_file_is_refused`, `too_many_opcodes_are_refused`), one file named two ways (`one_recording_named_two_ways_is_one_file`), what check would refuse (`what_check_would_refuse_is_refused_first`, `a_pack_check_refuses_leaves_nothing`), the values instrument-wide (`instrument_values_come_from_what_nearly_every_attack_region_says`), the parser's rules (`octave_and_note_offsets_move_the_notes`, `a_value_that_doesnt_read_leaves_its_region_out`, `an_undefined_name_is_an_error`, `a_byte_order_mark_is_skipped`, `key_beats_what_a_region_inherits`, `control_opcodes_and_loose_ones_are_named`), and the licence (`a_licence_is_recognised_only_when_clear`); `apps/m2/tests/pack.rs`: `an_sfz_imports_checks_and_renders_alike_on_1_and_4_threads`, `a_flac_sample_is_copied_and_renders_as_its_wav` (since track flac: the same bits as the pack of WAVs), `an_ogg_sample_stops_the_import`, `an_import_check_would_refuse_writes_nothing`, `an_sfz_beside_the_working_folder_reads_only_its_folder`). Not yet (gaps #22, #49): what the Sampler doesn't play (regions that play with a controller such as the pedal, crossfades, an envelope and a velocity amount for each region, `loop_continuous` after the note ends, SFZ's velocity curve, the root note a WAV holds), samples outside the SFZ's folder, and Decent Sampler's format.

**PACK-15.** music2's own sounds are five packs that ship with it ([PKG-17](#component-descriptors)): `@m2/synths` (the first instruments, the polysynth's and the FM synth's named sounds and their custom engines, with their presets), `@m2/drums` (the Drum, which plays any one of its drums, [CARD-33](#music2s-own-sounds-on-the-card), its kits, [KIT-4](#drum-kits), and the drum machine, which code still plays), `@m2/effects` (delay, reverb, chorus, phaser, tremolo, drive, bitcrush and widen, with their presets), `@m2/mixing` (the tools a mix needs: a parametric EQ, a gate, a transient shaper, a de-esser and a multiband compressor, [PACK-26](#packs)) and `@m2/wavetable` (the wavetable synth, [PACK-27](#packs)). Each is an ordinary pack in the repo at `packs/@m2/<name>/`: a strict `m2pkg.toml` ([PACK-2](#packs)) with its name, version, licence (`MIT OR Apache-2.0`, std's), description, authors, repository and tags, needing `@m2/core`, and std when it uses std's code; its TypeScript in `src/`, its presets in `presets/<component>/`. Its ComponentIds keep std's ids under the pack's name (`@m2/synths:synth-pluck`, [PKG-9](#component-descriptors)). Each passes `m2 pack check` ([PACK-12](#packs)), which CI runs on every one. They ship beside std the same way on every OS and from source: a resource folder `packs`, found as std is ([PLAT-8](#platforms)): `$M2_PACKS`, then the bundle's `Resources/packs` or `lib/music2/packs`, then the source tree's `packs/`.
- *Why:* one place for music2's sounds that is a pack like anyone's, checked by the robot's own check.
- *Today:* since track starter: `packs/@m2/synths` (`src/synth.ts`, `fm.ts` and `simple.ts`, std's `std/synth`, `std/fm` and `std/instruments`, exported together from `src/index.ts`), `packs/@m2/drums` and `packs/@m2/effects`, and since track effects2 `packs/@m2/mixing`; found by `resources::packs_dir` (`crates/server/src/resources.rs`), carried by the app (`tauri.conf.json`'s resources) and by m2's `.tar.gz` (`ci/release.sh`). `the_bundled_packs_pass_check` (`crates/editor/src/packs_tests.rs`); `every_shipped_manifest_is_met` reads their `m2pkg.toml`; `core/script/tests/std.rs` checks their descriptors, ids, labels, defaults and kernels as it checked std's. A component a pack exports from its own name is imported from there first (`merge` in `core/script/src/lib.rs`): `import { synth } from "@m2/synths"`. `@m2/synths`' first instruments (three since wave 3) say `editor.unlisted`: code plays them, the lists leave them out ([PKG-7](#component-descriptors), [PACK-12](#packs)). A pack's `README.md` is in its hash, so it links nowhere outside the pack: it names `m2 docs sounds`. Each asks for `"@m2/core" = "2"`, the pack contract, alone ([PACK-23](#packs)), and its manifest says why.

**PACK-16.** When the app's server, the headless server or `m2` starts, every bundled pack's version the store doesn't hold is installed into it, as `m2 pack install` installs ([PACK-6](#packs)): listed, copied and made read-only, never written into the folder it ships in. A version the store holds with the same hash, its files whole, is left as it is: a start that finds every bundled version there writes nothing. `m2` installs them when it first opens the store (a build, `m2 pack`, `m2 search`, `m2 get`); the app and the headless server when they give their server its store. Nothing else installs into a user's store: a server given no store (a test's, a tool's) only reads this user's store, and every test gives its server a store of its own and a program it runs a home folder of its own, so no test writes the `~/Music` of the `HOME` it runs with. Songs, the lists, presets and the code editor's types read a bundled pack only from the store, as any pack: nothing reads one from inside the app.
- *Why:* one store, one path (design D7). A song locks `@m2/synths` 1.0.0 as it locks any pack, and keeps it when the app moves to 1.1.0, since the store keeps 1.0.0; a pack read from inside the app would leave that song when the app updated.
- *Today:* since track starter: `Store::install` and `install_bundled` (`core/song/src/pack/install.rs`, `bundled.rs`; `m2 pack install` is the check, `m2 pack build`, then `Store::install`); the server's `AppState::keep_library` (the app's and the headless server's start) through `resources::install_packs`; m2's `packs_store` (`apps/m2/src/main.rs`). A server given no store builds with `m2_os::dirs::packs` and installs nothing (`packages` in `crates/server/src/graph/actor.rs`). `installing_the_bundled_packs_twice_writes_nothing` (`core/song/src/pack/tests.rs`), `the_servers_start_installs_the_packs_music2_ships_and_a_second_writes_nothing` (`crates/server/src/lib.rs`). Two programs installing one version at once is no failure: the first in place wins, and the other finds it whole. Tests build with a store of the source tree's packs in the temp folder, named by their hash, so none holds another tree's bytes under a version (`m2_song::pack::test_store`), and give a program they run (`m2`) the home folder that store is in (`test_home`); both are behind m2-song's `testing` feature, for tests and the gates. The PR tier fails when a Rust test left a file in the `~/Music` (or the cache folder) of the `HOME` it ran with (`ci/cache-left.sh`, the "home untouched" row). An install whose version's folder holds no listing (an interrupted copy, a restore) puts the version in its place, and `m2 pack remove` takes such a folder out (`a_version_folder_with_no_listing_is_replaced_by_an_install`); two threads installing one version copy into folders of their own (`two_threads_installing_one_version_both_see_it_installed`).

**PACK-17.** A bundled version that is in the store with another hash is a pack someone else installed under `@m2/`: it is refused, as [PACK-6](#packs) refuses it, the log says what puts music2's back (`m2 pack remove <pack> <version>`, then start music2 again), and the start goes on with the others. A bundled version whose installed copy no longer matches its listing is put back, as PACK-6 puts one back. A bundled pack's files never change under one version: a change to them moves its version, so a store that has the older one keeps it for the songs that lock it. `packs/versions.toml` records every version the source tree has bundled with its hash, and a check fails when a bundled pack's files aren't the ones recorded for its version, saying to move it to a new version, record that, and relock the songs. Every file of a pack whose hash a lock or the record names is checked out as committed on every OS (`.gitattributes`: `-text`, never a CRLF).
- *Why:* a version is never changed (the packs design's P5), and a store an app shares with other apps, or with a person, must never stop the app from starting.
- *Today:* since track starter (`a_bundled_version_installed_with_other_files_is_refused_and_the_others_go_on`; the log's words, `a_start_that_finds_another_copy_says_what_puts_music2s_back` in `crates/server/src/resources.rs`). The record and its check: `every_bundled_pack_is_the_version_its_record_names` (`core/song/src/pack/tests.rs`), which also names the line to add for a new version; the line endings: `pinned_packs_keep_their_bytes_on_every_checkout` (`core/session/src/lib.rs`).

**PACK-18.** Every song the repo ships that plays a bundled pack's sound (the templates, the examples, the test fixtures) lists the pack in `[dependencies]` (`"@m2/synths" = "2"`) and locks it in `music2.lock` ([PACK-8](#packs)) at the bundled version and hash, so a song made from a template (New Song, `m2 new`) is locked from its first build. A check fails, saying what to run, when a shipped lock names another version or hash than the one bundled: another version, to move the song with `m2 pack update <pack>`; the same version with other files, to move the pack to a new version first ([PACK-17](#packs)). A recorded version never changes from the first release that ships it; until a release ships it, it may (a branch records one version of each pack it changes, the one it will ship, never one for each step).
- *Why:* a new song keeps the sound it was made with from its first minute. A bundled pack that changed without a new version is caught by PACK-17's record, never locked again under its old version.
- *Today:* since track starter: every template, example and fixture that plays one lists and locks it; `every_shipped_song_locks_the_bundled_packs_it_plays` (`core/session/src/lib.rs`) says, for each that doesn't, what to run. New Song and `m2 new` copy the template's lock (`the_servers_start_installs_the_packs_music2_ships_and_a_second_writes_nothing` builds a new song from the starter with nothing but that store). Adding an item of a bundled pack the song doesn't list is [PACK-10](#packs)'s, Add bus ▸ Reverb and Delay included (`add_bus_reverb_writes_the_effects_packs_dependency_and_lock`), and two items of one pack in one Tx need it once (`two_items_of_one_pack_need_it_once`).

**PACK-19.** The bundled packs are published to the registry like any pack ([Registry](#registry)): the same versions, built from the same folders, checked by the same robot. A pack version is music2's own when its hash is one [PACK-17](#packs)'s record holds for that name and version (`m2_song::pack::own`), never by its name: anyone's folder can say `@m2/`. One rule for every place that asks: the lists say "music2" for music2's own and rank it first, std's with it ([LIB-1](#library), [REG-13](#registry), + Effect), and `m2 pack install <folder>` refuses a pack named `@m2/…` whose files aren't a version music2 comes with ("@m2/ is music2's own scope"). A song locked to the version of music2's own this app comes with, missing or changed, is told to start music2 again, which puts it back, never to install it from a folder. A song that asks for (or keeps, or finds in its store) a version of music2's own that this app no longer comes with, one of an older major, is told first to run `m2 upgrade <song>` or press Upgrade ([AGENT-14](#agents)), then the line to write for the version it comes with, and to lock again (`m2_song::pack::comes_with` and `move_on`, which read the bundled packs' manifests, never the record of past versions; review Y5). The time limit on a pack's kernels asks the same function ([ABI-11](#kernel-abi-reservations), `packages::checked`): music2's own packs' kernels run as std's.
- *Why:* a person who got `@m2/drums` 1.1.0 from the library, and an app that bundles it, hold the same bytes under the same hash.
- *Today:* the rule of music2's own since track starter's review: `own`, and since wave 3's review `comes_with` and `move_on` (`a_song_made_before_wave_3_is_told_the_line_to_write`, `core/session/src/pack_tests.rs`: locked, unlocked, and with a 1.0.0 made for @m2/core 1 in its store, then building once the line is written); and `library::own` for an installed version (`music2s_own_is_by_its_files_and_every_list_ranks_by_it` in `crates/editor/src/packs_tests.rs`: the library's `own`, the search's and + Effect's order, `library::origin`; `a_folders_m2_pack_installs_only_as_music2s_own` in `apps/m2/tests/pack.rs`; the words of a song's `pkg.missing` and `pkg.changed`, `a_song_locked_to_music2s_own_pack_is_told_how_to_get_it_back`). A From the library offer is music2's own by its catalog version's hash. Publishing: not yet, the registry's bucket isn't there (gaps #39), so the bundled packs come only with the app and `m2` (gaps.md, Packages).

**PACK-20.** A pack's kernels' source MAY live in its folder, as `kernels-src/` at its top, and is no part of the pack: never listed, so not in its hash, never installed, checked by `m2 pack check` or published. Only the module built from it, at the pack's `wasm` ([PACK-2](#packs)), is the pack's. One place says what a pack's files are, `pack_files` (`m2_song::pack::KERNELS_SRC`), which every listing, install, store check and `m2 pack check` reads. And since it is never installed, nothing of a pack is ever read from it: a `wasm` there (or one that leaves the pack) is refused as `m2pkg.toml` is read, an import that reaches it is `import.outside-code`, and a package file named there ([PACK-5](#packs)) isn't found, each in the words "kernels-src/ is no part of the pack (the source of its kernels, never installed): put the built wasm in kernels/". So `m2 pack check` refuses a pack that would break once installed. A folder of that name deeper in the pack is the pack's, as any other.
- *Why:* a sound's code lives with the sound (the owner, 2026-10-31: "Most things should go inside packs"), while a version is what runs: its source, a build left beside it, or a comment changed in it, changes no version and ships to no one. std keeps its source the same way, `std/kernels-src`. A pack that reads its source from its folder would work there and break once installed (the review's X4).
- *Today:* since track packcode: `a_packs_kernel_source_is_no_part_of_it` (`core/song/src/pack/tests.rs`: the listing, the install, `package_file`, `PkgManifest::read`), `check_refuses_a_pack_that_reads_its_kernel_source` (`crates/editor/src/packs_tests.rs`: a module there, and an import from there, each refused by `m2 pack check`); the script's resolver keeps its own copy of the name, held equal to the store's by `kernels_src_is_one_name_to_the_build_and_the_store`. The app's bundle and m2's `.tar.gz` carry the `packs` folder whole, so they hold `@m2/effects`' source beside the pack (a few kilobytes), which no install copies.

**PACK-21.** A kernel only one pack's sounds play is that pack's own, not std's: music2's own packs build theirs from `kernels-src/` by [ABI-10](#kernel-abi-reservations)'s script, and commit the module at their `wasm`, as std does. A change that changes the module changes the pack's files, so it is a new version, recorded in `packs/versions.toml` with every song the repo ships relocked ([PACK-17](#packs), [PACK-18](#packs)). A kernel that moves from std into a pack takes the pack's name (`std:bitcrush` is `@m2/effects:bitcrush`), and plays the same bits ([RENDER-2](#offline)); std loses it, so std's minor version moves ([FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions)). Whatever calls the old id is refused with `pkg.missing`, at the call, its fix first and in the words the user wrote, by who made the call (the innermost component around it): another pack's sound can't play until its author moves it, and the pack's effect plays in its place; the song's own code plays the pack's effect, with the line `[dependencies]` needs for the version music2 comes with (`"@m2/synths" = "2"`). An older version of the pack the kernel went to asks for an older `@m2/core`, so it is refused before ([PACK-22](#packs)). Then the kernel's new id. One table, `m2_ir::MOVED_KERNELS`. std's lower bound in a pack's `[dependencies]` ([PACK-2](#packs)) so no longer promises every std kernel it calls: a minor version of std may move one into a pack, and that pack's sound is then refused in those words.
- *Why:* the owner keeps the core small: std holds what sounds are made of, a pack what is its own ([design cards §7.1](designs/cards/design.md)). A kernel moved by name and version is told, never lost in silence.
- *Today:* since wave 3 (std 0.9.0): the polysynth's and the FM synth's voices are `@m2/synths`' (`@m2/synths:synth`, `@m2/synths:fm`, 2.0.0), the drum voice `@m2/drums`' (`@m2/drums:drum`, 2.0.0), and the delay, reverb, phaser, tremolo and widener `@m2/effects`' (1.2.0), each with its tests; `MOVED_KERNELS` names all nine, each with the line a song writes for its pack today, the bundled version's major (`moved_kernels_name_the_line_a_song_writes_today`; review Y4). `engines_play_from_their_packs_with_the_bits_they_had_in_std` (`core/session/src/gate_tests.rs`: a song of all seven moved in wave 3, its render recorded before the move, and each kernel's id the pack's), `the_engines_left_std_for_their_packs` (`core/runtime/src/tests.rs`: std has none of them, each pack's module has its own, and a call of the old id is told where it went); every pure fixture renders the same bits under std 0.9.0. since track packcode: the bitcrusher, std 0.8.0 and `@m2/effects` 1.1.0 (never released: wave 3's 1.2.0 is the first a release ships). `bitcrush_plays_from_its_pack_with_the_bits_it_had_in_std` (`core/session/src/gate_tests.rs`: the pack's id in the graph, and the render recorded before the move), `a_kernel_that_left_std_says_where_it_went` (`core/runtime/src/tests.rs`); every pure fixture renders the same bits under std 0.8.0 (`renders.json`, `e2e/renders.sha256` unchanged). The tests of every kernel play std's and the packs' own, installed as the app installs them and so run unchecked as there (`own_packages`: `every_std_kernel_keeps_its_inputs`, `every_std_kernel_resets_in_a_sealed_sandbox`, `every_std_kernel_fits_its_memory_at_the_highest_rate`, `std_manifest_matches_its_kernels`, `which_std_kernels_make_subnormals_in_a_five_minute_tail`). An `@m2/effects` 1.0.0 a store keeps still calls `std:bitcrush`: with std 0.8 its bitcrusher is refused in those words, its other effects play as before.

**PACK-22.** From `@m2/core` 2.0.0 the pack contract is frozen: it is `@m2/core` (the descriptor API, [Component descriptors](#component-descriptors)) and kernel ABI v1 ([Kernel ABI reservations](#kernel-abi-reservations)), and a change to either that would break a pack's code MUST be a new major of `@m2/core`; additions are a minor ([FMT-2](#manifest-and-versions)). A pack made for another major is refused by [PACK-2](#packs)'s caret rule, in words that name both majors and what to do: a song that uses it is told `@a/x, which this song uses, can't be used: it is made for @m2/core 1, and this app has @m2/core 2.1.0, whose changes may break it. Get a version of it made for @m2/core 2 (its m2pkg.toml says "@m2/core" = "2")` (`pkg.broken`), and `m2 pack check` the same with its version (`@a/x 1.3.0 is made for …`); one made for a newer major is told to update music2. One of music2's own is told first the line to write for the version music2 comes with ([PACK-19](#packs)). Songs keep no backwards compatibility before music2 1.0 ([goals](goals.md)); packs get this one promise.
- *Why:* other people's work depends on a pack's contract, and a pack can't be fixed by the musician who plays it (design cards §8).
- *Today:* since wave 3 (`SDK_VERSION` in `core/script/src/lib.rs`: 2.0.0, which also carries [CARD-30](#music2s-own-sounds-on-the-card)'s resonance; 2.1.0 since track `labels`, which adds a setting's label, [CARD-34](#labels-on-the-card); the words in `pack::meets`, `core/song/src/pack.rs`). Tested by `a_pack_made_for_another_major_of_core_is_refused_in_words` (`core/session/src/pack_tests.rs`: a test pack asking for 1, then 3, is `pkg.broken` in those words), `a_pack_made_for_an_older_minor_of_core_still_builds` (packs made for 2, 2.0 and 2.1 build with 2.1.0, the last with a labelled setting), and the SDK's version in `bus_tests`, `mute_tests`, `kernel_settings_tests` and `poly_tests` (`core/script/src`).

**PACK-23.** Every pack music2 ships ([PACK-15](#packs)) and every test pack in the repo with code MUST ask for `@m2/core` by the SDK's major and the minor its code uses: the major alone (`"@m2/core" = "2"`) when it uses nothing added since the major's first version, else that minor (`"2.1"` once it labels a setting, [CARD-34](#labels-on-the-card)). So every later minor runs it, and an older one that lacks what it uses refuses it in words (`pkg.broken`: "it needs @m2/core "2.1", but this app has 2.0.0"). A pack of recordings alone, which runs no code, may ask for none. Every pack music2 writes (`m2 pack new`, `m2 pack import-sfz`, the check's own song) uses nothing added since and asks for the major alone, from the SDK's version (`m2_script::sdk_requirement`), never a number written by hand.
- *Why:* a pack that asks for a later minor than it uses (`"2.3"`) shuts out the apps between; one that asks for an earlier minor than it uses breaks on an app between, in its code, not in words; and a number written by hand is left behind at the next major.
- *Today:* since wave 3 (`@m2/drums` 2.0.0, `@m2/effects` 1.2.0, the fixtures' and the browser suites' test packs; `@m2/mixing` 1.0.0 since it joined them), each asking for `"2"`; `@m2/synths` 2.0.0 and `@m2/wavetable` 1.0.0 ask for `"2.1"` since track `labels` (`synth.custom` labels its sub octave, the Wavetable its tables and files). Tested by `every_shipped_manifest_is_met` (`core/session/src/lib.rs`: every `m2pkg.toml` under the examples, templates, fixtures and `packs/` asks for the SDK's major, and `.1` when its descriptors label a setting, which `m2_script::describe` shows). A pack's author is told nothing when it asks for an older minor than it uses (gaps.md).

**PACK-24.** A song that asks for an older major of `@m2/core` than the app's is told first how to move, as one asking for an older std is ([FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions)): `Press Upgrade it in the status, or run m2 upgrade <song>: it moves the song to this music2 in one step. This app has @m2/core 2. To use it, change the @m2/core line in music2.toml to "@m2/core" = "2". The song's code may need changes.`, then what it asks for and what is installed. One asking for a newer major is not: only a newer music2 runs it.
- *Why:* with no older major kept before 1.0 ([FMT-3a](#manifest-and-versions)), a major of `@m2/core` stops every older song at once; `m2 upgrade` moves the whole song in one step ([FMT-25](#conversion)), so it is said first, then the one line to change by hand.
- *Today:* since wave 3 (`Manifest::check_dependencies`, `core/song/src/manifest.rs`), and its first words since track `upgrade` (`m2_song::UPGRADE`, which the std line's words and `pack::move_on` lead with too). Tested by `unmet_dependencies_block` (`core/session/src/lib.rs`: a song asking for 1 is told to upgrade, or to write 2; one asking for 3 is not told to move).

**PACK-26.** `@m2/mixing` is a bundled pack ([PACK-15](#packs)) of five tools a mix needs, each an effect (category EQ or Dynamics) that plays the pack's own kernel ([PACK-21](#packs)): `@m2/mixing:eq`, `:gate`, `:transient`, `:deesser` and `:multiband`. It uses none of std's code. Each tool MUST keep what it promises:
- **`eq`, Parametric EQ**: eight bands, each a shape (a menu: low cut, low shelf, bell, high shelf, high cut), a frequency, a gain, a Q and an on/off switch, all values that play at once, labelled with their band's number (`Gain 3`) so an undo step or a lane names one alone. A band's response MUST be the RBJ cookbook biquad of its shape (Robert Bristow-Johnson's formulas: bilinear, prewarped at its frequency), measured within 0.1 dB of it, so a bell's gain at its frequency is its gain. It runs as a TPT state-variable filter (`m2_dsp::fx::Filter`, as std's `eq3` does). A cut is 12 dB per octave, its Q the bump at its corner, and its gain does nothing. A band never moves through the settings between two: a resonant band moved through them booms, louder than both ends, and two shapes have nothing between them. So a band asked for a new setting starts a second filter there, from the first one's state; lets it settle while the first plays (eight times its ringing time, how long its resonance takes to fall by 1/e; a second at most); then crossfades to it (20 ms). Both scale with how far the setting moves (all of them for another shape, 3 dB of gain or half an octave of frequency or Q), so a small move, a knob's drag or a lane's line, is followed at once. No change of shape, gain, Q or frequency MUST go more than 1 dB over the louder of its settled levels, at any Q up to 18. On and off crossfade the band with its input. It starts flat (the cuts off, every gain 0 dB) and then changes no bit, and it never gives more than +80 dBFS, the bound every effect keeps. Its card has a group for each band, `Band 1` to `Band 8`, and no picture: no view draws an EQ's whole curve (the filter view draws a low-pass, [CARD-27](#views-on-the-card), which no band is at the EQ's start).
- **`gate`, Gate**: silences a track between its sounds. It opens when its key reaches `threshold`. It starts to close once the key has stayed 6 dB under the threshold (its hysteresis, so a sound that hovers there never chatters) for `hold`, and never sooner than 25 ms, so the gaps between a low note's waves never close it. It opens over `attack`, a straight line in level, and closes over `release`, a straight line in dB, down to `range`. Closed, its output MUST be its input at `range` dB, and open, its input, bit for bit. Its key is its own input, or another sound given after it, `gate(pad.out, kick.out)`: a sidechain ([PKG-13](#component-descriptors)), at most one, which TypeScript and the build both check. Core has no input that may be left out yet, so the key is declared `many()`, and the descriptor says `many` (gaps.md).
- **`transient`, Transient shaper**: `attack` and `sustain`, each −24 to +24 dB. It follows the sound's level (the loudest sample of the last 25 ms, so a low note's waves are one steady level) with two followers: one rises slowly (20 ms) and falls with the level at once, so it lags each hit's start; one falls slowly (300 ms) and rises with it at once, so it hangs over each tail. A follower lands on the level only once the level holds still, never while it moves. A hit's start is turned by up to `attack` dB, all of it while the level is 6 dB or more above the slow riser; its tail by up to `sustain` dB, all of it 6 dB or more under the slow faller: so the faster a tail fades, the more of `sustain` it gets (one that falls by 1/e in 120 ms all of it from 200 ms on; in 1 s, about a quarter there). Where the level holds, or falls with `sustain` at 0, its output MUST be its input, bit for bit.
- **`deesser`, De-esser**: tames the hiss of S sounds. The band around `freq` (a band-pass of Q 1, about an octave and a half wide) is turned down while it is louder than `threshold`, held there, by at most `amount` dB: the output is the input plus that band times the gain less 1, so a bell whose depth follows the band's level, deepest at `freq`. Its detector hears the band through a second band-pass of its own, so a loud low note never sets it off. While the band is under the threshold its output MUST be its input, bit for bit, a 200 Hz tone at full level among them; a 200 Hz tone beside a 7 kHz one it turns down keeps its level within 0.05 dB. Nearer tones lose a little while it works (at 6 kHz, the most `amount`: 3 kHz up to 1.5 dB, 2 kHz and 12 kHz up to 0.5 dB, 1 kHz nothing).
- **`multiband`, Multiband compressor**: three bands split at `lowMid` and `midHigh` by Linkwitz-Riley crossovers (24 dB per octave: two Butterworth filters in a row), each compressed on its own by `threshold`, `ratio`, `attack`, `release` and `gain`, as std's `compress` compresses. Split points that cross are put in order: the lower splits low from mid, so the mid band is never empty. Linkwitz-Riley bands sum to an all-pass, not to the input (flat in level, turned in phase): at ratio 1 and gain 0 in every band its output MUST be the input through the two crossovers' all-passes (RBJ's, Q 1/√2), within float rounding, and its level at every frequency the input's within 0.01 dB.
- Each kernel keeps [ABI-5](#kernel-abi-reservations) (none takes memory but its state, and none allocates while it plays), runs as music2's own ([ABI-11](#kernel-abi-reservations)), is held to what every effect guarantees (the harness `m2_dsp::testing::effects`), plays the same bits whatever its blocks, from run to run and on any number of threads, and comes back from silence to the state it was born in, so a song played after the engine sat stopped plays its export. Its controls glide with m2-dsp's `Glide`, as std's do; the transient shaper's followers are their own (`Glide` lands within a millionth of a target that moves), and the multiband's compressors copy std's `compress` until the two share one in `m2-dsp`: std's is still in std's own crate.
- *Why:* the owner's "most things should go inside packs" ([design cards §7.1](designs/cards/design.md)): std keeps the four effects every mix starts with (`eq3`, `compress`, `limit`, `duck`, [PKG-17](#component-descriptors)), and the tools a mix reaches for next are a pack. A tool that says what it does in plain words must do it, measured: an EQ that isn't the response it names or booms when a band moves, a gate that chatters, a transient shaper whose sustain does nothing, a de-esser that dulls the voice and a crossover that colours the sound are the faults such tools are known for.
- *Today:* since track effects2: `packs/@m2/mixing` 1.0.0 (`src/index.ts`, its presets, `kernels/mixing.wasm`), its crate `m2-mixing-kernels` (`std/kernels-src/packs/@m2/mixing/Cargo.toml`, its source `packs/@m2/mixing/kernels-src`: `parametric.rs`, `dynamics.rs`, `bands.rs`). `packs/@m2/mixing/kernels-src/src/tests.rs` holds each tool to the harness and to its promises, against oracles of its own where there are some (RBJ's biquads and all-pass in f64): `the_eq_is_rbjs_response` (every shape at three frequencies, two gains and four Qs, from two octaves under to two over: within 0.001 dB), `a_bells_gain_at_its_frequency_is_its_gain`, `every_band_plays_every_shape`, `the_eq_starts_flat_bit_for_bit`, `no_eq_change_clicks`, `no_shape_change_booms` (every pair of shapes, Q 0.71 to 18, 12 and 24 dB, at 150 Hz and 1 kHz: at worst 0.3 dB over), `no_jump_of_a_bands_values_booms` (gain, Q and frequency jumps, small and large: at worst 0.2 dB), `a_lanes_line_is_followed_at_once`, `a_closed_gate_is_its_range`, `the_gate_opens_holds_and_closes_in_its_times`, `the_gate_never_chatters`, `a_key_opens_the_gate`, `the_transient_shaper_raises_a_hits_start_not_its_tail`, `sustain_raises_the_tail_not_the_start`, `sustain_turns_tails_that_fade_slowly_too` (a tone and noise, fading by 1/e in 30 ms to 1 s), `a_steady_sound_passes_the_transient_shaper`, `the_deesser_turns_down_its_band`, `the_deessers_bell_spares_what_is_far_from_it`, `the_multiband_sums_to_its_crossovers_all_pass` (about 2·10⁻⁶ from it), `each_multiband_band_compresses_on_its_own`, `crossed_split_points_are_put_in_order`, `silence_brings_each_tool_back_to_its_birth`, and `no_tool_allocates_while_it_plays` (a counting allocator, every torture setting, blocks of every size). `the_mixing_tools_are_effects_that_start_where_their_kernels_do` (`core/script/tests/mixing.rs`) holds each descriptor's defaults to its kernel's ports, which those tests play. The tests of every kernel play them as music2's own (`own_packages`, [PACK-21](#packs)); `every_std_kernel_keeps_its_inputs` gives a choice's port a constant of its options. `core/session/tests/fixtures/every-component` calls each tool, so the step 5 gates hold for them, and the forms the planners write give a choice its name and a switch `true` or `false` (`planned_call_forms_are_current`). TypeScript's checks of its calls are `core/script/sdk/check/mixing.ts`. The pack is installed at start (`installing_the_bundled_packs_twice_writes_nothing`, `the_servers_start_installs_the_packs_music2_ships_and_a_second_writes_nothing`), and a song of all five, its gate keyed by another track, plays the same bits on 1 and 4 threads and live as in the export, its gate open after each kick as the pad ungated, and at its range, −80 dB, once its release is over (`the_mixing_tools_give_the_same_bits_on_1_and_4_threads`, `core/play/tests/bit_identity.rs`). The browser suite `mixing` adds the EQ from + Effect, sees its bands and no picture, picks a band's shape and turns its frequency, and undoes each. Not yet: a picture of an EQ's whole curve, a gate's key picked on its card, and an input that may be left out (gaps.md, Effects).

**PACK-27.** `@m2/wavetable` is a bundled pack ([PACK-15](#packs)): music2's wavetable synth, one instrument, `@m2/wavetable:wavetable` (Wavetable, in the Instruments of + Track), each voice of which is one call of the pack's own kernel ([PACK-21](#packs)), `@m2/wavetable:voice`. What it holds:
- **Tables.** A table is frames of 2048 samples, each one cycle of a wave. Each of the synth's two oscillators, A and B, plays one, and its `position` (0 to 1, a control, so it moves while it plays and can be automated) slides from the table's first frame to its last, crossfading in a straight line the two frames it falls between. Which table is a setting, a menu of five made by code from their harmonics at the voice's birth (no recording ships): `basic` (a sine, a triangle, a saw, a square), `pulse` (a square narrowing to a 2 % pulse, 32 frames), `vowels` (a saw through the formants of a, e, i, o and u, 32 frames), `harmonics` (a saw's harmonics added one by one, 32 frames) and `digital` (harmonics switched on in groups, 32 frames). Or a WAV dropped on the oscillator's file (`fileA`, `fileB`: recordings, [CARD-5](#cards)), which, when there is one, is that oscillator's table in place of the menu's: its samples cut into frames of 2048 (a file shorter than a frame is one cycle, whatever its length; one of more than 64 frames gives 64, spread evenly from its first to its last), its channels mixed, its DC taken out, scaled so its loudest frame peaks at 1. A file that is missing plays the menu's table.
- **No aliasing.** Each frame is kept as 9 mip levels, an octave each: level k holds the frame's harmonics up to 512 at level 0, then 256 down to 2, in 256 samples for each harmonic it holds (2048 at most) and never fewer than 16 for its top one (so 8192 at level 0 and 4096 at level 1), made from the frame's spectrum when the voice is born, never on the audio thread. A frame's harmonics above the 512th are left out: they would sound only on notes below 39 Hz (about D#1), above 20 kHz. A voice runs at twice the song's rate: each unison voice reads the level whose top harmonic stays below that rate's Nyquist (its frequency held under 20 kHz and under 0.45 of the song's rate), between two samples by a cubic through the four about them, and when its pitch moves it across a level's line (a glide, a bend, a vibrato) it crossfades from the old level to the new through that control tick (16 samples), then m2-dsp's halfband brings the voice back to the song's rate, cutting all above 20 kHz (at 48 kHz). So a note's spectrum holds nothing folded below its fundamental above -90 dB of it (measured at C7, E7 and C8, at 48 and 44.1 kHz, on the saw, the square, the narrowest pulse and a digital frame), and on every note from A0 to C8 all that isn't one of its harmonics stays under -65 dB of them for any table, A-weighted from 20 Hz to 16 kHz as std's synths are measured (the worst at 48 kHz: -89 dB for the saw, -84 for the narrowest pulse, -102 for a vowel, -98 for a digital frame, and -67 for the brightest a table can be, a file of an impulse or of white noise, every harmonic as loud as the first).
- **Made once for a sound.** One sound's voices share a sandbox ([RENDER-32](#engine-shape-step-7)) and play the same tables, so a table is made by the first voice born that plays it, into the module's static memory, its shelf (room for 128 frames, two tables of 64: 11.8 MiB in every sandbox of the module, touched at its seal), kept by its settings: a built-in table's place, or a file's handle (the host's name for that audio in the sandbox; today every state that reads the same audio gets the same one, `Sandbox::state`). A voice keeps only those settings, and finds its tables on the shelf by them at each call, never by an address. A table is never changed once made. This is ABI-5's sandbox-wide data: made only at a birth, the same for the same settings whatever order voices are born in, found by settings, holding no voice's own bytes. A table the shelf has no room for is made in the voice's own memory, and plays the same bits.
- **The rest of a voice.** Each oscillator's pitch (in semitones) and level; unison of 1 to 8 voices (a setting) spread evenly from `detune` semitones below the note to `detune` above and panned out by `spread`, neighbours on opposite sides; a filter whose shape is a choice control (lowpass, bandpass, highpass, notch: m2-dsp's TPT state-variable filter), with cutoff and q, its cutoff following the key (`keytrack`), its own envelope and the LFO; an amp envelope; an LFO, a sine from each note's start, to both positions and to the cutoff; velocity; level; and one note at a time with glide (`voices: 1`, std/dsp's `mono`). Its card's groups are Oscillators, Filter, Envelopes, LFO and Unison ([CARD-17](#kinds-of-values)), with a picture of each envelope and of the filter ([CARD-16](#kinds-of-values)).
- **Presets.** Slow pad, Round bass, Sharp lead and Short pluck. A preset holds numbers only ([FMT-22](#presets)), so each plays the tables the card has chosen.
- *Why:* the wavetable synth is the modern synth producers expect, and the owner keeps the core small: a sound and its kernel are a pack's. Tables made by code ship no recording and need no licence, and are a pure function of the kernel's code. The cubic read and the 16 samples a cycle are measured choices: copies of 1023 harmonics in 2048 samples, read in a straight line, left a saw at A1 at -45 dB, and 8 samples a cycle left an impulse at -48 dB, the images of the levels' top harmonics (above hearing themselves) folding back into hearing. 16 cost the shelf a third more (11.8 MiB, from 8.8). A table made once for a sound, not once a voice, keeps a 16-voice pad's memory and birth those of one voice; the kernel ABI has no other way for states to share what they make, and constant data in the module would have shipped every table's levels in the wasm.
- *Today:* since track wavetable: `packs/@m2/wavetable` (1.0.0: `src/index.ts`, its presets in `presets/wavetable/`), its kernel's source `packs/@m2/wavetable/kernels-src` (`lib.rs` the voice, `tables.rs` the tables and how a frame becomes its levels, `shelf.rs` the shelf and the levels' layout, `fft.rs`), its crate `m2-wavetable-kernels` (`std/kernels-src/packs/@m2/wavetable/Cargo.toml`). Its native tests (`kernels-src/src/tests.rs`): `a_note_plays_at_its_pitch` (within 0.05 cents, by zero crossings, from A1 to A7 at 44.1, 48 and 96 kHz, and each oscillator's pitch), `position_morphs_between_frames_as_declared` (within 0.1 dB of the crossfade), `a_high_note_folds_nothing_below_its_fundamental` (and a saw read with no band-limiting is far above the floor), `every_note_holds_little_but_its_harmonics`, `a_pitch_crossing_a_levels_line_never_clicks` (a vibrato across the lines at 750 Hz, 1.5 and 3 kHz holds nothing below its pitch above -90 dB: -117 dB at worst for the saw, -97 for the narrowest pulse, -93 for a file of every harmonic, where each level read with no crossfade held -71, -47 and -40), `unison_voices_detune_as_declared` (within 0.2 cents), `a_dropped_wav_becomes_a_table`, `every_kind_of_file_becomes_a_table`, `voices_share_their_tables`, `each_filter_shape_plays_its_place`, `a_fast_filter_sweep_never_steps`, `a_note_too_high_for_a_low_rate_stays_bounded` (8 and 11.025 kHz, a minute), `no_allocation_while_playing`, `deterministic_whatever_the_block_size`, `a_reset_starts_over`, `extreme_controls_never_make_nan`. In wasm, as music2's own (`core/runtime/src/tests/wavetable.rs`): `the_wavetable_plays_in_a_sealed_sandbox_and_allocates_nothing`, `the_wavetable_renders_the_same_bits_every_time`, `a_voice_moved_into_a_sandbox_born_in_another_order_plays_the_same_bits` (ABI-5), `sixteen_voices_grow_their_sandbox_by_less_than_one_table`, `the_oscillators_tables_swapped_play_the_same_bits` and `a_file_with_samples_that_arent_numbers_plays_in_the_wavetable_and_the_sampler`, `a_wavetable_song_gives_the_same_bits_on_1_to_7_threads` (`core/play/tests/wavetable.rs`), and every test of every kernel music2 ships. The browser suite `wavetable` adds it from + Track, picks a table, turns a position and plays a note.

## Homes of values

| Home | Holds | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Code literals** at declared slots | std knobs, mixer faders and pan, effect settings, "mute in song": the code is the preset (a preset file is loaded into the code as one edit, [FMT-22](#presets)) | `reverb(x, { mix: 0.25 })` |
| **Lanes** | values that change over time | `data/bass/cutoff.json` |
| **Plugin state blob** | a plugin's internals | `data/lead/lead.state` |
| **`layout.json`** | shared presentation: track order and colour (a palette name), keyed by track key; markers; the song's key | `{"tracks": {"kick": {"colour": "rose"}}, "key": {"root": "A", "scale": "minor"}}` |
| **`data/arrangement.json`** | the tempo and the time signature, each from a beat ([FMT-13](#arrangement)) | `{"tempo": [{"beat": 0, "bpm": 120}], "meter": []}` |
| **`.music2/`** | local state: mute and solo, override latches, the loop and the view settings (zoom, follow, heights, hidden lanes, grids, audition), the undo history ([WRITE-23](#undo)) and the code editor's unsaved text ([WRITE-27](#unsaved-code)) | `.music2/overlays.json`, `.music2/view.json`, `.music2/undo/`, `.music2/drafts/` |

**HOME-1.** Every value MUST have exactly one home, and the UI MUST show which. Every value that `controls()`, the view and leases return carries `home: code | lane | state | layout | arrangement | local`, and a test checks it for every control of every fixture.
- *Why:* each new feature (mixer, reorder and colour, automation, plugins) would otherwise pick its own convention.
- *Today:* matches since step 5: `m2_session::Home` is on every `Control`, knob, read-only value, lease (`LeaseInfo`) and lease begin (`Begun`), and is `code` for each (a literal, or a declared default whose edit writes the code) until lanes, latches and plugin state have knobs. `every_value_names_its_home` (`crates/editor/src/gate5_tests.rs`) checks the view as it is sent, for the five fixtures and a song that calls every std component. Since 8b.2 the timeline carries `home: arrangement` for its tempo and time signatures, as the server publishes it (`m2_editor::TimelineView`; `the_published_timeline_is_the_sessions_and_shares_what_didnt_change`), and the song bar's tooltips say "Saved in data/arrangement.json".

**HOME-2.** "Automate this knob" MUST be a `Confined` Tx that replaces the literal with `lane.control(key, unit, <the old literal>)` and creates an empty lane file. Dragging a knob that a lane or a cable drives sets an override latch ([MON-6](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)); it never edits the fallback, the lane or the cable.
- *Why:* the fallback keeps the song sounding the same where no clip is, and a drag must not quietly rewrite automation.
- *Today:* matches for "Automate" since step 6: one `Confined` Tx on the value's expression and the track function's parameters, with the empty lane file (`crates/editor/src/automate.rs`; renders before and after are the same bits). Override latches aren't built ([MON-6](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).
- *Today, from 8b.5:* the lane's key is the control's name (`decay`; a channel of the song's mixer by its track and the control's word, as the Mix view says it: `hats-pan` for Hats pan, `hats-volume` for Hats volume, never "hats db"), with the call's binding in front only when the track has that key already (`pad-decay`), and `lane.control` gets `{ name }` only when the key, read as words, isn't the control's label (`{ name: "Detune (cents)" }`). **Remove automation** is built, the inverse (`automate::remove`, `POST /api/graph/automate/remove`, `m2 unautomate`): one `Confined` Tx that writes what the lane plays at the playhead (its fallback, as written, with none) where the lane was read, spelled as the fallback is (`hz(1250)`), and removes the lane file; the track function keeps its `lane`, and undo puts both back. In the view, an input a control lane drives names it (`InputPort.lane`), so a knob can say "Follows the Cutoff lane" and offer Show lane and Remove automation. A drag on such an input has no knob to move until latches exist.

**HOME-3.** Presentation MUST NOT go in files that define behaviour: no colours in `track()` options, no canvas positions in code.

**HOME-4.** For a plugin parameter, precedence is: a signal connected in code, then a literal in code, then the state blob. The state's value for a parameter owned by code is ignored.
- *Why:* what saved state means must be decided once.
- *Today:* matches since step 7. A literal or a port is sent whenever it differs from the value last sent, and every parameter code owns is sent again at the first call after a reset, and at the first two after a state load or an export (`plugin_events` and `PluginNode::process` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`): a plugin may apply a loaded state at its next call, over what that call sends (Surge XT does). So the blob's value for one plays for at most two blocks (`a_state_load_resends_code_owned_parameters`). A plugin that applies a loaded state later than that, and says so in its output events, is set back by [HOME-5a](#homes-of-values)'s revert (since ph-2), silently for the three calls after a load.

**HOME-5.** A gesture in a plugin window on a parameter that is a code literal MUST write the literal through a coalesced Tx. On a parameter driven by a signal, it sets an override latch ([MON-6](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).
- *Why:* a plugin's GUI can't refuse a gesture, and a cable must not be overridden silently.
- *Today:* matches since ph-2 (step 9). A number written in a `plugin()` call is a control since ph-2 (`@m2/core` gives it an origin, `{site, arg: ["1", <port>]}`, so it is a cell, a knob and a lease target like any value in code; no new API, so the SDK stays 1.2.0). The plugin node reads its call's output events (HOST-4): a gesture's begin on a parameter set by a value in code holds it in the instance's override table (`HELD` in `core/runtime/src/plugins.rs`), so the node sends the window's value in its place. The session, at its next tick (every job, and the watcher's catch-up every 300 ms), begins a gesture lease (WRITE-22) on that control, follows the window's value, and at the gesture's end commits the lease: one value edit, one Undo step. Then it lets the hold go, and the node sends the code's value, now the window's (`a_gesture_on_a_literal_commits_one_tx` in `crates/server/src/graph/plugin_window_tests.rs`).

**HOME-5a.** The host MUST treat parameters that code owns like this:
- Between the plugin's gesture begin and end on such a parameter, the host stops sending it the code value, until the coalesced Tx commits. If the Tx is rejected, the host sends the code value again.
- A change the plugin reports outside a gesture (a preset loaded in its own browser) is set back to the code value at the next block, and the session raises the Notice `plugin.param-reverted`, listing the parameters.
- When code stops owning a parameter (its literal or cable is deleted), the plugin keeps its current value, and the next state save records it.
- *Why:* during the 100 ms or more before the Tx is accepted the host would fight the user's hand, a preset load must not silently overrule the code, and a released parameter must not jump.
- *Today:* matches since ph-2 (step 9). The first point: a held parameter isn't sent while the gesture lasts; a commit that is refused, or a value that isn't a written literal (a computed one), lets the hold go at the gesture's end, and the node sends the code's value again. The second: a parameter value the plugin outputs outside a gesture, on a parameter its code sets, makes the node send the code's value at its next call, and the session says `plugin.param-reverted` once per tick, naming the parameters (`a_preset_change_outside_a_gesture_is_set_back`); in the three calls after a state load it is set back without a word, as a plugin may say what a loaded state set. The third since step 7: a kept instance whose code stops setting a parameter keeps the value it was last sent, as nothing sends it another. It is flagged (`PluginInstance::released` in `core/runtime/src/plugins.rs`) until a state load lands, so an export plays its node with a new instance from the committed state, as `m2 render` does ([RENDER-1](#offline); `a_released_parameter_keeps_its_value_and_its_instance_isnt_lent`).

**HOME-6.** State autosave, once plugin windows ship, is a coalesced data-plane write: one Tx after a gesture ends, or after about 1 s of quiet following the plugin's `mark_dirty`, which is how CLAP reports many changes (a wavetable or a modulation edit) that have no gesture. It takes the prepared-data plane, outside the 20 ms budget, and the journal stores its before bytes once ([WRITE-15](#commit-journal-and-recovery)). A saved state is never pushed back into the instance that produced it: a load is skipped when its blob's hash equals the state the instance holds, the one it last saved or last loaded, at creation included.
- *Why:* state blobs are often 1–50 MB, and a write per parameter change would flood the disk and the undo history. A load skipped against the last save alone would skip a redo: save S, load C (an undo), load S.
- *Today:* matches since ph-2 (step 9). `HostEvent::Dirty`, or a value or a gesture's end on a parameter code doesn't set (the node marks its instance changed), starts a quiet second for that instance; each new change starts it again. Then the session asks the host for `save_state` (off the session's thread) and writes blob and sidecar as one `plugin_state` Tx (FMT-17), one Undo step; a save equal to the file writes nothing (`a_dirty_plugin_autosaves_its_state_once`: a storm of `mark_dirty` makes one Tx; the save isn't loaded back; an undo loads the old blob). A plugin with no state path (no key) can't be saved, and the session says so once. Until it is saved, its instance isn't lent to an export, which saves first ([RENDER-1](#offline)). The host keeps the hash of the state each instance holds, set by every load, at creation too, and by every save, and cleared by a load that fails, which may have landed in part (`MainSide.holds` in `core/plugins/src/inprocess.rs`); tested by `a_load_of_the_held_state_is_skipped`, `a_reload_after_another_load_is_never_skipped` and `after_a_refused_load_the_old_blob_loads_again` (`core/plugins/src/tests.rs`).

## Monitoring overlays and mute in song

**MON-1.** Mute and solo MUST be engine overlays: gain cells keyed by module key, on track modules. They work for every song, whatever shape its code has inside its tracks. The planner puts one overlay gain cell on each consumer port that a track's audio export feeds. The root module (`song`) can't be muted or soloed, and neither can parts within one track; `key()` frames are reserved as a later place for that.
- The consumer ports are the checker's sites (`Flat.overlays`): each input of a node, each capture of a voice and each feedback that reads the audio of a track other than its own module, except sidechain inputs ([MON-2](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)); and the song's output when it reads a track directly. The same code gives the same sites.
- The overlay is a built-in op, in every plan, offline renders included. At 1 it copies its input, so it changes no bit; at 0 it writes +0.0. One node and one cell per site.
- In an activation's window every audio edge that crosses tracks reads through an overlay: a fade on a site's input has the site's overlay on its new side; a fade's old side and a ghost's input that the plan before read through one get one of their own for the window, at the new plan's value for the same track and consumer, or, where the new plan has none (the track deleted, or read by another module now), at the gain the plan before had there.
- *Why:* a song written by hand or by an agent, with no mixer, still needs mute and solo.
- *Today:* the engine half is built in 8b.4 (`core/ir/src/check.rs` `overlays`, `core/runtime/src/overlay.rs`; `core/runtime/src/tests/overlay.rs`, including `an_edit_while_muted_keeps_the_track_silent`). Each overlay runs right after its source, so a feedback consumer reads this block's. The server's mute and solo come next.

**MON-2.** Mute MUST silence its module's audio export at every consumer port except sidechain ports ([PKG-13](#component-descriptors)), which always get the unmuted signal. Control exports such as `kick.env` keep flowing. So muting the kick still ducks the bass, whether the ducking reads `kick.env`, `follow(kick.out)` written in `song.ts`, or a compressor keyed from `kick.out`. The mixer shows this.
- *Why:* muting the kick must not suddenly un-duck the bass, and a refactor that keeps the sound must not change what mute does.
- *Today:* the engine half is built in 8b.4: sidechain inputs and control exports are never overlay sites ([MON-1](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)). What the mixer shows comes from `feeds` (`crates/editor/src/mute.rs`), in the graph state's `overlays.feeds`: for each track, each module and component that reads it through a sidechain input or a control export, such as first-song's kick, read by `song`'s Duck (`feeds_name_the_duck`). A node written in `song.ts` is named as `song` until the mixer's channel lookup can name its channel's track.

**MON-3.** Solo MUST keep every audio export on a path through a soloed module, and mute the others, with the same exception for sidechain ports.
- The paths are over the sites: a site `m → c` (track `m`'s audio read in module `c`) is heard when no track is soloed, or when some soloed `s` is `m` or feeds `m`, or is `c` or is fed by `c`. Sidechain inputs aren't sites, so they are no edges here. Each site has its own cell, so soloing a bus that reads a vocal itself keeps the vocal's way into it and mutes the same vocal's direct way to the song.
- A bus the song feeds ([RENDER-34](#buses)) is read from the song, which is no track, so the feed is no site. The checker's routes (`Flat.routes`, `m → b`: track `m`'s way into the song reaches bus `b`'s feed) join the "who feeds whom" graph, and a site `m → song` counts as feeding each bus `m` routes to:

  | You do | You hear |
  |---|---|
  | Mute a track | nothing from it: dry, sends and group, since all start after its one cell |
  | Solo a track | it, and every bus it feeds (through sends or `to`), and the buses those feed |
  | Solo a bus | it, and every track that feeds it, their dry sound too: one cell serves both ways |
  | Mute a group | its tracks go silent with it |
- Mute wins over solo on the same track, mute in song included: a track muted in the song stays silent when soloed.
- *Today:* built in 8b.4 (`values` in `crates/server/src/graph/overlay.rs`; `solo_keeps_a_bus_fed_and_its_sources`, `mute_wins_over_solo`, `a_track_muted_in_song_stays_silent_when_soloed`). Routes since track bus, slice 1 (`solo_keeps_the_buses_a_track_feeds`, `soloing_a_bus_keeps_what_feeds_it`, `muting_a_track_silences_its_sends`). Soloing a bus keeps its sources' dry sound too: a wet-only solo needs a cell per path (gaps).

**MON-4.** Mute and solo persist in `.music2/`. Overlays MUST NOT change files, revisions, exports, or an agent render that didn't ask for them (`m2 listen --as-heard` asks, and says so, [AGENT-7](#agents)). An export made while overlays are active carries the `overlays.active` Warning.
- The file is `.music2/overlays.json`, `{"mute": [keys], "solo": [keys]}`, sorted, written whole (a temp file, then a rename). Entries of a track that is gone stay, so it comes back as it was; meanwhile they are ignored, so a solo of a track that is gone silences nothing. A track renamed in code (`Rekeyed`) keeps its entries. A window open read-only keeps its mutes and solos in memory only.
- *Why:* owner decision 4: mute and solo are for listening only.
- *Today:* built in 8b.4. `POST /api/graph/overlay` (`{session, mute?: {key: bool}, solo?: {key: bool}, exclusive?: key, clear?: true}`, not a write, so it works read-only) writes the cells of what plays under the control plane's lock and never waits for the session; the answer, and `overlays` in the graph state, say what is muted and soloed, which tracks are muted in the song, how each track is heard (`heard`, `muted`, `muted-in-song` or `silenced`) and whether this window saves them (`saved`). The file is read when the project opens, so the first renderer plays them from its first block; the server's `music2-tell` thread writes it after a change, off the lock, and a change not yet written is written before the project closes, opens again or the app quits. Every new plan gets the values before it is sent. An export carries `overlays.active` when any of them changes what is heard, and its file is byte-identical either way (`crates/server/src/graph/overlay_tests.rs`). Since `ag-listen` (owner decision D4 of the agents design), `m2 listen --as-heard` reads the open app's mute and solo, else the file, gives its render's overlays the values they give, and says `"as_heard": true`; without it, an agent's listen hears none (`m2_listen_as_heard_applies_the_listening_mutes_and_says_so`).

**MON-5.** "Mute in song" is saved in code and applies to every render, export included. It is a track option in `@m2/core`, so every song has it: `track("kick", { muted: true }, fn)`, carried in the IR as `Module.muted`. The planner applies it as it applies a monitoring mute ([MON-2](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)), with the same cells and the same sidechain exception, in every render. Toggling it is a `Confined` Tx on the track call's options; the delta's `muted` change sets the cell, with a ramp and no rebuild.
- *Why:* owner decision 4 asks for a saved mute in every song. A mute that lived only in std's mixer would privilege one package (song-as-program principle 2) and leave a song without a mixer with none.
- *Today:* since 8b.4 the option is in `@m2/core` 1.2.0 (`core/script/src/mute_tests.rs`), the IR carries it, and every render starts each site of a muted track at 0 (`muted_in_song_silences_an_offline_render`). The delta reports the change as `ModuleChange::Muted` (`core/session/src/tx/muted_tests.rs`). The server takes a delta whose only module changes are `Muted` as overlay cell writes, which glide, with no new plan (`a_mute_in_song_toggle_is_a_cell_write`).

**MON-6.** A gesture, in the UI or a plugin window, on a control that a signal drives (a lane or a cable) sets an override latch. The control follows the gesture until "Re-enable" or "Write to code", and the UI marks it as latched. A latch is keyed by the ControlId of a descriptor control, or by the plugin instance and its CLAP parameter id. It persists in `.music2/` only when its whole key is saved keys (a keyed plugin's parameter: module key, frame keys, plugin key, parameter id); otherwise it lasts for the session. A latch never reaches an export.
- *Why:* a lane or a cable must not be overridden silently, and a ControlId, derived from where its calls are ([ID-13](#derived-identity)), doesn't survive a restart.
- *Today:* for plugin windows since ph-2 (step 9): a gesture's begin on a parameter a lane or a cable drives latches it in the instance's override table (`LATCHED`), keyed by the instance and the CLAP id, and the node sends the window's value in place of the driven one from then on. The graph state's `plugins` lists each node's latches; its box says "<name> latched" with Re-enable (`POST /api/graph/plugin/latch {session, node, id?}`), after which the lane plays again. For the session only (no `.music2/` file yet), and never in an export: a latched instance isn't lent (`a_gesture_on_a_lane_driven_parameter_latches_it`). Latches on descriptor controls, "Write to code", and keeping a keyed plugin's latches in `.music2/` are gaps.

**MON-7.** Audition is not an overlay. It is a `Live(Event)` source merged into a lane's event buffer ([RENDER-14](#events)).
- A live note is played on a notes lane, not written in its data. It belongs to a source, which numbers its own notes by handle: a window today, a MIDI keyboard later, on the same path. A message names the plan it is for (its generation) and the lane's slot in it; one for another plan is refused and counted.
- Each lane's scheduler holds up to 16 live notes; one more On is refused and counted with the dropped Ons. Their ids are the engine's, so a live note's id never meets a scheduled note's in any consumer.
- A live note ends only by its Off, its source's release (all its notes, on every lane), a panic, or an activation that replaces its consumer or removes its lane. Never by a stop, a seek or a loop wrap: a held key is a hand, not the song. Offline renders never get one.
- *Why:* you can't write a melody you can't hear. A key held while the song plays, loops or is edited must keep sounding, as a held MIDI key does in other DAWs.
- *Today:* the engine half is built in 8b.6 (`LiveEv` and `NoteScheduler::live` in `core/runtime/src/sched.rs`, `Renderer::live`, `Msg::Live`; `core/runtime/src/tests/audition.rs`). The table moves with its lane's scheduler at an activation, into a grown one too (`a_live_note_moves_with_its_lane_across_an_activation`). A replaced consumer is a ghost released at entry, so its live notes end there; the Off later reaches the new consumer, which never had the note and ignores it (`a_replaced_consumer_ends_its_live_notes`). A pitch outside 0 to 127, a velocity outside 0 to 1 or a slot the plan doesn't have is refused, never a panic on the audio thread. Held for an export, an On is refused (it would sound only when the export ends) and an Off waits for the resume. The player sends one with `Player::live`, and publishes how many are held after each callback (`Player::live_notes`). The server half is built too (`crates/server/src/graph/audition.rs`): a window sends `{"t": "audition", "module", "lane", "on": [{"h", "pitch", "velocity"}], "off": [h], "keep": [h]}` on its WebSocket, and an agent the same with its `client` to `POST /api/graph/audition` (not a write, so read-only windows play too). Each window is a source. The server finds the lane in the plan it last sent and sends the note under the control plane's lock, so it queues behind that plan's activation; it remembers each held note's lane, not its slot, so an Off after an edit finds it (`an_off_after_an_edit_finds_its_lane`). Since `next` two more sources play the selected track the same way: the keyboard piano (a window's audition, from the computer's keys) and each MIDI keyboard (`midi:<name>`, `crates/server/src/graph/midi.rs`, through `m2_os::midi`), with velocity and the sustain pedal. A keyboard is never let go by audition's watchdog: its notes end when it is unplugged, the song closes, or ⌘. stops all sound. `M2_MIDI=none` opens no MIDI device (tests, the check), and `inject` takes what a keyboard would send at `POST /api/graph/midi/inject` (the suites). A lane nothing plays answers that window only, `{"t": "audition", "silent": "Nothing plays this lane"}`. A window's notes are let go when its WebSocket closes, or when it said nothing for 10 s (the window sends a keepalive every 2 s while a key is held; the `music2-tell` thread checks). `/api/graph/status` and the graph state carry `live`, the live notes sounding (`crates/server/src/graph/audition_tests.rs`, `audition_over_http_and_the_websocket` in `crates/server/src/routes_8b_tests.rs`). Since track pm's slice 2 a keyboard's pitch wheel and mod wheel reach its target lane live too ([RENDER-14](#events)'s `Midi`, `Graph::wheel`), and go back to rest on the lane they moved when it lets go: unplugged, All Notes Off, turned off in Settings, another target (its held keys sound on where they are), ⌘. (in the engine); a song closed takes its lanes with it. Held for an export, a wheel isn't refused, as an On is: the latest waits in its slot for the resume, as an Off does, since one let go must never stay bent (`held_for_an_export_a_wheel_waits_for_the_resume`). An export puts a borrowed plugin's wheels at rest before it renders.
