The status carries `wheels`, the lanes whose wheels are away from rest (`a_keyboards_bend_reaches_its_lane`, `a_keyboard_let_go_centres_its_bend` in `crates/server/src/graph/midi_tests.rs`). The sustain pedal stays in the server. Since `studio` a take reads live notes on the control plane as they are played, never through the session. Recording the wheels isn't built.

**MON-8.** Hearing yourself while recording MUST be a live source on the take's audio lane, like audition ([MON-7](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)): the sound input is added to that lane's block before the graph runs, so the track's effects, fader, mute and solo play it. It never reaches an export, a bounce or an agent render ([RENDER-3](#offline), [MON-4](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)). It is off unless Settings turns it on, and lasts from Record (the count-in included) to the take's end, and on an armed track for as long as it is armed ([MON-9](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)). Each lane hears its own channels of the input ([MON-13](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)), and several lanes hear at once.
- A message names the plan it is for (its generation) and the lane's slot, as a live note does, and starts or stops that lane's hearing only; one for another plan is refused and counted. The server says again what each lane hears whenever a new plan plays, so a lane a new plan made hears, and one no one arms any more stops. While a lane hears, the engine never idles.
- *Why:* a singer needs to hear their voice with the song, through the effects it will have. Speakers feed back into the microphone, so it is off by default and Settings says to use headphones.
- *Today:* the engine half is built in step 9's io turn (slice 2): `Msg::Hear` and `Renderer::hear`, the input read through `m2_runtime::LiveInput`; the hearing moves with the lane's audio player across an activation (`core/runtime/src/audio.rs`). `Player::hear` sends it; the server sends it at Record when the take's options ask (`crates/server/src/graph/record.rs`). Tested by `hearing_yourself_plays_through_the_track_and_never_in_an_export` (`core/play/tests/live.rs`: the input comes out at the track's fader, an offline render has none of it) and `hearing_yourself_never_reaches_an_export` (`crates/server/src/graph/record_tests.rs`: an export made during a take is silent). The Settings toggle comes with slice 3. Since track `recording`, several lanes hear at once, each from its own ring: `Renderer::hear(slot, input)` starts or stops one lane (`hearing_two_lanes_each_hears_its_own`, `core/play/tests/live.rs`), and the server tells what hears whenever the arms or the take change, and again on every tick of the take thread, so a new plan's lanes hear too (`send_hearing` in `crates/server/src/graph/record.rs`). Tested by `an_armed_track_hears_across_a_new_plan` (its lane moved to another slot: heard once; two armed tracks at once) and `a_track_disarmed_right_after_a_new_plan_stops_hearing` (the lane that moved into the new plan hearing is told to stop, before any tick), in `record_tests.rs`.

**MON-9.** A track MAY be **armed** for recording: the R button on its header, or `POST /api/graph/arm`. Arming is no edit: it writes no file, makes no undo step, works in a window open read-only, and lasts until the song closes, or until the song no longer shows the track or its lane (an edit of the code, an undo, an agent's change): then the arm, and the track's choice of channels, go at once, so the keyboards play the selected track again. Arming names the lane Record records on: the track's notes lane, else its audio lane; of several, the one of the clip the Notes view shows for that track, else the first.
- Record records into every armed track at once, a take on each; with none armed, into the selected track, as before.
- One notes track is armed at a time: arming another one disarms it, since the keyboards play one track. While one is armed, the MIDI keyboards and the keyboard piano play it, whatever is selected. Any number of audio tracks may be armed.
- An armed audio track hears its input through the track, with no take, while Settings' Hear yourself is on ([MON-8](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)): the input opens when the track is armed.
- While a take records, the arms can't change.
- *Why:* a singer must hear themselves before they record, and a band records several inputs at once.
- *Today:* built in track `recording` (`crates/server/src/graph/record.rs`, `Arms`; the header's R in `web/src/components/Arm.tsx`). Arms on a track the song no longer shows are dropped as each revision is published (`prune_arms`). Tested by `an_armed_track_monitors_without_a_take`, `record_records_into_every_armed_track`, `arming_a_notes_track_disarms_the_other`, `an_armed_track_that_leaves_the_song_is_disarmed` and `the_pedal_holds_notes_on_an_armed_track_in_each_pass` (`crates/server/src/graph/record_tests.rs`), and `e2e/rec-takes.mjs`.

**MON-10.** **Punch** (in Record's menu) MUST record only between the loop's edges, whether the loop plays or not. The song plays from where it starts (after a count-in, as Record does), recording starts at the range's start (or where the song starts, inside it) and stops at its end, where the song plays on. The take replaces what was there, on each lane: a clip that crosses an edge of the range is cut there, as Split cuts it, and whatever lies inside goes; the take's clip covers exactly what was recorded. With Overdub on, a notes take keeps what was there ([MON-12](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)). The way in is a count too: a note struck in its last beat lands on the take's first beat, as one in a count-in's last beat does (D27). Record with Punch on is refused, in words, with no loop set, or with the song already past the range's end.
- *Why:* a singer fixes one line without touching the lines around it.
- *Today:* built in track `recording`: the take's span (`Span::To`), and the one gesture that writes a take (`Gesture::Record`, `How::Replace`, `crates/editor/src/clips.rs`). The take's gesture is the take thread's own, never on the wire (`#[serde(skip)]`): the clips and drag routes can't be asked for it. A take cut short makes its waveform from the file as kept. Tested by `a_punch_replaces_only_its_range` (notes), `an_audio_punch_splits_what_was_there`, `a_punchs_waveform_is_the_file_it_kept`, `a_key_struck_just_before_a_way_in_ends_lands_on_the_first_beat` and `undo_puts_a_punch_and_an_overdub_back_byte_for_byte` (`record_tests.rs`), `a_take_replaces_its_range_as_split_cuts` and `the_takes_gesture_is_off_the_wire` (`crates/editor/src/clips_tests.rs`).

**MON-11.** With the loop on and recording started before its end, a take MUST record the loop, round and round until it stops: from the loop's start (a song started before it plays up to it first: a way in, whose last beat counts as a count-in's does, [MON-10](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)), or from where it starts inside it. Each time round is a **pass**. A moment is placed by the loop, never a straight line: what the speakers play just before the loop's end belongs to its pass, though the engine is round already, and nothing lands before the loop's start.
- Notes: each pass is its own clip, written as the pass ends, so the next pass plays it: a part builds in layers.
- Audio: one file holds every pass, one after another, the first one led by silence from the loop's start; it is written once the take ends, as one clip over the loop. The clip's offset picks which pass plays, and the file's note (`<file>.json`, [LIB-7](#library)) lists where each pass starts in it, `{"takes": [0, 8, 16]}` in seconds. The newest whole pass plays; a pass the stop cut short is listed but plays only when it is the only one.
- A clip whose file has takes has a **Takes** menu (its right-click menu, or `POST /api/graph/take`): choosing one moves the clip's offset to the same place in that pass, and a clip that follows the tempo moves its warp markers with it, so it plays that pass too. A clip split or copied keeps them.
- A seek, a stop, a new player, or the loop changed after the first pass or during a punch, ends the take where it is heard; the loop changed during the first pass moves where it ends, as before.
- *Why:* singers stack takes and keep the best; a beat is built one layer a pass.
- *Today:* built in track `recording` (`Take::place`, `Take::next_pass` and `Take::placing` in `crates/server/src/graph/record.rs`; the note's takes in `crates/editor/src/take.rs`, `note_takes` and `takes_of`; the view lists them, `takes` on an audio lane). A take's writer that wrote past the take's end before it was known cuts it off (`Growing::keep`). Tested by `three_loop_passes_make_three_takes` (audio: the newest plays, choosing another is one undo step), `choosing_a_take_on_a_clip_that_follows_the_tempo_plays_it` (an export plays the take chosen), `each_pass_over_a_loop_is_its_own_clip` and `keys_either_side_of_a_wrap_stay_in_their_pass` (notes, a loop from beat 4), `record_tests.rs`.

**MON-12.** **Overdub** (in Record's menu) MUST put a notes take into the clip under it: the clip that holds the take's first note (of several, the one that starts last), which grows to hold the new notes. With no clip there, the take makes its own, as without Overdub. Audio clips play together anyway, so Overdub is for notes only.
- *Why:* a part is often played in two goes: the left hand, then the right.
- *Today:* built in track `recording` (`How::Into`, `Gesture::Record`). Tested by `overdub_merges_into_the_clip_under_it` (`record_tests.rs`) and `overdub_grows_the_clip_to_hold_the_take` (`clips_tests.rs`).

**MON-13.** Each audio track MUST record from **channels** of the input Settings chose: a pair (1–2, 3–4, …) as a stereo take, or one channel as a mono take. A track records 1–2 (1 on a mono input) until another is chosen, on its R button's menu; the choice lasts until the song closes. Record refuses, naming the track, when the input hasn't the channels chosen. The input's callback copies each pair into a ring of its own ([RENDER-30a](#audio-lanes)), and a lane hears its channels from it ([MON-8](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)), with nothing allocated on the audio threads.
- *Why:* an interface with four inputs records a band; one microphone on input 2 records mono.
- *Today:* built in track `recording` (`m2_play::Channels` and a ring for each pair in `core/play/src/inputs.rs`; the take's writer picks its channels, `crates/server/src/graph/take_audio.rs`). The silent input of the tests and suites may say its channels (`M2_AUDIO_INPUTS="Desk:4"`), and `POST /api/graph/input/inject` takes `channel`. The take's level bar reads its channels: one channel alone shows only it (`a_mono_takes_input_level_is_its_own_channel`, `meters_tests.rs`). Tested by `input_three_and_four_record_their_own_channels` (`record_tests.rs`) and `the_input_callback_never_allocates` (`core/play/tests/no_alloc.rs`: 3–4 on one lane and channel 1 alone on another, heard at once).

**MON-14.** Each musical result of recording MUST be one Tx and one undo step: each notes pass of a loop; the end of a take (every lane's clip together, its last notes pass included); choosing a take. Each is named ("recording on Vox", "Vox take 2"). A take's files are durable before the Tx names them, notes included ([WRITE-1](#the-two-write-primitives)). Arming, a track's channels, Punch and Overdub are no edit. Recording and arming are the musician's ([AGENT-11](#agents)): `POST /api/graph/record/start` (`punch`, `overdub`), `stop`, `cancel` and `/api/graph/arm`. Choosing a take is a song change, open to agents too: `POST /api/graph/take`, through `m2 call`.
- *Why:* undo must take back what the musician thinks of as one thing.
- *Today:* built in track `recording`. Tested by `three_loop_passes_make_three_takes` and `record_records_into_every_armed_track` (`record_tests.rs`).

**MON-15.** The keyboard piano MUST play at a velocity the musician sets: − and = step it down and up by 10%, from 10% to 100% (80% at first), as C and V do in Logic's and Live's musical typing. The card shows it. While the piano is on, − and = are its keys, as Z and X are; C stays the loop's key ([taste.md](designs/step8/taste.md) §3.9).
- *Why:* a part played all at one velocity sounds like a machine.
- *Today:* built in track `recording` (`web/src/lib/piano.ts`). Tested by `e2e/rec-takes.mjs`.

## Taps

**In short:** a tap copies a signal at one point of the graph for the window to show (a meter, a scope), without changing what plays. Rules TAP-1 to TAP-9 (track `taps`, [Typed sounds](designs/cards/design.md) §5).

| Words | Meaning here |
|---|---|
| **point** | Where a tap reads: the song's output, a top-level node's first output, or an overlay site (a track where its audio enters another module, [MON-1](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)). |
| **peaks** | Each channel's largest \|x\| since the server last took them: a meter. |
| **frames** | A ring of the last frames a point gave: a scope, or the master's loudness. |
| **LUFS** | Loudness as ears hear it (ITU-R BS.1770): −14 LUFS is about what streaming services play songs at. |

**TAP-1.** A tap MUST be a point of a live plan whose block is read after the graph ran, into slots the server reads, and never by the graph. A tap gives peaks or frames. The meters, the master's loudness and a scope are taps: one mechanism, three uses. An agent's listen copies the same points of its own offline render ([AGENT-7](#agents)'s copy taps), by the same `TapAt`.
- *Why:* one way to look, so a new way to look is a use of it, not an engine change.
- *Today:* since track `taps`. A tap's point is a `TapAt` (`Out`, `Node`, `Site`; `core/runtime/src/taps.rs`). A live plan's `TapSlots` (`PlanInfo.taps`) hold its meters' peaks and its frame rings (`RINGS`, 5: one for the master's loudness, four for scopes); `Renderer::tap` lays out its meters, each the louder of its points channel by channel, and `TapSlots::point` points a ring. The audio thread writes them in the block's post-pass (`Taps::write`), after the output. Copy taps read a node's block through the same `side`. Tested by `a_tap_reports_its_blocks_peak` and `a_rings_frames_equal_the_nodes_output` (`core/runtime/src/tests/taps.rs`).

**TAP-2.** A tap MUST change no bit, and MUST NOT reach an export or any offline render. Only a plan prepared for the live engine has taps; an export, a bounce, a stem and a listen have none. With every tap on, every pure fixture plays the export's bits ([RENDER-33](#the-bit-identity-test)).
- *Why:* looking at a sound must never change it ([Typed sounds](designs/cards/design.md) §2.7).
- *Today:* since track `taps`. Only `Renderer::prepare` (the live engine's, `RenderMode::Realtime`) makes `TapSlots`; `Renderer::new`, `lending` and `starting_at` make none, and `tap` refuses them. Tested by `taps_change_no_bit` (a live plan with a meter at every site and every node with sound, every ring pointed, against an offline render) and `every_tap_on_changes_no_bit` (`core/play/tests/bit_identity.rs`: every pure fixture, the whole song through the player, its rings moved on to the next nodes before every device buffer, the meters taken and the rings read meanwhile).

**TAP-3.** The audio thread MUST NOT wait, lock or allocate for a tap ([RENDER-28](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). A plan's taps are laid out, and its rings allocated, off the audio thread before the plan is sent. Peaks are raised with `fetch_max` into atomic slots. A ring is written with atomics; the frames a block may overwrite are marked before it writes, and its head moves after, so a reader drops what was overwritten while it read, and a read that a new point overlapped returns nothing: what a read returns is always one point's frames, in order, whoever points the ring meanwhile. A read never starts past the ring's head. Turning taps on or off, and pointing a ring at another point, are atomic stores the audio thread takes at its next block: never a new plan, never a message on the queue.
- *Why:* a meter or a scope that glitched playback would be worse than none.
- *Today:* since track `taps`. A ring is `asked`, `took`, `since`, `started`, `head` and its frames, all atomics (`Ring` in `core/runtime/src/taps.rs`); the audio thread takes a new point at its block and copies from then on; it marks `started` and fences before it writes; a reader fences after it copied, drops the frames before `started − RING`, and returns nothing when `took` changed meanwhile (`TapSlots::frames`). A `from` past the head (a place in another ring) reads from where the point was taken. Tested by `a_ring_read_while_written_is_never_torn` (one thread writes 2^19 blocks as fast as it can, each frame the number of its place, while another reads the whole ring over and over: turning the reader's check off tore hundreds of reads in each of three runs, review P1), `a_read_from_past_the_head_starts_where_the_point_was_taken` and `a_ring_keeps_its_last_frames`, and under the allocation trap by `taps_and_the_load_never_allocate` (`core/play/tests/no_alloc.rs`), whose reader turns the meters off and on and points every ring at the output and at every node with sound in turn, reading their frames, while the callback runs.

**TAP-4.** A tap MUST cost nothing while no one looks. Looking is: a socket that listens for meters (a window's always does; an agent's when it asks, as `m2 watch --meters` does), for the meters' peaks; a client that asks for the song's loudness (the Mix view, while it shows), for the output's ring and its loudness; a client that asks for a scope, for its ring. Unwatched, a plan's taps cost one atomic load per block, and one per ring, and the server works out nothing for them.
- *Why:* most of the time nobody watches a scope, and an agent's app has no window at all: an `m2 watch` that drops the meters must not make the app work them out.
- *Today:* since track `taps`. A socket subscribes to the meters only when it listens (`ws` in `crates/server/src/routes.rs`: a page's socket, or `meters=1`, which `m2 watch --meters` sends); the server's `music2-meters` thread says each 33 ms whether any does (`Graph::watch_meters`, the `watch` channel's receivers), and turns the plan's peaks on or off; every new plan is given it. The output's ring and the scopes' rings point somewhere only while asked ([TAP-6](#taps), [TAP-8](#taps)). Unwatched, nothing is sent. Tested by `unwatched_taps_read_nothing` (`core/runtime/src/tests/taps.rs`), `unwatched_meters_send_nothing` and `the_loudness_runs_only_while_asked` (`crates/server/src/graph/meters_tests.rs`), `an_agents_socket_hears_meters_only_when_it_asks` (`crates/server/src/routes_8b_tests.rs`), `m2_watch_meters_asks_for_the_meters` (`apps/m2/tests/watch.rs`), and the browser suite `taps` (the Mix view gone, no loudness).

**TAP-5.** Meters MUST read after the fader. The server lays out each live plan's meters before it is sent. Each channel of the song's one std mixer is read at its fader (after its pan and gain), so a fader move moves it; a bus's channel too. Master's meter is the song's output, after the master's fader and ceiling. A track's meter is its channel's when the channel is its own. A track whose audio goes two ways (into two channels, into a channel it shares with another track, into no channel, or in a song without one std mixer whose channels its code writes) has no fader of its own: its meter reads where its audio enters the song, the louder of its ways, before any fader. Mute and solo act before the fader ([MON-1](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)), so a muted track's meters read silence.
- *Why:* a fader that doesn't move its meter looks broken (gaps #20). A shared channel's fader turns down more than one track, so one track's meter can't be read after it.
- *Today:* since track `taps`. `meters::layout` (`crates/server/src/graph/meters.rs`) finds each channel's fader as `m2 listen` does (`m2_editor::mix::fader`: the gain that reads `channels.<key>.db`), and each track's own channel (`Mixer::channel_of`, the channel's one track); the server lays out each plan's meters as it builds it (`build` in `crates/server/src/graph/actor.rs`). A layout the runtime refuses, a bug, falls back to every track before its fader, which can't fail, and the status says so in words (`meters` in `/api/graph/status`, "Meters before faders" in the status pill; review P8; `meters_that_cant_be_laid_out_fall_back_and_say_so`). The message's `tracks` are by track key, its `channels` by channel key; the Mix view's strip with a channel reads `channels`, the header `tracks` (`web/src/lib/meters.ts`). Tested by `a_fader_at_minus_12_db_moves_its_meter_by_12_db` (`core/runtime/src/tests/taps.rs`, a cell write on a fader), `a_fader_at_minus_12_db_moves_its_tracks_meter_by_12_db` (the server: the kick's channel and track move 12 dB, a shared channel moves and its two tracks don't), `muting_the_kick_floors_its_meter_and_the_bass_still_ducks`, and the browser suite `taps` (a fader dragged 22 dB down moves its channel's meter 22.0 dB down, and its strip's and header's meters with it).

**TAP-6.** While a client asks for the song's loudness (`POST /api/graph/loudness`: the Mix view while it shows, or an agent), the server MUST work out the master's short-term loudness (the last 3 s, in LUFS) and its true peak (the largest of the last 3 s, in dBTP) from the song's output frames, off the audio thread, at the song's rate, by the code `m2 listen` measures with ([AGENT-7](#agents)), and send them with the meters and in each answer. Under 3 s heard, under the gate (−70 LUFS), or while the engine idles, there is no loudness. An ask lasts as a scope's does ([TAP-8](#taps)). The Mix view's master strip says them in words ("−14 LUFS"). It matches `m2 listen`'s within 0.1 LU.
- *Why:* how loud a song is, is the first thing a mastering beginner asks, and two meters that disagree would teach them nothing.
- *Today:* since track `taps`. `m2_editor::listen::Meter::recent` keeps the last 3 s of BS.1770's 10 ms steps, and says `short_term` and `take_true_peak`, by the filters a listen uses. The loudness ask points the output's ring (`Asks` in `crates/server/src/graph/scope.rs`, one mechanism with the scopes); each 33 ms the server reads it into the meter from where it last read, keyed by the plan's id, which no later plan reuses (review P4), and makes the meter anew when the song's rate changes (review P5) (`Loud` in `crates/server/src/graph/meters.rs`); it keeps each read's true peak for 3 s. The message's `loudness` is `{"short", "true_peak"}`; Master's strip asks while it shows, every 4 s, and says "−9 LUFS" and "Peak −1 dB" (`useLoudness` in `web/src/lib/meters.ts`, `MasterLoudness` in `web/src/components/Mix.tsx`). Tested by `a_recent_meter_reads_the_last_3_seconds` (`crates/editor/src/listen_tests.rs`), `the_masters_loudness_matches_m2_listen` (a −20 dBFS sine, live against `m2_editor::listen::hear`), `the_loudness_runs_only_while_asked`, `the_loudness_meter_follows_the_songs_rate` and the browser suite `taps`.

**TAP-7.** An export of the song MUST measure its integrated loudness (BS.1770, gated, by the same code) and say it when done. While the song stays the revision that export rendered, the graph state carries it (`loudness`), and the Export dialog shows it. No other render measures it: only the export's is shown.
- *Why:* the loudness that matters is the file's; a number measured live, from wherever play started, would be another. Measuring costs about 2 ms a second of audio, which a bounce, a stem or a preview would pay for nothing.
- *Today:* since track `taps`. A `Render` made `measuring` (the export's, `crates/server/src/export.rs`) measures what the file holds (clipped at 0 dBFS unless it is float) with a listen's `Meter` (`Rendered::lufs`, `crates/editor/src/render.rs`); the export's `done` event carries `lufs`, and `Graph::exported` keeps it with the session and revision, which the state's `loudness` carries while they are the accepted ones. Tested by `a_render_says_its_files_loudness` (and that a render not asked measures nothing), `an_export_says_its_loudness_and_the_state_carries_it_until_the_song_changes` (`crates/server/src/export.rs`) and the browser suite `taps`.

**TAP-8.** A window or an agent MAY ask for a scope on a box of the graph view, by its id: the call's own output, at the top of its track (`POST /api/graph/scope`). While it asks, that box's last 1,024 frames go, about 30 times a second, to the socket of the window that asked, and to no other; an agent gets them in each answer to its ask. An ask belongs to its client: an agent's client is its own (`agent:` and its name), so it can neither stop nor keep a window's. A scope lasts while its client asks: it ends when the client says stop, when its window closes, or 10 s after its last ask (a window asks again every few seconds). A plan has 4 rings for scopes; a fifth is refused in words, as is a box that makes no sound or plays inside an instrument's voices.
- *Why:* a card's scope ([Typed sounds](designs/cards/design.md) §3.2's `view.scope()`) needs the sound as it plays, and only while it is shown; frames sent to every socket would cost each of them about 20 KB a message for nothing.
- *Today:* since track `taps`; a card's scope view asks for it since track `views` ([CARD-29](#views-on-the-card)). `POST /api/graph/scope {session, client, module, node, on}` (`crates/server/src/routes_8b.rs`, for agents too) answers `{scope: {module, node, channels, frames}}`; the box is found by `m2_editor::scope::point` (the view's id `file#ordinal`, the call's one output at the top of its module, through imports) in the accepted revision, on the session's actor, which points its ring in what plays; an ask for one asked already is kept on the control plane alone (`crates/server/src/graph/scope.rs`). Each window's frames ride with the meters as a message of its own, `{"t": "scopes", "scopes": [{module, node, frames}]}`, a list per channel, sent on its socket alone (`ws_loop` in `crates/server/src/routes.rs`). Tested by `a_scope_reads_a_calls_own_output` and `a_scope_is_refused_in_words_for_what_isnt_sound` (`crates/editor/src/scope_tests.rs`), `a_scopes_frames_arrive_while_asked` and `a_scope_is_refused_in_words_and_ends_with_its_client` (`crates/server/src/graph/scope_tests.rs`), `a_scopes_frames_go_only_to_the_window_that_asked` (an agent naming a window's client stops nothing of it, review P9), and the browser suite `taps`.

**TAP-9.** Each new plan MUST get the taps asked before it is sent: its meters laid out (TAP-5), watched if the meters are, the output's ring pointed if the loudness is asked for, and each scope pointed at its box. A scope's box is named by the shown view's id, always: the accepted revision's. It is its call, as [ID-13](#derived-identity) names a call: an edit that moves the call keeps the scope on it, under the id the new view gives it; one that deletes the call leaves the scope sending nothing, waiting for that call (an undo brings it back), and an ask by its old id is for whatever box has that id now. While what plays is built from other code than the accepted revision's (Compare's Before, or the version before a change that couldn't get ready to play), a scope sends no frames, and says why in words. The server reads the taps of the plan that plays, and only those.
- *Why:* an edit makes a new plan, and a meter or a scope must not go blank because of it, nor show another box under its name.
- *Today:* since track `taps`. Before a settle sends a new renderer, under the control plane's lock, its taps are watched as the meters are and every ask is followed into the accepted revision and pointed (`Asks::follow`, `Asks::point`), as its overlays are set; a settle that sends none does the same in what plays; a new song's first plan too, and its asks start empty. A call is followed by its `(` carried through the edits between the revision it was last found in and the accepted one (`m2_editor::scope::carry`, the correspondence `Edits::between` gives every carried range), and its new id is the call found there. A scope's message says `why` in place of `frames` while what plays isn't the song as shown. Tested by `a_call_is_carried_by_its_open_bracket` (`crates/editor/src/scope_tests.rs`), `a_new_plan_moves_the_taps`, `a_scope_follows_its_box_into_each_new_plan`, `a_scope_on_a_deleted_call_sends_nothing` (review P2: before, the next call in the file took its id and the scope showed it) and `while_what_plays_isnt_the_view_a_scope_waits_and_says_why` (review P3).

## PluginHost

```rust
trait PluginHost {
    // the host's main thread
    fn create(&self, id: &PluginId, state: Option<Blob>) -> Handle;
    fn activate(&self, h: Handle, sample_rate: u32, max_frames: u32);
    fn deactivate(&self, h: Handle);
    fn load_state(&self, h: Handle, blob: Arc<Blob>) -> bool;  // false: skipped, its hash equals the state the instance holds (HOME-6)
    fn set_render_mode(&self, h: Handle, mode: RenderMode);  // Realtime | Offline
    fn main_call(&self, h: Handle, call: MainCall);
    fn destroy(&self, h: Handle);
    // queued to the host's main thread; each result comes back as a message
    fn latency(&self, h: Handle);                            // -> Latency(h, frames)
    fn save_state(&self, h: Handle);                         // -> Saved(h, Blob)
    // the audio thread
    fn process(&self, h: Handle, io: &mut SharedBuffers, ev_in: &Events, ev_out: &mut Events, t: &Transport);
    fn reset(&self, h: Handle);                              // panic, and before an offline render: voices and tails cleared
    // any thread but the audio thread
    fn presets(&self, id: &PluginId) -> Vec<Offered>;        // the plugin's own presets (HOST-8)
}
```

As built in step 3b (`core/plugins/src/host.rs`), pending the owner's decision on the step 3b design's clarification C3, the signatures differ from the sketch:

```rust
trait PluginHost: Send + Sync {
    // the host's main thread; the caller waits (inline on the main thread itself)
    fn create(&self, plugin: &Found, state: Option<Arc<Blob>>, owner: &Owner) -> Result<(Handle, Info)>;  // init, state load, ports and params
    fn activate(&self, h: Handle, sample_rate: u32, max_frames: u32) -> Result<u32>;                  // -> latency, after the state load
    fn deactivate(&self, h: Handle);                                 // only while no renderer calls it, or every one that does is held (RENDER-1)
    fn load_state(&self, h: Handle, blob: Arc<Blob>) -> Result<bool>;  // skipped (false) when its hash equals the state the instance holds:
                                                                     // last saved or last loaded, creation included; none after a failed load (HOME-6)
    fn set_render_mode(&self, h: Handle, mode: RenderMode) -> Result<bool>;  // before activating; offline for every render that isn't live (RENDER-1)
    fn main_call(&self, h: Handle, call: MainCall) -> Result<Reply>;  // a parameter's value or text, its info, its log, its window,
                                                                     // one of its own presets loaded (LoadPreset, HOST-8)
    fn destroy(&self, h: Handle);                                    // queued: never waits
    // queued to the host's main thread; the one result comes back on the reply each returns
    fn latency(&self, h: Handle) -> Pending<Result<u32, String>>;
    fn save_state(&self, h: Handle) -> Pending<Result<Blob, String>>;
    // the audio thread (or an offline render's): never allocates, locks or waits
    fn process(&self, h: Handle, io: &mut SharedBuffers, ev_in: &Events, ev_out: &mut Events, t: &Transport) -> Status;
    fn reset(&self, h: Handle);
    fn take_process_request(&self, h: Handle) -> bool;               // CLAP's request_process: wakes an idle engine
    // any thread but the audio thread
    fn scan(&self) -> (Vec<Found>, Vec<String>);                     // every plugin installed: the host loads them, never the app (HOST-3)
    fn presets(&self, plugin: &Found) -> Result<Vec<Offered>>;       // its own presets, found where it runs (HOST-8)
    // any thread
    fn live(&self) -> u64;
    fn counts(&self) -> (u64, u64);                                  // created and destroyed so far (gates, tests)
}
```

- `create` returns the `Info` the checker types a plugin from, so describing a plugin is `create`, `activate`, `destroy`, with no second path around the host. It takes an `Owner`: where the instance's unsolicited events (`Restart`, `Dirty`, `Latency`, and `Crashed` or `Hung` when its host process ended) go, and the count of its live instances, so two sessions in one process never see each other's events.
- `latency` and `save_state` answer as one message on their own reply, which no other waiter can take.
- `Events` is the host's CLAP-shaped list, sized when built (`m2-plugins` can't depend on the runtime's `Event`); the runtime translates into it without allocating.

**HOST-1.** The engine MUST reach plugins only through `PluginHost`, and call each method only on the thread named beside it. Nothing may assume a plugin shares the app's process or threads.
- *Why:* plugins will move into a host process, and the threading model must not be built twice. CLAP needs `activate` with the rate and block size to apply a latency change, and calls `latency` and `save_state` on the main thread only.
- *Today:* matches since step 3b. The runtime's plugin nodes hold an `Arc<PluginInstance>` (a handle and its host) and call `process` and `reset` through the host; renderers create instances, `PackageSet::describe_plugins` finds (`scan`) and describes them and `m2 plugin-state` saves them through `PackageSet::host()`, which is the process's host unless set (`m2_plugins::host()`): since ph-1 the host process (`m2_plugins::Remote`, [HOST-3](#pluginhost)) in every program, the in-process host only where none was installed (tests, and inside the host process). `m2_plugins::Instance` is gone.

**HOST-2.** The in-process implementation (step 3) MUST make every main-thread call on the plugin main thread below, and process plugins on the audio thread. That plugin main thread runs the OS's main loop (`m2_os::main_loop`). On macOS, where plugins post work to the process's main run loop, it is the process's main thread: in the app AppKit's, reached through the main dispatch queue; in the CLI, the headless server and the gates, the main thread runs a `CFRunLoop`. On Linux, where plugins get main-thread time only from the host (CLAP's fd and timer extensions), it is the process's main thread in the CLI, the headless server and the gates, and in the app a loop thread of its own, since GTK owns the app's main thread. The host's thread checks answer truthfully, and `on_main_thread` runs on that thread, never inside `process`. The host process ([HOST-3](#pluginhost)) keeps the same rule inside itself.
- *Since ph-1:* plugins run in the host process, which is the program run again: there the in-process host makes every main-thread call on that process's own main loop, under `run_main`, as below. The app's own process runs no plugin code: its main loop (`use_app_main_thread`) still starts, and serves nothing (a gap: fold it away). What follows is what runs inside the host process.
- *Why:* plugins kept alive across rebuilds turn today's threading violations into crashes. Many commercial plugins (JUCE-based ones among them) post timers and work to the process's main run loop, so a separate "plugin main thread" would give them two main threads that race. The cost on macOS: until the host process ships, a slow plugin load blocks the app's main thread. Linux has no process-wide run loop for plugins to post to, so its app keeps plugins off GTK's thread.
- *Today:* matches since step 3b (`core/plugins/src/main_thread.rs`, `inprocess.rs`, `audio.rs`):
  - every main-thread call (create, init, state, activate, parameters, `on_main_thread`, deactivate, destroy) runs on the plugin main thread; callers on other threads wait for it, and `destroy` is queued. Libraries load on the calling thread, once per bundle, never on the main thread;
  - the app calls `m2_plugins::use_app_main_thread()` first thing in `main` (`plugin_host::install`, `apps/music2-desktop/src/main.rs`): AppKit's main thread through the main dispatch queue on macOS, a loop thread of its own on Linux (`tests/app_main_thread.rs` runs this hook on both). The app opens its launch project off the main thread, which must never wait on a thread that creates plugins. Its quit closes the project first (`Graph::close`), waiting up to 2 s while the main thread destroys the instances, and leaves without exit handlers when some are still alive (`apps/music2-desktop/src/actions.rs`). On Linux the check's app row runs the open inside the app, with the test plugins; on macOS neither has run inside the app, and quit's close has run in neither;
  - `m2`, the headless server example and the gates binary run under `m2_plugins::run_main`: the program on a worker with an 8 MiB stack, the main thread running the OS's main loop (a `CFRunLoop` serving the main queue on macOS, a job queue on Linux); on return it serves until every instance is destroyed (2 s at most), then refuses further work. Tests, where libtest owns the main thread, opt in to one dedicated thread instead (`m2_plugins::test_main_thread()`); nothing falls back silently: with none installed, creating a plugin is an error naming what to call. Using a dedicated thread in tests is the step 3b design's clarification C1, awaiting the owner's decision; `core/plugins/tests/main_thread.rs` (no libtest harness) runs the real one, with Surge XT when `CLAP_PATH` has it;
  - `is_main_thread` asks the executor (`m2_os::main_loop::Loop::is_current`: `pthread_main_np` for macOS's main thread, else the loop thread's token); `is_audio_thread` holds when the caller holds the instance's audio role, which `process` and `reset` take and the main thread takes only to `stop_processing` before deactivating. No thread-local is touched, so the audio thread never allocates for one;
  - a plugin's requests (`request_callback`, `request_restart`, `latency.changed`, `mark_dirty`, `params.rescan`) only raise flags; every 5 ms a tick thread has the main thread sweep them, so `on_main_thread` never runs inside `process`, and the rest goes to the instance's owner.
  - since 8a's live half a song with plugins renders all its partitions on the device's thread: workers never call a plugin (`render_block_on`; `a_song_with_a_plugin_runs_no_task_on_a_worker`).

**HOST-3.** The host process, before plugin windows ship, uses shared-memory audio and event rings, message-based main-thread calls and its own main loop for windows (`m2_os::main_loop`: AppKit's on macOS; on Linux a loop over the X connection, the plugins' fds and timers, serving CLAP's `posix-fd-support` and `timer-support`). When it crashes, the affected instances become silence with `plugin.crashed`, and the app keeps running.
- *Why:* one plugin crash must not take down the session.
- *Today:* matches since ph-1 (step 9); windows, fds and timers since ph-2: the main loop (`m2_os::main_loop::Loop::watch` and `timer`; Linux a `poll` loop, macOS `CFRunLoopTimer` and `CFFileDescriptor`, Windows designed) serves CLAP's `timer-support` and `posix-fd-support` to every plugin, and the host process opens plugin windows on it ([HOST-7](#pluginhost)). In short:

  | Part | How |
  |---|---|
  | The process | The program run again with `--m2-plugin-host` (`run_main` and `use_app_main_thread` serve when they see it; tests run `m2-plugin-host`). One per app process, all plugins in it. It starts at the first call that needs it (a song with no plugins starts none), and again after it ended. Inside it, the in-process host runs under `run_main` (HOST-2). It writes no core dump, so a crash ends it at once |
  | Main-thread calls | Framed binary messages on its standard input and output, with request ids (`core/plugins/src/remote/wire.rs`); a plugin that prints goes to standard error. One reader thread in the app routes each reply, and each plugin's own events to its `Owner`. Both sides must be one build (a stamp, checked at the start) |
  | Audio and events | One shared-memory slot per active instance (`m2_os::ipc::Shm`; `remote/slot.rs`): the call's audio, transport and events in CLAP's own layout, at most `MAX_CALL_EVENTS` (16,384) in (a node that could need more is refused at build, by name) and 256 out. A call is synchronous: copy in, ring, spin 20 µs, then sleep on the done word (`m2_os::ipc::wait`: a futex, `__ulock` shared on macOS). The host process has one audio thread per instance, asleep on its slot, asking for real time, and puts the one float mode back after each call ([RENDER-2a](#offline)). Nothing on the app's side allocates, locks or waits on anything but the call (RENDER-28) |
  | A crash | The pipe closes: every instance of that process is marked, its calls fail at once (`process` returns `Status::Error`, so the fail-live path of HOST-5 runs) and its owner hears `HostEvent::Crashed`. The actor reports each of its instances into the fault ring, `plugin.crashed`; the session tells them as one problem naming the plugins ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), and the next activation makes them again from their saved states, in a new process. The audio thread tells a crash from the plugin's own error by the host (`PluginHost::gone`), so a crash is never also `plugin.failed`. The app plays on |
  | A hang | Every wait has a limit. A live call that waits 500 ms (RENDER-28's stall time) makes a watchdog end the process; the same path follows, `HostEvent::Hung`, said as "stopped answering". So does an offline call, a main-thread call or an answer awaited (a latency, a save) that waits 30 s (`remote::LIMIT`; a scan, which loads every library, 2 minutes): a plugin stuck on the host's main loop holds up the session's actor, or an export, for that long at most, and the caller hears why (`a_main_thread_call_that_hangs_ends_its_process`, `an_offline_call_that_hangs_ends_its_process`). Quitting ends the process, so it ends any wait at once |
  | Finding plugins | `scan` is on the trait: `m2_plugins::scan` and `find`, `describe_plugin` and `m2 plugins` ask the host. The app never loads plugin code. Since track br the app's lists come from the same scan: the side browser's Plugins groups, the Device view's + and + Track ask `GET /api/plugins`, which scans on the first ask, off the async threads, and keeps the list for the app run until Look again (`POST /api/plugins/scan`; `crates/server/src/routes_plugins.rs`). Each plugin is an instrument, an effect, or both, by its CLAP features (`Found::is_instrument`, `is_effect`). Adding one is one Tx built in `crates/editor/src/plugins.rs` (the add routes take `clap:<id>`): the plugin is described through the host first, so one that isn't installed, or is of the wrong kind, is refused in words with nothing written |

  Tested by `the_plugin_list_comes_from_the_host_scan` (`crates/server/tests/plugin_list.rs`, a process of its own), `the_host_suite_passes_on_both_hosts`, `a_crash_marks_every_instance_of_its_process`, `a_hung_call_ends_its_process` (`core/plugins/tests/remote.rs`), `the_app_never_loads_plugin_code` (`no_plugin_code.rs`: no `.clap` mapped after Surge XT and Airwindows played), `a_host_crash_is_one_plugin_crashed_problem`, `a_hung_host_process_is_ended_and_said`, `an_export_borrows_instances_in_the_host_process` (`crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`), `renders_through_the_host_process_are_the_same_bits` (`core/play/tests/bit_identity.rs`), the allocation trap on the host process (`no_alloc.rs`), and the app's hook test (`apps/music2-desktop/tests/app_main_thread.rs`). On Linux a call's round trip adds about 11 µs at the median and 35 µs at the 99th percentile (information, [PLAT-1](#platforms)). Tests that read the probe's records keep the in-process host (`test_main_thread`). macOS's shared memory and wake are lint-checked only.

**HOST-4.** Plugin output events (parameter gestures) MUST come back through `ev_out`, to an override latch, a literal Tx or automation recording.
- *Today:* to a latch and a literal Tx since ph-2 (step 9); recording is a gap. `Events` reads CLAP's gesture begin and end beside parameter values. After each live call the plugin node reads what it output (`heard` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`, atomics only, no allocation): a gesture on a parameter its code sets holds or latches it in the instance's fixed override table (32 parameters); a value outside a gesture on one is set back and its id put on a fixed ring of 64 for the session; one on a parameter code doesn't set marks the instance changed. The session reads them at its tick (`crates/server/src/graph/plugin_window.rs`). An export's nodes read none.

**HOST-5.** Plugin instances persist across plans like every instance ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), by RuntimeId ([ID-15](#derived-identity)) and shape (id, version, channels). A state change is loaded into the instance ([RENDER-26b](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). An instance that asked for a restart is new at the next activation, created from the committed blob. So is one that failed live, which plays silence until then. Offline renders in the app share the live instances ([RENDER-1](#offline)); the CLI creates its own.
- *Today:* matches since step 7 (`continues` in `core/session/src/tx/continues.rs`, `Shape::Plugin` in `core/runtime/src/plan.rs`); since ph-1 a crash of the host process takes the same path for each of its instances, told as one `plugin.crashed` problem ([HOST-3](#pluginhost)): a keyed plugin follows its key, wherever its call is; an unkeyed one keeps its instance by its name, which a wrap keeps ([ID-13](#derived-identity)), and one given a key continues by its name too. The shape doesn't hold the state: a changed blob is loaded into the kept instance ([RENDER-26b](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), and a blob taken away makes it new. A restart request (`HostEvent::Restart`) puts the instance in the actor's `Open.restarts`, and the catch-up activates at once with it new. A plugin that fails live (a processing error, or no activate after an export) is marked failed on the audio thread (`PluginInstance::kill`): its node plays silence and never calls it, the renderer plays on, the node reports `plugin.failed` into the fault ring ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps); the export's give-back reports one that won't come back), and the session puts it in `Open.restarts` (`a_plugin_that_fails_live_goes_silent_alone` in `core/runtime/src/tests/live/keep/plugins.rs`, `a_plugin_that_fails_live_is_one_problem` in `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`). One whose new instance fails within 10 s too is left silent, listed while it is, until the song is opened again, so a plugin that fails at every call isn't made again every 300 ms (`Open.revived`, `a_plugin_that_keeps_failing_is_started_again_once` in `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`). Offline, a plugin's error, or an instance that died while the render played it, fails the render, strict or not: `plugin.failed`, or `plugin.crashed` when its host process ended ([RENDER-1](#offline)). Only an activation in the same engine keeps instances: a new player or a rate change creates every one. The app's export borrows the live instances while playback is held ([RENDER-1](#offline)); `m2 render`, `m2 bounce` and range renders create their own. The last plan holding an instance drops it off the audio thread, and its destruction is queued to the plugin main thread.

**HOST-6.** Plugins get the transport from the engine: the playing and loop flags, tempo, the meter from the arrangement, a transport event at the frame of every jump, and `steady_time` from the engine clock.
- *Today:* matches since step 3b. A plugin is called once per run of a block, so a call never spans a jump, and each call carries its run's transport: playing or not, the loop, the tempo, the meter and bar from the arrangement, and its song position; `steady_time` is the engine clock (`PluginNode::process` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`, `transport_event` in `core/plugins/src/audio.rs`). Tested by `plugins_are_told_the_transport` (`core/runtime/src/tests/live/keep/plugins.rs`), with the probe recording what each call was told: playing and stopped, the loop across a mid-block wrap (two calls, each told its own run), the tempo, a 7/8 meter and its bar, and `steady_time` against the engine clock. Since ph-3 (step 9) a plugin whose inputs arrive late ([RENDER-18](#latency)) is told the transport its offset earlier: its calls split where that earlier transport's runs do, each told its run's position, play state and the arrangement there (`Past::told` in `core/runtime/src/delay.rs`); before the engine started it stood at the first position, stopped (`lanes_notes_and_time_arrive_late_by_the_offset`).

**HOST-7.** Plugin windows, VST3 and AU MUST be built only behind `PluginHost`.
- *Today:* plugin windows since ph-2 (step 9): `MainCall::Window(WindowCall::Open { title } | Close)` answers `Reply::Window(open)`, and the person's close comes as `HostEvent::WindowClosed`. Inside the host, CLAP's `gui` is embedded (`is_api_supported`, `create`, `get_size`, `set_parent`, `show`; `hide`, `destroy`) in a window of `m2_os::window`: X11 through `libX11.so.6` opened at run time on Linux (a run without a screen never needs it; Wayland through XWayland only), an `NSWindow` through the Objective-C runtime on macOS (the host process an accessory app, its main loop pumping AppKit's events; lint-checked only), an `HWND` designed for Windows. A plugin's `request_resize` resizes it. `Info.gui` says whether a plugin has a window this OS can show. The app opens one from the plugin's box (`POST /api/graph/plugin/window {session, node, open}`; the graph state's `plugins` says `open`, `closed` or `none` for each node, by its name in the checked graph, which its view node gives as `plugin`); the windows float on their own (D12). A window is asked about only by the process that owns it: a `Native` handle (on macOS an `NSView` pointer into the host process) is never used in another process, so tests ask the host process, through the debug builds' `WindowCall::Shown` (`Reply::Shown`: the handle, to compare, and whether it is shown) and `WindowCall::AskToClose` (the person's close, made there); `m2_os::window::Window::shown` and `ask_to_close` answer for the caller's own window only, on every OS. On macOS a window's `closed` runs next on the main queue, outside AppKit's `windowShouldClose:`. Tested by `a_plugin_window_opens_on_the_host_process_main_loop` (`core/plugins/tests/window.rs`, on Xvfb; without a screen it says why) and `e2e/ph-window.mjs` (Surge XT's window from the lead's box). VST3 and AU are not built.

**HOST-8.** A plugin's presets MUST reach it only through `PluginHost`. Its own presets are found, and loaded, where the plugin runs: in the host process, never in the app. One of them is loaded on an instance made for it, never on the instance that plays: that one changes only when the load's Tx commits, through the usual state load ([RENDER-26b](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)).
- *Why:* a preset load runs plugin code, which may be slow or crash ([HOST-3](#pluginhost)). Loading into the instance that plays would change the sound before the Tx is checked, so a refused Tx or an Undo would no longer be exact.
- *Today:* matches since track br. In short:

  | Part | How |
  |---|---|
  | Finding them | `PluginHost::presets(&Found)` answers `Offered { name, location, key }`, where `location` is `PresetAt::Plugin` (inside it) or `PresetAt::File(path)`. CLAP's preset-discovery factory, on a thread of its own in the host process, never the main thread: the factory is thread-safe, and a long search must not hold up the main thread, which serves every playing plugin's calls and windows. Each provider's locations, a file location's folder walked for the file types it declared (2,000 files at most, `MAX_PRESET_FILES`). A preset that names other plugins is left out. Over the wire as `Req::Presets` (`core/plugins/src/presets.rs`) |
  | Loading one | An instance made with no state, then `MainCall::LoadPreset { location, key }` (CLAP's `preset-load`, answers `Reply::Loaded`), `save_state`, the values of the parameters its code writes (`MainCall::Value`), and destroy. The state and those values then load as a preset of yours does: one Tx ([FMT-22](#presets)) |
  | The app | The card's **Presets** has the plugin's own as a group named for it ("Surge XT's"), shown when it offers some. The list is kept per plugin for the app run (`crates/server/src/graph/presets.rs`) |

  Tested by `the_probe_offers_its_presets_on_both_hosts`, `a_preset_search_never_runs_on_the_main_thread` and `a_plugin_preset_loads_on_a_scratch_instance` (`core/plugins/tests/remote.rs`; the probe offers Bright and Dark), `the_plugins_own_preset_loads_on_a_scratch_instance` (`crates/server/src/graph/presets_tests.rs`) and `e2e/br-presets.mjs`. Surge XT on Linux, by hand: 1,974 presets listed in about 0.5 s, and one loads in about 0.1 s (information, [PLAT-1](#platforms)). A plugin with no discovery factory has no group; yours still work.

## Kernel ABI reservations

The detail of kernel ABI v1, byte by byte, is in `docs/kernel-abi.md`. These rules are what it must keep.

**ABI-1.** Kernel input memory is host-owned and read-only to kernels: `k_process` MUST NOT write it, and `k_reset` MUST NOT change it. The host places every port buffer, so no two overlap. Debug builds check input memory after each call and fail on a change.
- *Why:* cells are written once ([RENDER-22](#cells)).
- *Today:* matches since step 5. The Rust kit keeps it by construction (`std/kernels-src/kernel/src/lib.rs`). Since track ab the host places each state's port buffers with the module's own allocator (`m2_alloc`), so the overlap check at build went. Debug builds compare each kernel's input memory before and after every `k_process` and `k_reset` call (a copy and a compare rather than a hash: cheaper, and exact), and a change fails the render naming the kernel and the input (`KernelNode` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`); a test plays every std kernel through it (`every_std_kernel_keeps_its_inputs`).

**ABI-2.** Kernel ABI v1 is the contract with kernel code, frozen in step 9:
- a module says its version in its custom section `m2_abi` (4 bytes, a little-endian u32), and exports `memory` (defined, 32-bit, 64 KiB pages, not shared) and `m2_alloc(size, align)`; each kernel `k` exports `k_ports`, `k_new(cfg) -> state`, `k_reset(state)`, `k_process(state, io, from, to)` and `k_active(state)`. Every call names its state;
- the host writes a settings block before `k_new`: the rate, `max_block`, flags, the channel count per port, the kernel's values (numbers) and its assets (a handle, frames, channels and rate each). It starts with its own length, every list in it is an address and a count, and fields are only ever added at the end;
- the port table holds one buffer address per port channel, inputs first;
- the one import is `m2.asset_read(handle, channel, frame, dst, frames)`: an asset's decoded audio copied into the kernel's memory, from where the host holds it, its choice per sandbox: decoded whole and shared outside every sandbox (a live kernel's: no allocation, no wait), or read from its file (an offline kernel's, off the audio thread). Live, a frame the host has not loaded yet reads as silence and counts as a miss, with the same returned count, so live kernels may stream later within v1; never in an export or an offline render;
- offline kernels (`offline = true` in the manifest, `flags` bit 0): run by the host off the audio thread to make something from a file, never by song code (`kernel.offline`). No inputs; the source is asset 0, read from its file; a time map of (source seconds, output seconds) pairs; the output in `max_block` chunks, each to a sink as it is made;
- reserved, shapes fixed: event ports (`{count, cap, events}`, [RENDER-14](#events)'s `Event` in 24 bytes; inputs built since track events, [ABI-8](#kernel-abi-reservations), outputs not yet), `k_save`/`k_load` (off the audio thread) and `k_latency`/`k_tail` (fixed by the settings), not built.
- *Why:* the sampler, arpeggiators and third-party kernels all need these, and their shape must not change once kernels are written against it.
- *Today:* built since track ab: the kit and std (`std/kernels-src`), the sandbox and the import (`core/runtime/src/sandbox.rs`), settings in the graph (`assets` and `values` on a kernel node, `core/ir/src/lib.rs`), and `kernel(id, inputs, { assets, values })`, `setting.asset()` and `setting.keymap()` in `@m2/core` 1.3.0 (and `setting.regions()` in 1.6.0, [PKG-15](#component-descriptors)). Tested by `a_kernel_reads_its_asset_through_the_import`, `an_asset_read_allocates_nothing`, `a_kernel_takes_its_settings` and `asset_and_keymap_settings_are_checked`. The reserved shapes are in the Deferred table. The Rust kit reads them through `Cfg::values`, `Cfg::assets` and `Asset::read` (off wasm32, a stand-in keeps files for tests). Its first user is `std:sampler` (std 0.2.1, `std/kernels-src/src/sampler.rs`; `std:regions` beside it since std 0.5.0, [PKG-16](#component-descriptors)), and `examples/sampler` renders the same bits on 1, 2, 3, 4 and 7 threads (`the_sampler_example_gives_the_same_bits_on_1_to_7_threads`). The files live kernels read are decoded whole, once, into the audio cache's own map by content, kept while builds read them, so a live edit shares them with the renderer it replaces (`a_kernels_file_is_decoded_once_across_edits`); clips stream from disk ([RENDER-30](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), live kernels don't yet (gaps). Since track sx a sandbox may hold an asset as its file instead (`Held::Disk` in `sandbox.rs`), read through the lanes' WAV reader (`stream.rs`) with `m2_os::fs::read_at`, for offline kernels; it reads the same bits as a whole decode (`an_asset_read_from_disk_is_the_decoded_asset`). Offline kernels are built since track sx (`core/runtime/src/offline.rs`; `Cfg::offline` and `Cfg::time_map` in the kit), kept out of the kernels songs call (`song_code_cannot_call_an_offline_kernel`). The first is `std:stretch` (std 0.2.2; std 0.3.0 since the pm merge, `std/kernels-src/src/stretch.rs`), which stretches every warped clip ([RENDER-30](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps), `a_warped_clip_is_stretched_by_std_stretch`): in a live session on the session's stretch thread, in the background, while the clip plays what is made (`core/runtime/src/renders.rs`, `a_tempo_change_never_waits_for_a_stretch`); in an export before it plays (`an_export_never_plays_a_render_half_made`). The app's Follow tempo gives a clip its warp markers (the clip menu; `Gesture::Audio`'s `follow`).

**ABI-3.** A module's `m2_abi` section says 1. The runtime reads it from the module's bytes before it compiles the module, and refuses one built for another version, naming it, whatever else the module holds; a module with no section is refused as v0. Every later ABI keeps this section. Its only import may be `m2.asset_read`. A shape that changes, or a rule, makes v2; an optional export or a field at the end of the settings block keeps v1.
- *Today:* matches since track sx (`abi_version`, a hand parser, and `check_abi` in `core/runtime/src/packages.rs`, before `Module::new`): a v2 module that also imports `m2.other` and has a 64-bit memory is refused by its version (`a_later_abi_module_is_refused_by_its_version_first`); a module with no section is refused with "built for kernel ABI v0, or isn't a music2 kernel", even one that exports an `m2_abi()` (`a_module_with_no_abi_section_is_refused_as_v0`); std.wasm says v1 there and exports no `m2_abi()` (`std_wasm_says_v1_in_its_section`). Any other import is refused, naming it (`only_the_asset_import_is_allowed`).

**ABI-4.** The IR reserves a `const-events` op for events generated by code (`notes([...])`, `euclid()`, a seeded rng). The rule that build code never reads lane data stays.
- *Why:* owner decision 7: room now, built later.

**ABI-5.** Every kernel MUST keep five rules: (1) take memory only in `k_new`, since a sealed sandbox can't grow; (2) `k_reset` takes no memory and leaves the state as `k_new` (and later `k_load`) left it; (3) never write an input, and read the port addresses from `io` at each call without keeping them; (4) write every frame of `from..to` on every output channel, and touch only the channels a port has; (5) keep no per-state data in statics, let no static point at memory a state took, and keep all one `k_new` takes in one run of bytes. A kernel MAY keep **sandbox-wide data** in its module's static memory: data made only while a state is born and never changed after, the same for the same settings whatever order the sandbox's states are born in, which a state finds through its settings each time it needs it (never through an address it keeps), and holding no state's own bytes. So a state's run of bytes still means the same in any sandbox of its module whose states were born: a host that moves a state's bytes into another sandbox MUST first birth that sandbox's states (today no host moves a state's bytes).
- *Why:* many states share a sandbox, the audio thread never waits for memory ([RENDER-28](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), and a later layout may move a state's bytes without calling the kernel. Sandbox-wide data lets every voice of a sound share what each would otherwise make for itself (a pad's 16 voices hold one copy of its wavetables, not 16, [PACK-27](#packs)); found by settings, not by an address, it is there for a moved state whatever order the sandbox it moves into was born in.
- *Today:* the host enforces (1) and (2) by the seal and the prewarm (`a_sealed_sandbox_never_grows`, `every_std_kernel_resets_in_a_sealed_sandbox`), and debug builds check (3) ([ABI-1](#kernel-abi-reservations)). The kit keeps (4) and (5) for Rust kernels: its allocator moves only forward and works only in `k_new`, and a mono port's channel 1 is mapped for the kernel. Sandbox-wide data since track wavetable: `@m2/wavetable`'s shelf of tables ([PACK-27](#packs)), kept by each table's settings; a voice's bytes copied into a sandbox whose voices were born in another order play the same bits (`a_voice_moved_into_a_sandbox_born_in_another_order_plays_the_same_bits` in `core/runtime/src/tests/wavetable.rs`, as the review's own host showed them not to before).

**ABI-6.** Every call into a pack's kernel MUST run with a deadline, and a call still running at it is stopped: wasmtime's epoch interruption ([design cards §6.1](designs/cards/design.md), D6), whose checks at each function's start and each loop's turn trap once the engine's epoch reaches the deadline. std's kernels, and those of music2's own packs ([ABI-11](#kernel-abi-reservations)), music2's own code, shipped with the app, reviewed and tested, run with no checks and no deadline: the checks cost a kernel's call about a fifth of its time, which only a stranger's code should pay. So there are two engines, the same but for that one switch, and the package decides which compiles its module: std's and music2's own packs', or every other's (`packages::checked`, the one place, [ABI-11](#kernel-abi-reservations)). One thread, the ticker, moves the checked engine's epoch on every 10 ms, from that engine's start (the first pack loaded) for as long as it lives; each call into a pack's sandbox is given its deadline just before it starts. A call is never stopped before its limit, and is stopped within a tick after it while the ticker keeps time (a ticker that runs late only stops it later). The limits:

| Calls | Limit | Why this long |
|---|---|---|
| **Live**: those the live engine makes into a pack's sandboxes of a plan prepared for it, once each is born and prewarmed (`k_process`, `k_reset`, `k_active`) | 100 ms | A block is 128 frames, 2.7 ms at 48 kHz, and every kernel of the song shares it, so a working kernel's call takes microseconds. 100 ms is about 37 blocks: on one core with 24 busy threads beside the engine's thread, its longest block took 78 ms and no sound was stopped; with 40, most were (the review's T8). The live engine's threads run at real-time priority where the OS gives it (RENDER-28a), which no busy thread takes the core from. And it is short: the song is heard again within a tenth of a second |
| **Offline**: every other call: a sandbox's birth (its module's instantiation, `m2_alloc`, `k_ports`, `k_new`) and its prewarm, live too; every call of an offline render (an export, `m2 render`, a stem, a bounce, ⌘F's previews, `m2 listen`); a pack's offline kernel's | 5 s | Nothing waits for a device, so the limit is generous: thousands of times what any kernel's call takes, and room for a render at background priority (⌘F's previews, an export's workers) that a busy computer gives the CPU late. It bounds what an edit or an export waits for a sound that never returns |

- Each sound stopped holds its thread at least the live limit. Stuck sounds in one callback on one thread add up: ten or more hold it past the second after which the server lets a player that doesn't call back go ([RENDER-28](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). Then the song stops ("Playback stopped: the audio device stopped responding. Press Play to go on."), a new player loads it where it was, and its next plan's new boxes get stuck again when it plays. Fewer, or spread over engine threads, cost one long callback, and the song plays on.
- The time the host spends reading a file for a kernel (`m2.asset_read` from its file, held as disk) isn't the kernel's: its deadline moves on by the ticks the read took.
- What the checks cost, timed with `kernel_call_cost` (by hand: `cargo test -p m2-runtime --lib kernel_call_cost -- --ignored --nocapture`): one `k_process` call of each of std's 36 kernels (a block, stereo, inputs at their defaults), its best of 7 rounds of 3,000 calls, as std's and as a pack's (std's own module under a pack's name); the sum of each kernel's best over 6 rounds, in turns with the commit before ABI-6 (`239f03f`) on one pinned core (Linux, Ryzen 9 7950X3D, a busy machine), 2026-10-31: before ABI-6 59.3 µs, std's now 59.0 µs (−0.5 %, noise: std costs what it did), the same kernels as a pack's 71.2 µs (+20.6 %; from +1.6 % for `noise` to +112 % for `fm`, the most in small kernels with tight loops). The review's own bench measured +16.0 % and +24.0 % for every kernel checked (T1). G7f and G12 play std's songs, which this leaves as they were.
- *Why:* a kernel is other people's code, from packs; one that never returns must not hang playback, an export, a preview or the app (gaps #39). std's is music2's own, tested as the app is, and the checks' cost on every call of every song is not worth paying for it. Epochs, not fuel ([design cards §9](designs/cards/design.md)): exactness isn't needed to stop a hang, and fuel costs more.
- *Today:* built in track `limits` (`core/runtime/src/limit.rs`; the engines and `checked` in `core/runtime/src/packages.rs`, the calls in `core/runtime/src/sandbox.rs`, a sandbox's limits chosen by its plan's mode in `Builder::deadline`, its plan's limit taken after the prewarm, `Sandbox::play`). The limits are `Limits`, which only `m2-runtime`'s own tests shorten (`PackageSet::with_limits`, test builds only). Only a pack's own kernels are checked: the same module, every call past the limit, renders as std's and is stopped as a pack's (`only_a_packs_own_kernels_are_checked`), and each set compiles std with std's one engine and a pack with the packs' (`every_set_shares_one_engine`). A never-returning kernel live is stopped no sooner than its limit and its other track plays on bit for bit, on one thread and on three, with no heap operation in the blocks before and after (`a_kernel_that_never_returns_live_is_stopped_alone`; every call is given its deadline under `rendering_live_allocates_nothing` and `core/play/tests/no_alloc.rs` too); ten stuck in one callback hold it a second or more, and the engine plays on (`ten_sounds_stuck_in_one_callback_hold_it_a_second`); an offline render past the limit fails, no sooner (`an_offline_render_past_its_limit_fails_in_time`); a kernel stuck in `k_new`, and a module whose start never ends, are refused at the build (`a_kernel_stuck_at_its_birth_is_refused`, `a_module_that_never_starts_is_refused`); a first reset slower than the live limit builds live (`a_slow_first_reset_builds_live_too`); a kernel slow across many ticks but within its limit plays live the bits it renders (`a_slow_kernel_within_its_limit_changes_no_bit`); a slow disk read isn't counted (`a_slow_file_read_is_not_the_kernels_time`). `a_deadline_is_never_short_of_its_limit` checks the ticks' sums; the timing, never before the limit, is checked by those tests' lower bounds. Every pure fixture still renders `e2e/renders.sha256`'s bits ([RENDER-33](#the-bit-identity-test)). Since track packcode a pack music2 ships runs unchecked too ([ABI-11](#kernel-abi-reservations)).

**ABI-7.** A call into a pack's kernel of a sound (a node of the song's graph) stopped by its deadline ([ABI-6](#kernel-abi-reservations)) MUST be told as `sound.too-slow`, in words that name the sound as the song's code wrote it, its track, and whose code it runs:
- made inside a package's component: the outermost such call, by the component's label, and its package: "Growl on Bass, from @alice/growl, took too long and was stopped."; when the pack code it runs is another package's, that one too: "…, from @bob/synth, with sound code from @alice/growl, …";
- a kernel the song's own code calls: the kernel by its name, and its package: "The growl on Bass, from @alice/growl, …";
- an instrument the song's own code writes (no package's component around it): as the song's own, with the packages whose pack code its voices run: "The instrument on Keys, from the song's own code, with sound code from @alice/growl, …".

The words say only that it took too long: a thread the computer starved for that long is told the same way (ABI-6's margin), so they don't blame the pack's author for the machine. An offline kernel past its limit fails what it was making, as that thing's own failure (a stretch: `stretch.failed`, [RENDER-30](#audio-lanes)); only std's, with no limit, is run today.
- **Live**, its box (a kernel outside an instrument, or the instrument whose voice it is in) is silent from that block on, and its sandbox is never called again in that plan: Stop All Sound leaves it silent, and it stays listed ("It stays silent until the song is built again"). The next plan, made by the next change to the song's graph (not a value's, which only writes a cell), resets it as it moves it ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)): there it is no longer stuck, so it plays, and Stop All Sound resets it, unless it gets stuck again. The audio thread notices it and reports it through the fault ring, and waits for nothing; the trap's error allocates, [RENDER-28](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)'s one exception.
- **Offline**, the render fails with the same words, "so the file wasn't made" ([RENDER-1](#offline)): never a silent, different file. A preview fails the same way, and the registry's robot refuses a version whose preview a kernel's limit stopped ([REG-5](#registry)).
- **At a sandbox's birth** (or its prewarm), the plan isn't made. The box is remembered by its shape ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)'s: the kernel, its module's hash and its settings; an instrument's voices and modules) for as long as the session's packages: a kernel is deterministic, so the next plan that would make it again, live or offline, is refused at once in the same words, never waiting the limit again; a rebuilt pack's module is another shape. Live, the version before plays on, and the app says which sound took too long to get ready, once, whichever later change meets it again, until a change builds; its catch-up doesn't try that revision again. Offline, the render fails with the same words. No toast holds a node's id or a rule's.
- *Why:* a sound that hung once will likely hang again, and each try costs a dropout, or the whole limit on the app's one actor; Stop All Sound must always be safe. The musician needs to know which sound, and whose code: the pack's author can fix it.
- *Today:* built in track `limits`. A stopped call becomes `TooSlow` (`Sandbox::ran_out`, which already named a call over its memory), its fault `Code::TooSlow`, and marks its sandbox stuck, which a panic skips; an instrument whose voice got stuck stops whole, the same; the next plan plays it again, and there a panic resets it (`stop_all_sound_never_calls_a_kernel_that_ran_out_of_time`). A birth's stuck box is remembered in the package set (`limit::Stuck`, `Builder::unstuck`; `a_box_stuck_at_its_birth_is_refused_at_once_after`). The words are `Namer::slow_sound`'s (`crates/editor/src/faults.rs`), from the node's frames, the build's descriptors and each package's `checked` as the build loaded it (its view's, so music2's own packs are never named as sound code: `a_song_written_instrument_too_slow_names_no_code_of_music2s_own_packs`, since track packcode): `a_sound_too_slow_is_named_with_its_pack_and_stays_through_stop_all_sound` ("Delay on Bass, from std, took too long and was stopped. It stays silent until the song is built again. The rest of the song plays on."), `a_song_written_instrument_too_slow_names_the_pack_whose_code_it_runs`. A birth's: `Namer::faulted` and `too_slow` in `crates/server/src/graph/actor.rs`, whose `slow_build` keeps the catch-up off that revision and `told_slow` says it once (`a_sound_too_slow_to_get_ready_is_said_once_and_not_tried_again`, with a pack whose `k_new` never returns, then two later edits, of code and of data, each refused in under a second and not said again). ⌘F's preview of it fails in its words in about 5 s (`a_preview_of_a_sound_too_slow_stops_and_says_so`), and the robot refuses it in the limit's words (`a_kernel_that_never_returns_is_refused_in_its_limits_words`).

**ABI-8.** A kernel MAY take notes: an input of kind `events<note>` in its manifest. Each block the host gives it the events of the notes lane connected to it (through its note delay when it is late, [RENDER-18](#latency)), in [ABI-2](#kernel-abi-reservations)'s reserved shape: the port's one entry in the port table is the address of a header `{count: u32, cap: u32, events: address}`, and `count` events of 24 bytes follow at `events`, [RENDER-14](#events)'s `Event` each, stamped with its frame in the block (0 to 127, never counted from `from`), in RENDER-14's order: at one frame the controllers, then Offs, then Ons. They are the lane's notes, live ones included, and its controllers from live input (the wheels, the pedal). The host places the header and its events at birth, in the state's own block, and they are input memory, read-only ([ABI-1](#kernel-abi-reservations)). `cap` is all its lane's buffer can bring in a block (812 at its smallest; twice its lane's through a note delay, which holds two blocks' notes), so no event is ever left out. Its lane, how late it comes (its note delay's length) and its `cap` are part of the kernel's shape ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)): rewired to another lane, late by another delay or none, or its lane grown past its room by more notes, it is new, and the one it replaces plays out the window reading no notes ([RENDER-26a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)); its notes never crossfade. A kernel that takes notes runs at a module's top level, on whole blocks (`from` 0, `to` 128), so an event's frame is also its index in the call's other inputs. An events input must be connected, and straight to a notes lane at a module's top level: inside a `poly` voice a kernel gets its note from the note fields, and the checker refuses one there in words. An output of kind `events` stays reserved.
- *Why:* a kernel that hears notes (a counter of notes, a drum machine with voices of its own) needs the notes themselves, not one voice's fields. It keeps kernel ABI v1 ([ABI-3](#kernel-abi-reservations)): its shape was fixed among v1's reservations, and building it changes nothing a module without an events input sees, so every v1 module plays as before. Room sized at birth from its lane's own keeps the audio thread from waiting for memory ([RENDER-28](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)) and drops nothing, so every note a kernel heard start, it hears end; and a kernel that heard one lane's notes start never hears another's end in their place.
- *Today:* since track events: `KernelSig::parse` takes `events<note>` inputs and still refuses events outputs (`a_kernel_takes_events_but_never_gives_them`); the checker (`a_kernel_takes_notes_straight_from_a_lane_outside_voices`, `core/ir/src/check.rs`); the host places each events input with `sandbox::Spec::notes`, its room `events_room` of its lane's caps, and fills it before each call (`give`, `Event::words`, `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`); `Shape::Kernel`'s `notes` and `keep::edges`, which leaves a kernel's notes out of what fades. Debug builds compare the whole region after each call. Tested in `core/runtime/src/tests/events.rs`: `a_kernel_reads_its_lanes_events` (count, cap, frames, order and values, from a hand-written kernel), `a_kernel_hears_the_lanes_wheels`, `a_kernel_that_writes_its_events_fails`, `every_event_of_a_crowded_lane_reaches_the_kernel`, `a_kernel_rewired_to_another_lane_is_new`, `a_kernel_whose_notes_come_late_or_on_time_is_new` and `a_kernel_whose_lane_outgrows_its_room_is_new`. No std kernel takes events yet: the first use the design gave them, a turn for each key, is a note field instead ([RENDER-39](#voices)).

**ABI-9.** The Rust kit reads events safely. A kernel names its events inputs in `Kernel::NOTES`, and `Io::events(i)` gives input `i`'s events of this call, in order, as RENDER-14's `Event`, each frame counted from the call's first frame (frames `from..to` of the block, as the ABI lets a host call; music2 calls from 0), so it indexes the other inputs as they are; asking for an input `NOTES` doesn't name panics, which traps. The events are lent as shared data: a kernel written with the kit can't write them. An event of a kind the kit doesn't know is skipped. Off wasm32, `Host::events` gives a test's kernel its events as a host lays them out.
- *Why:* one place reads the bytes, so every Rust kernel reads them the same way, and a frame always means the same as an input's index.
- *Today:* since track events: `Event`, `RawEvent`, `Notes`, `Events`, `Io::events` and `Host::events` in `std/kernels-src/kernel/src/lib.rs` (`a_kernel_reads_its_events_through_the_kit`, `an_event_is_24_bytes_as_the_host_writes_it`, `events_of_another_input_are_refused`).

**ABI-10.** Every kernel module music2 ships MUST be built from its source in the repo, and the committed module MUST be its source's build, byte for byte. That is std's (`std/kernels/std.wasm`) and each of music2's own packs' that has kernels of its own ([PACK-21](#packs)); a bundled pack whose `m2pkg.toml` names a `wasm` that no source builds is refused.
