The kernels are one Cargo workspace, `std/kernels-src`, with one `Cargo.lock`, one release profile and one `.cargo/config.toml` (the kernels' 64 KiB stack, [RENDER-32](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)): the kit (`kernel/`), the blocks more than one module is made of (`dsp/`, `m2-dsp`: the effects' helpers, the voices' blocks, the drum kit's map, and the tests' harness behind its feature `testing`), std's crate, and a crate for each pack, `packs/<owner>/<name>/Cargo.toml`. A pack's crate's source is beside the pack, its `kernels-src/` ([PACK-20](#packs)), named by `[lib] path`; its manifest is in the workspace's folder, because cargo derives a crate's `-C metadata` from its manifest's place, by a path relative to the workspace only for a crate inside it, and that hash orders the module's functions, so a manifest outside would make bytes that hang on the folder the repo is in. So every crate and every file reaches the build relative to the workspace, and nothing overrides the config's flags. One script builds them all, `std/build.sh`: one `cargo build --locked --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` of the workspace with the toolchain `rust-toolchain.toml` pins, each crate's module copied to the file its package's `m2pkg.toml` names (`wasm`, [PACK-2](#packs)). `std/build.sh --check` builds a copy of the sources in a temporary folder and fails, naming the file, when a module differs from the committed one or holds a machine's path; a pack's crate whose pack names no `wasm`, has no `kernels-src/` or builds from elsewhere fails it too, and so does a bundled module no crate builds. CI's quick tier runs it, and the kernels' tests and lints run over the whole workspace, so a pack's kernels are tested as std's.
- *Why:* a module no one can rebuild can't be reviewed, and ABI-11 trusts these. The owner keeps the core small and puts most things in packs ([design cards §7.1](designs/cards/design.md)), so a sound's own kernel lives with its pack, built the one way std's is: one script, one workspace, one check. The shared blocks are one crate, so a kernel that moves from std into a pack takes only its own code, and plays the same bits.
- *Today:* since track packcode: `std/build.sh`; `m2-dsp` (`std/kernels-src/dsp`); `@m2/effects`' crate `m2-effects-kernels` (`std/kernels-src/packs/@m2/effects/Cargo.toml`, its source `packs/@m2/effects/kernels-src`, the bitcrusher, [PACK-21](#packs)), whose 11 tests call the harness std's effects are held to (`m2_dsp::testing::effects`; `cargo test --workspace` in `std/kernels-src`, CI's "std kernels" row). Since wave 3 `@m2/synths` and `@m2/drums` have crates of their own too (`m2-synths-kernels`, `m2-drums-kernels`, `std/kernels-src/packs/@m2/synths` and `drums`), and `@m2/effects`' holds its delay, reverb, phaser, tremolo and widener, each with the tests it had in std. The check's own cases, `ci/wasm-test.sh` (CI's "wasm cases" row): a pack's committed module changed, a pack's source changed, std's source changed, a pack's crate whose pack names no module, a bundled module no crate builds; each fails `--check`, naming the file. It also builds the workspace in two folders of other names and compares every crate's `-C metadata` (the review's X2: with the pack's manifest beside the pack, its crate's differed, and one module in three rebuilt to other bytes). `every_module_music2_ships_has_the_kernels_stack` (`core/runtime/src/tests.rs`) reads each committed module's stack pointer against the config's size (the review's X1: a build that set its own flags dropped it, and every sandbox held 1 MiB more). Before, `std/build.sh --check` saw std.wasm alone.

**ABI-11.** A pack's kernels run as std's, with no time checks and no deadline ([ABI-6](#kernel-abi-reservations)), exactly when the pack is music2's own ([PACK-19](#packs)). One function decides, `packages::checked(name, version, hash)`: not std, and not a version `m2_song::pack::own` names. A loaded pack's hash is its folder's listing's (`m2pack.json`, [PACK-6](#packs)), and only when that listing names the very `m2pkg.toml` and wasm module the runtime read and compiled, byte for byte; a folder with no listing (a pack in a folder, [PACK-7](#packs)), or whose listing names other bytes for either, has none, and is checked. So a stranger's pack, a pack named `@m2/…` whose files aren't a version music2 ships, and a changed copy of music2's own (another module or manifest under its listing, or listed again) all run with the limit; the same files in another folder are the same code, and run without. The answer is kept with the package as it loads (`Package::checked`), and everything that asks reads it: the engine that compiles its module, and the words of `sound.too-slow` ([ABI-7](#kernel-abi-reservations)). std's own trust is the host's: std is the folder a host loads as std (`PackageSet::load`); a pack is loaded by the name its caller found (`PackageSet::load_pack`, and a song's view), and a folder whose `m2pkg.toml` then says `std`, or another package, is refused before anything of it is compiled or run.
- *Why:* music2's own packs are its own code, shipped with it, reviewed, tested and built from source in the repo ([ABI-10](#kernel-abi-reservations)): as with std's, the checks' cost isn't worth paying on every call of every song. Trust follows the bytes that run, never a name anyone can write.
- *Today:* since track packcode (`checked`, `listed` and `Package::checked` in `core/runtime/src/packages.rs`): `a_folder_that_says_it_is_std_is_refused_before_its_code_runs` (its module's start, which traps, never runs: through a song's view and `load_pack`, which `m2 pack check` and a preview use; loaded by the host as std, it runs; the review's X7); `music2s_own_pack_runs_unchecked_and_a_changed_copy_is_checked` (installed, and copied whole, unchecked; a changed module, a changed `m2pkg.toml`, a copy listed again and the source tree's folder, checked; each compiled as it says). What it saves, by `kernel_call_cost` (a block's `k_process`, stereo, the best of 7 rounds of 3,000 calls) on one pinned core of ABI-6's machine, 2026-10-31, three runs each: `@m2/effects:bitcrush` as music2's own, installed and unchecked, 1,217 to 1,226 ns a call, as `std:bitcrush` cost as std's before the move (1,198 to 1,210 ns, `3be7bc3`, earlier that day); the same pack as a stranger's copy, checked, 1,497 to 1,513 ns (+23 %). std's own kernels cost what they did (their sum 54.8 to 55.0 µs). Retaken after the review's X1 gave the modules back their 64 KiB stack.

## Library

What you can add to a song, found in one place: ⌘F's panel, the side browser's search and `m2 search` ask the same list with the same search. Built by track `spotlight` (2026-10-30), slice 3 of the packs design (`docs/designs/packs/design.md`, its §9.2 and §10).

**LIB-1.** The library MUST be one list of **items**, gathered in one place from its sources, and searched by one function in Rust that every search in music2 calls. An item has a kind (`instrument`, `effect`, `sound`, `sample` or `plugin`), an id, a label, a category, a description, tags and a package (`std`, `song` for the song's own components, `yours`, or `plugin`); a sample also has its type (`loop` or `one-shot`), its tempo, its key and its length.
- Sources: the components a build offers with a category ([PKG-7](#component-descriptors)): std's, the packs the lists offer ([PACK-11](#packs): the open song's, and the newest runnable version of every other pack in the store, with or without a song open) and the song's own code; every preset, std's, each such pack's and yours, as a **sound** each, with its sound's category ([FMT-22](#presets)); the **samples** of std, of each such pack and of your sample folders ([LIB-4](#library)); and the installed CLAP plugins the app has listed already. A search never scans for plugins, and never waits for a scan: a scan loads every plugin's library ([AGENT-11](#agents)) and can take minutes, so a search meanwhile uses the list from before it, or none. A new kind of item is one more source in that one place.
- What is typed is split into words as an item's text is: at every character that is not a letter or a digit, so `hi-hat` is `hi hat` and `synth.pluck` is `synth pluck`. Every word must match, case-insensitively: the start of a word of the label, the label itself (inside it), the start of a tag, or the start of a word of the description. An item's whole id typed (`@m2/effects:reverb`) finds it first.
- Some words are filters. A kind (`instrument`, `effects`, `fx`, `presets`…), `loop` and `one-shot` find the items of that kind or type, and also the items whose label has that word, so a name with one in it (`Surge XT Effects`, a `Loop…` instrument) is still found by it. A plugin is an instrument or an effect too, by what it plays: `effect` finds the effect plugins. A tempo (`90 bpm`, `90bpm`) and a key written with a capital (`Am`, `F#`, `Bbm`, `C major`) are filters only a sample can meet: a tempo finds the samples within 2 bpm of it, and a key the samples in that key or its relative (`Am` finds `C` too, and `C` finds `Am`). **Fits my song** is clicked, not typed: LIB-2's route with `fits=<beat>` (the playhead's) keeps only loops within 8% of the open song's tempo at that beat and, when the song has a key (`layout.json`'s `key`), in that key or its relative; a loop with no key (drums) fits any key.
- Ranked: the whole query is the label (word for word, its punctuation aside), then the label starts with it, then by each item's weakest word: a word of the label, inside the label, a tag, the description. Then std's and music2's own (a pack version music2 comes with, [PACK-19](#packs)) before yours and the song's, and those before plugins; then by kind (instruments, sounds, effects, samples, plugins); then by label, then id. The same list and query give the same order every time.
- A page shows what it found for the words in its field now: an answer to older words is dropped, and Enter waits for the answer to the words typed, never acting on rows of an older search. The side browser filters its own rows as you type until that answer comes.
- *Why:* two searches drift apart, and a musician learns one. Searching on this computer, over what it has, is instant and sends nowhere what is typed (the packs design §9.2).
- *Today:* since track `spotlight`: `crates/editor/src/library.rs` (`items`, `search`, `Query::parse`), tested in `library_tests.rs` (`what_is_typed_is_split_into_words_as_an_items_text_is`, `a_kind_word_finds_that_kind_and_names_with_it`, and one test for each rule above). The plugin list is kept apart from the scan's lock (`crates/server/src/lib.rs`, `a_search_never_waits_for_a_plugin_scan`). ⌘F's panel (`web/src/components/Spotlight.tsx`) and the side browser's field ask it through LIB-2's route; `e2e/spotlight.mjs` checks that Enter right after typing adds the newest words' best hit, in both. The + Track list and the Device view's + still search in the window (gaps). Since track `samples` the packs of the store are sources with no song open too, and samples are (LIB-4 to LIB-9); the filters' tolerances are tested in `library_tests.rs` (`a_tempo_finds_within_two_bpm_and_a_key_its_relative`, `fits_my_song_keeps_loops_near_the_songs_tempo_in_its_key`) and through the route (`crates/server/src/routes_library_tests.rs`, `the_samples_of_std_and_a_pack_are_found_and_fit_the_song`).

**LIB-2.** `GET /api/library?q=<words>` MUST answer the items the search finds, ranked, to the page and to agents ([AGENT-8](#agents)). `m2 search <words…> [--kind <kind>] [--json]` asks it when the app runs; with the app closed it gathers the same list itself from std, the store's packs, your presets and your sample folders (no plugins), and searches it with the same function.
- *Why:* an agent finds a sound the way the musician does, with or without the app.
- *Today:* since track `spotlight`: `crates/server/src/routes_library.rs` (`the_library_route_answers_the_one_search`); `m2 search` in `apps/m2/src/search.rs`, through the app with the tools key or by itself (`apps/m2/tests/search.rs`; a store pack's items since track `samples`, `an_installed_packs_items_are_found_with_the_app_closed` in `apps/m2/tests/pack.rs`). The server reads your presets and keeps previews in the folders `AppState::keep_library` gives it: the app and the headless server give the user's, tests scratch ones.

**LIB-3.** A preview (`GET /api/library/preview?id=<id>`) MUST be an offline render by music2's own engine ([RENDER-1](#offline)) of a small song written for it in the cache folder (`m2_os::dirs::cache`, [PLAT-7](#platforms)), never in a project and never through the live engine. It plays a phrase chosen by the item's category: a beat for drums (on the notes `editor.notes` names, else 36, 38 and 42), a bass line for a bass, a line for a lead, four chords for the rest; a sound plays its instrument's phrase with its preset's values; an effect plays a fixed loop of a pluck and a drum kit, the first bar without the effect and the second through it.
- It loads std and the item's own package, as the open song's build found it, so a pack's instrument plays from its pack.
- About 4.5 seconds, a 16-bit stereo WAV at 48 kHz, its last quarter second faded out. Every preview is brought to one loudness, measured as ears hear it: −23 LUFS (ITU-R BS.1770's integrated loudness: K-weighted, gated), or less where that would put a peak above −1 dBFS. A few sounds of short, sharp hits can't reach it: std's Hats and Rim play up to 2 dB quieter.
- A preview that makes no sound is none, said in words, and is never kept. An instrument that plays a file you give it (one with an asset setting, as the Sampler) has no preview, said in words.
- Kept in the cache folder by the item's id and the hash of what it renders (the song's files, every file of the packages it loads, and music2's version and its SDK's), so a changed preset, package or music2 renders anew, and a second ask renders nothing. Only one render of an item runs at a time: a second ask waits for it, and an answer is read before the next render of that item can replace it. A part file of a render that failed is removed. A pure render gives the same bits every time ([RENDER-2](#offline)).
- A render runs on a thread of its own at background priority, as an export's workers do beside playback ([AGENT-7](#agents)): off the audio thread and the session's.
- A plugin, a plugin's preset and the song's own components have no preview, said in words.
- *Why:* a preview must be the item playing, so it can't oversell (the packs design §10), and it must never cost the song that plays a glitch.
- *Today:* since track `spotlight`: `crates/editor/src/library/preview.rs` (the song, the render and the loudness; `a_preview_is_the_same_bits_twice_at_one_loudness`, `every_std_item_with_a_preview_renders_one`, `loudness_is_measured_as_bs_1770_does`, `a_preview_renders_on_a_thread_of_its_own`), `preview::of`, the song and the packages of an item's preview, which the registry's robot takes too since track `registry` ([REG-6](#registry)), the cache in `crates/server/src/library.rs` (`a_preview_is_kept_until_what_it_renders_changes`, `a_second_ask_waits_for_the_first_render`, `a_packs_instrument_plays_from_its_pack`). No check yet measures dropouts while previews render beside playback, as G13 does for `m2 listen` (gaps).

**LIB-4.** A **sample** is a WAV or FLAC file ([FMT-24](#assets-and-hashes)) the library lists as an item of kind `sample`: a file under a package's `samples/` folder (std's, and each pack's the lists offer, [PACK-11](#packs)), or under one of your sample folders ([LIB-5](#library)). Its id is `sample:<package>:<path in the package>` (`sample:std:samples/kit/kick.wav`), or `sample:folder:<the file's path>` for yours (a path that isn't UTF-8 ends with `#` and a short hash of its exact bytes, so two names that differ only there are two samples); its package is the package's name, or `yours`. A sample has:
- what is **measured** from the file, never typed: its length, channels, rate, peak and SHA-256;
- what is **said** about it: its type (`loop` or `one-shot`), its instrument (one word of a short list kept in one place, `m2_editor::library::samples::INSTRUMENTS`: kick, snare, clap, hat, cymbal, tom, perc, bass, keys, guitar, vocal, strings, brass, pad, lead, synth, fx, texture, foley), a loop's tempo (20 to 300 bpm), its key, and tags. A package says them in `samples.toml` at its root, with paths inside `samples/`: a `[folder."<path>"]` table for every file under that folder (the deepest folder wins), and a `[file."<path>"]` table for one file (which wins over its folders), each with `type`, `instrument`, `bpm`, `key` (`Am`, `F#`, `Bb minor`, `C`) and `tags = [..]`; `key = "none"` and `instrument = "none"` take back a guess (a drum loop whose name reads as a key). A package's samples are the files its `samples.toml` names, by a folder or by the file: the rest of `samples/` is its instruments' recordings, and a package with no `samples.toml` has none. It is read strictly: an unknown key, a word not on the list, a tempo out of range or a key that doesn't read is refused, naming it, and the package lists no sample until it is fixed.
- What isn't said, and all of your folders', is **guessed** from the file's path and its length, by one function (`library::samples::guess`): a tempo (`90 bpm`, `120bpm`, `bpm 95`; a bare 60 to 200 only in a name with a key or a loop word; never a number with a unit after it: `96k`, `24bit`, `100 Hz`, `90s`); a key (`Am`, `C#m`, `Ebmin`, `F# minor`, `Bb maj`; a bare letter isn't one, a lowercase root needs a sign or a mode written out, and neither `Am` after `I` (`I Am`) nor a key run into a capitalised word (`FmPiano`) is one); the type (below); and the instrument (the first word of the list, in the list's order, that a word of the file's name names, so `808 kick` is a kick and `pad synth` a pad; else its folders', deepest first, where `808` names none: a folder of 808s is a drum kit as often as basses).
- A **loop needs evidence of its own**. The nearest of the file's name and its folders (deepest first) with a type word decides: `loop` or `loops` makes a loop; `one-shot`, `oneshot`, `one shot(s)`, `hit`, `hits`, `shot` or `shots` a one-shot (a one-shot word wins in the same name). With no type word, it is a loop when its own name has a tempo and it lasts at least one bar at that tempo (4 beats, less 5%); else a one-shot. A tempo in a folder's name (a construction kit's `Kit 128 BPM/`) gives a loop its tempo, but never makes a file a loop: a kick under it is a one-shot. A name's words are split at marks, where letters meet digits and where a small letter meets a capital (`120bpm_PianoChords` is `120 bpm Piano Chords`).
- Only WAV and FLAC files are samples for now: another audio file (MP3, AIFF, OGG, M4A) is skipped and counted, and the library says "WAV and FLAC only for now" with the count.
- *Why:* a musician finds a loop by its tempo, key and sound, and a pack author shouldn't have to type what the file can say (the packs design P7). A guess that a pack can correct is right most of the time for no work.
- *Today:* since track `samples`: `crates/editor/src/library/samples.rs` (`guess`, `Said::read`, `measure`, `index`), tested in `samples_tests.rs` (`a_name_says_its_tempo_key_type_and_instrument`, over 40 names as sample packs write them, `a_loop_needs_evidence_of_its_own`, `words_and_units_are_no_keys_or_tempos`, `samples_toml_takes_back_a_guess`, `a_key_in_samples_toml_reads_as_written`, `samples_toml_is_read_strictly`, `samples_toml_wins_over_the_guess`, `only_wav_and_flac_files_are_samples_and_the_rest_are_counted`). Since track flac, FLAC samples too ([FMT-24](#assets-and-hashes); `a_flac_sample_is_measured_as_its_wav`).

**LIB-5.** **Your sample folders** are kept in this computer's settings (`samples`, a list of folders, in `~/.music2/settings.json` beside where new songs go), added and removed in the library (Add a Folder…, and Remove beside each). A folder is kept by its real path (links and `..` resolved); `/`, your home folder itself, and a folder inside or around one already there are refused, each in words. Nothing in them is copied until a sample is added to a song.
- **Every index**, of a package's `samples/` and of a folder of yours, is made in the background, off the session's and the request's threads, one at a time for each folder: when a search finds none, or one over a minute old (a package's, 10 seconds). A search meanwhile lists what was indexed before, or none, and never waits nor starts a second index of a folder being indexed; its answer counts the indexes running (`folders.indexing`), so the page asks again when they end. Asking for one sample (its preview, adding it) waits for an index of it that is running, up to 30 seconds.
- An index walks only folders and plain files: never a link (a package whose `samples` is one lists no sample), a pipe or a device, nor inside a hidden folder. It keeps WAV and FLAC names alone (`.wav`, `.wave`, `.flac`, in any capitals) and counts other audio files; what a kept file is, is then told by its first bytes ([FMT-24](#assets-and-hashes)). A file that music2 can't play from the library (it doesn't decode, a rate outside 8,000 to 384,000 Hz, a FLAC music2 doesn't read, more than a whole decode may hold, [FMT-24](#assets-and-hashes)) is unreadable: named, counted, and listed beside the folder. A float WAV's sample that isn't a number reads as 0, as everywhere ([FMT-24](#assets-and-hashes)), so its file plays and is listed.
- What is measured is kept in the cache folder (`samples/` in `m2_os::dirs::cache`, [PLAT-7](#platforms)), one file per folder, by each file's exact path, size and modified time. It is read entry by entry, so one bad entry costs only itself, and written by one index at a time for each folder, through a part file of its own: a folder indexed before is indexed again with no file read.
- A folder that isn't there (a drive unplugged) is not indexed: its last index is listed and its cache kept, and the library says it isn't there. The files of a subfolder that can't be read keep their entries. Removing a folder stops its index between two files and deletes its cache file; the cache file of a package folder that is gone (a pack's version removed) is deleted at the app's next start.
- `m2 search` with the app closed indexes your folders itself, through the same cache.
- *Why:* a folder of 10,000 samples must not hold up a search, and must open fast the second time.
- *Today:* since track `samples`: `index` in `crates/editor/src/library/samples.rs` (`a_folder_indexed_again_reads_no_file`, `a_missing_folder_keeps_its_cache`, `a_bad_file_costs_only_itself`, `only_plain_files_are_read`, `ids_are_the_exact_paths`, `a_crafted_header_is_unreadable`, `a_stopped_index_writes_nothing_and_caches_are_pruned`, `a_packages_samples_through_a_link_list_none`), the background index in `crates/server/src/library.rs` (`SampleIndex`; in `routes_library_tests.rs`: `a_packages_samples_are_indexed_once_in_the_background`, `a_panic_while_indexing_ends_the_index`, `removing_a_folder_stops_its_index`), the settings and routes in `crates/server/src/routes_library.rs` (`GET`/`POST /api/library/folders`; `a_folder_is_indexed_in_the_background`, `a_folder_is_kept_by_its_real_path_and_not_twice`, `removing_a_folder_deletes_its_cache`), ⌘F's panel asking again while indexes run (`e2e/spotlight.mjs`: a folder added, its row and a search filling in), and `m2 search` with the app closed (`apps/m2/tests/search.rs`, `with_the_app_closed_m2_searches_samples`). ⌘F's panel adds and removes them (Your folders); the side browser has no Samples group yet (gaps #48). A folder isn't watched: one changed is indexed again a minute after its last index, at the next search.

**LIB-6.** A sample's preview is its own file, streamed by LIB-3's route (`GET /api/library/preview?id=<id>`), and only for a sample the library lists at that moment: the route takes an id, never a path. The file is opened as it was indexed, a plain file never reached through a link (one put there since is refused), and a file over 64 MB (about six minutes of CD-quality stereo) has no preview, said in words: the page holds a preview whole. A FLAC's preview is the WAV of its samples ([FMT-24](#assets-and-hashes)), made when it is asked for, so the page needs no FLAC decoder; the 64 MB are that WAV's, checked from the FLAC's header before it is decoded.
- *Why:* hearing the sample itself is the preview that can't oversell; a route that served any path would read any file on the computer.
- *Today:* since track `samples` (`a_samples_preview_is_its_file_and_only_a_listed_one`, `a_preview_never_follows_a_link`, `a_long_file_has_no_preview`); a FLAC's since track flac (`a_flac_samples_preview_is_the_wav_of_it`).

**LIB-7.** **Adding a sample** to a song (Enter or a drop in the app, `POST /api/library/sample`, `m2 add-sample`) copies its file into `assets/` as an import does ([FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes): create-only, under the first free name whose note's name is free too; the same bytes already there are that file; a FLAC as the WAV of its samples, [FMT-24](#assets-and-hashes)), and writes its **note** beside it, `assets/<file>.json`, create-only (a note already there is kept, but for a note of yours, `{"from": {"folder": true}}`, which the note of a package's sample with the same bytes replaces, so the credit a licence asks for is never lost), before any change names the file ([WRITE-1](#the-two-write-primitives)): `{"from": {"pack": "<package>", "version": "<version>", "file": "samples/…", "author": "…", "license": "…"}}` for a package's (std's too), `{"from": {"folder": true}}` for one of yours, with no path, which would show your disk's layout to whoever gets the song. A FLAC's note says what its WAV was made from too, `"made_from"` ([FMT-24](#assets-and-hashes)); a loop take's note lists its passes instead, `{"takes": [seconds…]}` ([MON-11](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)), written once, before the Tx that names its file, and kept as it is by every later write of notes; a note whose `from` credits no one (yours, or an import's that has only `made_from`) is a note of yours. Then one Tx, one undo step: a clip at the beat given, on the audio lane given or on a new audio track. A loop whose tempo is known is added with Follow tempo on, at the song's own tempo too, so it follows a later change of tempo: warp markers made from its own tempo, `[[0, 0], [<its seconds>, <its beats>]]`, its beats its exact length at that tempo, rounded to a whole beat only within one sample of it, so it plays in time at once and every beat of it lands on the song's. Any import, a sample's or not, takes no name whose note's name is taken (`kick.wav.json` takes `kick.wav`), so no file inherits another's note. `m2 add-sample` with the app closed finds the id in the library the song's own build lists (std, the song's packs at their locked versions, the store's other packs; your folders only for a `sample:folder:` id), and checks `--track` before it copies anything. Dropped on a Sampler's card (one with no `zones` or `regions`), the sample becomes its `sample`, copied and noted the same way, one undo step.
- *Why:* a song keeps its files, so it plays anywhere; the note keeps the credit a licence asks for (the packs design §11), without a path.
- *Today:* since track `samples`: `crates/editor/src/library/samples.rs` (`note`, `write_note`), `import.rs` (`Staged::name_noted`, `place_with`), the server's job in `crates/server/src/graph/import.rs` (`add_sample`, `sample_to_sampler`), tested by `adding_a_sample_copies_it_notes_it_and_places_one_clip`, `a_loop_at_another_tempo_follows_the_songs` and `a_sample_dropped_on_a_sampler_is_its_file` (`crates/server/src/routes_library_tests.rs`), `a_note_is_written_once_beside_its_asset`, `a_loop_follows_with_its_exact_beats` and `a_folders_note_gives_way_to_a_packs` (`samples_tests.rs`), `a_plain_import_never_takes_a_noted_name` (`import_tests.rs`), `every_loop_follows_with_its_exact_beats_and_a_pack_keeps_its_credit` and `a_place_no_clip_can_go_is_refused_before_any_copy` (`routes_library_tests.rs`), `m2_adds_a_sample_with_the_app_closed` and `m2_adds_a_songs_own_packs_sample_at_its_locked_version` (`apps/m2/tests/search.rs`), and `e2e/spotlight.mjs` (search kick, Space, Enter, the file and its note, Undo). In the app Enter adds at the bar line at or before the playhead, as Import Audio does; a drop where it is dropped. `m2 credits` (listing the notes) is later (gaps #48).

**LIB-8.** **std's kit** is std's own small drum kit (the packs design D8): 14 one-shots in `std/samples/kit/`, each one note of music2's drum machine (`std/drums`' kit; since std 0.7 `@m2/drums`', [PACK-15](#packs)) rendered by music2 from beat 0, cut where it falls 80 dB under its peak, peak-normalised to −1 dBFS, written as 16-bit WAV at 48 kHz, mono as the drum is. `std/samples/make.sh` writes them; the test it runs checks, whenever it runs without being asked to write, that they are made again to the same bytes. They are listed in `std/samples.toml`, are under std's licence, ship with std ([PLAT-8](#platforms)), and a song names one as any package file, `"std:samples/kit/kick.wav"` ([PACK-5](#packs)). They change no render and no API, so std's version stays.
- *Why:* a beginner needs a few sounds to drop in before getting any pack, and music2 can make them itself, so they carry no one else's licence.
- *Today:* since track `samples`: `crates/editor/src/library/kit.rs` (`std_kit_is_made_again_to_the_same_bytes`); the bundle's config carries them (`apps/music2-desktop/tests/bundle.rs`, `the_bundle_carries_stds_kit`; `ci/release.sh` checks the built files). std stays 0.5.0.

## Registry

The list of packs anyone can get, and the robot that fills a bucket from it: slice 6 of the packs design ([design](designs/packs/design.md), §7 to §10 and §19, "The registry, as built"). Built by track `registry` (2026-10-30), and hardened after its review the same day: the list's format, the bucket's files, and `m2 registry`. The app's side (fetching the catalog, ⌘F's From the library, Get) is REG-11 to REG-17, after REG-10.

**REG-1.** The registry is a folder (a git repo, `BreadBowlAI/music2-registry`, started from music2's own `registry/`): `tags.txt`, the words a pack's tags may use (design P7), one a line, `#` lines and blank lines aside, each a word as a pack name's part ([PACK-1](#packs)); `removed.toml`, the versions taken down ([REG-7](#registry)), if any; and under `packs/` one file a pack, `packs/@owner/name.toml`, and nothing else but dot files. Nothing in it is a link, a pipe or a device. A pack file MUST be read strictly (an unknown key anywhere refused, as `m2pkg.toml` is, [PACK-2](#packs)):

```toml
name = "@alice/felt-piano"                        # the file's own path; never std
repository = "https://github.com/alice/felt-piano"

[[versions]]
version = "1.2.0"
hash = "sha256:…"          # the hash of the version's m2pack.json (PACK-6): what music2.lock pins (PACK-8)
source = "https://github.com/alice/felt-piano/releases/download/v1.2.0/felt-piano-1.2.0.tar.gz"
# yanked = "why"           hidden from search; a song that locks it still gets it
```

A file lists at least one version; no version twice; `yanked`, when there, says why. A take-down is never in a pack file (`removed` there is refused, saying where it goes). A hash is listed once in the whole registry: two versions, of one pack or two, are never the same files. `repository` is `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>`, and `source` a `.tar.gz` (or `.tgz`) in a repo under `https://github.com/<owner>/`, where `<owner>` is the pack's scope (in any capitals: GitHub's names don't care), and `BreadBowlAI` for `@m2/`. After `https://github.com/` an address holds only letters, digits and `-._/`, with no empty, `.` or `..` part: no `%`, since an encoded part (`%2e%2e`, `%2F`) could name another owner's repo once the server decodes it, and nothing the CI's shell could read as more than an address. One type reads a pack file, `m2_song::registry::PackFile`, and one the folder, `Registry::read`, each problem in words naming its file.
- *Why:* the list is reviewed in the open (design F5), so it must say exactly one thing; a pack comes from its owner's own GitHub (D2), and no address can smuggle anything into the CI's shell. A hash claimed by a second pack would make one version's facts another's.
- *Today:* since track `registry` (`core/song/src/registry.rs`; `a_pack_file_is_read_strictly`, `a_pack_files_addresses_are_its_scopes_github`, `a_registry_holds_only_pack_files_at_their_names`, `take_downs_and_hashes_are_the_registrys_own`).

**REG-2.** A listed version MUST never change (design P5). `m2 registry check <registry> --base <the registry before>` refuses a pack file gone, a version gone from its file, a listed version whose `hash` or `source` changed, and a take-down taken out of `removed.toml`; adding or taking back `yanked`, adding a version and adding a pack are the edits allowed. With `--author <github-login>`, every file whose bytes differ from the base's (every file, with no base) MUST be in a folder of the author's own scope, `packs/@<login>/` in any capitals: compared byte for byte, so a comment, a layout and a dot file count too. `tags.txt`, `removed.toml` and every other file are music2's people's, and `@m2/` is never an author's: their pull requests are checked without `--author` ([REG-10](#registry)). Every problem is printed in words, exit 1. This check is advice on a pull request (its workflow is the pull request's own, [REG-10](#registry)); what holds is [REG-5](#registry)'s check against what is published.
- *Why:* a song keeps the version it was made with (design M5) only if that version's files can never be swapped under its lock; identity is GitHub's (design §8), so a pull request can only touch its author's own packs, and nobody else's words.
- *Today:* since track `registry` (`Registry::broken_promises` and `Registry::not_authors` in `core/song/src/registry.rs`, `check` in `crates/editor/src/registry.rs`; `a_listed_version_never_changes`, `an_author_changes_only_their_own_files`, `check_refuses_an_edited_version_a_wrong_scope_and_a_foreign_source`, and through `m2`, `the_robot_runs_from_the_command_line`). No call to GitHub's API: the scope and the addresses say whose a pack is, the same answer every time and offline.

**REG-3.** The bucket MUST be plain files at plain paths, every byte addressed by its SHA-256, so any host, a mirror or a folder serves the same files:

| Path | Holds | Changes |
|---|---|---|
| `blobs/sha256/<hex>` | every file of every listed version, its `m2pack.json` among them, and every preview: the file's SHA-256 | never |
| `versions/<hex>.json` | the robot's facts for one version, by the version's hash ([REG-4](#registry)) | never: written once |
| `catalog/<hex>.json` | a catalog, by its own SHA-256 | never |
| `catalog.json` | which catalog is current: `{"format": 1, "catalog": "sha256:…", "size": <its bytes>, "made": "2026-10-30T12:00:00Z"}` | the one file that does |

Every JSON file the robot writes MUST be in RFC 8785 form (JCS, `m2_song::jcs`, the one form music2 hashes JSON in), so the same value is always the same bytes. Each has `"format": 1`, and its reader MUST refuse another format (saying to update music2) and any unknown field, at any depth; a time is UTC to the second, `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`. The readers are `m2_song::registry`'s, `Pointer::read`, `Facts::read` (and `read_for`, which checks that the facts are of the name, version and hash given) and `Catalog::read`; `Catalog::read_checked` refuses a catalog that isn't the size and hash `catalog.json` names, or whose `made` isn't the one `catalog.json` says, so a broken or changed one is never used. `Catalog::read` and `Facts::read` refuse a word that holds a control character (a line break, a tab, an escape: PACK-12's rule, which the robot holds packs to), anywhere in them. Hashes are written `sha256:<hex>` (as `music2.lock` writes them) and paths name the hex alone.
- *Why:* names by hash make exactness the hash's, never the host's (design F1, R2); one form of JSON is what lets the robot and the app agree to the byte, and a strict reader is what keeps a newer catalog from being half understood.
- *Today:* since track `registry` (`core/song/src/registry.rs`; `the_buckets_files_are_read_strictly`, `times_are_utc_to_the_second`). The control characters and `made` since track `library-get`'s review (`a_catalog_with_control_characters_is_refused`, `an_older_catalog_never_replaces_a_newer_one`).

**REG-4.** A version's facts, `versions/<hash>.json`, MUST hold: its `name`, `version` and `hash`; `about`, what its `m2pkg.toml` says (`description`, `license`, `authors`, `tags`); its `size` and `files`, all its files' bytes and count, its `m2pack.json` among them (what getting it whole fetches); `blobs`, every blob it put in the bucket, sorted (its files, its `m2pack.json`, its previews); `made_by`, what rendered its previews (`music2 0.1.0, @m2/core 1.8.0`); and its `items`, by id, each as the library would list it from the installed pack ([LIB-1](#library)): `kind` (`instrument`, `sound`, `effect` or `sample`), `id`, `label`, `category`, `description`, `tags`, `component` (what adding it names: a sample's is its package file, `@alice/drums:samples/kick.wav`), `instrument` and `effect` (what adding it does), a sound's `preset` (its name), a sample's `sample` (`type`, `instrument`, `bpm`, `key` as the library writes one, `Am`, `seconds`, `channels`, `rate`, `peak`: [LIB-4](#library)), `preview` (its blob, [REG-6](#registry)) or `no_preview` (why not, in words), and a sample's `file` (`path`, `size`, `sha256`: all that getting the sample alone fetches; an instrument, an effect or a sound comes with its whole version). A catalog MUST be made from the registry and the facts alone (`Catalog::make`): `{"format": 1, "made": …, "packs": [{name, repository, description, license, authors, tags, latest, versions: [{version, hash, source, size, files, yanked, removed}], items}]}`, packs by name, versions oldest first, `latest` the newest of them neither yanked nor taken down (none when every one is), and `items` the latest's; what a pack says of itself is its latest's facts, else its newest's there. A version with no facts (not built yet, or its build failed) is left out, and a pack none of whose versions is in; a taken-down version is in, with why, and has no `size` and no `files`; every other has both. The same registry, facts and `made` give the same bytes.
- *Why:* search runs on the musician's computer over the catalog (design §9.2), so the catalog must hold what ⌘F shows; facts written once mean an old version is never measured again, and a catalog made from inputs alone can be made again by anyone to check it. Each version's `source` is published so it can be held to it ([REG-5](#registry)).
- *Today:* since track `registry` (`Facts`, `Catalog::make` in `core/song/src/registry.rs`; `the_same_registry_and_facts_make_the_same_catalog`). Not kept yet: a data instrument's own `tags` (gaps #49), and download counts (design §9.2 ranks by them).

**REG-5.** `m2 registry build <registry> --sources <folder> --out <bucket> [--from <bucket>] [--made <time>] [--to-delete <file>]` MUST first check the registry against what is published already, the catalogs `catalog.json` names at `--from` and at `--out` (`Registry::breaks`): a published version gone from the registry or listed with another `hash` or `source`, a take-down taken back, or a hash a published version has under another name refuses the whole build, whoever made the change, and nothing is written. Then each listed version that isn't taken down and whose facts are in neither `--out` nor `--from` (what `m2 registry pending` prints, [REG-9](#registry)) is built on its own, from `<sources>/<its hash's hex>/` alone, where the CI unpacked its source, on a thread of its own given 10 minutes (`LONGEST`): its source is the one folder there whose `m2pkg.toml` names that pack and version (none, two, or another pack's folder is its failure); it is refused unless it is only folders and plain files (no link, pipe or device, found before any file is opened) and 10 GB at most by their sizes (D9), before any byte is hashed; then unless its files are the ones listed (their `m2pack.json`'s hash is the listed hash, and its own `m2pack.json`, when it has one, is that listing), looking no further when they aren't; then unless it passes `m2 pack check` ([PACK-12](#packs)), its licence is an SPDX expression of licences the registry takes (design D6: `CC0-1.0`, `CC-BY-3.0`, `CC-BY-4.0`, `MIT`, `Apache-2.0`, `BSD-2-Clause`, `BSD-3-Clause`, `ISC`), and its tags are in `tags.txt` (P7). Its files, previews ([REG-6](#registry)) and facts are written into a folder of its own, each file copied as it streams and its copy's hash checked, and moved into `--out` (blobs first, facts last) only when all of it passed. A version that fails, or runs out of time, is reported, writes nothing into `--out`, is left out of the catalog and stays pending; the others are built all the same, and `m2` exits 1 once the catalog is written. Then the catalog from every listed version's facts (from `--out`, else `--from`, each read with `Facts::read_for`), and `catalog.json`. `--made` is the catalog's time (now, when left out): the registry's CI gives its commit's time, so one commit always makes the same bytes. A write goes through a part file renamed into place.
- *Why:* the bucket's copy becomes the truth once it is made (design §8), so it must be exactly what the registry lists, and what it has published must never change whatever a merge let in; one author's missing or broken source must never stop everyone else's publishing.
- *Today:* since track `registry` (`build` in `crates/editor/src/registry.rs`; `a_registry_builds_into_a_bucket_and_again_to_the_same_bytes`, `a_source_that_isnt_what_is_listed_fails_alone`, `what_is_published_never_changes`, `a_version_past_its_time_is_refused_and_the_rest_are_built`, `a_kernel_that_never_returns_is_refused_in_its_limits_words`, `a_published_version_never_changes` in core/song). music2 has no tar or gzip code: the CI unpacks each source with `tar`, into its own folder, after checking from the archive's own list that it unpacks to 10 GB at most. A pack's kernel's call is stopped at its time limit ([ABI-6](#kernel-abi-reservations)), so a version whose preview a kernel never ends is refused in seconds, in [ABI-7](#kernel-abi-reservations)'s words (`a_kernel_that_never_returns_is_refused_in_its_limits_words`, since track `limits`); a version past its time for any other reason, such as a preview whose every call returns in time but which would take minutes, is refused, and its thread, which can't be stopped, is left to end with the robot (`a_version_past_its_time_is_refused_and_the_rest_are_built`).

**REG-6.** The robot's previews MUST be what the app's ⌘F would make ([LIB-3](#library)): an instrument's, an effect's and a sound's is the song `library::preview::of` writes for the item (the one step the server's preview route takes too) rendered by `library::preview::render`, with std and the pack loaded from its source folder, so it is the same bits the app makes from the installed version ([RENDER-2](#offline)). A sample's is its own file ([LIB-6](#library)), none over 64 MB. An item with none says why (`no_preview`), and a render that fails is no preview, in words, not a refusal, but for one a kernel's time limit stopped: that refuses the version ([ABI-7](#kernel-abi-reservations)), as a kernel that never returns is broken.
- *Why:* a preview can't oversell when it is the pack playing (design R3, §10), and two ways of making one would drift.
- *Today:* since track `registry` (`a_registry_builds_into_a_bucket_and_again_to_the_same_bytes` compares the robot's preview with the app's from the installed pack). The robot reads instruments written as data ([PACK-13](#packs)) with the session's reader, `m2_session::describe`; since track `library-get` so do the app's ⌘F with no song open, `m2 search` with the app closed and the preview route, so a data instrument is listed and previewed there too (`a_data_instrument_is_in_the_library_with_no_song_and_previews`, `a_data_instrument_is_listed_and_previewed_with_or_without_a_song`; it was gaps #47).

**REG-7.** A **yanked** version MUST be built, listed in the catalog with why, and never the latest; a song that locks it still gets it (design §9.3); its author yanks it, and may take the yank back. A **taken-down** version is music2's people's to list, in `removed.toml` at the registry's root, one `[[removed]]` with its pack's `name`, its `version`, its `hash` (the one its pack file lists, which stays as it is) and `why`, read strictly; it MUST NOT be built; it is listed with why and with no size, files or items; and `build --to-delete <file>` writes the bucket paths (`blobs/sha256/<hex>`) of its blobs that no other listed version uses, one a line, for the CI to delete. A take-down is never taken back ([REG-2](#registry), [REG-5](#registry)): its files are gone. Its facts stay (they hold no recording), so what the pack said of itself stays too; a taken-down version whose facts aren't there is named, its blobs unknown. The bucket's public address caches every file for a year, so a take-down also needs the deleted files purged from Cloudflare's cache, the owner's step (the registry's README); `--from` reads the bucket itself (its `r2.dev` address), never a cache, so a deleted file is never taken for one still there.
- *Why:* a take-down must remove the bytes (R2) without breaking the files another version shares, and only for a licence or legal reason (design §9.3), so its author can't take down a version songs use; a yank only hides.
- *Today:* since track `registry` (`Removal`, `read_removed`, `Registry::removed_blobs`; `take_downs_and_hashes_are_the_registrys_own`, `a_removed_versions_blobs_are_those_no_other_version_uses`, `a_removed_version_goes_and_a_bucket_is_read_where_it_is`, `what_is_published_never_changes`). The purge isn't automatic: it would need a Cloudflare token in the publish job.

**REG-8.** `m2 registry add <registry> <pack-folder> --source <url>` (for a pack's author) MUST check the pack as `m2 pack check` does, write its `m2pack.json` (as `m2 pack build`), and list its version in its pack file: a new file with its name and its `m2pkg.toml`'s `repository` (refused, in words, when it names none), or appended to the file there as it is, its comments kept. The same version listed already with these files and this source is nothing to do; with others, refused (P5). What is written MUST read as REG-1 says, so an address outside the scope's GitHub is refused before anything is written.
- *Why:* an author lists a pack with the same checks the robot runs, so a pull request that passes on their computer passes on the robot's.
- *Today:* since track `registry` (`add`; `add_lists_a_version_once_and_never_changes_it`). Making the `.tar.gz` and the GitHub release is the author's (the guide says how); `m2 pack publish` is later (design §8).

**REG-9.** `m2 registry pending <registry> --out <bucket> [--from <bucket>]` prints each version a build would build, `<its hash's hex> <name> <version> <source>`, one a line, by name and version: what the CI fetches, each into `<sources>/<hex>/`. `--from` is the bucket as it is: a folder's path, a `file:///…` URL, or a web address read through music2's downloads ([NET-1](#network): `https://`, or `http://` to this computer only), which MUST reach the bucket itself, never a cache in front of it ([REG-7](#registry)). Over the network each file has a limit and a deadline by its kind (`catalog.json` 64 KiB in 30 s, a version's facts 64 MiB in 2 minutes, a catalog 512 MiB in 10 minutes); a 404 or 410 is a file not there; a blob is never fetched to see whether it is there (it is put again, and the copy into the bucket keeps the one there), and a version's facts that can't be fetched are taken as not there, so the worst is a version built again.
- *Why:* the CI fetches only what is new, with the shell, so music2 needs no tar code for it; a folder named by the hash means one source can never stand in for another.
- *Today:* since track `registry` (`pending` and `Bucket`; `a_removed_version_goes_and_a_bucket_is_read_where_it_is`, `the_robot_runs_from_the_command_line`, and over loopback http `a_bucket_is_read_over_http`).

**REG-10.** The registry repo's CI (music2's `registry/.github/`) MUST keep the bucket's key from anything a pull request runs, and from anything a pack's code makes (design §8, GitHub's "pwn request" trap):
- **A pull request** is checked by a `pull_request` workflow (never `pull_request_target`), with a read-only token and no secret. Its run is advice: it is the pull request's own workflow file, so a pull request can change what it checks. Someone not of BreadBowlAI (`author_association` neither `OWNER` nor `MEMBER`) is checked with `--author` and may change only files under `packs/`, and `dependabot[bot]` only under `.github/`, by git's own list of what changed (a link is a change of its own, never followed).
- **Every pull request is merged by a person** of BreadBowlAI: the owner turns on branch protection for `main` (a pull request with one approval, review from code owners, stale approvals dismissed, no bypass, no direct push), leaves auto-merge off, and `CODEOWNERS` gives BreadBowlAI everything outside `packs/`, `removed.toml` named too. Merging a later version by itself needs a gate a pull request can't change: later work.
- **On `main`**, the build job is the gate: [REG-5](#registry)'s check against what the bucket publishes, whatever a pull request's run said. It runs pack code and has no secret, but runs on a GitHub runner, which has `sudo` and the network. So the publish job, the only one given the key (the `bucket` environment, limited to `main`), runs no pack code and no `m2`, and trusts nothing the build made: only folders and plain files at the four kinds of path (`blobs/sha256/<64 hex>`, `versions/<64 hex>.json`, `catalog/<64 hex>.json`, `catalog.json`), each blob and catalog hashing to its name, `catalog.json` naming a catalog that is there with its size; each key put only when the bucket hasn't got it (`If-None-Match: *`), so nothing there is replaced but `catalog.json`, which goes last; and only `blobs/sha256/<64 hex>` lines of `to-delete.txt` are deleted. A last job fails when a version was left out.
- m2's output in a workflow is fenced with `::stop-commands::`, so no line of a pack's words starts a workflow command. The `m2` it runs is a music2 release, pinned by version and SHA-256, every action by its full commit, and no checkout keeps its credentials.
- *Why:* a pack's code runs in the robot (its TypeScript in QuickJS, its kernels sealed), and the key to the bucket must never be within its reach, nor the reach of a pull request's own changes to the workflow; what a runner that ran a stranger's code made is a stranger's too.
- *Today:* since track `registry` (`registry/.github/workflows/pull-request.yml` and `main.yml`, `registry/.github/CODEOWNERS`). music2's own lint runs actionlint and zizmor over them too (`ci/pr.sh lint`); the publish job's checks were run by hand on a built bucket, a blob changed, a stray file, a link and a `to-delete.txt` naming `catalog.json`. They run once the owner makes the registry repo and the bucket (D10), with the first music2 release that has `m2 registry`.

The app's side, built by track `library-get` (2026-10-30): where the registry is, its catalog kept on this computer, ⌘F's From the library with its previews, and Get, in the app and with `m2 pack get`. In plain words in the guide's [Getting packs from the library](guide/library.md).

**REG-11.** The app MUST read one registry, at one address: `M2_REGISTRY` (`https://…`, `http://` to this computer, or `file:///…`, [NET-3](#network); or a folder's whole path), else `registry` in this computer's settings (`~/.music2/settings.json`), else `DEFAULT_REGISTRY` (`m2_editor::get`), which stays empty until the owner picks the bucket's address (design D10). With none, ⌘F has no From the library group, nothing is fetched, and `m2 pack get` says that no registry is set and how to set one. Only a program that keeps the cache folder ([PLAT-7](#platforms): the app, the headless server, `m2`) reads it, and what it fetches is kept there: a test's server reads only a registry the test gives it, which the test serves.
- *Why:* the bucket has no address yet; and no test may reach the internet, nor a cache folder that isn't its own.
- *Today:* since track `library-get` (`get::address`, read by `Folders::of_this_user` and by `m2`; `the_registry_is_the_one_the_environment_or_the_settings_name`, `the_registry_in_the_settings_is_kept_through_a_change`, `no_registry_is_said_plainly`; a server given no registry answers no library and fetches nothing, `the_library_route_answers_from_the_library_too`). No window sets it yet: the settings file, or `M2_REGISTRY`.

**REG-12.** The catalog MUST be fetched only when the app starts, when the one it has is a day old, and, after a fetch that failed, at a search ten minutes later: `catalog.json` (64 KiB in 30 s), then the catalog it names, `catalog/<hex>.json`, only when the cache doesn't hold it (by its size and SHA-256, at most 512 MiB in 10 minutes), read with `Catalog::read_checked`. Both are kept in the cache folder, in `registry/<a short hash of the address>/`, each through a part file renamed into place, the catalog before `catalog.json`. So a catalog that doesn't read, or isn't the one `catalog.json` names, is never used and never replaces the one kept, and offline the one kept is used. A `catalog.json` that names a catalog older than the one kept, or another as old (its `made`), is refused and the one kept stays: a cache in front of the bucket, or a host, serving an older catalog would undo a yank or a take-down. A search never waits for a fetch: it searches the catalog kept, and its answer says while one is being fetched (`registry.fetching`), so the page asks again when it ends. `m2` with the app closed reads the same cache, and fetches only what is a day old.
- *Why:* search runs over the catalog on this computer (design §9.2), so it must work offline; a catalog cut short or changed on the way must never be believed, nor an old one taken back. The catalog is checked by its hash against `catalog.json`, fetched over https from the same host, and is not signed: the registry's address is trusted for what its catalog lists, `@m2/` packs too (signatures: gaps #39).
- *Today:* since track `library-get` (`get::Remote`; `the_catalog_is_fetched_checked_and_kept`, `a_catalog_is_fetched_again_only_when_a_day_old`, `a_broken_catalog_never_replaces_a_good_one`, `offline_the_kept_catalog_is_used`, `an_older_catalog_never_replaces_a_newer_one`).

**REG-13.** ⌘F's **From the library** MUST be the items of the catalog's packs this computer hasn't got: of each pack with a latest version ([REG-4](#registry)) that the open song neither lists in `[dependencies]` nor locks, its latest's items, when no version of it is installed, or the newest installed is older. Of a pack the open song lists or locks it offers nothing: moving a song to another version is `m2 pack update`, never automatic (design §11), and a locked version that isn't installed is REG-17's Get. They are searched by LIB-1's one search over the catalog kept, so what is typed is never sent ([NET-2](#network)), and ranked by it, music2's own first: a version whose hash music2's record holds ([PACK-19](#packs)), never a name. `GET /api/library` answers them as `library`, after `items`, at most 20 rows (`LIBRARY_ROWS`), with `library_more`, how many more matched (⌘F then says to type more), each with the id `library:<its id>` and `library`: its pack, version, licence, authors, the bytes a Get fetches, and the version this computer has, if any. The offers are made once for a catalog, the store and the open song's packs, never again for a keystroke. `m2 search` lists them too, marked `library`, its words written out where they hold a control character.
- *Why:* what you have first, then what you can get (design §9.2); one search, so the two never rank apart.
- *Today:* since track `library-get` (`get::offers`; `from_the_library_is_what_you_havent_got`, through the route `the_library_route_answers_from_the_library_too`, `a_pack_the_open_song_uses_is_offered_nothing_of`, `from_the_library_is_capped_and_made_once_a_catalog` (100,000 items, the times printed), `m2_search_lists_the_librarys_items`, `a_control_character_is_printed_written_out`; ⌘F's group in `e2e/library.mjs`).

**REG-14.** A library item's preview MUST be its `preview` blob ([REG-4](#registry)), fetched only when it is played (`GET /api/library/preview?id=library:…`): by its hash, at most 64 MB ([LIB-6](#library)), and kept in the cache folder by it (`registry/blobs/<hex>`), so a second play fetches nothing; a kept one that no longer matches its hash is fetched again. One with none says why (`no_preview`).
- *Why:* hearing a pack before getting it is what makes the library usable, and a preview is the pack playing (REG-6), so it can't oversell.
- *Today:* since track `library-get` (`Remote::blob`; `a_preview_is_fetched_by_its_hash_and_kept`, `a_library_items_preview_is_its_blob`; a FLAC's is the WAV of it, as LIB-6's, `a_flac_sample_comes_in_as_a_wav`). The blobs kept are never pruned yet (gaps #39).

**REG-15.** **Get** (`POST /api/library/get`) of an instrument, an effect or a sound MUST get its pack's whole version, all or nothing. First its `m2pack.json`, by the version's hash, read strictly: in its one form, every path plain as [PACK-5](#packs)'s package files are, and adding up to the size the catalog says. Then its `m2pkg.toml`, which must name that pack and version and need nothing this app hasn't got ([PACK-2](#packs)), else the Get stops there, saying to update music2. Then every other file, by its size and hash, into a folder of the Get's own beside the store (`.getting-<process>-<n>`); then that folder is installed as `m2 pack install` installs ([PACK-6](#packs), [PACK-12](#packs)), its listing first compared with the one fetched. A size the listing gives is added up with a check: a sum past 10 GB, or past what a number holds, is refused before any other file is fetched. A cancel is taken until the install starts, and checked once more just before it; from then on (`installing` in the events) a cancel is refused in words, and the Get ends installed. A failure or a cancel taken leaves nothing installed and no folder, and adds nothing. A folder a Get that crashed left is removed by the next Get: another process's that no longer runs, or one with this process's id that none of its Gets is using (ids are reused). Then the open song's lists are made again, so they offer it ([PACK-11](#packs)), and the page adds the item as Enter adds an installed one ([PACK-10](#packs) writes its dependency and lock), only to the song it was got for: when another is open by then, nothing is added and the page says so; a sound of an instrument adds a track that plays the instrument, then loads the sound onto it (two undo steps, gaps #47). One Get runs at a time: the route answers when it is done; meanwhile `{"t": "get", "id", "done", "total", "installing"}` events say how far it is, at most ten a second; and `POST /api/library/get/cancel` stops it, within 50 ms while it waits on the network ([NET-6](#network)), until it is installing. A Get that fails says why in NET-6's words.
- *Why:* a version is the files its hash names or nothing (design P5); half a pack in the store would be a song that plays wrong.
- *Today:* since track `library-get` (`get::pack`; `get_installs_a_whole_version_checked`, `a_changed_blob_fails_get_and_leaves_nothing`, `a_cancelled_get_leaves_nothing`, `a_listing_that_leaves_its_folder_is_refused`, `a_pack_for_a_newer_music2_stops_before_its_files`, `a_crashed_gets_folder_is_removed`; through the server `get_installs_the_pack_and_the_song_offers_it`, `a_get_says_how_far_and_can_be_cancelled`; in the page `e2e/library.mjs`, and since its review `a_cancel_until_the_install_starts_leaves_nothing`, `a_listing_whose_sizes_overflow_is_refused`, `a_cancel_once_installing_is_refused`; in the page, a Get that ends after another song was opened, `e2e/library.mjs`).

**REG-16.** **Get** of a sample MUST fetch its one file (REG-4's `file`, by its size and hash) and add it as Add sample adds a package's ([LIB-7](#library)): copied into `assets/` with its note naming the pack, its version, author and licence, then a clip where the page says, one undo step, in the song it was got for: when another is open by the time its file is in, nothing is added, said in words. A file the catalog says is over 10 GB is refused before any byte; a cancel is taken until the add starts. A sample kept already as a preview is copied from the cache, checked by its hash on the way. Its file comes into a folder of the Get's own in the registry's cache (`getting-<process>-<n>`), swept as REG-15's are.
- *Why:* a sample is one file; getting its whole pack for it would fetch gigabytes for a kick.
- *Today:* since track `library-get` (`get::sample`; `get_a_sample_fetches_its_one_file`, `getting_a_sample_adds_it_with_its_note`, a FLAC as a WAV `a_flac_sample_comes_in_as_a_wav`; in the page `e2e/library.mjs`, and since its review `a_sample_larger_than_a_pack_may_be_is_refused`, `a_samples_folder_a_crash_left_is_swept`, `a_sample_got_for_a_song_no_longer_open_is_not_added`).

**REG-17.** A song whose lock names a version that isn't installed (`pkg.missing`) MUST be offered **Get** when the catalog lists that version with that very hash (`GET /api/library/missing`, and the status's problems in the app): a **yanked** version is got all the same ([REG-7](#registry)); a **taken-down** one never, said in words with why; one the catalog lists with another hash, or not at all, says so. Once got, the song is built again, so it plays, and an outside edit that waited for it is one undo step, as when its own batch builds ([AGENT-2](#agents)). `m2 pack get` in a song's folder gets every locked version missing; `m2 pack get <@owner/name>[@<version>]` gets that version, or the latest when none is given; with the app open it then tells it (`POST /api/library/store-changed`), so its open song builds and its lists offer what came, with no reopening.
- *Why:* a song shared without its packs must play as it was made (design M5), and a song that locks a yanked version still gets it (design §9.3).
- *Today:* since track `library-get` (`get::missing`, `get::chosen`, `Session::store_changed`; `missing_locked_versions_are_got_by_their_hash`, `the_version_got_is_the_latest_or_the_one_named`, `a_song_missing_a_locked_pack_builds_once_it_is_installed`, `a_song_missing_a_locked_pack_gets_it_and_plays`, `m2_pack_get_gets_a_pack_and_a_songs_missing_ones`, and since its review `an_outside_edit_that_waited_for_a_pack_keeps_its_undo_step`, `told_the_store_changed_a_song_that_waited_builds`, `m2_pack_get_tells_the_open_app`). The status popover's Get (`MissingPacks` in `web/src/components/Status.tsx`, asking again every 2 s while it shows) has no browser suite yet.

## Network

music2 works offline. It reaches the internet only to get something: a pack, a sample, the registry's catalog (the packs design, §7 and §9.2). These rules say how. Talking to this computer is not covered here: the local API and its callers, the window and `m2` ([AGENT-8](#agents)). Nor is a CLAP plugin: it is another maker's program, in its own process ([PluginHost](#pluginhost)).

**NET-1.** music2's own code MUST reach another computer only through `m2_editor::fetch`. Only `crates/editor` depends on the HTTP and TLS crates (`reqwest`, `rustls`), and only `fetch.rs` names them.
- *Why:* one place to read for what music2 sends, and one place where every download is checked.
- *Today:* since track `fetch` (`crates/editor/src/fetch.rs`; `only_fetch_reaches_the_network`). Since track `registry`, the registry's robot reads a bucket through it (`m2 registry … --from https://…`, [REG-9](#registry)); since track `library-get`, the app's catalog, previews and Gets do (`m2_editor::get`, [REG-12](#registry) to [REG-17](#registry)).

**NET-2.** music2 MUST download only what the person asked for (a Get, an install, an update they chose, a preview they play, in the app or with `m2`), and the registry's catalog ([REG-12](#registry)). A request is a GET of the address it names with `User-Agent: music2/<version>` and `Accept-Encoding: identity`, and nothing about the person: no account, no cookie, no id, no referrer, and never what they typed (search runs on their computer over the catalog, the design's §9.2). An address with a name or password in it is refused. `fetch` never retries and keeps no cache: its caller decides both.
- *Why:* music2 is a studio, not a service: what you do in it stays on your computer.
- *Today:* since track `fetch` (`a_request_says_only_what_it_asks_for`, `an_address_that_isnt_safe_is_refused_before_any_connection`). Since track `library-get`, ⌘F searches the catalog kept on this computer and asks the registry nothing for it (`a_search_sends_nothing`).

**NET-3.** A download's address MUST be `https://`; `http://` only to this computer (`127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `localhost`, which is always 127.0.0.1), for tests and a local registry; or `file://` on this computer, a plain file read from the disk with the same limits and checks, since a folder on a drive serves the same files as the bucket (the design's §7.3). Anything else is refused before any connection is made, and a `file://` that isn't a plain file (a folder, a pipe, a device) before it is opened. A redirect is followed at most 5 times, each new address checked again, and never from `https` to `http`. Certificates are checked by the OS's own verifier (Apple's on macOS, the system's store on Linux), loaded at the first https download only: http to this computer and `file://` work on a computer with none, and a failure to load them is tried again at the next https download. No check can be turned off. No proxy is used, whatever the environment says.
- *Why:* a download over plain http can be changed on the way by anyone on the same network.
- *Today:* since track `fetch`. TLS is rustls with the ring provider, given to fetch's own clients, so nothing is installed for the whole process (`a_redirect_to_http_is_refused`, `a_few_redirects_are_followed_and_no_more`, `file_addresses_read_from_disk_with_the_same_checks`, `a_file_address_that_isnt_a_plain_file_is_refused_at_once`, `a_certificate_that_isnt_trusted_or_isnt_for_its_host_is_refused` with the tests' own CA in `crates/editor/tests/tls/`, `https_to_a_server_that_isnt_is_unsafe`, `without_certificates_this_computer_and_files_still_download`, `a_proxy_in_the_environment_is_never_used`, `localhost_is_this_computer`).

**NET-4.** Every download MUST have a size limit, and MUST stop as soon as it is over it: before any byte when the server says its size (`Content-Length`), else as the bytes come. A size or SHA-256 the caller knows MUST be checked: the size against the server's word before the bytes and against the bytes at the end, the hash after the last byte. An answer the server compressed (a `Content-Encoding` other than `identity`) is refused in words, never kept or hashed as if it were the file. `get_to_file` returns the size and SHA-256 of what it put in place; `get` returns the bytes.
- *Why:* a server that is wrong or hostile must never fill the disk, and a file changed on the way must never be used (the design's §12 and R2).
- *Today:* since track `fetch` (`too_big_is_stopped_from_its_content_length`, `too_big_is_stopped_while_streaming`, `a_wrong_size_is_refused`, `a_wrong_hash_is_refused`, `a_compressed_answer_is_refused`).

**NET-5.** A download to a file MUST be written to a `.part` file of that call's own beside `dest` (`<dest>.<process>-<n>.part`, made new: never one another download writes, never through a link), hashed as it is written, and put in place only once every check passed: its bytes made durable (`F_FULLFSYNC` on macOS, `fsync` on Linux), then renamed onto `dest`, then its folder synced, so on Linux the name is durable when the call returns and on macOS at the drive's next flush. On any failure or a cancel, the `.part` is removed and `dest` is left as it was, except when the folder can't be synced after the rename: the file it replaced is gone by then, so the new one is removed too. A failed download never leaves a file at its destination. Two downloads to one `dest` never mix: each that returns Ok put its own whole bytes there. A `.part` a crash left keeps its name and is never used again; the folder's owner removes such leftovers.
- *Why:* a half file, or one with the wrong bytes, must never look like a good one, to a person or to the store ([PACK-6](#packs)); and two packs that share a file may get it at once.
- *Today:* since track `fetch` (`a_download_lands_only_when_every_check_passed`, `two_downloads_to_one_dest_never_mix`, `a_link_at_the_part_name_is_never_followed`, `a_download_is_on_the_drive_before_it_is_named`, `a_progress_that_panics_leaves_nothing`; every failure test checks that nothing was left).

**NET-6.** A failed download MUST say why in plain words: the host couldn't be reached, nothing came for too long, the whole download took too long, the server's answer, the size, the hash, a cancel. It names the host, never the whole address, so a token in a link is never shown. It waits 10 s at most to connect, 30 s for an answer, and 30 s with no bytes, and every download has a deadline for the whole of it, which its caller sets (`Expect::within`), so a server that drips a byte at a time can't hold it. `fetch` blocks its caller, so it is called from a thread of its own (`spawn_blocking` in the server), never from async code, where it panics. Both calls take a cancel. While a download waits on the network, it sees a cancel or its deadline within 50 ms, even while nothing comes; reading a `file://`, between pieces; never while the finished file is put on the drive, and not while the OS checks a certificate (on macOS that can take a moment).
- *Why:* a musician must know whether to check the internet, try later or tell the pack's author; and a blocked runtime, or a thread held for good, would stop the app's server.
- *Today:* since track `fetch` (`errors_never_show_the_address`, `a_dropped_connection_says_so`, `a_server_that_goes_quiet_times_out`, `cancel_stops_at_once_and_leaves_nothing`, `get_can_be_cancelled_while_a_server_drips`, `a_download_that_takes_too_long_stops`, `a_call_from_async_code_panics`).

## Platforms

**PLAT-1.** music2 MUST build and pass every check on macOS on Apple silicon (the reference platform: judged timings, the look, native menus) and on Linux x86_64 with the glibc it was built against (the development platform). Windows is designed for in every OS piece and built in its own step. Timings are judged on the [reference Mac](#acceptance-gates) only; other machines run the same gates and record their timings with the machine named.
- *Why:* the work moved to a Linux server; the Mac stays where music2 is used and timed.
- *Today:* since the platform step: Linux on the development server, macOS by the owner's run of `ci/check.sh`. Linux passes every check from that step's slice S8 ([PLAT-6](#platforms)).

**PLAT-2.** Rust code that differs between operating systems MUST live in the `m2-os` crate (`core/os`). Outside it, Rust code other than tests MUST NOT name an OS: no `cfg` on the OS, no `libc`, no `std::os`, no `extern "C"` block, no `$HOME`. The desktop shell (`apps/music2-desktop/src`) and the gates' fault injection are the exceptions. Tests may use OS-only fixtures under `cfg`. The web UI differs only in its keys and labels (⌘ or Ctrl, "Show in Finder" or "Show in Folder"), each spelled by `web/src/lib/native.ts` (`chord`, `opt`, `shifted`, `keyName`, `mod`, `revealLabel`) from `isMac`; in what Ctrl+Backspace does in a text field (the word before, where ⌘⌫ on a Mac goes to the line's start); in its font (Inter off the Mac, the Mac's own font on it, by `data-os`, which `main.tsx` sets before the first render); and, in the Mac app only (`data-app`), in the top bar's 80 px inset for the window's buttons, the time shown from 1312 px rather than 1212 (since `studio`'s Record button), and the song name's wider clearance. The shell's menu bar is one per OS (`apps/music2-desktop/src/menu.rs`): the Mac's with its app menu and Window menu; elsewhere Quit ends File and About ends Help.
- *Why:* one place per OS to read, port and check; later steps write each OS piece once.
- *Today:* since the platform step's slices S5, S6 and S8: `m2-os` holds thread identity, the plugin main loop, the disk calls, the lock, the machine's identity, the folders and file-system events, and `core/os/tests/one_place.rs` checks the rule, with an empty allow-list. Since `next` it holds MIDI input too (`m2_os::midi`, through `midir`, behind the server's `midi` feature): one file, the OS's differences inside `midir`.

**PLAT-3.** `m2-os` MUST do what the OS does, with the strongest call the volume accepts (`F_FULLFSYNC`, else `fsync`), or return `Unsupported` naming what. It never emulates: no copy for a clone, no compare-then-rename for a swap, no walk for events. Those fallbacks and their rules stay with the caller.
- *Why:* an emulation hidden in the OS layer would change a rule's meaning on one OS without the rule saying so.
- *Today:* since the platform step's slices S5, S6 and S8, for what `m2-os` holds (thread identity, the plugin main loop, the folders, the disk calls, the lock, the machine's identity, file-system events). The watcher's walks and its limit on names stay with it (`crates/server/src/graph/events.rs`, `watcher.rs`).

**PLAT-4.** A pure offline render MUST give the same bits on every supported OS and CPU. Host code that makes audio for a render, and song code run by QuickJS, MUST compute every transcendental function with the `libm` crate, the same code as `std.wasm`'s math. One record of render hashes serves every OS (`core/session/tests/renders.json`, `e2e/renders.sha256`).
- *Why:* an export must not depend on whose computer made it. Each OS's math library rounds some results differently in the last bit (glibc against `libm`: 0.04% to 9.7% of inputs, measured).
- *Today:* since the platform step ([RENDER-2](#offline)).

**PLAT-5.** Every kernel module music2 ships (`std/kernels/std.wasm`, and each of its own packs' module, [ABI-10](#kernel-abi-reservations)) MUST rebuild to the same bytes in any folder on any supported OS with the toolchain `rust-toolchain.toml` pins, and hold no machine path. A commit that changes one says that the kernel hashes changed (a pack's is also a new version, [PACK-21](#packs)).
- *Why:* a kernel's hash is part of its identity ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)); a rebuild must never change it by accident.
- *Today:* since the platform step for std.wasm, since track packcode for every module: every crate of the kernels' workspace has its manifest inside the workspace's folder (the kit, `m2-dsp`, std's crate, each pack's crate under `std/kernels-src/packs/`), so cargo hashes relative paths, and `std/build.sh --check` rebuilds them all in another folder and compares; `ci/wasm-test.sh` compares every crate's `-C metadata` built in two folders. ABI-10 says how they are built.

**PLAT-6.** `ci/check.sh` is the full check, the same script on Linux and macOS: the build, every Rust test, clippy with warnings denied (on Linux also for the macOS target), the desktop app's tests, the web build, every browser suite, std's tests and rebuild, and the offline renders against `e2e/renders.sha256`; on macOS also the timing gates `ci/gates.txt` names. A check passes only with the test plugins present ("PASS without plugins" is not a pass). Every slice passes it on Linux. The owner runs it on a Mac at the end of each step, and before a branch that changes macOS-only code is merged. Until it passes there, the step's commit message and `HANDOFF.md` say "macOS: unverified since <commit>".
- *Why:* a hosted Mac can't judge real-time timings and has no copy of the test plugins; the owner's Mac is checked by hand, as rarely as is safe.
- *Today:* since the platform step. Linux passes it since that step's slice S8, first at `9f0b19a` (twice, with no retry); before S8 it failed only where `docs/designs/platform/design.md` §13.1 said a later slice fixes it. On Linux it also opens the desktop app on an invisible Xvfb screen. At the step's end Linux passed it at `63c45da`, with no retry, and both passed at `1750322`: the owner's Mac (MacBookPro18,1, macOS 26.6.2) on its second run, the gates included; its first run, at `b90bacf`, failed two tests that assumed Linux. Step 7 passed it on Linux at `ffc36aa`, and the `sounds` merge at `e108e46`, each with the gates and with no retry. On the owner's Mac step 7 passed it at `cbac57c` (2026-10-17), the gates included, on its first run. At `85b069a` (2026-10-17) the Mac passed 11 of 13 rows: G7f store and one songs-folder test failed, and `macfix` fixed both. Since then every track of steps 8 and 9 passed it on Linux with the gates and no retry, the last at `6d212a8` (track ex). The owner's Mac ran all of them at `e7518f4` (2026-10-18) and passed 11 of 13 rows: two tests failed (a take's steps looked up by its folder's spelled path, and the plugin window test asking about a window another process owns), fixed on branch `mf2`, merged since; and G12 failed only its set-up, the output at 44.1 kHz. The Mac ran it again at `a1e3c77` (2026-10-25) and passed 13 of 16 rows: the Rust tests (the plugin host's background state read wrongly on macOS, ten listens at once at background priority, a fix's path through `/var`, a test that waited a fixed time), the browser suites (`bus-mix`, a known flake; `sx-follow` passed on its retry) and the gates (G12's set-up, again at 44.1 kHz; G13 landed no listen in its minute) failed; branch `mf3` fixes them ([build-notes-mac.md](designs/agents/build-notes-mac.md)). One more Mac run, at 48 kHz, is owed. The last Linux run with the gates is at `c928f5d` (2026-10-24, no retry). Since `152c39d` a pull request runs the PR tier on GitHub (`ci/check.sh --pr`, a job per step of `ci/pr.sh`): no timing gates, and none of the tests `ci/nightly-tests.txt` and `e2e/run.mjs` tag nightly. The nightly tier (`ci/check.sh --nightly`, `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`) runs every test on GitHub's Linux each night, and on a hosted Mac without the test plugins once the repo is public. The timing gates run only on a quiet machine (`ci/nightly.sh`) and in the owner's Mac run.

**PLAT-7.** Every folder music2 writes outside a project (songs, your presets, caches, the shell's `~/.music2`) MUST be under `$HOME` when `HOME` is set, so a test or a suite isolates itself by setting `HOME`. Outside the desktop shell those folders come from `m2_os::dirs`. The cache folder MUST be written only by a program whose `main` keeps it (`m2_os::dirs::keep_cache`: the app, `m2`, the headless server and `m2-gates`): in any other process `dirs::cache()` is none, and what would go there (the hash cache, peaks, warped renders, previews, what was measured of samples) is kept in memory or in a folder its caller gives, so no test writes the cache folder of the `HOME` it runs with, even a plain `cargo test` run with yours.
