Folders music2 only reads (the system's CLAP folders, files bundled with the app) are exempt. A new song's folder name MUST count as taken when its folder holds the same name in other capitals, on every OS, as on macOS: New Song numbers past it (a song called "Night drive" beside a folder `NIGHT DRIVE` goes into `Night drive 2`), and `m2 new` refuses it ("already exists").
- *Why:* a test must never write to the user's real folders. Song folders stay safe to copy between computers (the owner's answer to the platform design's Q1). A test can't set `HOME` for itself: the tests of a binary share one process and its environment, so a default that reaches for `HOME` reaches for yours.
- *Today:* every such folder is under `$HOME`; they come from `m2_os::dirs` since the platform step's slice S6, which also moved Linux's cache folder from `~/Library/Caches/music2` to `~/.cache/music2`. Your presets are in `~/Music/music2/Presets` on every OS since track br (`dirs::presets`, [FMT-22](#presets)), and installed packs in `~/Music/music2/Packs` since packs (`dirs::packs`, [PACK-6](#packs)). `XDG_*` folders are not read yet. Only a program keeps the cache folder since track samples' fixes (D17: before, over a hundred tests wrote hashes, peaks, renders and part files in it): `core/os/src/dirs.rs` (`only_a_program_keeps_the_cache`), and `ci/pr.sh rust` and `ci/check.sh` fail when a test run leaves a file in its scratch `HOME`'s cache folder (`ci/cache-left.sh`). A part file a program leaves when it ends mid-stretch (a kill) stays in `renders/` (gaps). A name in other capitals is taken by New Song since slice S2 and by `m2 new` since S6 (`crates/server/src/project.rs`: `a_name_taken_in_other_capitals_is_taken`, `create_at_refuses_a_name_taken_in_other_capitals`). In a folder that can be written but not listed only the exact name can be checked (`a_song_is_made_in_a_folder_that_cant_be_listed`).

**PLAT-8.** A release MUST be built by `.github/workflows/release.yml` from a pushed tag `v<version>` that matches the app's version, with no hand step but publishing its draft: `music2.app` for Apple silicon Macs (zipped), and for Linux on x86_64 an AppImage, a `.deb` and a `.tar.gz` of `m2` with what it needs but the app (for the AppImage's users, and anyone who wants the command line only), with a `SHA256SUMS` file and the notes in `docs/releases/<version>.md`. Each MUST run with no source tree: it carries std (with `std.wasm` and its kit, [LIB-8](#library)), the packs music2 ships ([PACK-15](#packs)), the templates, the examples, the UI and `m2`, and the plugin host is the program itself run again ([HOST-1](#pluginhost)). Each MUST also carry music2's licences and the notices of the code inside it that others wrote, made as it is built from its own lockfiles; and the `.deb` MUST ask for the glibc its programs need, the newest that isn't a weak need, computed from the built programs. The programs find what they carry from where they are, in order: the override (`$M2_STD`, `$M2_PACKS` for the packs music2 ships ([PACK-15](#packs)), `$M2_TEMPLATES`, `$M2_EXAMPLES`); beside the program in a bundle, `<program's folder>/../Resources/<name>` (`music2.app/Contents/MacOS/music2`) or `<program's folder>/../lib/music2/<name>` (`/usr/bin/music2` and `/usr/lib/music2/` in the `.deb`, the same inside the AppImage, `bin/m2` and `lib/music2/` in the `.tar.gz`), where Tauri puts its resources; else the source tree it was built from. Until signing is set up the Mac app is signed ad hoc and not notarized; the workflow signs and notarizes when the repo's Apple secrets are there, and only on a tag.
- *Why:* people who don't build from source need a download, and a downloaded app has no source tree to fall back on. One workflow from a tag makes every release the same way, and a person still decides when it goes out.
- *Today:* since the release track (0.1.0): `ci/release.sh` builds and checks each platform's files, as the workflow runs it; a pull request that changes the workflow, the bundle's config or how resources are found builds both platforms, without a release. `crates/server/src/resources.rs`'s tests fake each layout (`bundled`); `apps/music2-desktop/tests/bundle.rs` pins the config. Built and run on Linux under Xvfb from the `.deb`'s files and the AppImage; the Mac's build is owed to the workflow's first run (`docs/designs/release/build-notes.md`).

## Deferred, with triggers

Each item below is decided where this page says so, and built when its trigger arrives.

| Deferred | Trigger |
|---|---|
| Moving view, timeline view, lens, cable and effect planners, labels and `render.rs` into `crates/editor` | Built at the end of step 6: they are in `crates/editor`. |
| Gestures and tools (quantize, transpose, humanize, generators, track templates) as sandboxed TypeScript package tools that emit Tx JSON | The Tx JSON has stayed stable through one feature cycle, and the first tool-shaped feature arrives (std/gen). First check that QuickJS per gesture fits the code-plane budget. |
| Keyed iteration helpers and better structural matching (`key()` ships in step 4): ID-13's former rule 4, a unique structural match on callee, scope and argument shape, ignoring bindings and literal values, for a call cut and pasted, and for a call of the same callee written on lines of its own beside another, which can take that call's instance today ([ID-13](#derived-identity)) | Users hear state reset after refactors or loop reorders, a wrong keep is heard (an effect's state jumping), or generated banks need stable instances. |
| A saved editor index, and canvas positions in `layout.json` | Manual canvas layout. Track order and colour use track keys as soon as that feature lands. |
| Undo that survives a restart | Crash recovery is required, or users ask for undo after reopening. The journal and inverse-Tx format already allow it. |
| Playing a last-good copy (in `.music2/`) of a project whose code doesn't compile | Projects are often left broken, and "opens silent until fixed" becomes a complaint. |
| Package fetching (the registry, design packs §8), several bundled std versions, kernel ids relative to their package. `music2.lock` with content hashes and the store are built since packs ([PACK-6](#packs) to [PACK-9](#packs)) | Fetching: the owner's choice of where the bytes are kept (design packs D1, D10). Several std versions: the first std release, after people have songs, that changes existing renders ([FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions)). |
| `m2 upgrade` ([FMT-3a](#manifest-and-versions)), once called `m2 migrate` | Fired: the first SDK break after format 1 is wave 3's `@m2/core` 2.0.0. It is the track after wave 3, shipped in the same release (the owner, 2026-10-31). |
| Kernel ABI v1's reserved shapes: event outputs (inputs are built, [ABI-8](#kernel-abi-reservations)), `k_save`/`k_load`, `k_latency`/`k_tail` ([ABI-2](#kernel-abi-reservations), `docs/kernel-abi.md`) | Each with the first kernel that needs it: an arpeggiator, a kernel with state worth saving, a kernel with latency (a plugin's latency is compensated since ph-3, [RENDER-17](#latency)). |
| rtkit, and `RLIMIT_RTTIME` with `SIGXCPU` ignored, for Linux engine threads ([RENDER-28a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)): today they ask for `SCHED_FIFO` within `RLIMIT_RTPRIO` only | Someone plays music2 live on a Linux desktop. |
| Plugins on engine threads, with CLAP's `is_audio_thread` true on every engine thread and the probe's tests ([HOST-2](#pluginhost)) | A song with plugins measured over its budget on one thread. |
| `os_workgroup_testcancel` at each fill ([RENDER-28a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)): cpal's `DeviceChanged` already replaces the player | A dropout traced to a cancelled workgroup. |
| Clip stretching as a package kernel | Built in track sx: `std:stretch`, an offline kernel ([ABI-2](#kernel-abi-reservations)), run in the background in a live session ([RENDER-30](#audio-lanes)), and Follow tempo in the clip menu. |
| Disk streaming and the render cache ([RENDER-30](#audio-lanes)) | Built in step 9's io turn, with `read_at` and no memory map. |
| Exact range renders through kernel-only checkpoints (wasm memory snapshots cached by plan hash) | Agents need range renders exact in their tails. |
| Plugin output events as automation (recording a gesture into a lane), VST3 and AU. Plugin windows and state autosave are built (ph-2) | Automation recording from plugins, or a plugin only as VST3 or AU. |
| The `poly` op's `mode`, `retrigger`, `steal` and `glide` options: mono, legato and glide on any voice graph ([RENDER-16](#voices)) | Built in step 9's synth turns (track pm), on the arena's voice pools. std's `mono()` is one legato poly since std 0.3.0. Per-note slides written in the notes (`from_pitch`, [RENDER-14](#events)) and glide in `"poly"` mode stay deferred. |
| MIDI and computer-keyboard input, audio input with monitoring, recording takes as data Tx | Built: MIDI and the computer's keys in step 8 (`next`, `studio`); the sound input, hearing yourself ([MON-8](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)) and audio takes in step 9's io turn ([RENDER-30a](#audio-lanes)). |
| Note expression, controller, parameter and raw MIDI events ([RENDER-14](#events)) beyond note on and off | MPE editing, recording controllers into clips, or sample-accurate plugin automation. Built in track pm: a MIDI keyboard's pitch wheel and mod wheel, live (`Midi` from live input, `Note.modWheel`). Left: recording them (note expression data, a format bump), other controllers, `NoteExpression` and `Param`. |
| Chasing notes already sounding at a seek, and preroll | The first producer complaint about missing held notes after a seek or loop. |
| Incremental compile (one cache per module, by hash) | The compile goes over 100 ms p95 at 50 tracks ([PLANE-4](#planes-of-change)). |
| Note ids, linked clips and a pattern pool, MPE per note, exact rational times | Per-note expression editing, a linked-clip or pattern workflow, triplet quantize, or merging note by note. Each is a `format` bump. |
| Events generated by code ([ABI-4](#kernel-abi-reservations)) | std/gen or generative patterns. |
| Mute and solo inside a track (in `key()` frames) and on the root module ([MON-1](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)) | A producer needs to mute one part of a kit, or a sound written in `song.ts`. |
| Override latches on descriptor controls that survive a restart ([MON-6](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)) | Users ask for latches to survive reopening. Needs ControlIds that outlive a session. |
| Sending deltas to clients instead of refetching the whole state | When UI update latency is measured over budget. |
| Track freezing, and bounce as a Tx (render, then an asset, then a clip). Render taps and meters are built (8b.8, [Engine shape](#engine-shape-step-7)) | The resample loop in the UI. |
| Content-addressed asset storage, deduplication, git LFS guidance. An import of bytes already in `assets/` uses that file (`studio`, [FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes)) | Sample packs, or projects with large stem sets. |
| `[locks]` in the manifest for agent variants | Agent variants ship. Globs over keys (`data/vox/**`) are already stable. |
| Units defined by packages | Only if a real need appears. |
| Windows: `core/os/src/windows/`, the Windows twins of every piece in `m2-os`, the tests' `cfg(unix)` fixes, and the build (MSVC, QuickJS under `cl.exe`, `llvm-rc` for the desktop app) ([PLAT-1](#platforms)) | The owner asks for Windows users. |
| The `XDG_*` folders (music, cache) ([PLAT-7](#platforms)). Linux packaging (deb, AppImage) and a Linux bundle in `bundled()` are built since the release track ([PLAT-8](#platforms)) | A Linux user asks for them, or someone runs the app on a Linux desktop that moves them. |
| Stored spelling on case-folding Linux volumes (vfat, exFAT, casefold folders): `m2_os::fs::stored_name` answers nothing on Linux today | A project on such a volume, or a volume to test on. |
| `FICLONE` clones on btrfs and XFS: Linux has no clone today, so a commit copies each file's before bytes | A Linux user on such a volume with large plugin states. |
| An event source that tells the watcher "I can't cover this project" at once (inotify out of watches after start, the root unmounted). Today each asks for one walk (a Rescan); a change the events then miss is found by the 5 s walks, and two such misses move the project to walks ([WRITE-19](#outside-writers)) | A user report of a machine out of inotify watches. |
| An exFAT disk image on the Mac, to run the macOS fallbacks there (the save without a swap, the barrier's `fsync`, the folder syncs a volume refuses, the clone, the lock) | A Mac user with a project on an exFAT or network volume reports a problem. |
| An allocator for song code that counts the bytes asked for, so QuickJS's 256 MB limit falls at the same place on every OS ([RENDER-2](#offline)) | A song near the limit, or one that catches running out of memory, builds differently on two computers. |

## Order of work

Steps 3 and 4 may run in parallel; everything else is in order.

1. **Close today's silent paths** (2 days). **Built.**
   - The session's warnings reach every client: the app's state and every graph event over the WebSocket carry them, the app shows them in its status pill's popover, and every CLI command that opens a project prints them with the commands that fix them ([DIAG-4](#delivery)).
   - Lane data no lane reads, left behind when a track or a lane is renamed in code, is the Warning `data.unclaimed`, with Relink and Delete fixes: `m2 relink`, `m2 delete-data`, and the strip's Move and Delete. Each fix is a checked, undoable session edit, applied only when asked ([DIAG-6](#fixes)).
   - `revert()` uses the current origin of the control at the carried position, with the regression test ([ID-12](#derived-identity)).
2. **Write the contract** (this page) and have it reviewed.
3. **Live step A** (2–3 weeks). **Built.** Land 3a before 3b, and keep the offline path untouched.
   - **3a, runtime (built):** the engine and transport clocks, with the engine running while stopped; an integer transport and loop ends computed from beats; lane schedulers that fill event buffers and own their sounding notes; a jump that never resets kernels; a new renderer activated at the audio thread's own position, with a 5 ms crossfade; `SwapLane` for notes and control lanes when the old and new `Flat` graphs are equal; the range render for agents.
   - **3b, around the runtime (built):** gesture leases that survive saves which don't touch them; the session as an actor; `PluginHost` with its whole trait and the in-process executor on the real main thread; plugin instances carried across rebuilds.
4. **Format v1 and SDK keys, in one change** (1–2 weeks). **Built.**
   - Track, lane and plugin keys, and `key()` frames; `Module { key, label }`; a context that exposes the key, never the label.
   - `data/<module>/` with clip ids and lane units; the manifest with `format` read first, `sample_rate`, and caret requirements for every dependency; the live engine at `sample_rate`.
   - `DataPath` and its classes; snapshots that read the explicit set, and a build that imports only from them; the canonical form of data files, written in `core/song`; the arrangement and meter rules.
   - Per-lane data errors that keep the last accepted data; the asset hash cache and pins, so compile no longer decodes audio; asset names; plugin parameters addressed `<name>_<id>` and bound by id; `plugin.missing`; required state sidecars; the diagnostic model, with fixes still named session edits.
   - Convert the 5 fixtures by script, then delete `slug()`.
5. **Descriptors and cells** (about 2 weeks). **Built.**
   - `component()` with declared inputs, controls, settings, defaults and editor metadata, sidechain ports and smooth controls; std rewritten on it.
   - One const per declared control with an `(instance, slot)` origin; ComponentIds in frames; the unit table generated from `core.ts`; runtime cells, written through per-cell slots, ramped where declared.
   - Delete `[[effect]] args`, `EFFECT_CATEGORIES`, the `cable.rs` unit table and the `view.rs` range guesses.
6. **Tx and the write path** (about 3 weeks, then 1 week of mechanical moves). **Built.**
   - The `save`/`Tx` split, with validated paths, the `Clips` op and base texts; the project lock (no forwarding), its read-only open and `lock.unsupported`; content-hash bases.
   - `GraphDelta` with node names an insertion can't shift, and `Identical`, `Values`, `Confined` and `Seen`; the journal, with host names, staging in `.music2/tx/`, and recovery as a diagnostic.
   - `(code_rev, data_rev)`, and a data-only path that skips QuickJS, chosen by path class; the watcher's settle rule; `data.claimed` from Tx.
   - `Session::open` on a broken draft.
   - Port the 7 gestures, step 1's two fixes and the bypasses; fixes become Tx; delete the guess at `graph.rs:341-348`; make `write_whole` private.
   - First new builders: Relink of a whole module folder, rename label, automate a knob. Then the move into `crates/editor`.
6b. **The platform layer** (about 2 weeks). Development moved to a Linux server, so Linux is made green there first, natively, and the Mac stays green. **Built** when its gates pass.
   - `m2-os` (`core/os`), one folder per OS ([PLAT-2](#platforms)): renames and the durable sync, the lock and machine checks, file-system events (FSEvents, inotify), the plugin main loop, the folders.
   - The same render bits on every OS through `libm` ([PLAT-4](#platforms)); a reproducible `std.wasm` and a pinned toolchain ([PLAT-5](#platforms)); saves that a crash can't break on volumes without an atomic exchange, and new audio made durable before a song names it.
   - One check script for both OSes, `ci/check.sh`, run by hand on the owner's Mac, timing gates included ([PLAT-6](#platforms)).
   - Steps 8a and 9 add their OS pieces to `m2-os`; Windows is designed for in each piece and built in its own step.
7. **The engine** (4–6 weeks). **Built** on Linux; done once the owner's Mac run passes.
   - `Plan` split from `DataSet`; SiteId matching, InstancePath and RuntimeId.
   - The arena, with prepare and activate, keeping instances whose RuntimeId and shape (wasm hash included) match.
   - Arrangement switches prepared off the audio thread; persistent CLAP instances behind `PluginHost`, with state loads into them as a plan is prepared, and offline renders that reuse them.
8. **Partitions, then producer features.** **Built** on Linux; done once the owner's Mac run passes (G12's Mac sweep, `ci/sweep.sh`, included).
   - **8a (built):** parallel partitions on a thread pool, right after the arena, with renders that don't depend on the thread count. Its offline half (exports on every core) and its live half (playback on real-time engine threads, a sound that breaks goes silent alone, G12).
   - **8b (built):** each a Tx builder or an engine feature on this base: add, duplicate, delete and rename track; the mixer with monitoring mute and solo plus the `muted` track option; automate a knob; the tempo bar, with its lease; clip editing by id, with lane leases; audition. With them (tracks `taste-next`, `8b-ui`, `polish`, `next`, `studio`): the look, meters, the metronome, the keyboard piano and MIDI keyboards, Settings, audio import, recording notes, MIDI files and stems, and help in plain words.
9. **After the core,** synth and plugin turns alternate with audio and sample turns. **Built** on Linux (2026-10-18, every track at `main` `3c26fcd`); done once the owner's Mac run passes.
   - synth and plugin: the plugin host process (built in ph-1), then plugin windows (with state autosave and output events, built in ph-2), then latency compensation (built in ph-3), then the `poly` op's mono, legato, glide and steal options (built in track pm), then MIDI input (notes and the sustain pedal built in step 8's `next`; the pitch and mod wheels built in track pm);
   - audio and sample: disk streaming (built in track io), then kernel ABI v1 and the sampler (built in track ab, with one sandbox a box), then stretching as an offline kernel (built in track sx, with Follow tempo), then recording (notes built in step 8's `studio`; audio built in track io).
   - between them, one path for faults (built in track ex): every fault found while sound is made goes through the fault ring and is one problem, live ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps), the sound input's too, [RENDER-30a](#audio-lanes)); offline, any fault that changed the sound fails the render ([RENDER-1](#offline)).
   - Then agents beside the musician, the [agents design](designs/agents/design.md)'s five tracks (`ag-steps`, `ag-fwd`, `ag-see`, `ag-routes`, `ag-listen`): **built** on Linux (2026-10-24), with the [Agents](#agents) rules and gate G13.
   - Next: Windows, as its own step ([PLAT-1](#platforms), the Deferred row).

   TypeScript package tools and the other deferred items come in when their triggers arrive.

## Acceptance gates

A step is done when its gates pass in `ci/check.sh` on Linux and on the owner's Mac ([PLAT-6](#platforms)). Timed gates are judged in the Mac's run, which runs the gates `ci/gates.txt` names, in release, on the reference projects. **p16** and **p50** are the 16- and 50-track reference projects: tracks cycle through std `bass` (with `delay`), `pluck` and `pad` (with `reverb`), playing `examples/first-song`'s notes repeated to several minutes. The script that generates them is checked in with step 3. Timings are taken on the **reference Mac**, the oldest supported Apple-silicon Mac (an M1), at 48 kHz. Other machines run the same gates and record their timings with the machine named; only the reference Mac's are judged ([PLAT-1](#platforms)).

| Step | Gates |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Passed.** Lane data left behind by a rename shows in the app with its fixes, and in the output of every CLI command that opens the project. Relink makes it play, and undo moves it back; Delete removes it, and undo restores its bytes. A test inserts a soundless call at the top of `song.ts` after a knob edit in `tracks/bass.ts`: undo succeeds and restores the bytes, and `can_undo` agrees with what undo does. |
| 2 | The owner has reviewed this page. Later changes cite rule ids. |
| 3 | On p16 at 1:00 and on `examples/with-plugins`: a seek is audible within 50 ms; a 4-bar loop at 127 bpm runs 10 minutes with no tail cut and no drift against the grid; a note edit is audible within compile time + 20 ms with no tails cut; deleting or shortening a sounding note while playing leaves no note stuck; an added effect is audible within compile time + 100 ms with no jump back; adding or removing an effect while a sine plays makes no sample-to-sample step larger than twice the sine's own largest; after stop, a reverb tail decays instead of freezing; previews never wait on a compile; an outside save to another file during a drag doesn't interrupt the drag. A range render from bar 64 takes no longer than one of the same length from bar 1 (within 10%), and says it is approximate. The bit-identity test ([RENDER-33](#the-bit-identity-test)) passes on every pure fixture. Every pure fixture's offline render is byte-identical to before step 3. |
| 4 | Each pure fixture, converted by the script, renders byte-identical to its render before conversion. Changing a track's label changes no file outside its `track()` call and no audio, and no property of the track's context or any handle returns the label. Renaming a track key by hand raises `data.unclaimed` on its folder, and its Relink restores the sound; the same holds, separately, for a lane key. A lane file with an unknown field plays its last accepted data in a running session, and silences only that lane on open, with an Error naming the file and the field. A manifest with `format = 2` and an unknown key gives `format.newer`, and the project doesn't open. Changing an imported `.js` file, or an import spelled with other capitals, fails to compile on every OS. Reading and writing each canonical data file gives the same bytes, and permuting any array in a fixture renders the same bits. Removing a plugin from the search path silences only that node, and every other track renders the same bits as before. A plugin build that renames and swaps parameter names leaves every connection on the same CLAP id. Compile time doesn't depend on asset size. Editing `layout.json` creates no revision. The live engine runs at the project's `sample_rate`. |

Every declared control, written or inherited, appears as a knob with its declared range. `import { delay as echo }` is recognised as `delay`. | agents | Each track of the [agents design](designs/agents/design.md) adds its gate here. Track 1 (`ag-steps`): an outside save of a text file that builds is one undo step, which ⌘Z and the notice's Undo take back; an import makes one step and no outside step; an undo for a step that is no longer the newest changes nothing; the notice and the glow show an outside step (`e2e/ag-notice.mjs`); the offline renders still equal `e2e/renders.sha256`. Track 2 (`ag-fwd`): every `m2` write command works with the app open and lands as one undo step in the app, named by `--as` and `--why` (`apps/m2/tests/forward.rs`, `apps/m2/tests/edit.rs`); no lease, take or unsaved text of the musician's ends because of an agent's write, which is refused on arrival or taken back at its settle, never heard (`crates/server/src/graph/busy_tests.rs`); `m2 undo` takes back only its own step. Track 3 (`ag-see`): `m2 focus` says what the page selected, by key and by note, and focus and pointers change no file; `m2 watch` prints one JSON line an event and ends when the app quits; a pointer never moves the view or the selection; V plays the newest outside step's Before from memory while the loop plays, writing nothing, and an agent's write is busy while the pill is open; `m2 watch` follows one song (`e2e/ag-focus.mjs`, `apps/m2/tests/watch.rs`, `crates/server/src/graph/compare_tests.rs`). Track 4 (`ag-routes`): every route is declared once, in the one table the router, the 423, the 403 and `m2 routes` are made from, and every example body reads; the tools key gets 403 on every route not for agents; `m2 call` reaches an action with no command of its own, names its step, and refuses the musician's controls before sending (`crates/server/src/api_tests.rs`, `apps/m2/tests/call.rs`). Track 5 (`ag-listen`): copy taps change no bit of any pure fixture; the measures read known signals as EBU Tech 3341 and BS.1770 say (a 1 kHz sine at −23 dBFS reads −23 LUFS, a peak between samples is found, pink noise has flat octave bands, mono correlates 1 and inverted −1), a meter warmed before its range reads a loud sound at its start as it is, and a sample that isn't a number is counted and read as 0; masking names two tracks in one band but none turned down in the mix, none that take turns, a ducked bass only where it meets the kick, and a clash beside a loud track's band (`crates/editor/src/listen_tests.rs`); `m2 listen` reports every track and the mix, writes nothing with `--with`, hears the listening mutes only with `--as-heard`, by key in the change too, and says so, compares the same bars across a change of tempo, measures a group bus as it reaches the mix, draws its picture, runs at background priority with its plugin host, one at a time, refuses what it doesn't know, and never waits on a busy app (`apps/m2/tests/listen.rs`). G13 (`m2-gates realtime --beside agents`): p50 plays live while `m2 listen` runs back to back on it (every other listen on `examples/with-plugins` when its plugins are there) and forwarded `m2 set` edits land in an app beside it; judged everywhere, no crash or non-finite sample, that listens and edits ran, and every listen and its plugin host at background priority; its dropouts and callback times are recorded, on the Mac too until its first runs there are recorded, then judged as G12's (no dropout, p99 at most 70% of the period). **Passed on Linux** at `c928f5d` (2026-10-24, `ci/check.sh --gates=auto`, the 8 gate runs, nothing passed only on a retry). The Mac run is owed. |

These gates keep running in `ci/check.sh` once their step lands:
- offline determinism: two renders of each pure fixture are byte-identical ([RENDER-2](#offline));
- the bit-identity test, from step 3 ([RENDER-33](#the-bit-identity-test));
- every id-bearing field in the format v1 schemas is a key, a clip id or a CLAP parameter id, from step 4 ([ID-16](#derived-identity));
- the audio thread runs under the allocation and lock trap, from step 5 ([RENDER-28](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps));
- every value the view returns names its home, from step 5 ([HOME-1](#homes-of-values));
- every OS renders the bits `e2e/renders.sha256` records, and `std.wasm` rebuilds to its bytes in any folder, from the platform step ([PLAT-4](#platforms), [PLAT-5](#platforms));
- Rust code outside `m2-os` and the desktop shell names no OS (`core/os/tests/one_place.rs`), from the platform step ([PLAT-2](#platforms));
- renders don't depend on the thread count, from step 8a ([RENDER-2](#offline));
- overlays never change an export, from step 8 ([MON-4](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).
